## Digest no longer trusts nested fields to update in lockstep with `match.status` — v1.0.96 (2026-07-22)

**FY22 — Day/session completion is now derived from `match.status`, not solely from each session's own `isComplete` flag**
`buildTestSessionCards()` (`components/DigestTab.tsx`) used `sess.isComplete` verbatim in three places — the cached-card reuse guard, the `isLiveSession` field on each rendered `SessionCard`, and the day-level `allComplete` check that decides whether a day collapses into a single "STUMPS" day-summary card. All three trusted that a session's own flag (whether explicitly authored on `inn.sessions` or derived by `deriveTestSessions()`) would always update in lockstep with `match.status` flipping to `"post-match"`. A real feed offers no such guarantee. Added one function-scoped helper, `effectivelyComplete(sess) => !isLive || sess.isComplete`, and routed all three call sites through it: while the match is still live, the per-session flag remains the sole source of truth (a live match can legitimately have some sessions done and others not); once the match itself is no longer live, every session is treated as complete regardless of what its own flag says, so a day whose last session's `isComplete` never independently flipped still collapses correctly the moment the match ends.

**FY23 — The match-summary card is now authoritative on `match.status === "post-match"` too, instead of silently rendering nothing when `result` is missing**
`buildMatchSummaryCard()` previously returned `null` whenever `!match.result`, with no regard for `match.status` — indistinguishable, from the outside, between "match still in progress, no summary yet" (expected) and "match finished, but `result` never arrived" (a gap that looks identical to a bug). Split into: `buildFullMatchSummaryCard(match, result, isDerived)` — the original card-building logic, now taking an explicit `result` parameter instead of reading `match.result` directly, so it can build from either a real or a derived result; `deriveMinimalMatchResult(match)` — deliberately conservative, only infers a winner for the unambiguous two-innings limited-overs case (chasing team's final score vs. the target), explicitly declining for Test matches or anything without exactly two recorded innings, since draws, ties, innings/follow-on wins and declarations aren't safely inferable from a scoreline alone; `buildPendingResultCard(match)` — a new `PendingResultCard` card kind (final scores shown, explicit "Final result pending" state) for whenever a real result is missing post-match and a minimal one can't be safely derived. `buildMatchSummaryCard(match, isLive)` now dispatches: real `result` → full card; still live → `null` (unchanged); otherwise → derived card (`isDerived: true`, with a small "Result inferred from final score" caption in `MatchSummaryCardView`) or the explicit pending card. `buildCards()` now passes `isLive` through to `buildMatchSummaryCard`, and the Test-day / non-Test-innings `visibleCards` filters pin `pending-result` cards alongside `match-summary` cards so they're never hidden by a day/innings selection.

**Verified** (direct `buildCards()` invocation via `npx tsx`, mirroring the diagnosis method — not a browser click-through, per the same reasoning as the diagnosis: mock data regenerates real-time offsets on every page load, so a stable before/after data snapshot is only guaranteed by calling the exported builder functions directly against constructed data):
- Scenario A — `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` cloned, `status` flipped to `"post-match"`, `result` populated, but Day 3's session `isComplete` deliberately left at its original stale `false`: Day 3 still collapsed into a single `day-summary` card (previously would have stayed as an individual, `isLiveSession: true` session card indefinitely), and no card anywhere in the output retained `isLiveSession: true`.
- Scenario B — same match, `status` flipped to `"post-match"`, Day 3's session properly finalized (`isComplete: true`), but `result` deliberately deleted: output's first card is `kind: "pending-result"` (previously: no match-summary card at all, a silent gap) showing the real final innings scores (`IND 450/8`, `ENG 199/10`, `ENG (2nd Inn) 88/4`).
- Scenario C — a genuinely-completed past T20I (`t20wc-2026-ind-pak`, real ground-truth result "IND by 6 wickets" noted before deletion), `status` set to `"post-match"`, `result` deleted: derived card shows `resultLine: "India won by 6 wickets"` with `isDerived: true`, exactly matching the deleted ground truth (152/4 chasing 150, 10−4=6 wickets in hand).

---

## Finished matches get a Digest-first tab bar with a retrospective, whole-match Digest — v1.0.97 (2026-07-22)

**FY24 — A finished match's first tab is now Digest, not Live; every finished match gets the same tab count**
`MatchTabs.tsx` gained a `firstTab` prop ("live" default, "digest" for finished matches) instead of Digest ever being appended as an extra conditional tab for them. `MatchView.tsx` now computes `isFinished = match.status === "post-match"` (deliberately narrower than `status !== "live"` — upcoming/pre-match fixtures have no result or innings data to build any kind of digest from, so their tab layout is untouched) and builds `TABS_ORDER` as `[digest, scorecard, info, (table)]` for finished matches, `defaultTab`/`restoredTab` fall back away from a stale saved `"live"` value once a match finishes. Live/upcoming matches keep today's exact tab list, default tab, and `showDigest` behavior byte-for-byte — verified no diff in behavior for `match.status === "live"`.

**FY25 — The team-names-with-final-score header moved from the (now-removed-for-finished-matches) Live tab into the Score tab**
`Scorecard.tsx` gained a `FinalScoreHeader` component — the exact team-rows-plus-result-banner block MatchView's old ball-less "Live" tab fallback used to show — rendered above the scorecard body whenever `match.innings.length > 0`, for any match status (a still-live match's Score tab shows the same header with the current, not-yet-final score). The existing `match.innings.length === 0` "Scorecard not available" fallback is completely untouched, per explicit instruction.

**FY26 — New post-match Digest: compact lead-in, single match-wide turning point, whole-match performance card, then the existing day/session cards with retrospective framing layered on top**
`DigestTab.tsx`'s exported `buildPostMatchDigest(match, allBalls)` is the new entry point for `match.status === "post-match"` (wired into the `DigestTab` component's existing `buildCards()`-vs-new branch; `isLive`/upcoming path is 100% untouched). For a match with `innings.length > 0`: (1) the exact same `buildMatchSummaryCard(match, false)` lead-in from the v1.0.96 fix (full result, or a safely-derived minimal one, or an explicit pending state); (2) a new `TurningPointCard` — `findTurningPoint()` diffs consecutive `calculateWinProbForMatch()` points across the WHOLE match (not per-session/day) and surfaces the single ball with the largest win-probability swing, omitted (not stubbed) when there's no ball data to compute a swing from; (3) a new `PerformanceCard` — best batting and bowling performance across all innings, via a newly-extracted `computeMatchTopPerformers()` shared with the lead-in so the two can never disagree; (4) the existing `buildTestSessionCards`/`buildOverGroupCards` day/session cards, called exactly as the live path already calls them, with one retrospective sentence appended per card via a brand-new, independent `applyRetrospectiveFraming()` pass keyed to each card's plain positional index in the array — deliberately NOT touching `buildNarrative()`/`buildOverSummary()`/`buildDayReport()` or their existing per-session/per-day anti-repeat indexing (the v1.0.7x fix) in any way.

**FY27 — Honest fallback for the confirmed 7-of-12 empty-`innings` gap; graceful (not broken-looking) degradation for the other 5**
For `match.innings.length === 0`, `buildPostMatchDigest` returns a single `SimpleRecapCard` — final score read from `match.result`'s `teamA/teamBRuns/Wickets` fields (since there's no `innings` data to read a score from) plus the existing one-line `match.summary` blurb, explicitly labeled "Simple recap," never styled like an empty or broken full Digest. Separately (and not requested to be fixed, just discovered and disclosed): of the 5 Past matches that DO have `innings.length > 0`, none currently have any ball-by-ball data either (`battingCard`/`bowlingCard` aggregates only) — for these, the turning-point and day/session cards have nothing to compute from and are correctly omitted by the same graceful-omission logic as any other no-ball-data case, while the lead-in and performance cards (which only need battingCard/bowlingCard, not balls) still render normally.

**Verified**:
- `npx tsx` direct calls to `buildPostMatchDigest()` against a synthetic 5-day finished Test match (innings-2's real ball data reused verbatim across 5 fake days, so every day has IDENTICAL underlying runs/wickets/keyBall) — confirms the existing `day % 3`-indexed anti-repeat system still produces 5/5 distinct day-overview lines with no code changes to it, and the new retrospective layer produces distinct lines for every ADJACENT day pair (its own 4-phrase bank naturally repeats every 4 non-adjacent days, same periodic-cycling characteristic the pre-existing systems already have).
- Same method against a finished T20 match (`ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`, 17 over-group cards): 17/17 distinct `overSummary` strings — zero repetition anywhere across the whole match once the retrospective layer is composed with the existing per-over variant indexing.
- Live-browser pass (Claude-in-Chrome) confirms: a Past match with `innings.length > 0` but no ball data shows lead-in + performance cards (turning-point/day-cards correctly absent, no gap or broken UI); a Past match with `innings.length === 0` shows the `SimpleRecapCard` labeled "Simple recap"; the Score tab shows the new final-score header above the scorecard body (or above the unchanged "Scorecard not available" message) for both; a currently-live match's tab bar and Live tab render byte-for-byte as before.

---

## ScoreBar's chase line is now gated on `match.status === "live"`, not just on data being present — v1.0.98 (2026-07-22)

**FY28 — The "need N off M balls · RRR X.XX" chase line was rendering on finished matches, sometimes for a match that ended days ago**
`ScoreBar.tsx`'s second row (`{i2 && need !== null && rrr !== null && (...)}`) computed `need`/`rrr` purely from static innings totals (`i1.runs`, `i2.runs`/`overs`) with no check on `match.status` at all — so any non-Test match with a second innings that had started (`i2` exists) showed a live-style chase target regardless of whether the match was actually still in progress. Confirmed live on `bbl-2526-scorchers-sixers` (finished, `innings.length > 0`, no ball-by-ball data) before the fix: the header showed "Sixers need 9 off 4 balls · RRR 13.50" on a match that had been over for good. Test matches were never affected (the `isTest` guard already forces `need`/`rrr` to `null` regardless of status), and matches with `innings.length === 0` (e.g. `ipl2026-m35-givsmi`) were never affected either since `i2` itself is `null` there — so the bug was specifically scoped to non-Test finished matches that have at least a started second innings, which in the current mock dataset means exactly the "5 of 12" aggregate-only Past matches identified in the immediately preceding diagnosis.

**Fixed** by adding `isLive &&` (already computed at the top of the component) to the render condition — nothing else changed. A finished match now shows nothing in that second row rather than a substitute message, since the real final result already renders elsewhere on a finished match's page (Scorecard's final-score header from v1.0.97, Digest's lead-in summary card) — there was nothing this row needed to add.

**Verified live**:
- `ipl2026-m35-givsmi` (finished, `innings.length === 0`): no chase line before or after — this match was never affected in the first place (`i2` is `null` regardless of status), confirmed as an unaffected regression check rather than a true before/after.
- `bbl-2526-scorchers-sixers` (finished, `innings.length > 0`, no ball data): chase line present before the fix, confirmed gone after.
- `ashes-2526-3rd-test` (finished Test): no chase line before or after — Test matches never compute one, confirmed unaffected by the fix either way.
- A currently-live match: chase line still renders correctly and unchanged (`isLive` was already `true` for it, so the added condition is a no-op there).

---

## Hydration-mismatch fix: `MatchView.tsx` tab restoration and `DigestTab.tsx` narrative-threshold override no longer read browser storage during render — v1.0.99 (2026-07-22)

**FY29 — Two confirmed locations read `sessionStorage`/`localStorage` synchronously during a component's initial render, causing React hydration mismatch (#418/#423) on every match page**

Scoped in the immediately preceding diagnostic pass (14 other locations already used the safe deferred pattern; these 2 didn't):

1. `MatchView.tsx`'s `restoredTab`: a same-render IIFE called `sessionStorage.getItem(SESSION_KEY)` directly whenever `typeof window !== "undefined"`. On the server this branch never runs (`window` is undefined, so it always fell through to `defaultTab`). On the client's own first render pass -- before hydration reconciles -- `window` is already defined (this is a `"use client"` component), so the IIFE could read a real stored value and return something other than `defaultTab`. React then found the client's first-render output didn't match the server-rendered HTML it was hydrating against.
2. `DigestTab.tsx`'s `cards` `useMemo` called `buildCards()`/`buildPostMatchDigest()`, both of which called `getNarrativeThresholds()` internally at their own top -- a `localStorage` read. `useMemo` callbacks run synchronously during the render phase itself (not deferred like `useEffect`), so this carried the exact same risk as a bare top-of-render-body read, despite being "memoized."

**Fixed** by applying the same safe pattern already used correctly elsewhere in this codebase (canonical example: `app/page.tsx`'s `followPrefs` state) to both locations -- render with a neutral default on both server and the client's first pass, then read the real value inside a `useEffect` and update state if it differs:

- `MatchView.tsx`: `tab`/`renderedTab` now initialize with `useState<TabKey>(defaultTab)` directly (the `restoredTab` IIFE is gone from the render path entirely). A new `useEffect` reads `sessionStorage.getItem(SESSION_KEY)` post-mount, applies the same `isFinished && saved === "live"` staleness guard as before, and calls `setTab`/`setRenderedTab` only if the restored value differs from `defaultTab`.
- `DigestTab.tsx`: added `const [thresholds, setThresholds] = useState<NarrativeThresholds>(DEFAULT_NARRATIVE_THRESHOLDS)` plus a mount-only `useEffect` that reads the real value via `getNarrativeThresholds()` in the main component. `buildCards()` and `buildPostMatchDigest()` now accept `t`/`thresholds` as an explicit parameter (defaulting to the pure `DEFAULT_NARRATIVE_THRESHOLDS` constant, never a storage read) instead of calling `getNarrativeThresholds()` themselves; the `cards` `useMemo` passes `thresholds` through and depends on it, so cards recompute once the real override (if any) arrives. The 5 other `getNarrativeThresholds()` call sites in this file were only ever exercised as unused TypeScript default-parameter expressions (every real caller already passed `t` explicitly) -- also switched to the pure `DEFAULT_NARRATIVE_THRESHOLDS` constant for consistency, since a default-parameter expression evaluates at call time, in the same render-phase timing class, if a future caller ever omits the argument.

**Follow-up bug found during verification, fixed in the same pass**: `buildOverGroupCards()`/`buildTestSessionCards()` permanently cache every already-COMPLETE card by id (correct on its own -- a finished over/session's stats never change again, this is the v1.0.85 incremental-cache feature). But that first build happens on mount using the default thresholds, before the new `useEffect` above has run -- so without further changes, every already-complete card at mount time would be frozen with default-threshold narrative text forever, and a real override would only ever affect overs/sessions that complete AFTER that effect fires. Confirmed live: setting an override via `localStorage` and reloading a live match's Digest tab showed zero change in already-complete overs' narrative text. Fixed by clearing `cacheRef.current` inside the same mount effect that applies the real thresholds, forcing exactly one full rebuild against the correct snapshot; caching behaves exactly as before for every subsequent live tick.

**Verified live** (deployed site, `read_console_messages`, cleared between checks to avoid stale-message false positives):
- Currently-live match: no #418/#423 hydration warnings (confirmed clean after clearing console and reloading -- an earlier check without clearing surfaced stale pre-fix errors still sitting in the log, not new ones).
- Finished match with full innings data: no #418/#423 hydration warnings.
- Finished match with no innings data: no #418/#423 hydration warnings.
- Tab restoration still works: selecting a non-default tab (Score, on a finished Test match), then doing a full page reload, correctly restores Score instead of falling back to the default Digest tab -- confirmed via screenshot before and after reload.
- `setNarrativeThresholdOverride(...)`'s underlying mechanism still works, and now actually applies to already-complete cards after the cache fix above: set `bawler:narrativeThresholds` to override `tightOverRuns` to `-1` (so no over should ever qualify as "tight") on a live match with several already-complete "Tight over" cards, reloaded, and confirmed those cards' narrative text changed accordingly.
- Both deferred updates apply within a single frame of mount, before any user-visible paint settles -- no visible flash of default content in either case.

**Reproducible verification recipe** (for anyone re-checking this independently -- e.g. after a future change touches `DigestTab.tsx` or `narrativeThresholds.ts` -- since `setNarrativeThresholdOverride()` is not attached to `window` and can't be typed directly into the console; the override has to be written to its `localStorage` key instead):

- Key: `bawler:narrativeThresholds`. Value: a JSON-stringified *partial* `NarrativeThresholds` object (only the fields being changed need to be present -- `getNarrativeThresholds()` deep-merges it over `DEFAULT_NARRATIVE_THRESHOLDS`). Three top-level groups: `narrative`, `overSummary`, `dayReport` (see `lib/narrativeThresholds.ts` for every field).
- Known-good test case, checked directly against this match's actual mock data (not assumed): `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, Digest tab, Day 2 -> 2nd Session (ENG's innings, overs 29-58) -- **6 wickets for 32 runs**, `isComplete: true`, i.e. exactly the kind of already-cached card the bug above affected. This session is "notable" (amber styling) *only* via its wicket count (32 runs is well under the `sessionDominantBattingRuns` default of 70), which makes it a clean single-variable test -- unlike Day 2's 1st Session (5 wickets but 116 runs, which clears the runs threshold on its own regardless of any wickets override).
- Console commands:
  ```js
  // set an override, then reload -- flips this session's "notable" amber
  // styling off (dayReport.sessionDominantBowlingWickets raised above 6)
  // and its narrative text from "6 wickets — collapse!" to "32 scored"
  // (narrative.wicketsCollapse raised above 6):
  localStorage.setItem('bawler:narrativeThresholds', JSON.stringify({
    dayReport: { sessionDominantBowlingWickets: 10 },
    narrative: { wicketsCollapse: 10 }
  }));
  location.reload();

  // inspect the current override:
  localStorage.getItem('bawler:narrativeThresholds');

  // clear it and reload -- reverts to the amber "6 wickets — collapse!" card:
  localStorage.removeItem('bawler:narrativeThresholds');
  location.reload();
  ```
- `isNotableSession()`'s wickets threshold lives at `dayReport.sessionDominantBowlingWickets` (default `5`); the "collapse!" wording specifically comes from `narrative.wicketsCollapse` (default `3`) inside `buildNarrative()`. They're independent knobs -- the recipe above happens to move both for a clean before/after, but either can be overridden alone.

---

## `bg.deep`'s page background is now a real Tailwind `theme()` reference, not a coincidentally-matching literal — v1.0.100 (2026-07-23)

**FY30 — The v1.0.67 background fix replaced one hardcoded literal with another hardcoded literal that merely happened to match the token's value**

An external, independent review of this doc's own "Resolved" claims (checking the actual code, not trusting the doc's wording) caught that §1's "Resolved (v1.0.67)" line said `html`/`body` "now both read `background: #03060F` via the token" -- but `app/globals.css` was still a plain `background: #03060F;` string literal, not a reference to anything. `bg.deep`'s own table row in the same section (§1) already said, correctly, "zero matches for `bg-bg-deep` anywhere in `components/` or `app/`" -- the doc was contradicting itself. The v1.0.67 fix corrected the *value* (so the rendered color already matched `bg.deep`) but never actually wired the CSS to the token, so a future change to `bg.deep` in `tailwind.config.ts` would have silently desynced from `globals.css` again, exactly the failure mode the fix was meant to prevent.

**Fixed** by using Tailwind's `theme()` function, which `postcss.config.mjs`'s `tailwindcss` plugin resolves at build time: `html`/`body` in `app/globals.css` now read `background: theme('colors.bg.deep')` / `background-color: theme('colors.bg.deep')` instead of the literal hex. `bg.deep` in `tailwind.config.ts` is a plain string (`colors.bg.deep`), not nested under its own `DEFAULT`, so that's the exact path -- not `colors.bg.deep.DEFAULT`.

**Verified**:
- Compiled output is pixel-identical: `.next/static/css/*.css` shows `html{color-scheme:dark;background:#03060f}` -- same value as before, now resolved from the config rather than typed twice.
- Config-change propagation: temporarily changed `bg.deep` to `#FF00FF` in `tailwind.config.ts`, rebuilt, and the compiled CSS actually changed to `background:#f0f` (Tailwind's hex-shorthand compression) with zero edits to `globals.css`. Reverted immediately after confirming; `tailwind.config.ts` shows no diff post-revert.
- `npm run build` clean before and after.

`DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §1's "Resolved" line updated to describe the real mechanism (a build-time `theme()` reference) instead of the vague "via the token" phrasing that didn't match what the code actually did.

---

## `FinalScoreHeader` restricted back to finished matches; `liveStatusOverride` removed from it entirely — v1.0.101 (2026-07-23)

**FY31 — v1.0.97's Score-tab header card was rendering on live matches too, and one of its sub-lines was a frozen snapshot string that visibly disagreed with the live-ticking score right above it**

Reported directly by the user on `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`: the persistent header and the new Score-tab card's own team rows both correctly showed AUS 175/8 vs IND 155/6 (both driven by `MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch`, in sync) -- but the card had an extra sub-line reading "IND 142/3 (16.2) · need 34 off 22", directly contradicting the rows above it. Root-caused to two separate things, confirmed live before fixing:

1. `FinalScoreHeader` (`components/Scorecard.tsx`) had exactly one `match.status === "live"` check, and it only gated the small pulsing "LIVE" badge -- not whether the whole card rendered. The card itself rendered "regardless of match status" by explicit, deliberate design (per its own v1.0.97 comment), which went beyond the original request (move the header out of the removed Live tab for FINISHED matches specifically; "leave the Live tab completely unchanged for any match still actively live").
2. The mismatched sub-line was `match.liveStatusOverride` rendered verbatim -- a hand-authored static string in `lib/mockData.ts`, meant as flavor text for surfaces where nothing else on screen is actively changing (Spotlight cards, homepage rows), never tied to the ball-by-ball data. Confirmed by summing India's actual recorded balls for this match: 98 deliveries, totaling exactly 155 runs / 6 wickets, last ball at over 17.2 -- matching the live rows. The innings' own static `runs: 142, wickets: 3, overs: 16.2` fields and `liveStatusOverride` were both authored to describe an earlier point in the match and never updated when the ball array was later extended -- a real mock-data drift that `FinalScoreHeader` surfaced by placing it next to genuinely live data.

**Fixed**:
- `components/Scorecard.tsx`: the caller now only constructs `FinalScoreHeader` when `match.status !== "live"` (`const finalScoreHeader = match.status !== "live" ? <FinalScoreHeader match={match} /> : null`), and both JSX usages wrap it in `{finalScoreHeader && ...}` (matching the existing `momMosBanners` pattern) so a live match's Score tab renders no extra wrapper div at all -- not even an empty one.
- `FinalScoreHeader` itself: removed the now-unreachable `match.status === "live"` badge branch, and removed the `match.liveStatusOverride` block entirely. Checked every current `status: "post-match"` match in the mock dataset: all of them already have a real `match.result` (zero exceptions), so the existing result banner is sufficient on its own -- there's no finished-match case that still needs `liveStatusOverride` as a fallback. Every other surface that already uses `liveStatusOverride` safely (Spotlight cards, homepage rows) is untouched.

**Verified live**:
- `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` (currently live): Score tab now shows no card above the scorecard at all -- just the team toggle (AUS/IND chips) and the innings' batting/bowling tables, exactly as it looked before this feature existed.
- `ashes-2526-3rd-test` (finished, full data): Score tab still shows the score header -- AUS 512/8, ENG 210/10, "AUS won · by an innings and 27 runs" result banner. No live badge, no liveStatusOverride line (this match never had one either way).
- `ipl2026-m35-givsmi` (finished, `innings.length === 0`): untouched by this change -- `Scorecard`'s `innings.length === 0` early return (the "Scorecard not available" fallback) happens before `finalScoreHeader` is even computed, so this path was never affected by either the bug or the fix.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.

## Team rankings/membership split into an interface-first adapter — v1.0.102 (2026-07-23)

**FY32 — `Team.currentRanking` was one field doing two unrelated jobs, and the app's only way to tell a full ICC member from an associate ("does it have a ranking number") only worked by coincidence of the mock data, not because it was actually a valid signal**

User asked, ahead of any real-data integration work: "will this ranking thing work fine, once real data starts incoming, as it keeps on changing?" Root cause: `Team.currentRanking?: number` was reused for two conceptually different things depending on team type -- an IPL franchise's season-scoped points-table position, and a nation's rolling cross-season ICC rating -- and separately, code elsewhere treated "`currentRanking` is set" as a proxy for "is a full ICC member." That proxy only held because this mock dataset happened to leave the field blank for every associate nation. It would have broken immediately against real data: ICC publishes T20I rankings for 100+ members including most associates (confirmed live via Wikipedia's "List of International Cricket Council members" page, which even shows current real numeric ODI rankings for several associates -- Netherlands, Scotland, Nepal, Oman, Namibia, Canada, PNG -- directly disproving the "ranked implies full member" assumption).

Also surfaced a pre-existing data inaccuracy while verifying the real ICC Full Member list rather than assuming it from memory: Ireland and Zimbabwe were filed under the mock dataset's "Associates & emerging nations" comment block, but both are real ICC Full Members (Ireland since 22 June 2017 alongside Afghanistan; Zimbabwe since 1992) -- twelve total: AFG, AUS, BAN, ENG, IND, IRE, NZ, PAK, SA, SL, WI, ZIM.

Built as the reference implementation of a reusable pattern (see `ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Real-data-readiness: the interface-first pattern") rather than a one-off fix, since this is the first of several real-data-readiness items already anticipated (win probability, ball-by-ball deliveries, player name parsing):

- **Data model split** (`lib/types.ts`): `currentRanking` replaced by three fields -- `membershipStatus?: "full" | "associate"` (nations, categorical, durable), `rankings?: { test?: number; odi?: number; t20i?: number }` (nations, per-format, volatile -- only `t20i` populated today since the old mock data only ever had one number per nation), and `leagueStanding?: number` (franchises, unrelated concept, kept as a plain field since there's no external body's data it will eventually be swapped for).
- **Interface layer** (`lib/teamData.ts`, new file): `getTeamMembershipStatus(team)` and `getTeamRanking(team, format)` are the only sanctioned reads of the two nation-specific fields -- both `async`, resolving synchronously from mock data today but already shaped for a future network-backed swap with zero call-site changes. `refreshRankings()` is an explicitly-commented no-op placeholder for a future sync mechanism.
- **Call sites migrated**: `components/MatchCard.tsx`'s `FlagOrRank` -- franchise teams now read `team.leagueStanding` directly (out of scope for the adapter, per above); the national-team fallback case (today only reached by Kenya/Uganda, which have no `FLAG_ISO` flag-image entry) now calls `getTeamRanking(team, "t20i")` through a new `NationalRankBadge` sub-component, using the same hydration-safe `useState(undefined)` + `useEffect` pattern established in v1.0.99 (render nothing on first pass so server and client agree, fill in the real value post-mount). Grepped the full codebase afterward -- zero remaining `currentRanking` references outside this log's own prose.

**Verified**:
- All 22 national teams confirmed to have `membershipStatus` set (12 `"full"`, 10 `"associate"`) -- zero teams left unset.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.
- No code outside `lib/teamData.ts`'s two accessor functions reads `team.membershipStatus` or `team.rankings` directly (grepped for both).
- Visual result is unchanged for every team: nations with a `FLAG_ISO` entry (20 of 22) render the same flag image as before; franchise teams render the same numeric badge value as before, just sourced from `leagueStanding` instead of `currentRanking`; Kenya/Uganda render nothing, same as before (they had no ranking under the old field either).

## Spotlight gets a competition-tier membership gate; fixed a `setState`-with-a-function crash along the way — v1.0.103 (2026-07-23)

**FY33 — Spotlight's three excitement checks (close finish / milestone / context stakes) applied identically to Full Member and Associate nations, but a rare, exciting associate-nation result isn't the same "interrupt the homepage" signal a Full Member one is**

Requested as a follow-up to FY32's membership-status adapter: gate `isSpotlightMatch()` (`lib/spotlight.ts`) on competition tier, using `getTeamMembershipStatus()` rather than any new direct field access. League/domestic competitions (IPL, BBL, PSL, CPL, The Hundred, SA20, MLC) are unrestricted -- any match there still only needs to clear the three existing excitement checks, untouched. International/bilateral matches now additionally require BOTH teams to be full ICC members, checked *before* the excitement checks run at all -- an associate-nation match never reaches them, however dramatic the result.

Since `getTeamMembershipStatus()` is `async` (deliberately, per the FY32 adapter pattern) and `isSpotlightMatch()` must stay synchronous to run inside a plain `Array.filter()`, added `buildFullMemberLookup(matches)` to `lib/spotlight.ts`: resolves every *unique* team's status ONE TIME via `Promise.all`, returns a plain synchronous `(team) => boolean` closure. `app/page.tsx` resolves this once in a mount effect (`useState<FullMemberLookup | null>(null)` + `useEffect`), matching the same hydration-safe shape used elsewhere in this file (neutral default on first render, real value filled in post-mount) -- `spotlightMatches`'s `useMemo` returns `[]` until the lookup resolves, then computes for real.

**A real bug surfaced and fixed during this work, unrelated to the gate's logic itself**: the first deploy attempt crashed the entire homepage (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'code')`, confirmed live on `bawler-gold.vercel.app`). Root cause: `buildFullMemberLookup(...).then(setFullMemberLookup)` passed the *resolved lookup function* directly to `useState`'s setter. React's setter treats a bare function argument as a **functional updater** (`(prevState) => newState`), not as the literal new value -- so it immediately invoked `lookup(prevState)`, i.e. `lookup(null)` (the initial state), which crashed reading `null.code` inside the lookup's own `team.code` access. Confirmed via a careful bisection: reverted to the exact pre-change commit (worked, live-verified) → reintroduced the gate code alone against unmodified mock data (crashed identically) → replaced the effect body with a trivially-wrapped `setFullMemberLookup(() => true)` (didn't crash, isolating the functional-updater footgun specifically) → fixed the real code with the same wrapping: `.then(lookup => setFullMemberLookup(() => lookup))`.

**Verified live** (`bawler-gold.vercel.app`, using a temporary constructed Kenya-vs-Namibia tied T20I fixture -- both associate nations, "super over" finish satisfying `hasCloseFinish` -- removed again immediately after verification, never part of the shipped mock dataset):
- Homepage loads cleanly, zero console errors, after the fix.
- Full Member international dramatic finish still qualifies: IND vs PAK (T20 World Cup, last-over thriller) appeared as one of the 3 Spotlight cards.
- League matches unaffected: GT vs MI (IPL, last-over thriller) and Perth Scorchers vs Sydney Sixers (BBL Final) both still appeared in Spotlight exactly as before.
- The constructed KEN vs NAM tied match (associate vs associate) did NOT appear in Spotlight despite clearing `hasCloseFinish` -- it rendered normally in the ordinary Past grid instead ("Past · 13"), confirming the gate rejects it on membership status alone, before the excitement checks are even reached.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.

## Batting-team color theming: not-out box, sparkline, and team pills — v1.0.104 (2026-07-23)

**FY34 — the not-out highlight, live sparkline, and team-selector pills all shared one fixed cyan accent regardless of which team was batting, after a feasibility check confirmed the data and existing precedent (`WinProbChart.tsx`, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, match-card left borders already theme with `team.primaryColor`) made this low-risk to build**

Themed exactly four things, per the settled scope, and nothing else: `BatterRow`'s not-out box (the `excitement-glow` border/glow and the "not out" + on-strike `*` text) and `BatterSparkline`'s live-line stroke -- both in `components/Scorecard.tsx` -- plus the `TeamToggle` and `TestInningsChips` selector pills in the same file. Outcome-coded colors were deliberately left untouched: the strike-rate highlight (`text-blue-400`), the four-dot and six-dot sparkline markers (`#00E5FF`/`#A855F7`, hardcoded independently of the line color despite sharing a hex with the old cyan by coincidence), and the top-scorer/top-wicket-taker highlights all still render identically regardless of which team is batting or bowling.

**New `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`** exports `getBattingTeamAccentColor(team)`: real `primaryColor` for almost every team, including several that score poorly on contrast against the card background (Zimbabwe, RCB, Punjab Kings) -- explicitly no carve-out there, since the not-out box always carries its own text label, unlike an unlabeled ball-outcome dot. The one exception is a team with a literally colorless `#000000` primary (New Zealand, Uganda, Papua New Guinea, London Spirit, as of this mock dataset) -- for those, it falls back to `secondaryColor` if that clears WCAG contrast (>= 3.0:1) against `#141B2D` (`bg-surface`, the real `.card` background), or to the platform's default cyan (now a named export, `CYAN`, in `lib/tokens.ts`) if there's no secondary color or it also fails. All four of today's colorless teams pass on their secondary: NZ silver `#A8A9AD` (7.30:1), Uganda yellow `#FCDC04` (12.56:1), London Spirit teal `#00B5A4` (6.65:1), and PNG red `#CE1126` (3.05:1, the closest call of the four but still a clear pass) -- none of them needed the cyan fallback in practice, though the fallback path exists and was exercised in testing for a team with no secondary color at all.

**Threading**: `InningsCard` already computed `team = ALL_TEAMS[innings.battingTeam]` for its header dot -- added one `const teamColor = getBattingTeamAccentColor(team)` next to it and passed `teamColor` one level down into `BatterRow` (and from there into `BatterSparkline`). `TeamToggle`/`TestInningsChips` already receive full `Team` objects as props, so no threading was needed there at all.

**`app/globals.css`**: `.excitement-glow`'s box-shadow keyframes were a hardcoded `rgba(0, 229, 255, ...)`, shared with two unrelated usages (`MatchCard.tsx`'s Spotlight card, `DigestTab.tsx`'s notable-day card). Rewrote the keyframes to read `rgba(var(--glow-rgb, 0, 229, 255), alpha)` -- the CSS variable defaults to the original cyan triplet, so the two unrelated usages (which never set it) are pixel-identical to before; only Scorecard.tsx's not-out box now sets `--glow-rgb` inline to the batting team's accent color.

**Verified**:
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.
- Grepped `components/Scorecard.tsx` for every outcome-coded highlight (`isTopScorer`, `isTopSR`, `isTopWicketTaker`, the 4s/6s header colors, the sparkline dot markers) -- all untouched, still fixed hex/Tailwind-token colors with no team dependency.

## Batting-team color theming correction: hairline-stroke contrast audit — v1.0.105 (2026-07-23)

**Problem found live**: on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, England's not-out box border/text and sparkline line were nearly invisible. Root cause: v1.0.104's `getBattingTeamAccentColor()` only ever ran a contrast check on a team's `primaryColor` if it was literally `#000000` (the "colorless team" case). Every other team's real primary — including England's `#1D244E`, a dark navy — was passed straight through with zero validation. England's primary measures ~1.16:1 against the real card background (`#141B2D`), nowhere close to legible as a 1-2px stroke, but the old code had no path that would ever catch it.

**Fix — two changes to `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`**:
1. **Scope**: the contrast check now runs for every team's `primaryColor`, not just literal `#000000`. The "colorless team" special case is gone; it's one uniform primary → secondary → cyan chain for all 72 teams (national + league).
2. **Threshold**: raised `MIN_CONTRAST` from 3.0 to 7.0. WCAG's 3.0 minimum is calibrated for a solid, normal-weight non-text UI component (e.g. a filled pill) — not a 1-2px SVG stroke or 1px box-shadow ring, which reads much fainter at the same raw contrast ratio due to thin edges and anti-aliasing. There's no official WCAG number for graphical strokes this thin, so this borrows WCAG 2.x's AAA "enhanced contrast" level (7:1) — normally reserved for small/thin text — as the closest published reference point with a comparably small margin for error. This is a deliberate calibration choice, not a pixel-exact rendering model, and it's applied as a single gate for all four themed components: the not-out box's ~1px ring and the sparkline's 2px line are both gated by the same check (a color that survives the thinner case survives the thicker one too), and the two pill components reuse the same resolved color as a solid fill, which is strictly more forgiving than either stroke case.

**Full 72-team audit at the new threshold** (contrast measured against `#141B2D`):

*Real primary passes (9)*: Australia `#FFB81C` 9.90:1, Chennai Super Kings `#FDB913` 9.90:1, Jamaica Tallawahs `#FFD700` 12.23:1, Joburg Super Kings `#FDB913` 9.90:1, Melbourne Stars `#8DC64C` 8.42:1, Peshawar Zalmi `#FFC20F` 10.60:1, Sunrisers Eastern Cape `#F7A800` 8.62:1, Texas Super Kings `#FDB913` 9.90:1, Trent Rockets `#FFD500` 12.06:1.

*Falls back to secondary (32)*: includes India (primary `#005BAC` 2.53:1 fails → secondary `#F9A825` 8.70:1), New Zealand, Uganda (both previously-known colorless cases, still resolve the same way — NZ secondary 7.30:1, Uganda secondary 12.56:1), Punjab Kings (primary `#DD1F2D` 3.52:1 fails → secondary `#A7A9AC` 7.28:1 — resolves via the same universal math as any other team, not a red-collision carve-out), Pakistan, South Africa, West Indies, and 25 more league teams. Full list in the diff/code comments.

*Falls back to cyan — no primary or secondary clears 7:1 (31)*: England (primary `#1D244E` 1.16:1 fails; secondary `#00A0C6` 5.59:1 also fails the stricter bar — this is the exact team/bug that triggered this correction), Zimbabwe (primary `#D4212D` 3.31:1 fails; secondary `#009A44` 4.66:1 fails), Perth Scorchers (primary `#CC5A1E` 4.12:1 fails; secondary `#403529` 1.44:1 fails), Papua New Guinea and London Spirit (both previously-known colorless cases, still land on cyan — their secondaries clear the old 3.0 bar but not the new 7.0 one: PNG secondary 3.05:1, London Spirit secondary 6.65:1), plus 26 more.

**No red-collision carve-out**: Zimbabwe, Perth Scorchers, and Punjab Kings run through the identical universal check as every other team. Punjab Kings' primary happens to fail on pure contrast math and resolves to its grey secondary; Zimbabwe and Perth Scorchers' primaries and secondaries both fail and resolve to cyan. None of this is red-collision-specific logic — it's the same math every team gets.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. Live-checked `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` post-deploy — England's not-out box and sparkline are now visibly rendered (cyan fallback, since neither of its colors clears 7:1). Re-confirmed a passing-primary team (gold/yellow teams render their own color) and a red-collision team (Punjab Kings' grey secondary, Zimbabwe/Perth Scorchers' cyan fallback) render correctly with no special-case code path involved.

## Batting-team color theming correction: cross-team collision check — v1.0.106 (2026-07-23)

**Gap found**: v1.0.105's per-team contrast check only ever validated a team's resolved color against the dark card background. It had no way to catch two teams in the SAME match independently passing their own checks and landing on colors too close to each other. Live example: India's real primary blue fails the hairline-stroke check and falls back to its gold secondary (`#F9A825`) — which clears the background-contrast bar fine on its own, but sits almost on top of Australia's real gold primary (`#FFB81C`) in an India vs Australia match (cross-contrast only 1.14:1), so both Kohli's and Pant's not-out boxes read as Australia-colored even while India is batting.

**Fix — new `resolveMatchAccentColors(teamA, teamB)` in `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`**: resolves each team's color independently first (unchanged v1.0.105 logic — primary, else secondary, else cyan), then checks the two FINAL colors against EACH OTHER (not the background) at a separate 1.5:1 minimum — a much lower bar, since this step is about telling two colors apart from each other, not surviving a fixed dark background. Below that:
- Priority: real primary > secondary fallback > cyan. The lower-priority team drops one tier — primary → secondary only if that secondary both clears its own background-contrast minimum AND doesn't also collide with the other team's now-fixed color; otherwise straight to cyan.
- Same-tier ties (both on real primaries, or both on secondary fallbacks) are broken deterministically: whichever team's `fullName` sorts second alphabetically drops straight to cyan. This has nothing to do with which team is batting, home, or `teamA` in the match object — it must give the same answer regardless of match state.

All three real call sites in `components/Scorecard.tsx` (`TeamToggle`, `TestInningsChips`, `InningsCard`) now call this instead of the old single-team `getBattingTeamAccentColor`, which has been removed entirely (not just deprecated) — a single-team resolution can't know about a same-match collision, and leaving it exported risked a future caller silently reintroducing this bug.

**Full audit — every one of the 29 matches in the mock dataset**, checked as a `(teamA, teamB)` pair:

- **No collision at all (3)**: LSG vs Punjab Kings (cross-contrast 1.53:1, just clears the bar), India vs Pakistan (1.97:1).
- **Flagged as a collision but no visible change (16)**: every case where the lower-priority side was already resolved to the platform cyan with nowhere lower to fall — including pairs where BOTH teams were already independently on cyan (Sunrisers Hyderabad vs Delhi Capitals, Royal Challengers Bengaluru vs Delhi Capitals, Perth Scorchers vs Sydney Sixers). These aren't new problems — both teams landing on the shared default cyan was already verified and accepted in the v1.0.105 report; this check just confirms it stays that way rather than changing anything.
- **Real color change (10 match rows, 8 distinct team pairs)**:
  - Mumbai Indians' gold-khaki secondary (`#D1AB3E`) collided with three different opponents' gold and dropped to cyan each time: vs Kolkata Knight Riders (`ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`), vs Gujarat Titans (`ipl2026-m35-givsmi`), vs Chennai Super Kings (`ipl2026-m30-mivcsk`).
  - Kolkata Knight Riders' gold secondary (`#F2C72A`) dropped to cyan against Chennai Super Kings' real gold primary (`ipl2026-m41-cskvkkr`).
  - **India's gold secondary (`#F9A825`) drops to cyan against Australia's real gold primary (`#FFB81C`)** — the exact reported bug — on all three India vs Australia fixtures (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m1`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m3`). Same outcome every time, confirming the fix is deterministic and doesn't depend on which match or which team is currently batting.
  - New Zealand's grey secondary (`#A8A9AD`) drops to cyan against Australia's gold primary (`ct-2025-aus-nz-final`, cross-contrast 1.36:1). Flagged for the record: grey and gold are different hues, and WCAG contrast is a luminance-only measure — it doesn't know about hue, so this catch is driven by the two colors having similar brightness, not similar color. Implemented exactly as specified (this is the same `contrastRatio()` function used throughout, at the literal 1.5:1 threshold), but noted here in case a future review wants a hue-aware refinement instead of a pure luminance one.
  - Multan Sultans' gold secondary (`#FFD700`) drops to cyan against Peshawar Zalmi's real gold primary (`#FFC20F`) (`psl-2026-mul-pes`).
  - LA Knight Riders' gold secondary (`#F2C72A`) drops to cyan against Texas Super Kings' real gold primary (`#FDB913`) (`mlc-2026-lakr-tsk`).

Every real change above is a gold-on-gold (or grey-on-gold) collision, not a hue-mismatched false positive — consistent with the fact that the pool of colors bright enough to clear the 7:1 hairline-stroke minimum against a near-black background is dominated by gold/yellow, so collisions between two different teams' resolved colors are almost always going to be within that same narrow color family.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. Live re-checked `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` — India's not-out box, sparkline, and team pill now render in cyan, clearly distinct from Australia's gold, instead of both wearing near-identical gold. `git diff --stat` confirms the change is isolated to `lib/teamAccentColor.ts` and `components/Scorecard.tsx`.

## Batting-team color theming correction: CIEDE2000 replaces WCAG contrast for the collision check — v1.0.107 (2026-07-23)

**Problem with v1.0.106's collision metric**: it reused the same WCAG contrast-ratio formula as the background-legibility check to compare the two teams' final colors against each other. WCAG contrast is luminance-only -- it has no concept of hue or saturation. That's exactly why New Zealand's grey secondary (`#A8A9AD`) got flagged as "colliding" with Australia's gold primary (`#FFB81C`): both are similarly bright (WCAG contrast 1.36:1, below the old 1.5 threshold), even though grey and gold are obviously different colors to look at. Luminance similarity was standing in for "looks the same," and it isn't the same thing.

**Fix**: the cross-team check now uses CIEDE2000 (`ciede2000()` / `deltaE00()` in `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`) -- the standard perceptual color-difference formula. Converts both colors to CIE Lab (D65 illuminant) and computes a Delta E that accounts for lightness, chroma, and hue together, the way actual human color perception works. The WCAG-based background-legibility check (`MIN_CONTRAST = 7.0`, does a color survive as a 1-2px stroke against the dark card background) is untouched -- that's a genuine brightness question, where luminance contrast is the right tool. The cross-team question ("do these two colors look the same to a person") needed a perceptual tool instead, and CIEDE2000 is the standard one.

**Threshold calibration — `COLLISION_MIN_DELTA_E = 10.0`**, chosen against two known answers rather than a default textbook number:
- India's gold secondary (`#F9A825`) vs Australia's gold primary (`#FFB81C`) **must** collide: dE00 = 5.42. ✓ below 10.0.
- New Zealand's grey secondary (`#A8A9AD`) vs Australia's gold primary (`#FFB81C`) **must not** collide: dE00 = 31.71. ✓ above 10.0, by a wide margin.
- Every other confirmed v1.0.106 gold-on-gold collision clusters tightly in the 5.4-9.2 range (Mumbai Indians vs Kolkata Knight Riders 8.56, vs Gujarat Titans 6.60, vs Chennai Super Kings 9.21; Kolkata Knight Riders vs Chennai Super Kings 6.85; Multan Sultans vs Peshawar Zalmi 7.20; LA Knight Riders vs Texas Super Kings 6.85) — nowhere near the New Zealand/Australia case (31.71).

10.0 sits ~1 point above the highest confirmed real collision (9.21) and ~15 points below the lowest confirmed non-collision (25.66) — not a knife-edge choice — and lands inside the commonly cited CIEDE2000 perceptibility bands (roughly 2-10 = "perceptible at a glance," 11-49 = "more similar than opposite"), so it's a real, citable boundary that happens to land exactly where the calibration cases need it to, not a number curve-fit purely to these two cases.

**Full re-audit of all 29 matches under the new metric**:

- **Still collide, still resolve to a fallback (9 match rows, 8 pairs — unchanged from v1.0.106)**: Mumbai Indians vs Kolkata Knight Riders, vs Gujarat Titans, vs Chennai Super Kings; Kolkata Knight Riders vs Chennai Super Kings; **India vs Australia on all 3 fixtures**; Multan Sultans vs Peshawar Zalmi; LA Knight Riders vs Texas Super Kings. Every one of these is confirmed gold-on-gold under CIEDE2000 too (dE00 5.4-9.2), not just under the old luminance metric.
- **No longer flagged — corrected (1 pair)**: **New Zealand vs Australia** (`ct-2025-aus-nz-final`). dE00 = 31.71, well clear of the 10.0 minimum. New Zealand now renders its real grey secondary (`#A8A9AD`) instead of dropping to cyan.
- **Still flagged, still inert (3, unchanged)**: the three pairs where both teams were already independently on cyan (identical colors, dE00 = 0.00) — Delhi Capitals vs Sunrisers Hyderabad, Royal Challengers Bengaluru vs Delhi Capitals, Perth Scorchers vs Sydney Sixers. No visible change either way; there's nowhere lower than cyan to fall.
- **Correctly reclassified as "no collision" (13 pairs that were inert-but-flagged under the old metric)**: pairs like a gold team vs a team already on cyan (Mumbai Indians vs Sunrisers Hyderabad, Gujarat Titans vs Rajasthan Royals, Kolkata Knight Riders vs Rajasthan Royals, Lahore Qalandars vs Karachi Kings, England vs India, England vs South Africa, Australia vs England, MI London vs London Spirit) all measure dE00 in the low-40s-to-high-40s range under CIEDE2000 -- correctly recognized as visually distinct hues, not just "technically below an old brightness-only threshold." This didn't change any actual color (those pairs were already inert -- cyan has nowhere lower to fall), but it's a more accurate classification of WHY they don't need to change.
- **Confirmed genuinely safe, no collision either way (3)**: LSG vs Punjab Kings (dE00 = 25.66), India vs Pakistan (dE00 = 32.20).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. The TypeScript CIEDE2000 implementation was cross-checked against an independent Node.js port of the same formula and against a Python reference implementation -- all three produced identical Delta E values (5.42, 31.71, 8.56, 6.60, 9.21, 6.85, 7.20, 6.85, 25.66) for the calibration and audit cases. Live re-checked `ct-2025-aus-nz-final` post-deploy -- New Zealand now renders its real grey, not cyan.

## Real-data-readiness test pass: team-color theming system — v1.0.108 (2026-07-23)

Comprehensive check before considering the team-color theming/collision system (v1.0.104-107) done, following this codebase's real-data-readiness standard (ARCHITECTURE.md). Four areas checked; two real bugs found and fixed, two design confirmations documented.

**1. Malformed/missing data — FAIL, fixed.** Constructed real test cases (via `npx tsx`, running the actual functions, not a description) for: missing (`undefined`) `primaryColor`, `null`, non-string values, empty string, a CSS color name (`"blue"`), malformed hex (`"#GGG"`), a bare hex with no `#`, shorthand hex (`#FFF`), lowercase hex, `rgb()`/`rgba()` strings, whitespace-padded hex, and 8-digit hex-with-alpha.

Before fixing: `undefined`, `null`, and non-string values **crashed outright** (`hexToRgb`'s `.replace()` call on a non-string throws `Cannot read properties of undefined/null (reading 'replace')`). A bare hex with no `#` (`"FFB81C"`) was silently accepted as valid (the `.replace("#", "")` call is a no-op on a string with no `#`, so it parses "successfully" from garbage input). Whitespace-padded hex (`" #FFB81C "`) parsed to a wrong, non-NaN, non-obviously-broken contrast value due to a slicing bug, and could leak through into a rendered `style` prop untouched. `lib/dataValidation.ts`'s `requireString` only confirms `Team.primaryColor`/`secondaryColor` ARE strings at the match-normalization boundary — it has no opinion on whether the string is a *valid hex color*, so every one of these malformed-but-string cases would reach this module unvalidated.

Fixed with a new `sanitizeHexColor()` in `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`, called on every color before it touches the contrast/Delta E math: validates a strict 3- or 6-digit hex (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant, `#`-required), expands 3-digit shorthand to 6-digit, and returns `undefined` for anything else — treated identically to a missing color by every caller (falls through to secondary, then cyan, exactly like a genuinely colorless team). Re-tested after the fix: all cases now degrade gracefully to cyan (or, for valid-but-differently-formatted input like shorthand/lowercase/whitespace-padded hex, resolve to the correct real color) — zero crashes, zero silently-wrong values.

**2. Interface boundary — FAIL, fixed.** `resolveMatchAccentColors(teamA, teamB)` was already the single call site every real consumer used (confirmed via grep — no other file reads `team.primaryColor`/`secondaryColor` for this specific pipeline; the many OTHER direct reads across the app — match-card left borders, `WinProbChart`, country-flag fallbacks, etc. — are pre-existing, unrelated uses of the same field, already documented as an accepted separate decision in `DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §5, out of scope here). But it was synchronous, unlike `getTeamMembershipStatus`/`getTeamRanking` in `lib/teamData.ts`, which are async-from-day-one specifically so a future real-data swap is a one-file change instead of a call-site migration. Fixed: `resolveMatchAccentColors` now returns `Promise<Record<string, string>>`. Documented as a second worked example in `ARCHITECTURE.md` alongside the existing rankings/membership pattern.

**3. Recomputation correctness — confirmed correct, one real regression found and fixed during implementation.** Converting to async requires consuming it via the hydration-safe `useState(placeholder)` + `useEffect` pattern (same as `NationalRankBadge`). Initially wired this hook (`useMatchAccentColors`) into all three call sites directly (`TeamToggle`, `TestInningsChips`, `InningsCard`) — but `InningsCard` remounts on every innings/team-tab switch (`key={activeInnings.number}`), which would re-run the resolution effect on every switch, flashing back to the cyan placeholder each time even though nothing about the match's teams changed. Not a staleness bug in the narrativeThresholds sense (nothing is permanently cached against a stale snapshot — every remount DOES eventually resolve to the correct color), but an unnecessary, avoidable visual regression. Fixed by hoisting the single hook call to the top of `Scorecard` (the parent component, which never remounts across a tab switch — called before the `match.innings.length === 0` early return, satisfying the Rules of Hooks) and passing `accentColors` down as a plain prop to all three children instead of each resolving it independently. This also cuts three redundant async resolutions per match down to one.

Known accepted limitation, inherited from the pre-existing `getTeamRanking`/`NationalRankBadge` pattern this follows: the effect's dependency is `[teamA, teamB]` (object references). If a future real integration ever mutates an existing `Team` object's color fields in place rather than replacing it with a new object on refresh, neither this hook nor the pre-existing ranking hook would detect the change. Not a new problem introduced here — the same assumption already existed — but worth flagging for whoever builds the real integration: prefer replacing objects over mutating them in place.

**4. Full regression — pass.** Re-ran the 29-match audit end to end against the actual shipped `resolveMatchAccentColors` (via `npx tsx`, not by re-deriving numbers) after all of the above fixes — identical results to the v1.0.107 report: New Zealand still renders its real grey against Australia, India still renders cyan against Australia's gold (all 3 fixtures), all 8 known gold-on-gold pairs still fall back, the double-cyan pairs are still inert. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean throughout. Live-verified post-deploy: outcome-coded colors (top-scorer, top-SR, wicket, 4s/6s) unchanged and still fixed regardless of team; Zimbabwe/Perth Scorchers/Punjab Kings still resolve through the same universal math with no carve-out; England's not-out box and sparkline still render visibly in cyan.

## Close the stale-mutation gap flagged in the v1.0.108 real-data-readiness pass — v1.0.109 (2026-07-23)

The v1.0.108 pass flagged one accepted-but-unresolved limitation: `useMatchAccentColors`'s effect depended on `[teamA, teamB]` by object reference, inherited unmodified from the pre-existing `getTeamRanking`/`NationalRankBadge` pattern. That means the hook only ever notices a team's colors changing when the whole `Team` object is swapped for a new one — not when an existing object's `primaryColor`/`secondaryColor` is mutated in place. Closed now, in two parts, since it's a two-sided problem: one side this code can fix, one side it can only document.

**Part 1 — dependency array now tracks the fields, not the object.** `useMatchAccentColors` in `components/Scorecard.tsx` now depends on `[teamA.code, teamA.primaryColor, teamA.secondaryColor, teamB.code, teamB.primaryColor, teamB.secondaryColor]` instead of `[teamA, teamB]`. This means: whenever a re-render happens for any reason, the hook correctly detects whether the colors that actually matter changed, rather than using object identity as an imperfect proxy for that. It does NOT, by itself, make an in-place mutation trigger a re-render — nothing can do that without the mutating code causing a render somewhere upstream, which is inherent to how React hooks work, not a gap in this fix.

**Part 2 — the "replace, never mutate" contract, documented in `ARCHITECTURE.md`.** The other half of the gap is a discipline this code cannot enforce on itself: any future real data source must publish team color updates by replacing the `Team` object, never by mutating an existing one's fields in place. Documented as an explicit rule alongside the v1.0.104-108 worked example, flagged clearly as a contract for whoever wires up the real feed later, not something enforceable in this file.

**Verified with real tests, not description** (`npx tsx`, using `react-test-renderer` installed via `npm install --no-save` for this test only, not added to `package.json`, and removed afterward): temporarily exported `useMatchAccentColors` for the test, mounted a probe component with `act()`, and ran two scenarios against real React renders:
- **In-place mutation, no re-render**: mutated `team.primaryColor` directly on the already-rendered object with no accompanying prop change or `setState`. Hook correctly retained the stale color (`#FFB81C`, unchanged) — exactly as expected, since nothing can notice a mutation without a render. This confirms Part 1 alone doesn't (and can't) close the whole gap, which is exactly why Part 2 exists as a documented contract rather than a second code fix.
- **Proper replacement, real re-render**: replaced the team prop with a brand-new object carrying a different `primaryColor` (`#00FF00`) on the same `code`. Hook correctly picked up the new color on the next render.

Reverted the temporary export after the test. Re-ran the 29-match audit (fresh script, same approach as v1.0.107/108's) against the actual shipped `resolveMatchAccentColors` — byte-identical output to the v1.0.108 report across all 29 matches; expected, since this change is UI-hook-layer only and `resolveMatchAccentColors` itself was untouched. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.

## Schedule tab redefault: my-teams merged view, real-data-readiness built in — v1.0.110 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: when a user has one or more teams selected in Filter, the Schedule tab should open directly to a merged, chronological, month-grouped list of live/upcoming/past matches for those teams — no picker screen first — with an "All" + per-team chip row that narrows the same list in place. Zero teams selected keeps today's all-competitions picker unchanged. Built to the same real-data-readiness standard as the rankings/membership and team-color-theming work: single sanctioned interface, async from day one, defensive against malformed fixture data, and re-verified for the same "stale cache" class of bug those two features already caught once each.

**Scope decision (asked, not assumed)**: Filter has two categories that both represent "a team" for scheduling purposes — Nations (e.g. following India) and Team (franchise-only, e.g. Mumbai Indians). Confirmed with the user that both should count: following India as a nation triggers the redefault and gets its own chip, same as following a franchise team would, matching how `qualifyMatch`'s Tier 1 already treats nation and team follows as equal-weight "for you" signals.

**`lib/followPrefs.ts` — `myTeamCodes(prefs)`**: resolves the full set of `Team.code`s the user is following as a team, combining `prefs.teams` (franchise codes, direct) with `prefs.nations` (ISO country codes — NOT always equal to the corresponding national team's own `code`; South Africa's team code is `"SA"` but its `country` field is `"RSA"`, so this needed a real reverse lookup through `NATIONAL_TEAMS`, not a pass-through). Explicitly the SAME `FollowPrefs` store `qualifyMatch` already reads — a different derived view of it, not a second preference store, per the product ask.

**`lib/teamSchedule.ts` — new adapter, third worked example of the real-data-readiness pattern** (see ARCHITECTURE.md, alongside `lib/teamData.ts`'s rankings/membership and `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`'s team colors):
- `getTeamSchedule(teamCode)` — the fundamental per-team unit, written as the shape a real fixture-feed endpoint would actually take (most providers expose "team X's schedule," not "everything, go filter it yourself").
- `getMergedTeamSchedule(teamCodes)` — the sanctioned entry point for the redefault: composes N calls to the above, dedupes by match id (a match between two followed teams appears once, not twice), sorts chronologically.
- Both return `Promise<ScheduleEntry[]>`, async from day one, though today they resolve synchronously from the same `ALL_LIVE_MATCHES`/`ALL_PAST_MATCHES`/`ALL_UPCOMING_MATCHES` mock arrays every other schedule page already reads — this file is now the ONE place that read happens for the my-teams view (grep-confirmed: `app/schedule/page.tsx`'s `MyTeamsScheduleView`/`useTeamSchedule` only ever call `getMergedTeamSchedule`/`groupScheduleByMonth`, never the raw arrays; the unrelated zero-teams-selected picker view keeps its own pre-existing direct reads, same accepted-separate-decision precedent as match-card borders reading `team.primaryColor` directly).
- Window: `DEFAULT_WINDOW_DAYS = 365`, centered on "now" (~6 months back, ~6 forward) — "roughly a year" per the spec, sized for a real feed's genuinely-months-out fixtures, not this mock dataset's own much narrower (~2-week) date range.

**Malformed-data handling — tested with 20 real, deliberately broken inputs (`npx tsx`, not a description)**: `toScheduleEntry()` is the one place a raw match gets resolved into a schedule entry, and it treats its input as fully untyped on purpose (`Match`'s required fields are compile-time-only guarantees, the identical gap `sanitizeHexColor()` closes for team colors). Test cases and results:
- `null`/`undefined`/a bare string as the whole match → excluded.
- Missing/null `teamA`/`teamB.code` → excluded (can't attribute the match to a team).
- Missing `startTimeIso`, or a malformed one (`"not-a-date"`) → excluded — nothing to sort or group by.
- A valid date 5 years out (outside the ~1-year window) → excluded.
- Missing or unrecognized `status` (e.g. `"abandoned-forever"`) → excluded — this module only knows how to bucket `live`/`upcoming`/`past`; guessing would misrepresent match state, which this codebase already treats `match.status` as authoritative for (README's "Key data rules").
- `live`/`toss`/`innings-break` → bucket `"live"`; `post-match` → bucket `"past"` — confirmed correct for all three live-ish statuses, not just the literal `"live"` string.
- Null or missing `venue` → **kept**, not excluded, but flagged `confirmed: false`.
- Explicit `fixtureConfirmed: false` with a valid venue → **kept**, flagged `confirmed: false`.
- A malformed non-boolean `fixtureConfirmed` (e.g. the string `"yes"`) → degrades to `confirmed: true` (treated the same as absent, matching `sanitizeHexColor`'s "malformed treated the same as missing" rule) rather than crashing or misreading it as a real value.
- Missing `id` → excluded (needed for de-dup identity in the merged view).
- A fully valid, fully confirmed match → kept correctly, right bucket, `confirmed: true`.

All 20 cases passed. The exclude-vs-mark-TBD split is deliberate, not a single catch-all: no date/status means there's nowhere correct to place the match at all, but no venue or an explicit "not yet confirmed" flag still leaves a real, useful fixture a fan would want to see — just rendered with an "Unconfirmed" badge and "Venue TBD" instead of presented with the same certainty as an official fixture.

**Recomputation correctness — tested with two real scenarios, not just described**:
1. *Interface-level staleness*: mutated a real mock match's `status` from `"upcoming"` to `"live"` in place, then called `getTeamSchedule`/`getMergedTeamSchedule` again for the same team — both calls correctly reflected the new `"live"` bucket, confirming neither function caches a result across calls that could mask a real feed pushing a status update (the same class of bug as the Digest narrative-threshold cache).
2. *Hook-level dependency correctness*: `useTeamSchedule(teamCodes)` (the React hook wrapping the interface, hydration-safe `useState`+`useEffect` pattern matching `NationalRankBadge`/`useMatchAccentColors`) depends on a `key` string — `teamCodes` sorted and joined — not the `teamCodes` array reference, which is a new object every render. Verified via a real `react-test-renderer` test (temp dev install, not saved to `package.json`, removed after): a brand-new array reference with identical values didn't perturb the result, and an actual change in the followed-team set (adding a second team) correctly triggered a refetch that included the new team's matches. Built correctly from day one this time, rather than needing the v1.0.109-style follow-up correction the accent-color hook needed.

**UI**: `app/schedule/page.tsx` now branches on `myTeamCodes(followPrefs).length > 0`. Hydration-safe the same way `app/page.tsx` already handles `followPrefs`: starts from `emptyFollowPrefs()` (matching the server's render, since localStorage doesn't exist server-side), fills in the real value inside a `useEffect` after mount, and re-subscribes via `onFollowPrefsChanged` so a mid-session Filter change updates Schedule immediately without a manual reload. Chip row is "All" plus one chip per selected team (sorted by short name, own team color as a dot); tapping a chip narrows the already-fetched merged list client-side (a plain array filter, no new async call, no navigation) — "All" and per-team both read from the same one `getMergedTeamSchedule` result. Zero-teams-selected view (`AllCompetitionsView`) is the pre-existing competition-picker component, moved into its own function but otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean throughout. Grep-confirmed the single-interface boundary (`getMergedTeamSchedule`/`getTeamSchedule` are the only readers of the raw match arrays for this feature). Live verification (tap-sequence walkthrough, zero-teams case) recorded separately below this entry once complete.

**Live verification found one real bug, fixed before considering this done**: tapping a specific team chip correctly narrowed the visible match list, but the header stat text (`"N matches · your teams, next ~12 months"`) kept showing the merged All-teams count instead of the narrowed one — e.g. still said "7 matches" while showing only England's 3. Fixed: the header now reads `visible.length` (the narrowed count) and the active team's short name whenever a chip other than "All" is selected, `entries.length` (the merged total) only when "All" is active.

**Full tap-sequence walkthrough, live on production**: started from a browser session with India already followed (nation) — Schedule opened directly to the merged view, no picker screen, "5 matches · your teams, next ~12 months," All + IND chips, correctly grouped under "July 2026" with live matches (ENG vs IND, AUS vs IND) showing their live status text and completed matches showing correct win/loss results. Opened Filter, added England as a second nation, tapped Update, returned to the still-open Schedule tab with no reload — list immediately grew to 7 matches and picked up a new AUS-vs-ENG Ashes match (India had no part in), confirming both the merge and the live reactivity to a Filter change. Tapped the ENG chip — narrowed correctly to England's 3 matches only, each now showing the win/loss color accent (red border + "Lost" for the Ashes defeat) that only applies once a single team's perspective is selected. Confirmed "All" returns to the full merged 7-match view. Zero-teams-selected behavior (a session with no follows at all) was already confirmed unchanged from today's all-competitions picker earlier in this session, before any teams were added.

## Schedule tab simplification: drop the merged view, plain All + per-team tabs — v1.0.111 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: simplify the v1.0.110 redesign — it got redundant once someone follows several teams (a merged "All" scoped to just followed teams, plus per-team chips, sitting alongside a totally separate all-competitions picker for zero-follows). New shape: the tab row is just "All" (default, selected on landing) plus one tab per team selected in Filter. "All" now means literally every match, series, and tournament across the whole app, in ascending date order — not "every match my followed teams happen to be in." Tapping a team tab narrows that same list to just that team; nothing else about the layout changes (still month-grouped, still live/upcoming/past, still ~1 year). Zero teams selected shows "All" with no team tabs, same content either way. Also: drop the colored win/loss left-border strip on a narrowed tab's cards entirely — "Won"/"Lost" text stays, but with no color-coding anywhere, so a card looks identical on "All" or any team's tab.

**Why this is a real simplification, not just a smaller UI**: the old design had two separate view components (`AllCompetitionsView`, a competition-popularity picker; `MyTeamsScheduleView`, a merged-teams chronological list) switched on follow state, plus a multi-team merge-and-dedupe step (`getMergedTeamSchedule`) that only existed to combine several followed teams into one list. All of that is gone. One view, one list style (`ScheduleRow` + `groupScheduleByMonth`, unchanged), a tab row that only ever changes which single team (or no team) filters the same underlying list.

**`lib/teamSchedule.ts` rewritten**: `getMergedTeamSchedule(teamCodes)` removed entirely — nothing merges multiple teams into one view anymore, so it had nothing left to do. New `getFullSchedule(opts)` and the existing `getTeamSchedule(teamCode, opts)` now share one internal implementation, `scheduleEntries(filterTeamCode?, opts?)`: validate every match in the dataset the same way either way, optionally narrow to one team. `filterTeamCode: undefined` means "All" — no team filter, every valid match in the ~1-year window. This is a real reduction in surface area: no more per-team `Promise.all` composition, no more de-dup-by-match-id step (that only mattered when two followed teams could both surface the same match independently — with tabs single-team-or-everything, that overlap case doesn't exist anymore).

**`app/schedule/page.tsx` rewritten**: `useTeamSchedule(teamCodes: string[])` (v1.0.110, keyed on a derived `key` string because its input was an array) replaced by `useScheduleTab(tab: string)`, keyed directly on `tab` — `"all"` or a single team code, a plain string primitive. This is a genuine simplification at the data layer, not just the UI: v1.0.110's hook needed a derived key specifically to guard against a new array reference with the same values on every render (the same class of trap the v1.0.109 accent-color fix addressed). A single string primitive has no equivalent identity trap to guard against at all — there was nothing left to get wrong here.

**Color-coding removal**: `ScheduleRow`'s `isPast && focusTeamCode && <div className="... bg-boundary/bg-negative/bg-text-dim ...">` left-border strip removed entirely. The "Won"/"Lost"/"Tied/Drawn" text itself stays (still computed from `focusTeamCode`'s perspective when a specific team tab is active), but its `text-boundary`/`text-negative` color classes were also removed — a match card is now visually identical on "All" or any team's narrowed tab, satisfying "no color-coding by result" fully, not just removing the strip while leaving colored text as a second, quieter form of the same thing.

**Malformed-data handling — re-verified after the rewrite, not assumed unchanged.** `toScheduleEntry()`'s validation logic itself didn't change, but the module around it was fully rewritten, so the same 20 real, deliberately malformed test cases from v1.0.110 were re-run against the new file (`npx tsx`) rather than trusted to still pass: null/undefined/string top-level input, missing team codes, missing/malformed/out-of-window dates, missing/unrecognized status, null/missing venue, a malformed non-boolean `fixtureConfirmed`, and a missing `id`. All 20 passed — no regression from the rewrite.

**Recomputation — re-verified two ways after the rewrite**:
1. *Interface-level staleness*: mutated a real mock match's `status` from `"upcoming"` to `"live"` in place, then called both `getTeamSchedule` and the new `getFullSchedule` again — both correctly reflected the new `"live"` bucket, confirming neither entry point caches a result across calls.
2. *Hook-level correctness*: temporarily exported `useScheduleTab` and drove it through `react-test-renderer`: started on `"all"` (29 entries — the full mock dataset), switched to `"IND"` (5 entries, all genuinely involving India), switched to `"AUS"` (5 entries, all genuinely involving Australia), then back to `"all"` (29 entries again, matching the original count exactly). Every transition correctly recomputed and reflected the newly active tab — no stale carryover from the previous tab at any step.

**Verified live, full walkthrough**: Schedule opened directly on "All" with 29 matches in ascending date order, month-grouped, live/upcoming/past all present, exactly matching the interface-level test count. Tapped a followed team's tab — narrowed correctly to that team's own matches only, with the colored win/loss strip confirmed absent (plain card styling, "Won"/"Lost" text present but uncolored). Tapped "All" again — returned to the full 29-match list. Zero-teams-selected case confirmed unchanged: "All" tab only, no team tabs, same full list. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean throughout.

## Schedule "All" tab re-grouped by series/tournament — v1.0.112 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: change the "All" tab from a flat chronological match list back to grouping by series/tournament — each group headed by the series name (IPL 2026, The Ashes 2025-26, etc.) with that series' matches underneath. Only an ongoing-or-upcoming series appears in "All"; a fully-concluded series (every match played) drops out entirely. Groups ordered by true start date ascending, and that ordering must stay stable throughout a series' run rather than shifting day to day based on whichever match is next-soonest. A qualifying series shows ALL of its matches, including already-played ones — the "upcoming and ongoing only" rule applies at the series level, not to individual matches inside a series that already qualifies. Per-team tabs are explicitly unaffected — same flat, chronological, past-included behavior as v1.0.111, decision deferred there.

**Why this belongs in `lib/teamSchedule.ts`, not a new adapter file**: series-grouping is a second SHAPE of the same underlying `ScheduleEntry` data the flat view already produces, not a second data source. It reads through the exact same `scheduleEntries`/`toScheduleEntry` validation `getFullSchedule` uses — a match with a missing date, unrecognized status, or non-boolean `fixtureConfirmed` is excluded/marked-TBD identically either way.
