Splitting that validation across two files would risk the flat and grouped views silently disagreeing about which matches are even valid.

**New `safeCompetition(match)`**: `Match.competition` is typed as a required `Competition` object, but — the same compile-time-only guarantee as every other field this pattern treats defensively — a real feed can send a match with a null/missing `competition`, or one missing `id`/`name`. A match that fails this check is excluded from series grouping entirely: there's no correct group for it, and guessing would risk silently merging unrelated series under a placeholder name. This does NOT remove the match from the app — it can still surface on a per-team tab via `getTeamSchedule`, which has no dependency on `competition` at all.

**New `getSeriesGroupedSchedule()`**: computes two things from an effectively-unbounded (`UNBOUNDED_WINDOW_DAYS = 36500`, ~100 years — stands in for "no window" without `Infinity`-arithmetic edge cases) call to `getFullSchedule`, separately from what's actually displayed:
- **Qualification**: a competition qualifies for "All" if ANY of its matches (anywhere in the dataset, not just inside the display window) is live or upcoming. A competition where every match has already been played never qualifies.
- **Ordering**: each qualifying competition's sort key is its TRUE earliest match date across every match it has, not just the earliest one still inside the display window or still upcoming — this is what keeps a series' position stable as its own matches complete over time.

What's actually rendered per qualifying series is still the normal ~1-year windowed set (`getFullSchedule(opts)`) — the unbounded lookup decides qualification and ordering only, never what a user sees on screen. Ties in start date break deterministically by competition id.

**Malformed series metadata — tested with 10 real, deliberately broken inputs (`npx tsx`)**: null `competition`, `{}` (missing `id`/`name`), `id: ""`, `name: ""`, `id` wrong type (number), `name` wrong type (number), `competition` as a bare string instead of an object, and the `competition` field missing entirely. All 10 excluded cleanly from series grouping with no crash — confirmed the pipeline degrades gracefully rather than mis-grouping or throwing. (A missing/malformed `startTimeIso` was also re-confirmed excluded, inherited unchanged from `toScheduleEntry`.)

**Functional + ordering + stability — verified with real constructed data, not description (16 more cases, `npx tsx`)**:
- A synthetic two-match series with both matches `post-match` → correctly absent from `getSeriesGroupedSchedule()`'s output.
- A synthetic series with one past + one upcoming match → correctly present, and BOTH matches (past included) appear in its `entries`.
- Three synthetic series with known, distinct earliest dates, inserted into the mock arrays in scrambled order (late, early, mid) → came back sorted ascending by true start date (early, mid, late), confirming insertion order has no bearing on output order.
- A synthetic series with an earlier already-played match and a later upcoming one: confirmed its position among other series, then flipped the later match to `post-match` (simulating it completing) — the series correctly dropped from the output entirely (both its matches now past). Reverted the later match back to `upcoming` — the series correctly reappeared, at the exact same position as before, confirming its ordering key is genuinely anchored to the untouched earliest match, not recomputed from whatever's currently soonest.
- Against the real (unmutated) mock dataset: confirmed `IPL 2026` (16 matches, a mix of live/past/upcoming) is present and shows past and upcoming matches together, while `ICC T20 World Cup 2026`, `ICC Champions Trophy 2025`, `The Ashes 2025-26`, and `Big Bash League 2025-26` — each exactly one match, already `post-match` — are all correctly absent.

All 26 interface-level cases passed.

**Recomputation at the hook level — verified with a real render, not just the interface (`react-test-renderer`, temporarily exported `useScheduleTab`, 5 cases)**: mounted on `"all"` with a synthetic ongoing series present in `seriesGroups`; switched to a team tab and confirmed `seriesGroups` is empty there (grouping only ever applies to "All", `entries` still populated for the team's flat list); flipped the synthetic series' remaining match to `post-match` while away from "All"; switched back to "All" and confirmed the series was correctly dropped — no stale cache from the mount-time fetch; reverted the match to `upcoming` and confirmed the series reappeared on the next visit to "All". All 5 passed, confirming `useScheduleTab` re-fetches fresh on every switch back to "All" rather than reusing a cached result, the same recompute discipline `getFullSchedule`/`getTeamSchedule` already had.

**UI (`app/schedule/page.tsx`)**: `useScheduleTab(tab)` now fetches genuinely different shapes depending on the tab — `getSeriesGroupedSchedule()` for `"all"` (returns `seriesGroups`), `getTeamSchedule(tab)` for a team code (returns flat `entries`, unchanged from v1.0.111). "All" renders one section per `SeriesGroup`, headed by `competition.name`, with `ScheduleRow` unchanged underneath (same card styling, no color-coding, same "Unconfirmed"/live/date treatment). A team tab still renders via `groupScheduleByMonth(entries)`, byte-for-byte the same as before. Header match count for "All" now reflects the flattened, already-qualifying-series-only total, not the full (possibly larger, pre-exclusion) `getFullSchedule()` count.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean throughout. Grep-confirmed `getSeriesGroupedSchedule` has exactly one caller (`app/schedule/page.tsx`) and lives in exactly one file (`lib/teamSchedule.ts`) alongside the other schedule accessors — no stray direct reads of `match.competition` were introduced for this feature (pre-existing direct reads in `app/schedule/[competitionId]/page.tsx`, `[teamCode]/page.tsx`, and `app/page.tsx`'s popularity sort predate this change and are out of scope, the same accepted-separate-decision precedent as match-card borders reading `team.primaryColor` directly). Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Schedule "All" tab collapsed to one row per series + new dedicated series page — v1.0.113 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: the v1.0.112 series-grouping/exclusion/ordering logic was confirmed correct and stays unchanged — what needed to change was presentation. "All" was showing every qualifying series' matches inline under its heading; that's more detail than intended. Collapse each qualifying series to one summary row: name, a "LIVE" badge if any match in that series is currently in progress, the date of that series' next upcoming-or-live match, and a single "Last: ..." recap line of its most recently completed match (e.g. "Last: KKR won by 7 wickets vs RR"). No match list, no score details beyond the one-line recap. Tapping a row opens a new dedicated page for that series showing every match it contains — past, live, upcoming — in ascending date order, same match-card format used elsewhere. Per-team tabs unaffected.

**Why this stays in `lib/teamSchedule.ts`, no new adapter file**: `summarizeSeriesGroup()` and `formatLastResult()` are pure presentation derivations over an already-fetched `SeriesGroup` — the same category as `groupScheduleByMonth`, not a data-access boundary, so no async/interface treatment needed on their own. `getMatchesForCompetition(competitionId)` (the dedicated page's data source) and `getAllCompetitionIds()` (its `generateStaticParams` source) both read through `getFullSchedule`/`safeCompetition`, the same validation every other function in this file already uses — no duplicated logic, no second place a match could be judged valid by one function and invalid by another.

**`getMatchesForCompetition` deliberately has NO inclusion-rule filtering** — unlike `getSeriesGroupedSchedule`'s "ongoing/upcoming only" rule for "All" rows, a series' own dedicated page shows every match it has ever had, fully-concluded or not. Confirmed: `getMatchesForCompetition("ashes-2025-26")` (a fully-concluded real series, absent from "All") still returns its match. `getAllCompetitionIds()` matches this — it enumerates every competition id that's ever had a valid match, not just qualifying ones, so `generateStaticParams` builds a page for every series (11 total in the mock dataset), not just the 7 currently shown as rows on "All."

**The two fail-safe cases called out explicitly in the product ask, both handled and tested**:
- *No completed matches yet*: `SeriesSummary.lastCompletedEntry` is `undefined`; the row simply omits the "Last:" line. Verified with a constructed series that has only an upcoming match — no line rendered, no crash.
- *Qualifies but nothing live/upcoming inside the display window (shouldn't happen given the exclusion rule, but confirmed to fail safe)*: `SeriesSummary.nextEntry` is `undefined` in this case; the row omits the date entirely rather than showing blank or garbage. Verified with a constructed `SeriesGroup` whose only entry is past-bucket despite the group technically "qualifying" upstream.

**Malformed-result hardening — `hasUsableResult()` strengthened**: previously only checked that `result.winner` was a non-empty string. Now also requires it to be `"draw"`/`"tie"`/`"no-result"`, or to genuinely match one of that match's own `teamA`/`teamB` codes, before a past match is eligible to become a series' `lastCompletedEntry` — a `winner` that matches neither team (stale/malformed data) is treated the same as "no usable result." `formatLastResult()` keeps its own independent fallback for this same case regardless (names just the winner, no fabricated "vs" opponent) as a second line of defense, verified directly. Tested: a series whose most-recent past match has an unusable winner correctly skips it and falls back to an earlier match with a usable one, rather than showing nothing or crashing.

**Recap text accuracy — tested against the product spec's own example and more (6/6 pass, `npx tsx`)**: a real win ("KKR won by 7 wickets vs RR," verbatim match to the spec's example) with `teamA`/`teamB` in both orders (confirming the winner is identified by matching `result.winner` to a team code, not by positional assumption), a draw ("Drawn: KKR vs RR"), a tie ("Tied: KKR vs RR"), a no-result ("No result: KKR vs RR"), a missing `result` object entirely (empty string, not a crash), and a malformed winner matching neither team (falls back to "SRH won by 10 runs" with no fabricated opponent).

**Recomputation — 8/8 pass, real constructed mutation sequence (`npx tsx`)**: a single-match synthetic series walked through `upcoming → live → post-match (with result) → upcoming again`, calling `getSeriesGroupedSchedule()`/`summarizeSeriesGroup()` and `getMatchesForCompetition()` fresh after each in-place status mutation. `isLive` flipped to `true` immediately on the live transition; the series correctly dropped from "All" the instant its only match completed; `getMatchesForCompetition()` kept showing the match throughout (confirming its lack of inclusion-rule filtering is real, not accidental); the series correctly reappeared in "All" once the match was reverted to upcoming. No caching anywhere in this new path, matching the discipline already established for `getFullSchedule`/`getTeamSchedule`/`getSeriesGroupedSchedule`.

**Component reuse — `ScheduleRow` extracted, not duplicated**: `ScheduleRow` (plus `TeamChip`/`fmtDate`/`fmtTime`) moved out of `app/schedule/page.tsx` into `components/ScheduleRow.tsx` specifically so the new dedicated series page renders the exact same match card already used on every Schedule tab, structurally guaranteed identical rather than merely copy-pasted to look the same at one point in time.

**New route**: `app/schedule/series/[competitionId]/page.tsx` — a plain async server component (no client-side state needed; its only interactivity is `Link` navigation), `generateStaticParams` sourced from `getAllCompetitionIds()`. Deliberately a new, separate route from the pre-existing `/schedule/[competitionId]` (still linked from `components/MiniStandings.tsx` for its own standings-drill-down purpose, reads the raw mock arrays directly, live/upcoming only) — that route predates this real-data-readiness pattern and stays out of scope here, the same accepted-separate-decision precedent as match-card borders reading `team.primaryColor` directly.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean throughout — the build now generates 11 static pages under `/schedule/series/[competitionId]`, one per competition id in the mock dataset (7 currently qualifying for an "All" row, plus 4 fully-concluded ones still individually reachable). Grep-confirmed `getMatchesForCompetition`/`getAllCompetitionIds`/`summarizeSeriesGroup`/`formatLastResult` each have exactly the callers expected (the new page, and `app/schedule/page.tsx`), and `ScheduleRow` is imported only by those same two files. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

**Live-verification bug found and fixed**: the first live check showed "Last: CSK won by by 4 wickets vs RCB" — a doubled "by." Root cause: every `Match.result.margin` value in this mock dataset already includes its own "by" prefix (`"by 4 wickets"`, `"by an innings and 27 runs"`, etc. — confirmed by grepping every `margin:` literal in `lib/mockData.ts`, no exceptions), the same convention `ScheduleRow` already relies on when it appends `match.result.margin` directly with no "by" of its own. `formatLastResult()`'s initial version added its own `` by ${margin}``, producing the double prefix. Fixed by joining with a plain space (`` ${margin}``) instead, matching the existing convention. Re-verified against the real dataset (`npx tsx`): IPL 2026's row now correctly reads "CSK won by 4 wickets vs RCB." Re-ran `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean, re-committed, re-pushed, re-verified live before considering this feature done.

## Fix Schedule tab row ordering: nations before franchise teams — v1.0.114 (2026-07-24)

**Bug**: the Schedule tab row's followed-team tabs (everything after "All") were sorted with one flat alphabetical pass across every followed team code combined, regardless of whether it was a nation or a franchise/league team. That meant a franchise team like CSK could land ahead of a nation like IND purely because "C" < "I" — e.g. `All, CSK, IND` instead of the expected `All, IND, CSK`.

**Fix**: `tabTeams` in `app/schedule/page.tsx` now sorts by category first (nation before franchise), then alphabetically by `shortName` within each category — nations always precede franchise/league teams regardless of letter order, and each group is still internally alphabetical. Category comes from `Team.type` (`"national"` vs `"franchise"`), which is the exact same categorization Filter's own Nations/Teams sections use: `ALL_TEAMS` in `lib/mockData.ts` is literally `{ ...TEAMS, ...NATIONAL_TEAMS, ...LEAGUE_TEAMS }`, and every entry in `NATIONAL_TEAMS` has `type: "national"`, every entry in `LEAGUE_TEAMS` has `type: "franchise"` (confirmed by grep — 40/40 in `LEAGUE_TEAMS`). Reading `team.type` here is reading the same categorization Filter uses, not a second one that could drift out of sync with it.

**Verified with a real test** (`npx tsx`, sort function extracted and run directly against constructed team objects): two nations + one franchise team in scrambled input order came back correctly grouped and alphabetized (`AUS, ENG, IND` before `CSK`); a mixed four-team case (two nations, two franchise teams, scrambled) came back `AUS, IND, CSK, MI` — nations alphabetical first, then franchise teams alphabetical; nations-only and franchise-only cases each stayed correctly alphabetical; single-item and empty cases handled without error. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. No other change — this only touches tab ordering, not what any tab shows, how "All" is scoped, or any of the v1.0.113 series-row/dedicated-page work.


## Unify Filter/Follow sheet's selection accent from purple to cyan — v1.0.115 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: `FollowSheet`'s selection-state UI — checkbox checkmarks, each category's "N selected" count badge (Nations, Tournaments, Series, Teams, Players, Formats), and the "Update (N)" submit button — used a dedicated purple as its own accent, distinct from the platform's one cyan active/selected accent used everywhere else. Unify all three to cyan. Explicit scoping constraint: the `six` token (`#A855F7`, the ball-outcome six-run color) is a different purple and must not change; this is a `FollowSheet`-only fix, not a purple removal pass across the codebase.

**Scope-boundary check performed first**: grepped every file referencing `FollowSheet`/"Follow your cricket" (`lib/followPrefs.ts`, `app/page.tsx`, `components/FollowSheet.tsx`, `components/BottomSheet.tsx`, `components/BottomNav.tsx`) for purple/`7C3AED`/`follow`-token usage before changing anything, to separate what's genuinely inside the sheet's own selection UI (in scope) from other consumers of the same shared `follow` design token (out of scope). Two legitimate out-of-scope consumers found and confirmed untouched:
- `components/BottomNav.tsx`'s Filter tab — turns `text-follow`/`bg-follow` (Violet 600, `#7C3AED`) while the sheet is open. Left as-is; this is the nav trigger, not the sheet's own selection state, and the user's scoping note implies it should stay purple.
- `app/page.tsx`'s "FOR YOU" label and its empty-state nudge card (raw `#7C3AED` in 5 places) — a separate homepage feature that happens to share the same violet, unrelated to the Follow sheet itself.

The `follow` token's own definition in `tailwind.config.ts` was deliberately left unchanged (not edited) — editing it would have silently also recolored `BottomNav.tsx`'s Filter tab, which is explicitly meant to stay purple per the scoping note. Instead, every inline `#7C3AED` occurrence and the one `focus:border-follow` Tailwind class were replaced directly and only inside `components/FollowSheet.tsx`.

**Contrast convention applied, not a naive color swap**: cyan (`#00E5FF`) is a much brighter fill than the purple it replaced, so a straight hex swap would have left white checkmark/badge/button text sitting on bright cyan — poor contrast, and inconsistent with how every other cyan-filled element in this app already renders. Grepped `MatchCard.tsx`, `ViewSwitcher.tsx`, `InsightsPanel.tsx`, `DigestTab.tsx`, `DemoControls.tsx` and confirmed the established convention is always `bg-cyan` + dark text/icon (`text-bg`, `#0A0E1A`), never white-on-cyan. Applied that same convention here: the checkmark's `stroke` changed from `"white"` to `"#0A0E1A"`, and both the count-badge and "Update" button's text color classes changed from `text-white` to `text-bg`.

**Changed in `components/FollowSheet.tsx` only** (verified via `git diff --stat`, single file, 41 insertions / 9 deletions):
- `CheckIndicator` — fill/border `#7C3AED` → `#00E5FF`; checkmark stroke `white` → `#0A0E1A`.
- Category left-rail active-state border — `#7C3AED` → `#00E5FF`.
- Category "N selected" count badge — fill `#7C3AED` → `#00E5FF`; text `text-white` → `text-bg`.
- "Update (N)" submit button — fill `#7C3AED` → `#00E5FF`; text `text-white` → `text-bg`.
- Search input focus border — Tailwind class `focus:border-follow` → `focus:border-cyan`.

**Confirmed untouched**: the `six` token (`#A855F7`) and every one of its ~11+ consumers (`BallGIF`, `MiniBallGIF`, `MomentStoryCard`, `WinProbChart`'s event dots, `Scorecard`'s "6s" column, `OverSummary`, `DigestTab.tsx`, `MomentsStrip.tsx`, `MatchTabs.tsx`, `MatchupCard.tsx`, `lib/outcomeColors.ts`) — zero diff anywhere in that set. The `follow` token's definition in `tailwind.config.ts`, `components/BottomNav.tsx`, and `app/page.tsx`'s "FOR YOU" nudge — all confirmed unchanged via `git diff`.

**DESIGN-SYSTEM.md updated** to reflect `follow`'s now-narrower actual scope: the §3 token table and the §4 "Filter/personalization violet" table both corrected to remove the now-inaccurate claim that `follow` covers `FollowSheet`'s checkbox fill, category badges, and "Update" button — those three now use `cyan`. `follow`'s documented remaining scope is the bottom-nav Filter icon/label (while the sheet is open) and the homepage "FOR YOU" label + nudge card border.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.


## Sort Filter/Follow sheet's Nations tab by ICC membership tier — v1.0.116 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: sort the Nations list inside `FollowSheet` by membership status -- full ICC members first, associate nations after, alphabetical within each group -- using the existing `getTeamMembershipStatus()` adapter (`lib/teamData.ts`, built in v1.0.102 and already consumed by Spotlight's international-match gate) rather than a hardcoded list of full-member names, so the sort re-resolves correctly with zero code changes whenever real ICC membership data eventually replaces the mock `Team.membershipStatus` field. Handle a nation with no resolved status defensively -- no crash, no silent drop from the list.

**Why this is the adapter's second real consumer, not a new one**: `lib/spotlight.ts`'s `buildFullMemberLookup()` already established the shape needed here -- `getTeamMembershipStatus()` is async (by design, since a real ranking/membership API is necessarily a network call), but the actual filter/sort consuming it (`Array.filter` for Spotlight, `Array.sort` for this) has to stay synchronous. Both resolve the adapter once, upfront, into a plain synchronous lookup, then read that lookup inside the sync operation. `components/FollowSheet.tsx`'s new `membershipRank()` follows the exact same shape: a `nationMembership` state (`Map<string, MembershipStatus | undefined>`), populated by a `useEffect` that resolves `getTeamMembershipStatus(team)` for every entry in `NATIONAL_TEAMS` via `Promise.all` once per sheet-open (mirroring the app's established "recompute fresh on every open, no stale cache" discipline already used by `useScheduleTab`/`useMatchAccentColors`), then read synchronously inside the nations `Array.sort` comparator in `buildOptions()`.

**Fail-safe placement for missing/malformed status, decided and documented**: a nation whose status doesn't resolve to exactly `"full"` or `"associate"` -- `undefined` (missing from the data) or, despite the type, some other value that slipped through at runtime -- gets `membershipRank() === 2`, placing it in its own trailing group after both real tiers, sorted alphabetically among any other unclassified nations. Rejected alternatives: folding it into "associate" would assert a specific tier the data doesn't actually support (a stronger and possibly wrong claim than "unknown"); dropping it from the list outright would make a followable nation disappear. Trailing-on-its-own is the one option that stays honest about the uncertainty while keeping the nation visible and followable.

**Changed in `components/FollowSheet.tsx` only**:
- New `membershipRank(status)` (exported for direct testing) -- `"full"` -> 0, `"associate"` -> 1, anything else (including `undefined`) -> 2.
- `buildOptions()` signature gained a `nationMembership: Map<string, MembershipStatus | undefined>` parameter; only the `"nations"` case reads it -- `teams`/`tournaments`/`series`/`players`/`formats` are unchanged, still `a.label.localeCompare(b.label)` alone.
- New `nationMembership` state + resolution `useEffect`, gated on `open` (skips work while the sheet is closed) with a `cancelled` guard against a stale resolve landing after an early close/reopen.
- `options` `useMemo` now depends on `nationMembership` in addition to `activeCategory`, so the list re-sorts once resolution completes.
- Search (`filteredOptions`) is untouched -- it `.filter()`s whatever `buildOptions()` already produced, so it inherits the new order for free rather than needing its own sort-awareness.

**Verified with a real test (`npx tsx`, `membershipRank` imported directly from the component, 7/7 pass)**: full+associate mix in scrambled order sorts correctly (full members alphabetical, then associates alphabetical); all-full and all-associate lists stay purely alphabetical; a nation with `status: undefined` trails after both real tiers without crashing or disappearing; a nation with a malformed non-"full"/"associate" string value is treated identically to "missing" (same trailing group, not silently accepted as a real tier); an empty list produces no crash; and the pre-resolve state (every nation unclassified, simulating the one render before the `useEffect` fills in `nationMembership`) degrades safely to plain alphabetical, matching the documented fallback.

**Verified scope**: `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Grep-confirmed no other `buildOptions()` case reads `nationMembership`, and `git diff --stat` shows only `components/FollowSheet.tsx` changed. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.


## Player profile: recent-form graph + achievements callout — v1.0.117 (2026-07-24)

**Product ask**: add two things to the player profile page (`/player/[id]`): a recent-form graph (one point per innings/spell across the player's last 10 for the currently selected format tab, same thin-line/dot-marker visual language as the existing `BatterSparkline`, colored via the real team-accent-color pipeline) and an achievements callout below it (one line per qualifying recent achievement -- e.g. "Won 3 Man of the Match awards in last 10 matches", "Man of the Series vs South Africa, July 2026" -- stacking as many as genuinely apply, correct singular/plural, whole section omitted if nothing qualifies). Explicitly out of scope: anything about a player's upcoming matches -- playing XI isn't confirmed until close to a match, so showing it with any confidence would be misleading.

**Data model split (`lib/types.ts`)**: `PlayerProfile` gained `testRecentForm`/`odiRecentForm`/`t20iRecentForm`/`franchiseRecentForm` (`RecentFormWindow`), mirroring the exact per-format optional-field shape `testStats`/`odiStats`/`t20iStats`/`franchiseStats` already used -- not a new disambiguation scheme, the same one. Each window: a chronological `values: number[]` (real recorded entries only, NEVER padded to a fixed length of 10) plus an optional `achievements` object (`manOfMatchAwards?: number`, `manOfSeriesAwards?: { opponent, dateLabel }[]`). Also centralized the four-tab vocabulary (`"test" | "odi" | "t20i" | "franchise"`) as an exported `PlayerFormatKey` in `lib/types.ts` -- previously redeclared locally inside `PlayerProfileView.tsx` as a private `FormatKey`; now both that component and the new adapter share one definition instead of two that could drift.

**New `lib/playerForm.ts` adapter** -- the ONLY sanctioned read path for those four fields, following the same interface-first pattern as `lib/teamData.ts`/`lib/teamAccentColor.ts`:
- `getRecentForm(player, format): Promise<RecentFormSeries>` -- resolves the last-10 window for one format, defensively: no window recorded at all -> empty series (the graph renders nothing, correctly, for a format with zero recorded innings); `values` not an array -> empty series; individual malformed entries (`null`/`NaN`/negative/wrong-typed) filtered out, valid ones kept; more than 10 real entries -> takes the most recent 10; a malformed `metric` value defaults to `"runs"`.
- `getPlayerAchievements(player, format): Promise<AchievementLine[]>` -- returns one pre-formatted line per qualifying achievement. `manOfMatchAwards` only emits a line when it's a real positive finite number (`0`, negative, `NaN`, or wrong-typed all correctly omit the line rather than rendering "Won 0 Man of the Match awards"); singular/plural resolved in code (`"award"` vs `"awards"`) -- the same class of microcopy bug fixed in the Filter sheet's "N selected" badges (v1.0.61-65 era). `manOfSeriesAwards` emits one line PER array entry (not a count), so multiple qualifying series in the window stack as multiple lines rather than collapsing to one; each entry validated independently -- a malformed entry (missing/blank `opponent`) is skipped without dropping the other valid entries in the same array.
- Both are async-from-day-one (resolve synchronously from mock data today, written against the shape a real per-player stats feed would need). `refreshPlayerForm()` is the same no-op placeholder shape as `refreshRankings()`.

**Reused the existing team-accent-color pipeline, did not reimplement it**: the recent-form graph is themed by the player's own team, with no second team in view to collide against -- `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`'s own v1.0.104-109 header comment explicitly noted every real call site "always has both teams in scope" and there was "no single-team-only entry point exported from this module." This is the first real context that genuinely doesn't have a second team. Rather than duplicating the hairline-contrast/secondary-fallback/cyan-fallback logic, added exactly one new export: `resolveTeamAccentColor(team): Promise<string>`, which is `resolveTeamColorTier(team).color` -- the same private per-team step `resolveMatchAccentColors` already calls twice internally, now also reachable on its own for a context with no collision question to ask. `resolveMatchAccentColors` itself, `resolveTeamColorTier`'s own privacy, and every existing match-context call site (`TeamToggle`/`TestInningsChips`/`InningsCard` in `Scorecard.tsx`) are unchanged. Updated the module's header comment (previously claiming no single-team export existed) and `resolveTeamColorTier`'s own comment to describe the new relationship accurately.

**Team resolution per format tab**: international tabs (test/odi/t20i) theme off the player's national team (`player.teamCode`); the franchise tab themes off their league team (`player.franchiseCode`). A player missing the relevant code, or whose code doesn't resolve to a real `Team` in `ALL_TEAMS`, falls straight to the platform cyan -- the same "no usable color" fallback `resolveTeamAccentColor` already applies to a team with no usable color of its own; no new fallback logic needed at this call site.

**New components**: `components/RecentFormGraph.tsx` (thin smoothed line + a dot at every point -- every point IS the plotted value here, unlike `BatterSparkline` which only dots boundary balls; renders nothing for zero points, a single dot with no line for exactly one point) and `components/PlayerAchievements.tsx` (one line per achievement, using the `special` design token DESIGN-SYSTEM.md already reserves for premium/achievement recognition -- Man of the Series highlight, "Never dismissed" chip, five-for milestone chip -- rather than a new arbitrary color; renders nothing at all, no placeholder, when `lines` is empty).

**Wired into `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`**: a `useEffect` keyed on `[player, activeTab]` fetches `getRecentForm`/`getPlayerAchievements`/the resolved team color together, explicitly resetting all three to empty/`null` SYNCHRONOUSLY before the async calls resolve (plus a `cancelled` guard) -- so a fast tab switch can never show the previous format's graph, achievements, or team color while the new format's data is still in flight. Rendered as siblings below the existing stats grid, independent of whether `stats` itself exists for that tab.

**Malformed-data + recomputation testing (`npx tsx`, 34/34 pass)**: fewer than 10 recorded innings (exactly that many, never padded); no window recorded at all; `values` not an array; individual malformed entries dropped, valid ones kept; more than 10 entries takes the most recent 10; malformed `metric` defaults to `"runs"`; zero innings for a format (empty series, no crash); `manOfMatchAwards` zero/negative/`NaN`/wrong-typed (no line); singular vs plural microcopy (1 -> "award", 3 -> "awards"); multiple achievements stacking (MOM line + 2 MOS lines, not just one); `manOfSeriesAwards` not an array; malformed individual MOS entries skipped without dropping valid siblings; and format-scoping (test/odi/t20i never bleed into each other, confirmed by fetching all three from the same player object and diffing). Also sanity-checked against the real shipped mock data: Bumrah's Test tab (10 spells, 3 MOM + 2 MOS lines -- matching the product spec's own literal examples), his ODI tab (singular MOM line), his T20I tab (zero recorded spells -- no recentForm authored yet for that format), and Crawley's Test tab (6 innings, zero achievements -- section correctly omitted).

**Mock data added** (`lib/mockData.ts`): Bumrah's Test window (10 spells -- the full-window case; 3 MOM awards -- plural; Man of the Series vs South Africa July 2026 and vs Australia November 2025 -- multiple stacking lines, matching the product spec's own examples verbatim), his ODI window (5 spells -- fewer-than-10 case; 1 MOM award -- singular), his franchise/MI window (7 spells, no achievements), and deliberately no T20I window at all (the "zero innings recorded for this format" case, on a real reachable tab -- Bumrah's `t20iStats` aggregate still exists, just no recent-form data authored for it yet). Crawley's Test window (6 innings -- fewer-than-10 case; zero achievements -- the "no achievements" case for a batter).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Documented as a new worked example in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, alongside the existing rankings/membership, accent-color, and Schedule-adapter examples. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Player recent-form/achievements: rebuilt on real match data, not a hand-typed field — v1.0.118 (2026-07-27)

**Why this shipped**: a diagnostic (requested after Zak Crawley's page showed "Last 6 Innings" instead of 10) traced the gap to its root cause. It was NOT a slicing bug — `.filter().slice(-10)` correctly returned all 6 entries in Crawley's `testRecentForm.values`, which was itself just 6 hand-typed numbers `[12, 45, 8, 76, 23, 61]` with no relationship to any real match. Worse: the same mock dataset separately held 4+ real per-match `battingCard` entries for Crawley that the v1.0.117 feature never read at all, because it read exclusively from the disconnected `testRecentForm`/`odiRecentForm`/`t20iRecentForm`/`franchiseRecentForm` fields added in v1.0.117. Only 2 of the 21 seeded players (Bumrah, Crawley) had any of those fields populated — every other player's recent-form section was silently empty not because they lacked real match history, but because nobody had hand-authored a matching side-field for them.

**Data source, fully removed**: `RecentFormWindow` and the four `PlayerProfile` fields were deleted from `lib/types.ts` and `lib/mockData.ts` entirely. Grep-confirmed zero live references anywhere in the codebase — the only remaining hits are two historical explanation comments (one in `lib/types.ts`, one in `lib/playerForm.ts`) documenting why the field existed and why it was removed, for anyone who finds an old reference to it in git history or an old build.

**`lib/playerForm.ts` fully rewritten to derive from real records**: `getRecentForm`/`getPlayerAchievements` keep the exact same public interface and return shapes as v1.0.117 (`RecentFormSeries { points, metric }`, `AchievementLine { text }`) — zero changes needed in `RecentFormGraph.tsx`/`PlayerAchievements.tsx`, the direct payoff of routing everything through one sanctioned interface in the first place. Internally, both now read `Match.innings[].battingCard`/`bowlingCard` (matched to a player via `resolvePlayerSlug()`, reused from `lib/mockData.ts` rather than reinventing name-to-id matching) and `Match.result.manOfMatch`/`manOfTournament` (the same real award fields `Scorecard.tsx` already surfaces as "Man of Series") — never a second, disconnected mock field.

**Scoping decisions, each with a concrete reason**:
- *Settled matches = usable result, never `status`.* A private `hasUsableResult(match)` (real, attributable `result.winner`) gates inclusion across BOTH `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` and `ALL_LIVE_MATCHES` — never `ALL_UPCOMING_MATCHES`, and never filtered by `match.status`. This is load-bearing: `FEATURED_MATCH` is deliberately kept at `status: "live"` for carousel purposes despite being a fully completed match with real batting/bowling cards and real award credits (Andre Russell's MOM, Virat Kohli's MOT). Filtering on `status` would have silently dropped that match's real data.
- *Deterministic sort, not the source array's incidental order.* `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` is sorted newest-first for schedule-list purposes, which does NOT produce correct chronological order once a single match's multiple innings are involved (worked through a concrete counter-example during design: reversing a "grouped-by-match, ascending-within-match" list gives `[B2,B1,A2,A1]` instead of the correct `[B1,B2,A1,A2]`). Every extracted entry instead carries its own `startTimeIso`, and the full set sorts ascending by `(startTimeIso, match.id tiebreak, innings number)` before any `slice(-10)`.
- *Two different "last 10" populations.* The graph plots last-10 innings/spells (per-entry, one Test match can contribute 2); achievements count last-10 *distinct matches* (deduped by `match.id`) — matching the product spec's own wording difference verbatim.
- *Opponent name from the match's own recorded team, not a static profile field.* An achievement line's opponent comes from the specific match's `battingTeam`/`bowlingTeam`, not `player.teamCode`/`franchiseCode` — a profile field says who a player plays for today, not who they played for in a specific historical match.
- *Name matching tolerates real inconsistent data.* The dataset credits the same player's award two different ways across different matches ("Virat Kohli" vs "V Kohli"). `namesMatch()` checks both `player.name` and `player.shortName`, trimmed/case-insensitive.
- *Metric selection for all-rounders.* If a player has real entries in only one discipline for a format, that discipline wins; if both exist, falls back to `player.role === "bowler" ? "wickets" : "runs"`.

**Testable hook extracted, same precedent as `useScheduleTab`/`useMatchAccentColors`**: pulled the fetch-on-`[player, format]` `useEffect`/`useState` block out of `PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s component body into a standalone, exported `usePlayerFormState(player, format)` with zero dependency on `next/navigation`'s `useRouter`, so it can be mounted/remounted directly with `react-test-renderer` without mocking the app router. `PlayerProfileView` now just calls the hook; no other behavior in that file changed.

**Real-data test pass, not a description of expected behavior**:
- *Coverage across all 21 seeded players* (`npx tsx`, direct adapter calls × 4 format tabs × 21 players): 15 of 21 now produce at least one real recent-form point in some format — up from 2 before this rebuild (and those 2 were reading the disconnected field, not real records). The other 6 have zero matches with usable per-innings data anywhere in the mock dataset for any format, and correctly show nothing.
- *Crawley/Bumrah, old vs new*: Crawley's Test tab was `[12,45,8,76,23,61]` runs (6 fake points, 0 achievements) under v1.0.117; it's now `[34,51]` runs (2 real points, from the real `ashes-2526-3rd-test` match) — fewer points than before, correctly, because that's genuinely how many settled Test innings the mock dataset has for him. Bumrah's Test tab was `[2,3,1,4,0,2,5,1,3,2]` wickets (10 fake points, 3 achievements) under v1.0.117; it's now empty on Test (Bumrah has no settled Test match with a recorded bowling figure in this dataset) but shows `[3,2]` wickets on T20I and `[2]` on franchise — real recorded spells the old feature never surfaced on any tab.
- *Malformed real-match-data robustness* (`npx tsx`, 8 constructed cases against `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` mutated in-memory): numeric `playerId`, missing `innings` array, non-array `battingCard`, negative/`NaN` runs, missing `result` object, `result.winner` naming neither team, inconsistent-case/whitespace award name, `null` `startTimeIso`. All 8 handled without throwing; each excluded or included exactly as it should have (the inconsistent-case name still correctly matched and credited, confirming `namesMatch()` works, not just that malformed cases don't crash).
- *Recomputation on live data mutation, direct adapter calls*: appended a brand-new completed match to `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` with a fresh Man of the Match credit for Joe Root — the new runs value and new achievement line both appeared on the very next `getRecentForm`/`getPlayerAchievements` call. Then flipped that same match's `result` to remove the MOM credit — the achievement line disappeared while the runs entry itself remained, confirming form-points and achievements are derived independently rather than sharing one stale snapshot. Removed the simulated match afterward and confirmed the adapter's output returned to byte-identical with the pre-mutation baseline.
- *Recomputation + remount, hook level* (`react-test-renderer`, temp dev install via `npm install --no-save`, removed after — same as the `useMatchAccentColors`/`useScheduleTab` precedent): mounted `usePlayerFormState` on Root's Test tab (real `[62,44]`), switched to ODI (no real Root ODI history — confirmed the Test tab's values did NOT leak through as stale state), switched back to Test (confirmed it reloaded `[62,44]`, not an empty carryover from ODI), then unmounted, mutated `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` with a new 88-run match, and mounted a fresh instance — the new mount immediately reflected `[62,44,88]`, not a cached snapshot from before the unmount.

**Full regression, confirmed by both diff scope and typecheck/build**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.118 commit shows exactly 4 files changed — `lib/types.ts`, `lib/mockData.ts`, `lib/playerForm.ts`, `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`. `components/Scorecard.tsx`, `components/FollowSheet.tsx`, `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`, and `lib/teamData.ts` are untouched, so the fixed outcome colors (wickets/fours/sixes/strike-rate/top-scorer), team-color theming, and the Filter/Follow sheet's cyan accent + membership-tier nations sort are structurally guaranteed unaffected by this change. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean.

**Verified**: documented as an update to the existing worked example in `ARCHITECTURE.md` (not a new example — same interface, rebuilt data source). Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Recent-form graph: labeled axis chart supersedes sparkline styling — v1.0.119 (2026-07-27)

**Product decision, explicitly superseding an earlier one**: v1.0.117 deliberately matched the recent-form graph's visual language to `Scorecard.tsx`'s `BatterSparkline` -- axis-less, minimal, dot-and-line only -- because the graph was originally scoped to sit in a dense scorecard row where there's no room for labels. That reasoning was correct at the time but the product decision has changed: the recent-form graph lives in its own dedicated section of the player page, with room to spare, so a properly labeled small line chart is now judged more useful there. `BatterSparkline` itself is untouched -- it was never a shared component, and its own scorecard-row context still calls for the minimal style.

**Y-axis, computed per player and per metric, never a shared fixed scale**: two new pure functions in `components/RecentFormGraph.tsx`, exported directly as real reusable utilities (not "temporarily exported for testing"):
- `computeYAxisTop(maxValue)`: the scale always starts at 0; the top is that specific player's own highest plotted value in the current window, rounded up to a clean ceiling. The rounding unit itself is chosen by the value's own magnitude, not a per-format constant -- `<=10` rounds to the nearest 5, `<=50` to the nearest 10, `<=100` to the nearest 25, `<=250` to the nearest 50, else the nearest 100. This is the direct, deliberate fix for why a fixed scale across all players would be wrong: a bowler's wickets-per-innings has a hard real-world ceiling of 10 (you cannot take more than 10 wickets in an innings), while a batter's runs can run into the hundreds. One shared scale would either flatten every bowler's graph to an unreadable sliver near zero, or crush a batter's real variation into a few pixels.
- `computeYAxisTop(0)` deliberately returns 4, not 0. An unbroken run of ducks or wicketless spells is real, valid recent-form data -- not missing data -- and still needs a genuine axis; a 0-to-0 scale would collapse every gridline onto the same line, which would look like a bug even though the underlying data is correct. Non-finite/negative inputs fall back to the same path defensively.
- `buildYAxisTicks(top)`: builds 4-5 evenly spaced ticks from 0 to `top`, rounds each to the nearest whole number for display (runs/wickets are always integers), and de-duplicates by rounded label so two ticks can never render the same number stacked on each other. In practice this guard never fires given the clean tops `computeYAxisTop` produces at every tier boundary -- verified directly rather than assumed -- but it's a real runtime check, not just a comment.

**X-axis, minimal by design**: only the two endpoints are labeled -- "N ago" on the left, "Most recent" on the right, where N is exactly the same point count already stated in the section header above the chart ("last N innings/spells"), keeping the two numbers on the card consistent with each other rather than introducing a second, differently-derived count. No per-point labels, no dates, no opponent names anywhere on the graph -- those live in the achievements callout beneath it, not here. A single data point gets one centered "Most recent" label instead of a contradictory "1 ago"/"Most recent" pair, since there's no second point for it to be "ago" relative to.

**Unchanged, by explicit instruction**: the line's color is still the caller's already-resolved team accent color via the existing `lib/teamAccentColor.ts` pipeline -- zero changes there. Every plotted point still gets a colored dot (not just "notable" ones) -- highlighting standout individual performances is the achievements callout's job, not this graph's. A single point still renders as one real dot, now against the full labeled axis rather than a bare, context-less one -- the axis is what gives a lone data point actual meaning (low or high relative to what's genuinely possible for that player/metric).

**Real edge-case tests, not a description of expected behavior (`npx tsx`, 44/44 pass)**: highest value in the window is 0 (ducks/wicketless spells -- top=4, no duplicate ticks); exactly one data point (scale still derives correctly from a single value); a value that already sits exactly on a round number within its own tier's rounding unit (10 -> top 10, the real per-innings wicket ceiling; 20 -> top 20; 50 -> top 50; 100 -> top 100; 250 -> top 250 -- each tier boundary checked against its own applicable unit, not assumed clean across every tier -- an earlier draft of this test incorrectly expected 25 to map to itself, which surfaced that 25 falls in the `<=50`/unit-10 tier and correctly rounds up to 30, not a bug); the largest realistic values (142, the real dataset's actual highest recorded single-innings score; 200; 267; 400 -- confirming large scores still round to a clean, non-misleading ceiling); and a direct side-by-side confirming a bowler's scale (max 3 wickets -> top 5) and a batter's scale (max 142 runs -> top 150) are genuinely different, not a shared fixed range.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.119 commit shows exactly one file changed -- `components/RecentFormGraph.tsx`. `PlayerProfileView.tsx`, `lib/playerForm.ts`, `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`, and `Scorecard.tsx`/`BatterSparkline` are all untouched, so team colors, the achievements callout, and the sparkline's own scorecard-row styling are unaffected by this change. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Player display names: centralized formatting utility, `lastName()` fragility finally resolved — v1.0.120 (2026-07-27)

**Why this shipped**: two confirmed display deviations were reported -- the Live/Score top stat pills showing surname-only ("Kohli") and player profile headers showing the full name ("Zak Crawley") -- with an explicit instruction to fix this comprehensively rather than patch the display convention on top of unresolved parsing. The underlying parsing fragility (`lastName()` naively splitting on the last space, breaking real multi-part surnames) was flagged and logged much earlier in this project and only half-fixed at the time: `getPlayerShortName()` checked the PLAYERS registry's hand-verified `shortName` first, but for anyone not in that local registry it gave up and returned the full name unchanged rather than actually deriving a correct short form.

**New `lib/playerName.ts` -- the single centralized name-formatting module**:
- `parsePlayerName(raw)`: the ONLY place in the codebase that splits a name string. Returns `{ initial, surname, suffix, hasSingleName }`.
- `formatPlayerName(raw)`: the single sanctioned display function. Resolution order: (1) PLAYERS registry lookup by full name or existing shortName, case-insensitive, whitespace-normalized -- authoritative field always wins; (2) algorithmic derivation via `parsePlayerName` for anyone not in the registry, producing "Initial Surname" instead of a non-answer.
- Handles, with real tests for each: multi-word surnames/particles (a contiguous run of recognized particle words -- "de", "van", "der", "von", "du", plus the capitalized-convention "Al" -- immediately before the final surname token folds in; the first token is never folded in, protecting a genuine first name like "Del" or "Van"); suffixes ("Jr.", "III" -- stripped before surname derivation, never rendered, available on the parsed result); hyphenated surnames (handled for free -- splitting only happens on whitespace, never hyphens); single-name players (no first/last split forced, no invented initial); inconsistent capitalization (ALL-CAPS/all-lowercase normalized to Title Case with explicit Mc/Mac/O' surname-prefix rules; genuine mixed case like "McGurk" left completely untouched so it's never mangled into "Mcgurk"); stray whitespace (collapsed before splitting); and a "Surname, First" comma feed format (reversed into plain order up front, then every other rule applies uniformly).

**Consolidation, not just a new module alongside old ones**: found and folded in a SECOND, independent implementation of this exact concern living in `lib/transformers.ts`'s API-ingestion boundary -- `normaliseName()`, applied inside `normalizeBall()`, with the identical last-token fragility plus its own comma-format handling. Rather than leave two competing name-formatters (which is precisely how the display convention drifted across the app in the first place), `parsePlayerName` absorbed the comma-format contract and `normalizeBall()` now calls `formatPlayerName` directly. Also fixed `transformSportRadarPlayer`'s `shortName` field, previously a bare `nameParts[length-1]` last-token guess (would produce "Kohli", not "V Kohli") -- now `formatPlayerName(fullName)`. `getPlayerShortName`/`nameToShortNameMap` were removed from `lib/mockData.ts` entirely (historical comment left in their place, same convention as the v1.0.118 `RecentFormWindow` removal).

**~15 real display call sites migrated** (not just the two originally reported): `MiniInsightsBar.tsx` (top stat pills), `PlayerProfileView.tsx` (profile header), `Scorecard.tsx` (batting/bowling card names via `PlayerNameLink`, plus the Man of Match/Man of Series banner text), `MatchupCard.tsx` and `MatchupShareCard.tsx` (matchup rows -- both header names and the previously-inconsistent surname-only insight-box text within the SAME card), `MomentStoryCard.tsx` (moments cards), `DigestTab.tsx` (8 call sites, the Digest narrative text this area started from -- `lastName()` removed entirely, direct `formatPlayerName` calls), `BallGIF.tsx` (the partnership name label only -- the separate avatar-monogram initials chip is a different UI affordance, deliberately untouched), `CommentaryFeed.tsx` and `DeliveryCard.tsx` (narrative ball-description sentences -- `ball.oneLiner` free-text prose deliberately left alone, since a name embedded in arbitrary pre-authored prose isn't a structured field this function can safely rewrite), `OverSummary.tsx`, `LineupsCard.tsx` (Playing XI list), and `MatchView.tsx` (the native-share caption text -- a real user-visible name display, external to the app).

**Deliberately out of scope, documented rather than silently skipped**: `DigestTab.tsx`'s Man-of-Match team-color heuristic (`b.playerName.toLowerCase().split(" ").pop()`) and `Scorecard.tsx`'s `row.playerName === motm` equality checks are name-*matching* logic, not name-*display* logic -- a related but different concern (partially already addressed for achievements specifically by `lib/playerForm.ts`'s `namesMatch()`), left alone so as not to conflate two different bugs under one fix.

**Real messy-name tests, not a description of expected behavior (`npx tsx`, 49/49 pass)**: multi-word surnames/particles (9 cases, including "Faf du Plessis" -- a real string already present in this app's own mock data, and both all-caps and all-lowercase variants of 3-part particle runs); suffixes (7 cases, confirming a suffix is never mistaken for a second surname word); hyphenated surnames (4 cases, including all-caps/all-lowercase variants of "J Fraser-McGurk" -- a real squad name); single-name players (5 cases, confirming no invented initial); inconsistent capitalization (6 cases, including confirming a genuinely correct mixed-case surname like "McGurk" is never re-title-cased into "Mcgurk"); stray whitespace (3 cases, including tabs/newlines); null/undefined/empty safety (5 cases); registry-first resolution (4 cases, including "Suryakumar Yadav" correctly resolving to the registry's nickname-style "SKY" rather than the generic algorithm's "S Yadav"); real messy strings pulled directly from this app's own existing mock data (3 cases -- "A. Russell"'s stray trailing period after the initial, "G. Coetzee"); and the newly-consolidated comma format (3 cases, including a particle surname in comma order).

**Verified scope**: grep-confirmed the only remaining `.split(" ")`-style name-splitting logic anywhere in the codebase is the one deliberately-out-of-scope matching heuristic named above -- every display site now imports `formatPlayerName` from `lib/playerName.ts`. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. The pre-existing committed `scripts/edge-case-check.ts` (which had literally encoded the OLD deferred behavior as its expected result -- "unknown compound surname returns full name unchanged") was updated to assert the new, correctly-derived output instead of the old non-answer. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Win probability: emphasized in the matchup row, removed from the duplicate stat-chip pill — v1.0.121 (2026-07-27)

**Why this shipped**: win probability was rendering in two places on the Live tab at once — a small "TEAM XX%" pill living inline among the batter/bowler stat chips (`MiniInsightsBar.tsx`), and the matchup row directly beneath it ("Batter vs Bowler · tap for H2H", `MatchupCard.tsx`'s collapsed teaser). Two simultaneous renderings of the same figure is a duplication bug, not a feature — the fix consolidates it into one location with real visual weight, rather than patching either rendering in isolation.

**Removed entirely, not just hidden**: the win-prob chip (`deriveMiniInsights`'s chip 4) is gone from `MiniInsightsBar.tsx` along with the `winProbPoints`/`onExpandWinProb` props that only existed to feed it — the call site in `MatchView.tsx` no longer passes them. The chip's `reverse: true` rendering branch (label-before-value order) was the ONLY consumer of that flag anywhere in the file, so it was removed too rather than left as dead code with no caller — `MiniChip.reverse` and its conditional JSX in `Chip()` are gone, with a comment explaining why.

**Matchup row restructured, not just the pill relocated verbatim**: `MatchupCard.tsx`'s collapsed-teaser row dropped its "tap for H2H" text label — the chevron icon alone is now the tap-to-expand-H2H affordance, and the underlying tap targets (the whole batter/bowler name region, plus the chevron itself) are unchanged. The freed space now shows a small muted "WIN PROB" label above a larger bold value ("IND 87%") on the row's right side. This value's tap target opens the same full-screen `WinProbChart` modal the old pill opened (`onExpandWinProb`) — a genuinely separate tap target from the batter/bowler-vs-H2H-expand button beside it, not a shared click handler.

**Deliberately fixed white, never team-colored — a rejected alternative, not an oversight**: the win-prob value renders in plain `text-white`, matching the app's other plain numeric readouts, specifically NOT the leading team's real color. Three concrete reasons this was rejected: a team's real color can misleadingly read as "losing" when it happens to be red-toned (unrelated to which team is actually ahead); the color would flicker distractingly as the leader swings back and forth during a close finish; and several simultaneous live matches could independently land on the same color (via `teamAccentColor`'s existing fallback/collision-resolution logic) and lose all meaning as a result. Plain, fixed, high-contrast white gets visual weight from size and boldness instead, which doesn't have any of those three failure modes.

**New centralized accessor, not inline derivation duplicated at the new call site**: `lib/winProb.ts` gained `getLeadingTeamWinProb(match, points)`, extracted from the exact logic the old chip used inline (last point's `winProbTeamA`, rounded, attributed to whichever team is `>=50`). `MatchupCard.tsx` calls this directly rather than re-deriving "who's leading and by how much" a second time — the same interface-first principle already applied to every other adapter in this project. Returns `null` (not a fake 50/50) when `points` is empty, so callers render "nothing" rather than a misleading placeholder percentage.

**No-matchup degradation — already structurally correct, verified rather than assumed**: `MatchView.tsx` only renders `MatchupCard` at all when `matchupInfo` is non-null (there's a real current ball + innings) — pre-match and truly-no-ball-by-ball-data states never reach this component in the first place, and were already handled by the separate bespoke "Win Probability" block in that branch (unchanged, out of scope here). Within `MatchupCard` itself, `getLeadingTeamWinProb` returning `null` (defensively guarding against a malformed/empty `winProbPoints` array even in states where it's not expected to happen) hides the WIN PROB block entirely rather than rendering broken or blank text — the batter/bowler side of the row still renders normally on its own.

**Layout guarantee against the longest realistic name pair, verified against real data**: `getLeadingTeamWinProb` doesn't affect layout risk — the batter/bowler button uses `flex-1 min-w-0` with `truncate` on both name spans, and the win-prob block is `shrink-0` with a fixed intrinsic width. Structurally, the name side always yields (ellipsizes) before it can crowd or overlap the win-prob side. Checked against the actual longest-combined-display-name batter/bowler pair anywhere in the mock dataset — "I Kishan" vs "V Chakravarthy" (22 combined characters, occurring in the live `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` MI-vs-KKR match) — confirmed live, not just by code inspection.

**Real edge-case tests for `getLeadingTeamWinProb`, not a description of expected behavior (`npx tsx`, 8/8 pass)**: empty points array returns `null` (not a fake percentage); team A clearly leading; team B clearly leading (percentage correctly flipped to `100 - pctA`); an exact 50/50 tie (boundary case, attributed to team A per the `>=50` rule); a multi-point array where only the LAST point is read, confirming earlier swings don't leak into the displayed figure; a rounding case (`0.865` -> `87%`); and near-certain wins for each team (`0.99`/`0.01`).

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.121 commit shows exactly 4 files changed — `lib/winProb.ts`, `components/MiniInsightsBar.tsx`, `components/MatchupCard.tsx`, `components/MatchView.tsx`. `Scorecard.tsx`, `MatchupShareCard.tsx`, `lib/teamAccentColor.ts`, and `lib/playerName.ts` are untouched, so team colors, the matchup-share-card layout, and player-name display are structurally guaranteed unaffected. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean, `scripts/version-check.ts` passes. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Sticky header team/score attribution bug: follow-on breaks the positional pairing — v1.0.122 (2026-07-28)

**Bug report, confirmed live**: `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` (India vs England, 2nd Test, Day 3). India enforced the follow-on after leading by 251 runs. Real per-innings state (confirmed correct on the Score tab): India inn1 450/8; England inn1 199 all out; England inn2 (follow-on, in progress) 92/4. The match-page header displayed "ENG 450/8 vs 92/4 IND" — India's 450/8 attributed to England, England's live 92/4 attributed to India. The Score tab got this right, confirming the underlying data was fine and the bug was isolated to the header's own display composition.

**Root cause**: `components/ScoreBar.tsx` already computed `lastInnA`/`lastInnB` correctly — `innings.filter(i => i.battingTeam === teamA.code)` etc., the same battingTeam-lookup pattern `Scorecard.tsx` and `MatchCard.tsx`'s `LiveMatchCard` already use — but never actually rendered them. The header's two score slots instead pulled from `i1` (`innings[0]`) and `i2` (`innings[innings.length - 1]`), which are purely positional. Position and team happen to coincide in a single-innings white-ball match or a normal alternating Test, which is exactly why this went unnoticed until a follow-on produced the sequence `[IND inn1, ENG inn1, ENG inn2]` — India's second innings never happens, so `i2` ("last in the array") is England's innings, but it was being rendered in the slot visually anchored next to India's team tag.

**Fix, one file**: the header's two score slots now render `lastInnA`/`lastInnB` instead of `i1`/`i2`. `i1`/`i2` are kept exactly as before for their other, legitimate purpose in the same file — the white-ball chase-context line (target/need/RRR) and the projected-score line, both chronological concepts ("first innings," "currently batting") that were already resolving team identity correctly via explicit `battingTeam ===` checks wherever a team name is actually printed. No new function, no new interface — the fix reuses code that was already sitting in the file, unused.

**No hardcoded team-order or bat-first assumption**: `lastInnA`/`lastInnB` are derived purely from each innings' own `battingTeam` field. This is the same field real match data carries with the identical shape, so the fix requires zero changes once real data replaces mock data — correctness comes from reading the actual linkage, never from an assumption about which team bats first or which team follows on.

**Real tests against constructed fixtures for all 6 required states (`npx tsx`, 18/18 pass)**:
- White-ball, team A bats first — teamA slot shows their score, teamB slot shows theirs.
- White-ball, team B bats first instead — proves the fix isn't assuming a fixed bat-first team; teamB's slot still shows teamB's own score even though they batted first chronologically.
- Normal 4-innings Test, no follow-on — each team's slot shows their own latest innings (2nd innings once it starts), matching the pre-existing 2-number header convention.
- Follow-on enforced by team A on team B (today's live bug, reproduced) — teamA slot shows their only innings; teamB slot shows their LATEST (2nd, follow-on) innings, not their first.
- The SAME follow-on scenario with team B enforcing it on team A instead — teamA slot correctly shows team A's 2nd/follow-on innings, teamB slot shows their only innings. Proves there's no hardcoded assumption about which named team follows on.
- Early Test, only one team has batted — the other team's slot resolves to `undefined`, which the existing `{lastInnA && (...)}`/`{lastInnB && (...)}` conditional rendering already treats as "show nothing," not blank/0/NaN/garbage text. Tested with each team in the batting-first position.
- Declaration at a non-standard wicket count (3) vs. all-out at 10 — the header shows exactly what the innings record says, since the fix only changes WHICH innings maps to which slot, not how a score is formatted.
- Drawn/abandoned Test where a team never gets a 2nd innings at all — each team's slot shows their one and only (possibly incomplete) innings, no phantom 2nd-innings assumption.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.122 commit shows exactly one file changed — `components/ScoreBar.tsx`. `Scorecard.tsx` (Score tab, already correct) and `MatchCard.tsx` (homepage live card, already fixed the same way in an earlier version, confirmed by inspection rather than touched again) are untouched — the regression-safety requirement that the Score tab's already-correct rendering stay untouched is satisfied by construction, since this fix only ever reads from `Scorecard.tsx`'s equivalent, not the reverse. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean, `scripts/version-check.ts` passes. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Win-probability fallback view: neutral-color decision never propagated past the main live view — v1.0.123 (2026-07-28)

**Bug report, confirmed live**: `ipl2026-l2-rcbvcsk` (RCB vs CSK) and `ipl2026-l3-gtvrr` (GT vs RR) — both matches with no ball-by-ball data, showing the "Ball-by-ball data unavailable · Info tab for squads & details" fallback card. On this card, win probability rendered as a horizontal bar split by team color, with each team's own percentage text colored to match ("56% CSK" in CSK's yellow, "28% RR" in RR's pink). This is exactly the treatment v1.0.121 deliberately rejected for the main live view's matchup-row readout — plain fixed white, no team coloring — for four concrete reasons documented there: a team's own color can misleadingly read as "losing" regardless of who's actually ahead; team-colored text can flicker distractingly as a close finish swings; several simultaneous matches can land on the same fallback color and lose all meaning; and a colorless-fallback team's readout looks visibly different/inauthentic next to every other team's. v1.0.121 applied that decision to `MatchupCard.tsx` only — the separate fallback block in `MatchView.tsx`, written independently and never touched by that change, kept its own bespoke team-colored implementation.

**Full platform audit performed** (the explicit ask was "one single presentation, not two implementations that can drift again," which required checking every render site, not just the reported one). Found four distinct categories:
1. **Single leading-team readout, team-colored (the reported anti-pattern)** — `MatchView.tsx`'s fallback card (reported), and a second, previously-unreported instance: `WinProbChart.tsx`'s full-screen modal header ("Big probability display" — the center number was colored by the leading team, `leadingTeam.primaryColor`). Both fixed in this release.
2. **Single leading-team readout, already neutral** — `MatchupCard.tsx`'s matchup-row badge (the v1.0.121 reference implementation). Its inline JSX was extracted into the new shared component below, rather than left as a second copy of styling that happens to look the same today.
3. **Dual-team comparison/delta displays, team-colored — a different display concept, not the reported anti-pattern** — `MatchCard.tsx`'s homepage-card `WinProbBar`/`LiveWinProbSpark` (both teams' percentages shown side-by-side), `MomentStoryCard.tsx`'s shareable-moment win-prob numbers/sparkline/delta badge, and `DigestTab.tsx`'s post-match turning-point "X% shift toward TEAM" narrative line. These intentionally show or narrate BOTH teams at once for comparison, which is a genuinely different design concept from "who's ahead right now" — removing team color here would remove real comparative signal, not just swap a color. Explicitly confirmed with the user as out of scope for this fix; left unchanged.
4. **Dead code** — `MiniWinProb.tsx`, `AIMetrics.tsx`. Grep-confirmed neither is imported/rendered anywhere; no action needed.

**New shared component — the actual fix for "one presentation, not two implementations"**: `components/WinProbBadge.tsx`, a single component with `compact` (matchup-row/fallback-card sizing) and `large` (full-screen-header sizing) variants, both rendering the identical fixed-white value. This is a structural distinction only — neither variant takes or uses a color input from its caller, so there is no code path left anywhere that can independently re-introduce team coloring into this specific readout. `MatchupCard.tsx`, `MatchView.tsx`'s fallback card, and `WinProbChart.tsx`'s modal header all now render this one component rather than their own copies of the same-looking markup.

**New shared accessor for the override-based path**: `lib/winProb.ts` gained `getLeadingTeamFromOverride(match, override)`, mirroring the existing ball-by-ball-based `getLeadingTeamWinProb(match, points)` — same "leader label + percentage" return shape, same `null`-when-nothing-to-show contract, but reading `Match.liveWinProbOverride` (the single static value used by mock matches with no ball history at all) instead of a `WinProbPoint[]` trend. `MatchView.tsx`'s fallback card now calls this instead of deriving the leader inline — the derivation and the presentation are both centralized, not just the presentation.

**Deliberately unchanged**: `WinProbChart.tsx`'s two side-by-side team-colored progress bars and the neutral percentage row beneath them (`pctA`/`pctB`, `teamA.primaryColor`/`teamB.primaryColor`) — these are the SAME dual-team comparison concept confirmed out of scope in category 3 above, just living inside the full-screen modal rather than a card; only the modal's single center "leader" number was in scope, and `pctA`/`pctB` themselves are unchanged local values still feeding those bars. `MatchCard.tsx`, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, `DigestTab.tsx` — zero code changes, per the explicit scope decision.

**Real edge-case tests for `getLeadingTeamFromOverride`, not a description of expected behavior (`npx tsx`, 12/12 pass)**: undefined override returns `null`; team A leading (0-1 scale input); team B leading (0-1 scale input); override attributed to team A while team A is actually behind (pct<50) — leader correctly flips to team B; the mirrored case with team B's override; an exact 50/50 tie (attributed to team A per the same `>=50` convention `getLeadingTeamWinProb` already uses); near-certain wins for each team (0.97/0.98); a 0-100-scale input tolerated defensively (the field is documented as always 0-1, this only guards against a malformed value); a floating-point rounding case; plus 2 regression checks confirming the pre-existing `getLeadingTeamWinProb` (empty points, team B leading) is unaffected by this change.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.123 commit shows exactly 4 files modified (`components/MatchView.tsx`, `components/MatchupCard.tsx`, `components/WinProbChart.tsx`, `lib/winProb.ts`) plus 1 new file (`components/WinProbBadge.tsx`) — matching the confirmed scope exactly, with `MatchCard.tsx`, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, and `DigestTab.tsx` untouched. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Digest showed a premature "FULL TIME / won by X" verdict for a genuinely in-progress chase — v1.0.124 (2026-07-28)

**Bug report, confirmed live**: `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` (MI vs KKR). While KKR was genuinely still batting mid-chase (LIVE tab: "need 49 off 41 balls"), the Digest tab's match-summary card simultaneously read "T20 · FULL TIME — Kolkata Knight Riders won by 4 wickets" AND "KKR fell 49 short" in the same card — two directly contradictory pieces of text, both wrong, each reading a different data source for the same current moment.

**Root cause**: `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` (the live-playback snapshot fed to every tab, including Digest) correctly slices `innings` down to the balls delivered so far in the simulated live ticking (`activeBallIdx`, driven by a `setInterval` that loops back into the final ~10 balls forever once it reaches the end — the mechanism that keeps `FEATURED_MATCH` perpetually "live" for the homepage carousel, per its own deliberate, documented design). But the object was built via `return { ...match, innings }` — spreading `match` first means `result` carried through completely unchanged from the untouched original, even while `innings` were genuinely mid-playback. `FEATURED_MATCH` has a permanently baked-in final `result` (`{ winner: "KKR", margin: "by 4 wickets", ... }`) specifically so it never goes "resultless" while parked in the live carousel — so `truncatedMatch.result` was ALWAYS that final object, at every point in the simulated chase, not just once the chase genuinely concluded.

`components/DigestTab.tsx`'s `buildMatchSummaryCard(match, isLive)` checked `if (match.result) return buildFullMatchSummaryCard(...)` BEFORE checking `isLive` at all — so the leaked, always-present `result` short-circuited straight into the full "FULL TIME" card regardless of how far the chase had actually progressed. Meanwhile, that same card's narrative text (`buildMatchNarrative`) read the CORRECTLY-truncated `innings` (the current, incomplete score) and — by its own, separately-computed logic — correctly determined the chase hadn't reached the target yet, producing "KKR fell 49 short." Two genuine, independently-correct-for-their-own-input pieces of logic, fed two different truths about the same match at the same instant, is exactly why the header and the narrative visibly disagreed.

**Fix, two parts**:

1. **Stop the leak at the source** — `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` now only passes `result` through when playback has genuinely caught up to the real end of the recorded ball data (`allBalls.length === 0` — no ball-by-ball data at all, nothing to truncate, trust `result` as-is — or `activeBallIdx >= allBalls.length - 1` — every recorded ball has been delivered). Otherwise `result` is `undefined` in the truncated snapshot, regardless of what the underlying match object says. This makes `truncatedMatch` an honest "what's actually true right now" object rather than "the innings are truncated but the verdict isn't."
2. **New shared accessor, `lib/matchStatus.ts`** — the ONE function every completion-dependent narrative platform-wide should call instead of independently inferring "is this over" from score/wickets/overs state: `isMatchConcluded(match)` (`result != null` — trustworthy everywhere once fix #1 holds, and real-data-compatible by construction, since a real feed likewise only populates `result` once a match has actually finished) plus `observableStateSupportsConclusion(match)` — a defense-in-depth cross-check, deliberately scoped exactly like the existing `deriveMinimalMatchResult` (same file, unchanged): for a normal two-innings limited-overs match, independently confirms the CURRENTLY OBSERVABLE innings state (not the `result` field itself) shows a genuine conclusion — target reached, all out, or overs exhausted — before a "FULL TIME" verdict is trusted. Returns "no opinion" (true) for Tests and anything without exactly two recorded innings, for the same reason `deriveMinimalMatchResult` already declines to guess there: draws, ties, follow-on wins, and declarations aren't verifiable from the scoreline alone. `isMatchConclusivelyOver(match)` combines both checks — this is what `buildMatchSummaryCard` now gates on, first, before ever trusting `match.result`.

**New in-progress state, not just "no card"**: `buildMatchSummaryCard` previously returned `null` while `isLive` and unconcluded (silently no summary card at all). It now returns a new `LiveSummaryCard` instead — current scores for each innings, plus (for a limited-overs chase genuinely in progress) the exact same need/balls-left/required-run-rate figures `ScoreBar.tsx`'s own live header already computes, so this card can never numerically disagree with what the LIVE tab shows for the same match at the same moment. Styled close to the existing `PendingResultCardView` (same neutral, non-verdict framing) but labeled "In Progress," never a resultLine. For a Test, `chase` is simply `null` — there's no single "need X off Y" figure for that format, current scores alone are shown, exactly as intended.

**Full platform audit performed** (the ask was every location rendering a completion-dependent narrative, not just the reported one):
- **Fixed**: `components/MatchView.tsx` (`truncatedMatch`'s leak), `components/DigestTab.tsx` (`buildMatchSummaryCard` reordered + gated via the new accessor, new `LiveSummaryCard` state), new `lib/matchStatus.ts`.
- **Audited, confirmed already correct, no change**: `lib/teamSchedule.ts` (`summarizeSeriesGroup`/`formatLastResult`/`hasUsableResult`) and `components/ScheduleRow.tsx` — both gate on `match.status === "post-match"` (via `bucket === "past"`) BEFORE ever consulting `result`, so a match kept at `status: "live"` forever (like `FEATURED_MATCH`) can never be selected as a series' "last completed match" or shown a "won by" line on its own schedule row, regardless of what `result` contains. `components/MatchCard.tsx`'s `PastMatchCard`/`SpotlightMatchCard(isPast)` — only ever fed matches from `ALL_PAST_MATCHES`, never from the live-bucketed pool, by construction of the homepage's own match-bucketing in `app/page.tsx`. `components/MomentStoryCard.tsx` — no completion-dependent narrative at all. `DigestTab.tsx`'s own `buildPostMatchDigest` path (`buildSimpleRecapCard`/`findTurningPoint`/`buildPerformanceCard`) — only ever invoked once `match.status === "post-match"` at the component level, always genuinely concluded by the time it runs.
- **Found, confirmed dead code, no render-time impact**: `components/MatchCard.tsx`'s `liveStatusOf()` contains the identical anti-pattern (infers `"TEAM won"` from raw, untruncated `innings.runs` once `need <= 0`) — but has zero call sites anywhere in the codebase. Flagged for whoever might wire it up later; not fixed, since nothing currently renders it.
- **Found, related but explicitly out of scope**: `lib/playerForm.ts`'s `settledMatches()` treats any `ALL_LIVE_MATCHES` match with a `hasUsableResult()`-passing `result` as "settled" for player recent-form stats — reading the RAW, untruncated mock array directly, never through `MatchView.tsx`'s truncation, so fix #1 above doesn't reach it. Its own comment explicitly cites `FEATURED_MATCH` as the reason `status` alone isn't used ("kept at `status: 'live'` on purpose... even though it's a fully finished match with a complete result and full ball-by-ball data") — that premise is now stale, since `FEATURED_MATCH`'s ball-by-ball data is actively simulated as an in-progress chase (this investigation's core finding). Left unchanged in this pass: it aggregates numeric stats, not a "FULL TIME"/"won by X" display narrative, so it's adjacent to but not literally inside this fix's reported scope — noted here as a candidate follow-up.
