**Real tests against constructed fixtures for all 6 required states, plus a recomputation/loop check (`npx tsx`, 26/26 pass)**: a genuinely completed chase with balls to spare (must still show FULL TIME — no regression); the exact reported bug case (leaked `result`, chase genuinely mid-innings — must show the new in-progress card, not FULL TIME, with chase math matching `ScoreBar`'s own formula exactly); a failed chase, both while still live-with-an-unlanded-result (in-progress card) and once the real result lands (correct "won by runs" for the defending team) — covering both all-out and overs-exhausted endings; the exact ball-by-ball transition boundary (one ball before target reached vs. the instant it's reached — confirms the flip to FULL TIME is immediate, not early or delayed); a Test match genuinely in progress (no result at all — in-progress card, no chase figure, no false "match over" narrative); a tie and an abandoned/no-result match (both correctly reach FULL TIME once `result` is genuinely present); and a simulated "loop" — target reached (FULL TIME) → looped back mid-chase (reverts to in-progress) → target reached again (FULL TIME again) — confirming this recomputes fresh on every tick rather than freezing on whichever state it first resolved to.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.124 commit shows exactly 2 files modified (`components/MatchView.tsx`, `components/DigestTab.tsx`) plus 1 new file (`lib/matchStatus.ts`). `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

**Follow-up caught during live verification**: the new `LiveSummaryCard` was being built correctly but never actually appeared -- `visibleCards`' innings-chip filter (and its Test day-filter equivalent) whitelisted `match-summary`/`pending-result`/`turning-point`/`performance`/`simple-recap` as "always pinned regardless of the active innings/day," but the newly-added `live-summary` kind was left out of that list, so it was silently filtered away by the innings-chip selection (active by default on any non-Test match with over-group data). A `buildCards()`-only test harness can't catch this class of gap -- the card exists in the returned array, it's just excluded one render step later. Fixed by adding `live-summary` to both whitelists.

## "Your Players" homepage strip: chip strip + favourites + honest live-status sort — v1.0.125 (2026-07-28)

**Feature request**: a new homepage section, directly below Spotlight and above the Past/Coming Up grids — a horizontally scrollable strip of avatar chips, one per player selected in the Filter sheet's "Players" tab. Zero players selected → the entire section (including any heading) renders nothing, no empty-state placeholder. Tapping a chip navigates to that player's profile page. A new favourite star toggle on the player profile header, which must auto-add the player to the followed Players selection if not already there (never the other way around — un-favouriting doesn't un-follow). Favourited players get a visible marker on their chip. Sort order, exact priority: (1) favourited AND currently live, (2) favourited not live, (3) not favourited live, (4) not favourited not live — alphabetical by SURNAME within each tier, not by the displayed leading initial. "Currently live" status must recompute reactively as match data changes, not be computed once and left stale, and must work unchanged once real match/player data replaces mock data.

**Design decision — rejected the obvious "currently live" signal after direct inspection.** `Innings.battingCard`/`bowlingCard`'s `out`/`onStrike` fields looked like the natural source for "who's at the crease right now," but this is the exact same bug class just fixed in v1.0.124: direct inspection of `FEATURED_MATCH` confirmed those fields represent the END-OF-INNINGS aggregate, and `MatchView.tsx`'s live-ticking `truncatedMatch` spreads them through UNCHANGED even while `innings.balls` is genuinely truncated to the current simulated playback position. Trusting `onStrike` here would mean reading a field that can represent a FUTURE/final snapshot as if it were "right now" — precisely the leak v1.0.124 just closed for the Digest tab's verdict text.

**Chosen signal**: the last ball in `match.innings.flatMap(i => i.balls)` (new `lib/playerActivity.ts`, `getLiveActivePlayerIds`), gated on `match.status === "live"`. This mirrors the exact flattening `MatchView.tsx` already uses for its own `allBalls` — innings appear chronologically in `Match.innings`, and each innings' own `balls` are already chronological, so the LAST entry in that flattened list is unambiguously "the most recent recorded event," honestly. A real live feed only ever returns balls bowled so far — there's no analogous "final snapshot leaking through" risk with ball-by-ball data the way there is with an aggregate card. A live match with an empty `balls` array (several exist in this mock dataset) contributes nothing — no guessing. Documented, accepted limitation: `Ball` has no `nonStrikerId` field, so this only ever identifies the striker (and bowler) of the last ball, never the non-striker waiting at the other end.

**ID reconciliation reused, not reinvented.** Investigation confirmed `Ball.batterId`/`bowlerId` values in this mock dataset are inconsistent with PLAYERS-registry slugs — `"dwarner"`, `"B Duckett"`, `"vkohli"` all appear as raw ball IDs against registry keys like `"v-kohli"`. This is the exact same mismatch `lib/playerForm.ts` already solved for `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` `playerId`s via `lib/mockData.ts`'s `resolvePlayerSlug()` (itself built on `slugifyPlayer()` + a hand-maintained `PLAYER_ALIASES` map). `lib/playerActivity.ts` calls that same function rather than writing a second reconciliation layer — both derivations degrade identically for the same unresolvable IDs, and any alias added for one benefits the other automatically.

**New data model — two independent localStorage stores, deliberately not merged**: `lib/followPrefs.ts`'s existing `players` array (the Filter sheet's "Players" tab selection) stays the single source of truth for "should this player's matches count for For You / show up in this strip at all." A new `lib/playerFavourites.ts` (same localStorage + `CHANGE_EVENT` shape as `followPrefs.ts` — `STORAGE_KEY = "bawler:favouritePlayers"`, `CHANGE_EVENT = "bawler:favourite-players-changed"`, same sanitize-on-read self-healing against a live `PLAYERS` registry check) tracks favourited state, which additionally earns a star badge/ring and outranks a merely-followed player in the sort. `toggleFavouritePlayer(playerId)` is the one sanctioned way to flip it: favouriting ALWAYS adds the player to `FollowPrefs.players` if absent (via `setFollowPrefs`, which already dispatches its own `CHANGE_EVENT`) — the exact "never favourite from your profile and then not see yourself in the strip" contract from the spec. Un-favouriting only clears the favourite flag; the follow selection is left untouched, since the two aren't specified as symmetric and silently un-following would be a surprising side effect.

**Pure sort function, no localStorage/React dependency** — `lib/yourPlayers.ts`'s `getYourPlayers(followedIds, favouriteIds, liveMatches)`, mirroring the `lib/playerForm.ts`/`lib/teamSchedule.ts` accessor/hook split: a plain function owns the tier-and-surname derivation (directly unit-testable with constructed inputs), while `components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`'s exported `useYourPlayers(liveMatches)` hook owns the reactive plumbing (localStorage subscriptions + memoization). Surname extracted via the existing `lib/playerName.ts`'s `parsePlayerName(player.name).surname` — never the raw "V Kohli" display string, which would incorrectly sort under V instead of K.

**Reactive recomputation, hook-level, same replace-not-mutate contract as v1.0.109's `useMatchAccentColors`**: `useYourPlayers` subscribes to BOTH `onFollowPrefsChanged` and `onFavouritesChanged` (the Filter sheet and the player profile page are both siblings of the homepage, not parents — the same reason `app/page.tsx` already subscribes to `followPrefs` changes for the "For You" row) and memoizes on primitive, field-derived signature strings — `followPrefs.players` sorted+joined, `favourites` sorted+joined, and a new `liveActivitySignature(matches)` (`lib/playerActivity.ts`: a `matchId:status:lastBallId` join across all matches) — rather than the array/object references themselves, which get a brand-new identity on every change notification regardless of whether the underlying IDs/live-status actually differ.

**New homepage section** (`components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`), wired into `app/page.tsx` directly after the Spotlight section's closing tag (same `isBooting`-gated fragment, no separate skeleton needed — mirrors how the empty-state nudge and "For You" row already behave without their own loading state). Returns `null` outright when zero entries — no heading, no placeholder. Each chip: photo (if `PlayerProfile.imageUrl` is ever populated — it's optional and unused everywhere else in this app today) or an initials fallback derived from `parsePlayerName`, name via the existing `formatPlayerName`, an amber ring + star badge when favourited, wrapped in a `next/link` to `/player/[id]`.

**Favourite toggle** added to `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s sticky header, between the player name block and the role badge — a star-shaped button (amber-filled when favourited, outline otherwise), calling `toggleFavouritePlayer(player.id)` and syncing local UI state from `getFavouritePlayers()`/`onFavouritesChanged` on mount, the same subscription shape as everywhere else in this file.

**Real tests, not a description (`npx tsx`, `react-test-renderer` installed via `npm install --no-save`, removed after — same precedent as v1.0.109/v1.0.111/v1.0.117), 32/32 pass**: zero players selected (empty array, confirming the section would render nothing); exactly one player selected; five players selected with an intentional duplicate ID (deduped correctly); the deliberately disambiguating surname-sort pair "A Russell" (initial A, surname Russell) vs "Z Crawley" (initial Z, surname Crawley) — sorting by displayed initial would put Russell first, correct surname sort puts Crawley first, confirmed; a live/non-live mix (the live player's `batterId` resolved via the same ball-flattening + `resolvePlayerSlug` reconciliation, ranking ahead of a non-live, non-favourited player); a favourited/non-favourited mix (favourited ranks first despite a later surname); all 4 tiers present simultaneously with the exact required order (favourited+live, favourited+not-live, not-favourited+live, not-favourited+not-live); a live match with an empty `balls` array correctly detecting zero live players (no guessing); a `post-match`-status match with real ball data correctly contributing nothing (status gate, not just ball presence); and — the scenario the spec called out by name — a `react-test-renderer` mount of `useYourPlayers` re-rendered with a DIFFERENT `liveMatches` prop on the SAME component instance (no remount), confirming the sort re-ranks a player to first place and their `isLive` flag flips to `true` the instant their match data says they're batting, then reverting the prop and confirming it un-ranks cleanly (no stale one-way ratchet in either direction); plus a live reactivity check of the favourite-auto-follow linkage itself — toggling a favourite mid-test (no explicit refetch) caused the previously-absent player to appear in the strip on the very next read via the `CHANGE_EVENT` subscription, and un-favouriting afterward correctly left them in the strip (still followed) while dropping them out of the top tier.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.125 commit shows 3 new files (`lib/playerFavourites.ts`, `lib/playerActivity.ts`, `lib/yourPlayers.ts`, `components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx` — 4 new files) and 2 modified (`app/page.tsx`: import + one new `<YourPlayersStrip>` call after the Spotlight section; `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`: import + favourite-state hook + header button). `package.json`/`package-lock.json` unaffected by the temporary `react-test-renderer` dev install (`--no-save`, uninstalled after the test run, confirmed via `git status`). `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## "Your Players" strip: 3 bugs — follow-on live-detection miss, name-format regression, and a sort-order symptom traced back to bug #1 — v1.0.126 (2026-07-28)

**Bug reports, all confirmed live before any fix**: (1) in the genuinely live, follow-on-in-progress `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, B Stokes — clearly part of that match's currently-open innings per its own Score tab — never showed as "currently live" in the strip, landing behind two players in no live match at all, while J Bumrah (a different, live T20I) correctly did. (2) Babar Azam's chip showed his full name ("Babar Azam") instead of the platform's Initial+Surname convention ("B Azam"), used correctly by the other four chips in the same strip. (3) non-favourited, non-live players appeared ordered by first name ("A Russell, Babar Azam, B Stokes") rather than surname (Azam, Russell, Stokes).

**Bug 1 root cause — scope, not selection.** v1.0.125's `lib/playerActivity.ts` flattened every innings' balls together (`match.innings.flatMap(i => i.balls)`) and read only the SINGLE LAST ball's `batterId`/`bowlerId` as "who's live." That flattening does land on the correct, currently-active innings even across a follow-on — a follow-on doesn't change which innings is chronologically last. The bug was treating "the last ball's 2 participants" as a proxy for "everyone involved in the currently-open innings," which is exactly wrong once that innings is 4+ overs and multiple batters deep, as Stokes' follow-on innings was — his own contribution simply wasn't the literal final recorded ball at the moment checked, so he was invisible to a check that only ever looks at one ball.

**Bug 1 fix — one shared lookup between `ScoreBar.tsx` and `lib/playerActivity.ts`, not two independently-derived ones.** New `getCurrentInnings(match)` in `lib/matchStatus.ts` is now the SAME "last innings in the array" expression `ScoreBar.tsx` already used inline (`lastInn`) for its own v1.0.122 follow-on fix — extracted once both call sites were confirmed to need the identical concept, so the two features can no longer silently re-diverge the way they did between v1.0.122 and v1.0.125. `ScoreBar.tsx` now imports and calls it instead of keeping its own copy. `lib/playerActivity.ts`'s `getLiveActivePlayerIds` now reads that SAME current innings' full `battingCard` + `bowlingCard` — every player with an entry, not just the last ball's 2 — while keeping its own `current.balls.length > 0` guard on top (this mock dataset has at least one innings with a fully pre-authored placeholder card despite zero recorded balls, and trusting a card from an innings with no ball-level evidence it's started would repeat the exact "guess from data that hasn't actually happened" mistake ruled out in v1.0.124/v1.0.125). `liveActivitySignature` updated to the same full-card shape so `useYourPlayers`'s memoization still invalidates correctly on any change to who's involved.

**Deliberately unchanged**: `FEATURED_MATCH`'s permanently-mislabeled `status: "live"` (by design, so it stays in the homepage carousel forever) is trusted exactly as before — this fix widens WHO counts as live once a match is already gated as live, it doesn't touch whether the match itself is genuinely still in progress. In real data this can't produce a false positive at all, since a genuinely finished match's `status` would correctly stop being `"live"`.

**Bug 2 investigated, NOT reproduced as an independent comparator defect.** `lib/yourPlayers.ts`'s surname-key sort (`parsePlayerName(player.name).surname.toLowerCase()`, tier-then-surname) was re-tested in isolation with a deliberately first-name/surname-disagreeing set and sorted correctly both before and after the Bug 1 fix — no code change made. Conclusion: the originally-observed ordering was almost certainly a downstream SYMPTOM of Bug 1 — a player unexpectedly (and, under the old narrow last-ball check, inconsistently) qualifying as "live" jumps tiers, which looks like "wrong alphabetization" to an observer who can't see the internal live flag flip. Flagged for the user to re-verify live now that Bug 1 no longer produces that inconsistency.

**Bug 3 root cause — a data-authoring error, not a logic bug.** `lib/playerName.ts`'s `formatPlayerName()` deliberately checks the PLAYERS registry's hand-authored `shortName` field FIRST, trusted over the algorithmic parser — correct by design (it's how the legitimate `"SKY"`/Suryakumar Yadav nickname exception, documented above, is preserved). Two registry entries had a WRONG hand-authored value: `b-azam`'s `shortName` was `"Babar Azam"` (the full name, not Initial+Surname) and `a-iqbal`'s was `"Arshad"` (a bare first name). Fixed directly in `lib/mockData.ts` to `"B Azam"` / `"A Iqbal"` — `name` fields left untouched. A full-registry audit (script-derived expected value from each of the 21 seeded players' real `name`, diffed against the authored `shortName`) confirmed these were the ONLY 2 genuine mismatches; `s-yadav`/`"SKY"` was re-confirmed as the sole, intentional, already-tested exception and left untouched.

**Real tests, not descriptions (`npx tsx`, 21/21 pass)**: a white-ball match (single innings per team) — live-detection still correct, no regression; a constructed normal 4-innings Test with no follow-on — correct; the real follow-on Test (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`) — Stokes now correctly detected live; a constructed white-ball fixture as an explicit negative control; the full real-data 4-tier sort (favourited+live, favourited+not-live, not-favourited+live-from-follow-on, not-favourited+live-from-white-ball, multiple not-favourited/not-live players in a first-name-disagreeing order) — correct tiers and correct surname order within the last tier; the full 21-player `shortName` audit — only `s-yadav` flagged, confirmed intentional.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.126 commit shows 3 files modified (`lib/matchStatus.ts` — new `getCurrentInnings`; `components/ScoreBar.tsx` — reuses it; `lib/playerActivity.ts` — rewritten to read full current-innings cards; `lib/mockData.ts` — 2 `shortName` corrections) and `package.json` (version bump). `lib/yourPlayers.ts` unchanged — investigated, not modified. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

**Follow-up: "the live bump looks completely broken" report, investigated — not a regression, and no code change made.** A second live-verification pass reported zero live-bump effect at all, with a pure surname-alphabetical result across a 5-player set (`b-azam, j-bumrah, j-root, a-russell, b-stokes`) — read as evidence the live-check might have been "accidentally disconnected" while the Bug 2/3 fixes were being made. Direct instrumentation (`isPlayerCurrentlyLive()` called and logged for each of the 4 explicitly-named players) showed the check IS running and wired into the sort exactly as before: it returned `true` for all 4, including Babar Azam, who'd been assumed a safe "not live" control. Root cause of the confusing symptom: BOTH of the two players chosen as "definitely not live" negative controls (Babar Azam, Andre Russell) are, in today's mock dataset, each dismissed (`out: true`) within the still-open current innings of a DIFFERENT match genuinely tagged `status: "live"` on the same homepage (`psl-2026-lah-kar-live` for Azam, `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` for Russell) — structurally identical to B Stokes' own situation (also `out: true` within his still-open follow-on innings). With every tested player evaluating `true`, tier assignment collapsed to a single tier, leaving only the (correctly-working) surname sort visible — indistinguishable, from the outside, from "live-detection returns false for everyone." Re-ran the identical methodology with genuinely-confirmed non-live controls (`j-buttler`, `t-boult`, `r-ashwin`, `d-miller` — none have any entry in a currently-open innings) alongside `j-bumrah`/`j-root`/`b-stokes`: correct tier separation and correct surname order within each tier, confirmed live on the deployed site.

Flagged the underlying tension directly rather than guessing at a fix: making Stokes `true` while making Azam `false` is impossible under any general, real-data-compatible rule, since both are dismissed batters within a still-open innings of a match tagged live — the two cases are structurally identical, and the only way to separate them would be a hardcoded per-player/per-match exception, which is exactly what real-data compatibility rules out. Asked the user directly: keep the current broad rule (any entry in the currently-open innings counts, matching original Bug 1 intent — Stokes correctly counts despite his own dismissal, at the cost of also correctly flagging already-dismissed players in OTHER live matches) or tighten to "must currently be not-out" (which would flip Stokes to `false` too). **Decision: keep the current broad rule.** No code changed as a result of this investigation.

## Recent-form graph missing for India's Test batters despite real settled data existing — v1.0.127 (2026-07-28)

**Bug report, confirmed live**: on player profile pages, Kohli/Rohit Sharma/Gill's Test recent-form graphs were empty despite their real, already-happened first-innings performances (121, 83, 110 respectively) in the live "India tour of England 2026" 2nd Test being correctly visible and correctly attributed on that match's own Score tab. England's players in the SAME match (Root, Stokes) showed correct, populated Test graphs, and Kohli's own T20I graph (a separate live match) also worked — ruling out a mock-data gap and pointing at a code-level linkage issue, per the user's own diagnosis.

**Investigated as a player-to-innings linkage bug first, per the user's hypothesis — not what was found.** Direct inspection of `resolvePlayerSlug()`/`extractPlayerEntries()`'s matching logic showed no team-ID mismatch, no data-shape difference between India's and England's side of the same innings record, and no follow-on-structure bug in how players are matched to their innings entries. The actual root cause was one level up: `lib/playerForm.ts`'s `settledMatches()` (now removed) gated the ENTIRE match on `hasUsableResult(match)` — a match-level check — before any innings within it were even considered. India's only Test appearance in this mock dataset, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, is genuinely still in progress (Day 3, England on the follow-on, `Match.result` not yet populated) — so the old gate discarded the WHOLE match, including India's 1st innings, which had already been over for two whole innings by the time anyone checked. This wasn't specific to India: England's own already-closed 1st-innings entries (Root's 68, Crawley's 45, Duckett's 18, Stokes' first knock's 22) from this SAME match were equally discarded by the old code — Root and Stokes only *appeared* to work because they separately have entries from an unrelated, genuinely concluded past Test (the Ashes), which happened to mask the gap for them specifically.

**Fix: move the eligibility decision from match-level to innings-level.** New `eligibleEntriesFor()` in `lib/playerForm.ts`, keyed off the SAME `getCurrentInnings()` shared lookup (`lib/matchStatus.ts`) `ScoreBar.tsx` and `lib/playerActivity.ts` already reuse — a third independent re-derivation of "which innings is current" was exactly the mistake that caused the last two bug rounds, so this one calls the existing function instead. An innings that ISN'T the match's current one is closed by construction (the match moved past it) — every entry in it is trustworthy regardless of the match's own result status. For the CURRENT innings of a still-unresolved live match: a BATTING entry counts once the player is personally dismissed (`out: true` — the same "team/innings still open doesn't mean this specific number is still changing" reasoning already established for the "Your Players" live-detection fix); a BOWLING entry is excluded outright until the innings closes or the match concludes, since a bowler's tally (unlike a dismissed batter's final runs total) can still increase in a later spell within the same innings, and there's no equivalently safe "personally done" signal available for it yet. The pre-existing `balls.length === 0` placeholder guard (same one `lib/playerActivity.ts` already uses) is checked first, so an unstarted innings' pre-authored card is never trusted regardless of any player's `out` status. A genuinely concluded match (or the `FEATURED_MATCH`-shaped "kept live on purpose but has a real result" case) still trusts its current innings unconditionally, exactly as before — no behavior change for either.

**Platform-wide audit performed, not just the reported match.** Every live match without a usable result was checked for real entries sitting in an already-closed innings that the old code was discarding. Found and fixed by the same change: `j-hazlewood` and `y-chahal`'s closed-innings bowling figures in the AUS-vs-IND T20I (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`) had the identical gap — unrelated to India specifically, confirming this was a general match-vs-innings granularity bug, not anything India- or Test-specific. Also explicitly re-confirmed (per the prior round's user decision to keep the broad "team/innings still open counts as live" rule) that Babar Azam's and Andre Russell's dismissed-batter entries in their own still-live matches (PSL, KKR-MI) correctly count now too, via the identical `out: true` logic — consistent with, not a new exception to, that earlier decision. ODI: India has zero matches of any kind (settled or live) in the current mock dataset, so this fix is genuinely untestable for India/ODI until real or additional mock data exists — noted rather than assumed fine, per the user's explicit ask.

**Real tests against real mock data (`npx tsx`), all results logged and cross-checked against the Score tab's own figures**: Kohli/Rohit/Gill's Test graphs now show `[121]`/`[83]`/`[110]` respectively, matching the Score tab exactly. England regression check: Root `[62,44,68]`, Stokes `[22,41,22,4]`, Crawley `[34,51,45,12]`, Duckett `[41,68,18,31]` — all unchanged in shape, now correctly including this match's closed 1st-innings entries too. India bowlers in this Test's still-live current innings (Bumrah, Jadeja) correctly show only their earlier, already-closed-innings figures (`[3]`, `[2]`) — their current spell is deliberately excluded until it closes, per the new rule. Kohli's T20I graph regression check: unchanged (`[43,31]`, from unrelated past T20I matches — his still-live, not-out entry in the AUS-IND T20I is correctly excluded, exactly as intended). Achievements (`getPlayerAchievements`) spot-checked unaffected, since Man of Match/Series still keys off `Match.result` fields that only exist on genuinely concluded matches.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.127 commit shows 1 file modified (`lib/playerForm.ts`) plus the version bump. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Avatar photo-first-fallback-to-initials audit — v1.0.128 (2026-07-28)

**Ask**: confirm whether player avatars (starting with the "Your Players" homepage strip) check for a real photo URL first and only fall back to initials when it's empty, so every avatar switches to real photos automatically once real data populates that field — no code change needed later.

**Findings, audited across every avatar render site in the codebase** (grepped for `imageUrl`/`photoUrl`/`Avatar` across all components):

- **`components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`** — already correct. `showPhoto = !!player.imageUrl && !imgFailed` gates an `<img src={player.imageUrl}>` against an initials `<span>` fallback, with an `onError` handler flipping `imgFailed` so a broken/unreachable URL degrades to initials at runtime too, not just at the empty-field check. No change needed.
- **`components/DigestTab.tsx`'s Man of the Match avatar** — genuinely broken for real-data-readiness, fixed. The derivation read `(PLAYERS[slug] as { photoUrl?: string })?.photoUrl` — a field called `photoUrl` that does not exist anywhere on `PlayerProfile` (`lib/types.ts` declares it `imageUrl`, the exact field `YourPlayersStrip.tsx` already reads correctly). The cast was a type-level lie: it always evaluated to `undefined ?? null`, so this avatar was permanently stuck on its (otherwise correctly-built) initials fallback — and would have STAYED stuck even after real photo data started populating `imageUrl`, since the code was never looking at that field at all. Fixed by reading `imageUrl` instead. Verified end-to-end with a real test: populated `imageUrl` on a real `PLAYERS` entry with zero further code changes, re-ran `buildCards()`, confirmed `manOfMatchPhotoUrl` picked it up automatically.
- **`components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s sticky header** — not a fallback-logic bug; there is no avatar element here at all today (no photo, no initials circle) — just back button, name, favourite star, role badge. Flagged as a gap rather than fixed unilaterally, since adding one is new UI (size/placement/whether it's wanted in an already-dense sticky header), not a fix to broken logic.
- **`components/BallGIF.tsx`'s inline batter/bowler initials** — small (8px) text labels drawn directly on the ball-trajectory SVG during the animation, not a photo-capable avatar component; a different design context (space-constrained overlay, not a persistent UI element), left as-is and called out rather than silently left unexamined.

**Verified real-data readiness directly, not just by inspection**: a temporary test script set `imageUrl` on a real `PLAYERS` entry (Rohit Sharma, resolved via the same `slugifyPlayer()` this card already uses) and re-ran `buildCards()` with zero `DigestTab.tsx` changes made after that point — `manOfMatchPhotoUrl` went from `null` to the populated URL automatically, confirming the fix is wired through the real derivation path, not just type-correct on paper.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.128 commit shows 1 file modified (`components/DigestTab.tsx`) plus the version bump. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean.

## Player profile header avatar, added via one shared component reused by all three avatar sites — v1.0.129 (2026-07-28)

**Ask**: add a player avatar to the profile page header (currently has none at all — no photo, no initials, nothing next to the name), reusing the exact same photo-first-fallback-to-initials pattern already correctly built for the "Your Players" homepage strip, rather than writing a third independent implementation — explicitly framed as avoiding the same duplication risk that let the Digest tab's Man of the Match avatar drift onto a wrong field name in the first place (v1.0.128).

**New `components/PlayerAvatar.tsx`**, extracted from `YourPlayersStrip.tsx`'s `PlayerChip` (which already had the fallback logic right) — now the ONE implementation of "how a player's avatar renders," consumed by all three sites instead of three separate copies. Takes a raw `name: string` (not a full `PlayerProfile`), since `DigestTab.tsx`'s Man of the Match card only ever has a display-name string (`Match.result.manOfMatch`) to work with — resolving a full player object just to render an avatar would be an unnecessary coupling. `imageUrl` first, initials-in-a-circle fallback (via the same `parsePlayerName()` every other display site already uses for consistency), React-state-driven `onError` handling for a broken/unreachable URL at runtime — not just an empty field, and not the CSS-attribute-toggle hack `DigestTab.tsx`'s old copy used.

**Deliberately role/format-agnostic by construction**: `PlayerAvatar` reads no `role`, `battingStyle`, or format-scoped field at all. Every visual customization a caller needs — the strip's favourited-amber ring, the Digest card's team-tinted ring/background, the profile header's role-tinted ring/background — is passed in as plain `ringColor`/`textColor`/`backgroundColor` props, with `sizePx` controlling diameter. This is what makes "identical behavior for a batter, bowler, or all-rounder" true by construction rather than by convention — there's no role-specific branch anywhere in the component that a future role could fall through incorrectly.

**Three call sites migrated, not two migrated and one left as a third parallel copy**:
- `components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`'s `PlayerChip` — 48px, favourited-amber ring/text when applicable. The old inline `initialsFor()` helper and inline fallback markup were removed entirely, not left dead alongside the new component.
- `components/DigestTab.tsx`'s Man of the Match card — 32px, the card's own `manOfMatchColor`-tinted ring/background (unchanged visual treatment, now delivered through the shared component). The old local `initials()` helper (only ever used here) and the CSS-attribute-toggle broken-image hack were both deleted.
- **New**: `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s sticky header — 56px (larger than the strip's 48px chip, since this is the page's primary visual anchor), placed between the back button and the name/nationality block. Ring/text/background tinted with the exact same per-role color (`ROLE_COLORS`) the adjacent role badge already uses, so the two feel like one coherent header rather than two unrelated color choices — reusing an existing convention, not inventing a new one.

**Real-data-ready by construction, not by inspection**: since all three callers now read `imageUrl` through the one shared component, a real photo URL populating `PlayerProfile.imageUrl` reaches every avatar on the platform simultaneously the moment it's set — there is no second call site left that could independently drift onto a wrong field name the way `DigestTab.tsx` once did (v1.0.128).

**Real tests, not a description (`npx tsx`, `react-test-renderer` pinned to the project's actual installed React version — `18.3.1`, not latest, which threw an internals-shape mismatch when installed unpinned — via `npm install --no-save`, removed after)**: photo present renders an `<img>` with no initials span; no photo renders initials with no `<img>`; a photo present but failing (`onError` fired programmatically, not just an empty field) falls back to initials at runtime; initials correctness spot-checked across several real name shapes (confirmed unchanged from the pre-refactor behavior, including the pre-existing "SKY" edge case, which was not something this task was scoped to touch); identical rendered structure regardless of an arbitrary role label passed alongside the same props (confirming role-agnosticism); all three real call-site sizes (32px Digest / 48px strip / 56px profile header) scale their dimensions and font size correctly; a `backgroundColor` override (the Digest/profile-header case) applies correctly on top of the default `bg-surface` fallback.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.129 commit shows 1 new file (`components/PlayerAvatar.tsx`) and 3 modified (`components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`, `components/DigestTab.tsx`, `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`) plus the version bump. `package.json`/`package-lock.json` unaffected by the temporary `react-test-renderer` dev install (`--no-save`, uninstalled after the test run, confirmed via `git status`). `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Win-probability figure: visual-prominence pass — larger text, neutral pill, scale-pulse — v1.0.130 (2026-07-28)

**Ask**: make the "WIN PROB" readout more visually prominent in its existing spot (matchup row, ball-by-ball-unavailable fallback, full-screen chart header) — bigger text than the nearby score digits, a soft neutral pill background so it reads as one distinct badge, and a brief scale-pulse on ball-by-ball updates — without touching placement, without any color change, and consistent across every place the readout appears.

**All three changes made in the ONE shared `components/WinProbBadge.tsx`**, not per call site — the same reasoning that justified extracting this component in the first place (v1.0.123): a change made in one of its three call sites and not the others is exactly how the original team-color inconsistency happened.

- **Size**: compact variant's value bumped `text-[13px]` → `text-[18px]` (previously smaller than the sticky header's own `text-sm`/14px score digits, now clearly larger, per the reported complaint). Large variant bumped `text-xl` (20px) → `text-2xl` (24px) — it already exceeded its local score context, but this keeps the "genuinely more prominent" outcome consistent across both variants rather than leaving one variant's boost implicit.
- **Pill**: both variants now wrap their text in one shared `rounded-xl bg-white/[0.06] border border-white/10` container — a translucent, fixed-neutral lightening over whatever sits behind it (the matchup row's dark card, the fallback card, the modal's plain background), never a solid design-token fill or anything that could itself start to look "team-colored" by coincidence. Removed a stale `className="!px-0"` override at the `MatchView.tsx` fallback call site that existed only to zero out padding on the OLD plain-text version — the new pill needs (and now gets) real padding there too, matching the other two call sites instead of diverging from them.
- **Pulse**: new `.winprob-pulse` CSS keyframe (`app/globals.css`) — `transform: scale()` only, 180ms, no color/opacity/box-shadow component, so it structurally cannot reintroduce the flicker risk already ruled out for team-coloring this figure (see the original neutral-color decision this file documents above). Triggered by giving the value node `key={pct}` — React remounts that node (and only that node) whenever the underlying percentage genuinely changes, which retriggers the CSS animation automatically; a re-render that doesn't change `pct` never touches the key and never re-triggers the pulse.
- **Real-data compatible by construction**: nothing here reads a specific team, match, or hardcoded percentage — `pct`/`label` are still resolved exactly as before via `getLeadingTeamWinProb`/`getLeadingTeamFromOverride` (`lib/winProb.ts`), unchanged by this pass. The pill, size, and pulse are pure presentation over whatever real value flows in.

**Real test, not a description (`npx tsx`, `react-test-renderer` pinned to the project's installed React 18.3.1, `--no-save`, removed after)**: rendering the same `<WinProbBadge>` instance with an unchanged `pct` across a re-render did NOT reset the value node's identity (no remount, no pulse retrigger) — confirmed via a ref-identity check; rendering it with a genuinely different `pct` DID create a new node instance, confirming the pulse only fires on a real change. Both variants confirmed to render the pill wrapper classes and the larger text-size classes. `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean.

**Verified scope**: `git diff --stat` against the pre-v1.0.130 commit shows 3 files modified (`components/WinProbBadge.tsx`, `components/MatchView.tsx` — the stale padding-override removal, `app/globals.css` — the new keyframe) plus the version bump. `components/MatchupCard.tsx` and `components/WinProbChart.tsx` unchanged — their calls into `WinProbBadge` already had no per-site overrides to reconcile. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Matchup-card diagnostic follow-up: 3 fixes — mid-scrub card recompute, fixture double-wicket cleanup, mock-simulation ticker gate — v1.0.131 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: a prior diagnostic-only pass (no code changes) investigated three issues reported while testing the batter-vs-bowler matchup card: (1) a duplicate "matches" value across two different bowler pairings for the same batter, (2) a wicket badge disagreeing with the batting-status header about whether a player was out, (3) non-monotonic live scores across page reloads. Findings: issue 1 was a mock-data-content coincidence only (the lookup itself is correctly pair-and-format-scoped — no code bug); issue 3 was a mock-harness-only demo ticker with no real-data-affecting resolution-logic bug, though flagged as an unguarded forward risk; issue 2 was a genuine code bug. This entry covers the follow-up: fixing the confirmed bug (issue 2), fixing the specific fixture data error that exposed it (plus a platform-wide audit for the same pattern), and adding the real-data-readiness guard flagged for issue 3.

**Fix 1 — `truncatedMatch` now recomputes `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` from the truncated ball slice, not just `runs`/`wickets`/`overs`.**

Root cause (confirmed): `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` memo recomputed `runs`/`wickets`/`overs` for the current ball-by-ball scrub position, but spread `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` through unchanged (`{ ...inn, balls: truncBalls }`) — so `MiniInsightsBar.tsx`'s striker/bowler header chips, and the Scorecard tab (both consume `truncatedMatch`), always showed a player's END-OF-INNINGS totals regardless of playback position.

Two new functions in `lib/matchStatus.ts` — `deriveBattingCardFromBalls(balls, originalCard)` and `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls(balls, originalCard, format)` — recompute every mutable per-player field (runs, ballsFaced, fours, sixes, strikeRate, out, dismissal, onStrike for batting; oversBowled, maidens, runsConceded, wickets, economy for bowling) fresh from whatever ball slice is passed in, using `originalCard` only as a scaffold for player identity/order. `truncatedMatch`'s mid-innings branch now calls both, from the exact same `truncBalls` slice `runs`/`wickets`/`overs` are already derived from — one source of truth, no partial recompute. The `isComplete` branch (whole innings visible) is untouched — it already correctly uses the innings' real, authoritative cards.

`out`/`dismissal` are derived purely from whether a player's OWN ball carries `isWicket: true` within the given slice — never borrowed from the original card's `out` flag, since that describes the innings' end state, which may not have been reached yet at the current playback position (and, for a genuinely live real match, may never resolve the way the frozen mock card assumed).

**Fix 2 — the fixture data error that exposed Fix 1: a platform-wide audit found the same "batter re-appears as striker after their own recorded dismissal" pattern in 5 innings across 3 matches** (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` innings 1 & 2, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` innings 2, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live` innings 1 & 2) — not just R Pant's originally-reported case. Root cause varied by instance:

- Some flagged wicket balls were themselves spurious: the batter's own authoritative `battingCard` entry says `out: false` (e.g. R Pant's two `isWicket: true` balls at `ia-2-14.4`/`ia-2-15.4` — he finishes the innings not out per the real scorecard, so neither flag was ever real).
- Some were genuine dismissals whose incoming-batter handoff was simply never applied to the balls that followed (the raw log kept using the dismissed player's name for several more deliveries before eventually, inconsistently, switching over).
- A few names (e.g. `Hasan Ali`/`Z Khan` in the PSL innings 1 tail) were bowler names mistakenly reused as placeholder batters in bulk-generated filler overs, never real batters at all.

Fix applied ball-by-ball, cross-checked against each innings' own authoritative `battingCard` as ground truth: a wicket is accepted as genuine only if the card confirms that specific player `out: true` and it's their first (not a repeat) flag; genuine dismissals correctly hand off to whichever player the surrounding data establishes as the real incoming batter; any name that keeps appearing after the innings' full quota of card-confirmed dismissals is reattributed to the card's actual not-out finisher(s) (e.g. the PSL innings-1 tail now correctly reads "A Imad" throughout instead of cycling through bowler names). Spurious wicket flags have their `dismissalType`/`nextBatterName`/narrative `oneLiner` text cleared along with `isWicket`, so no ball still narrates an out that didn't happen. 273 individual ball-field edits applied via a verified script (not by hand); re-running the audit against the patched data confirms **zero remaining instances of the pattern anywhere in the mock dataset**.

**Fix 3 — `isMockSimulation` flag gates the demo-only rewind ticker so it can never run against real data.**

`MatchView.tsx`'s `liveBallIdx` auto-advance effect loops backward once it reaches the end of a match's ball log (`idx >= allBalls.length - 1 ? allBalls.length - 10 : idx + 1`) on a repeating ~24s cycle, purely so a mock "live" match with no real backing clock never looks finished/frozen. It previously had no guard distinguishing mock data from real — flagged in the prior diagnostic pass as a forward risk (this same rewind would still fire against a correctly, monotonically-progressing real feed once wired in, fabricating a fake rewind on top of genuinely correct data).

Added `isMockSimulation?: boolean` to the `Match` type (`lib/types.ts`), documented as defaulting to false/absent for every match — a real data adapter has no reason to ever set it, so real matches are inert by construction, not by remembering to opt out. The gating decision itself is a small, dependency-free exported function, `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker(match, isLiveFollowing)` (`lib/matchStatus.ts`), which `MatchView.tsx`'s effect now calls instead of inlining the condition — kept as its own function specifically so it's directly unit-testable without needing to mount the full component tree. Set `isMockSimulation: true` on exactly the 4 mock fixtures that are deliberately kept `status: "live"` forever with a scripted ball log and no real clock: `FEATURED_MATCH` (`ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`), `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`. Every other match in the dataset (25 of 29) is unflagged.

**Real tests, not descriptions** (`npx tsx`, run directly against the actual shipped functions — no react-test-renderer needed for this round, since the meaningful logic lives in plain, dependency-free functions rather than component render output):

- Truncated `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` innings 2's ball log to right after R Pant's very first ball faced: `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` correctly reports `ballsFaced: 1, runs: 0, out: false, onStrike: true` — genuinely computed at that point, not the frozen final-card value (which happens to also say not-out, but for the right reason now). Truncated to S Gill's own wicket ball: his derived card entry already shows `out: true` at that exact ball — header and wicket badge are now reading the same source at the same moment by construction, closing the exact gap the original report surfaced.
- Re-ran the platform-wide "dismissed batter reappears" audit against the live, patched `lib/mockData.ts`: 0 issues (down from 331 originally found across the 5 affected innings).
- `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker`: confirmed true only for `(isLiveFollowing: true, isMockSimulation: true)`; confirmed false for live-following with `isMockSimulation` explicitly `false`, absent entirely, or with `isLiveFollowing: false`. Confirmed exactly the 4 intended fixtures carry the flag and the remaining 25 matches in the full dataset default to unflagged.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout.

**Verified scope**: files changed are `lib/matchStatus.ts` (new derivation functions + `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker`), `components/MatchView.tsx` (wired both into `truncatedMatch` and the ticker effect), `lib/types.ts` (`isMockSimulation` field), `lib/mockData.ts` (273 ball-field corrections across 5 innings + 4 `isMockSimulation: true` flags), plus the version bump. No component beyond `MatchView.tsx` needed changes — `MiniInsightsBar.tsx` and `Scorecard.tsx` automatically read the corrected cards through the same `truncatedMatch` prop they already consumed. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## Matchup-card follow-up round 2: Digest innings mislabel, retired-not-out modeling gap, R Sharma fixture correction — v1.0.132 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: Live verification of the v1.0.131 fix round surfaced two new issues plus one item requiring direct code confirmation rather than a live test: (1) every Digest-tab over-group card in a live T20I showed "1ST INN" regardless of actual innings, and the innings toggle appeared to do nothing; (2) the Score tab's full scorecard for `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` showed three simultaneous "not out" batters against only 3 fallen wickets -- reported as a possible missing "retired" concept; (3) confirmation requested, without a live test being possible, that the `isMockSimulation` ticker gate added in v1.0.131 is correctly wired and defaults safely for real data.

**Fix 1 -- `buildOverGroupCards`'s innings label used the wrong occurrence function for non-Test formats.**

Root cause: the per-card `inningsLabel` (`components/DigestTab.tsx`) was computed via `teamInningsOccurrence(match.innings, inn)` unconditionally. That function answers "is this the batting team's 1st or 2nd innings" -- correct for Test follow-on (where a team's 2nd innings must read "2nd Inn", never the global "3rd/4th Inn"), but wrong for every non-Test format: a T20I/ODI team only ever bats once, so the function's own doc comment already states it "always returns 1" there. Every over-group card in `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` -- AUS's innings 1 AND India's innings 2 -- was therefore labeled "1st Inn", uppercased to "1ST INN" by the badge's CSS. The innings toggle chips were never actually broken: filtering by `c.inningsNumber` (which correctly holds the real match-wide innings number, 1 or 2, straight from `inn.number`) already returned the right, differently-numbered overs for each innings -- confirmed by constructing both filtered lists directly (20 cards for innings 1, overs 1-20; 16 cards for innings 2, overs 1-16, non-overlapping). The toggle just LOOKED broken because the one visible differentiator next to each card, the innings tag, never changed.

Fix: `inningsLabel` now branches on format -- `isTest ? teamInningsOccurrence(...) : inn.number` -- keeping Test follow-on behavior byte-for-byte identical (same function, same call) while non-Test formats get the match-wide innings position instead. `buildTestSessionCards`'s own, separate use of `teamInningsOccurrence` (Test-only, never shared with this function) is untouched.

**Fix 2 -- closed the "retired not out" modeling gap; corrected the specific fixture data bug it was mistaken for.**

Investigation found the reported 3-simultaneous-not-out state was NOT a retirement -- `lib/mockData.ts` has zero balls anywhere with `dismissalType: "retired"`. The real cause: R Sharma's `battingCard` entry for `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` innings 2 said `runs: 31, ballsFaced: 22, out: true, dismissal: "c Maxwell b Starc"`, but his actual ball-by-ball data (`inn.balls` filtered by `batterName`) shows 106 runs off 49 balls with zero `isWicket: true` balls -- a stale scorecard entry left over from an earlier draft of the innings, never updated to match the ball log it now contradicts. Since the live Score tab always derives real stats from balls (`deriveBattingCardFromBalls`, v1.0.131), never trusting the static card's own `runs`/`ballsFaced`/`out` fields, this ball data is what actually renders -- and with no wicket ball for him, ever, he shows "not out" indefinitely, indistinguishable from Kohli and Pant, the two genuine current partners.

That said, the question was legitimate on its own merits: the data model's support for a real "retired, not currently batting" state was found to be effectively nonexistent. `Ball.dismissalType` (`lib/types.ts`) already listed `"retired"` as an enum member, but nothing consumed it anywhere -- `BattingEntry` had no field to carry it, and `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` branched only on `isWicket`. A genuine future retirement ball would have rendered exactly like ordinary "not out" today. Closed this gap:

- `BattingEntry` (`lib/types.ts`) gains `retiredNotOut?: boolean`, documented as always false when `out` is true, and as covering only "retired -- not out" (the common, voluntary case) -- the rarer umpire-given "retired -- out" would need `out: true` plus a way to disambiguate from a normal dismissal, which the current flat `dismissalType` enum can't do, so it's explicitly left unmodeled rather than silently mishandled.
- `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` (`lib/matchStatus.ts`) now separately checks for a `dismissalType === "retired"` ball (only when the player isn't already genuinely out) and sets `retiredNotOut`, with its own distinct dismissal text ("retired not out") instead of falling into the generic not-out path.
- `components/Scorecard.tsx`'s `BatterRow`: `isLiveBatter` (drives the excitement-glow row background, the onStrike asterisk, and the "not out" live tag) now also excludes `retiredNotOut`, and a new render branch shows the dimmed, dismissal-style "retired not out" line (same treatment as a genuine dismissal's text line, not the live-glow one) instead.

Separately, corrected the stale R Sharma fixture entry to match his real ball data (`runs: 106, ballsFaced: 49, fours: 10, sixes: 5, strikeRate: 216.33, out: false, dismissal: "not out"`) so the static card is no longer internally self-contradictory. This does not change today's live rendering (already deriving "not out" from balls regardless), but matters if this match is ever read without ball data. Deliberately did NOT fabricate a new "retired" ball to force his live rendering to change: his last ball (`ia-2-9.1`) is followed immediately by an already-scripted, unrelated Kohli delivery (`ia-2-9.2`) as part of a normal strike rotation, and inserting a synthetic mid-over event would require renumbering the following overs' ball sequence -- risking new bugs in over-completion/ball-count logic elsewhere (Digest's `buildOverGroupCards`, live-ball-count checks) for the sake of a narrative event this fixture was never actually authored to have. The 3-simultaneous-"not out" *visual* in this one match therefore persists until a real terminal event (wicket or an authored retirement) is added to this fixture -- a separate, narrower follow-up from the modeling gap, which is now genuinely closed for any match/feed that does supply one.

**Fix 3 (confirmation only, no code change) -- `isMockSimulation` ticker gate.**

Per direct request, confirmed via code inspection plus fresh `npx tsx` runs (no live UI test possible/needed): `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker(match, isLiveFollowing)` in `lib/matchStatus.ts` is exactly `return isLiveFollowing && match.isMockSimulation === true;` -- a strict `=== true` check, so any match without the field (the default for all real data, per `lib/types.ts`'s doc comment on `isMockSimulation?: boolean`) evaluates `undefined === true` as `false` and never engages the ticker, with no separate opt-out needed. `MatchView.tsx`'s auto-advance effect (`components/MatchView.tsx`, the `useEffect` around `liveBallIdx`) calls this function as its sole guard clause before starting the `setInterval`. Confirmed by grep that exactly 4 fixtures set `isMockSimulation: true` (`FEATURED_MATCH`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`), matching the v1.0.131 changelog's stated intent, and re-ran the gate function directly against `(live-following, flagged)`, `(live-following, unflagged)`, `(not live-following, flagged)` -- true only for the first.

**Real tests, not descriptions** (`npx tsx`, run directly against the shipped functions):

- `buildOverGroupCards` on `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`: innings 1 cards now read `inningsLabel: "1st Inn"` (was already correct), innings 2 cards now read `"2nd Inn"` (was "1st Inn" before the fix). Innings filtering re-confirmed unchanged: 20 cards/overs 1-20 for innings 1, 16 cards/overs 1-16 for innings 2.
- Synthetic 3-batter, 6-ball scenario exercising all three terminal states at once (`deriveBattingCardFromBalls`): a retiring batter (`dismissalType: "retired"`, no `isWicket`) correctly derives `out: false, retiredNotOut: true, dismissal: "retired not out"`; a genuinely dismissed batter derives `out: true, retiredNotOut: false`; the batter still facing balls derives `out: false, retiredNotOut: false, dismissal: undefined`. All three mutually exclusive as expected.
- Regression re-run of the exact two v1.0.131 test cases plus the reappearance audit: R Pant truncated to his first ball still derives `ballsFaced: 1, out: false, retiredNotOut: false`; S Gill truncated to his own wicket ball still derives `out: true`, correct dismissal text, `retiredNotOut: false`; full reappearance-after-dismissal audit across every match/innings in `LIVE_INTERNATIONAL` still returns 0 issues.
- `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker` re-confirmed true only for `(isLiveFollowing: true, isMockSimulation: true)`.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout.

**Verified scope**: files changed are `components/DigestTab.tsx` (innings-label branch), `lib/types.ts` (`retiredNotOut` field), `lib/matchStatus.ts` (retired-ball detection in `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`), `components/Scorecard.tsx` (`isLiveBatter` exclusion + new render branch), `lib/mockData.ts` (R Sharma's stale `battingCard` entry corrected), plus the version bump. No fixture ball data was added or renumbered. Live verification recorded separately below this entry.

## R Sharma fixture retirement request — investigated, structurally declined; "Retired" label change shipped — v1.0.133 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: follow-up request to author an actual retirement event for R Sharma in `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` (innings 2), using the `retiredNotOut`/`dismissalType: "retired"` mechanism from v1.0.132, so the Score tab stops showing him alongside the two genuine current partners as a third "not out" batter. Explicitly asked to insert the event without renumbering subsequent overs/balls, and to say so explicitly if that isn't cleanly possible rather than force it.

**Finding: not cleanly possible, and the constraint is now demonstrated, not assumed.**

The `Ball`/`Innings` model has no concept of a "non-delivery match event" — every entry in `innings.balls[]` is treated by every downstream consumer as exactly one bowled delivery, grouped into its over via `.over` alone. There is no field that marks an entry as "don't count this toward the over's `ballsPerSet(format)` legal-ball quota" other than `extraType: "wd"/"nb"`, both of which have specific, different real meanings a retirement can't correctly borrow. Two concrete insertion strategies were tried and both provably corrupt something else:

1. **Same real over (attach the retirement to over 9, where R Sharma's actual last ball is).** `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`'s `legalBalls` filter only excludes `wd`/`nb`, so the new entry is counted as a 7th legal delivery in what was a clean 6-ball over. Verified with `npx tsx`: before insertion, M Labuschagne's full-innings figures are `oversBowled: 3, economy: 10.33`; after inserting one zero-run, non-wicket "retired" ball into his over 9, they become `oversBowled: 3.1, economy: 9.79` — a bowler's figures silently changed despite him bowling zero additional real deliveries.
2. **An out-of-band sentinel over (e.g. `over: 97`, clearly outside the match's real range) to avoid touching a real over's ball count.** This breaks a different piece of logic: `buildOverGroupCards`'s live-innings "is the last over still in progress" check (`isLive && isLastInn && legalBalls(lastOverBalls).length < 6`) uses whichever over number is numerically last in the ball array to decide which over to treat as "still being bowled, exclude it from card generation." Making a fabricated over 97 the new numerically-last over demotes the real last over (17, genuinely mid-over with only 1 ball bowled) out of "still in progress" status — verified with `npx tsx`: card count goes from 36 to 37, and the new card is a premature "Over 17" summary for an over that has only 1 of its 6 balls bowled, not the fabricated over 97 at all.

Both failure modes trace to the same root cause: retirement, in real cricket, happens *between* deliveries and consumes no ball, but this data model has no slot for a between-deliveries event — only for deliveries themselves. Renumbering every subsequent ball to make room was the one approach not tried, since that's exactly the risk flagged and ruled out in v1.0.131/v1.0.132 and again explicitly ruled out in this request.

**Not implemented (fixture unchanged):** no ball was added to `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`. R Sharma's Score tab entry remains "not out" (his real, already-corrected 106/49 line from v1.0.132) alongside Kohli and Pant — the 3-simultaneous-"not out" visual for this one specific match is unresolved, pending either (a) a genuine model extension (see below) or (b) accepting a full renumbering pass, which was not authorized here.

**Shipped instead — "Retired" display label.** Per the request's secondary instruction (display as "Retired", not "Retired not out"), updated the already-correct, already-tested `retiredNotOut` mechanism's OWN label, independent of this fixture: `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`'s dismissal text for a `retiredNotOut` batter (`lib/matchStatus.ts`) and `Scorecard.tsx`'s fallback text both changed from `"retired not out"` to `"Retired"`. This applies to any match that legitimately exercises the mechanism (none currently do, per v1.0.132's audit — zero `dismissalType: "retired"` balls exist anywhere in `lib/mockData.ts`), so it's a forward-looking correction with no visible effect on any match today. Re-verified with a fresh synthetic retirement scenario via `npx tsx`: derived `dismissal: "Retired"`, `retiredNotOut: true`, `out: false`.

**Flagged as a real option, not implemented unilaterally**: modeling retirement as an *innings-level* side-channel (e.g. a set of player IDs on `Innings` marked retired-as-of a given ball index) rather than a ball-array entry would sidestep both failure modes above entirely, since it wouldn't touch `.over`/ball-count bookkeeping at all — arguably the structurally correct shape, since a retirement isn't a delivery. Not built here: it's a genuine type/model design decision (new `Innings` field, new consumption path in `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`) beyond "patch this fixture," left for a dedicated follow-up if wanted.

**Tests**: `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. Fixture-corruption claims backed by real `npx tsx` runs against the actual shipped functions (`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`, `buildOverGroupCards`), not description -- both included above with before/after numbers.

**Verified scope**: files changed are `lib/matchStatus.ts` and `components/Scorecard.tsx` (label text only), `README.md` (label reference), plus the version bump. `lib/mockData.ts` is untouched.

## Real ball-by-ball feed adapter + retirement side-channel (both variants) — v1.0.134 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: follow-up to v1.0.133's finding that match/ball-by-ball data was the one subsystem without a real-data-readiness boundary function, and that a retirement can't safely be inserted into `Innings.balls` without corrupting over/wicket bookkeeping. Requested: (1) a sanctioned adapter translating a realistic raw provider feed into Bawler's internal shape, (2) retirement handled via an innings-level side-channel instead of the ball array, (3) the rarer "retired -- out" variant modeled alongside the existing "retired -- not out", (4) concrete proof the over-counting corruption can't recur, (5) a clear statement of whether mock data flows through the same path.

**Correction to the prior framing**: match data was not, in fact, entirely without a boundary layer — `lib/dataValidation.ts`'s `normalizeMatch()` (RR1, documented earlier in this log) already validates a `Match`/`Innings`/`Ball`-shaped object at runtime. What it doesn't do is TRANSLATE a genuinely different raw wire format into that shape — it assumes the input already speaks Bawler's own field names. That's the real, narrower gap this round closes; see ARCHITECTURE.md's new worked example for the full comparison against `normalizeMatch()` and the other three (`teamData`/`teamSchedule`/`playerForm`) READ-side interface-first adapters, which this isn't quite the same shape as (this is an ingestion/write-side boundary, not a read accessor).

**New — `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`, `ingestMatchFeed(raw: RawFeedMatch, opts?)`.** The one sanctioned entry point for a raw provider payload. `RawFeedMatch`/`RawFeedInnings`/`RawFeedDelivery`/`RawFeedRetirementEvent` (all exported from this file) are a best-informed assumption about a realistic live-scores API shape — snake_case field names distinct from Bawler's own camelCase, and, critically, `RawFeedInnings.events` models deliveries and retirement events as one interleaved sequence per innings rather than a deliveries-only array, since several real providers do mix delivery and card-level events that way. `ingestMatchFeed` reshapes every field, splits each innings' `events` into real deliveries (mapped to `Ball`) and retirement events (mapped to the new side-channel, below) via `adaptInnings()`, then delegates the reshaped object to `normalizeMatch()` for field-level validation — no duplicated validation logic. Never throws; a malformed raw payload (including one that throws mid-transform on a missing nested field) degrades to `{ ok: false, errors: [...] }`, matching `normalizeMatch()`'s own contract.

**New — `RetirementRecord`/`RetirementType` (`lib/types.ts`), `Innings.retirements?: RetirementRecord[]`.** A retirement happens BETWEEN deliveries in real cricket and consumes no ball — modeling it as a `balls[]` entry was tried and rejected with two concrete corruption proofs in v1.0.133 (a real over's ball count inflating, corrupting that over's bowler's figures; an out-of-range sentinel over instead fooling "which over is currently live" resolution, prematurely closing the real current over). `RetirementRecord` carries `playerId`/`playerName`, `type: "retired-not-out" | "retired-out"`, and an optional `afterBallId` — the `id` of the last real ball bowled before the retirement, used only to determine whether it's "happened yet" at a given truncated/live playback position (never an array index, which shifts under truncation).

**New helpers — `lib/matchStatus.ts`.** `isRetirementVisible(record, balls)` — true if `afterBallId` is unset or that ball id is present in the given slice. `countWicketEquivalentRetirements(retirements, balls)` — counts only the `"retired-out"` variant (visible ones), since "retired-not-out" deliberately never counts toward the innings' wicket tally, matching real cricket. `MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` (the ONE place that recomputes a live innings' wickets from `balls.filter(isWicket)`) now adds this count in — the only call site that needed to change, since it's the sole place a live wicket tally is independently derived.

**Extended — `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` (`lib/matchStatus.ts`).** Now takes an optional third `retirements` param (default `[]`). A player's retirement status is read exclusively from this side-channel, gated by `isRetirementVisible` (a retirement scheduled later in the innings must not retroactively apply to an earlier scrub position) and only when they aren't already genuinely out some other way. `"retired-not-out"` sets `retiredNotOut: true`, `out` stays `false`, dismissal text `"Retired"`. `"retired-out"` sets `retiredOut: true`, `out: true` (folds into the wicket-equivalent count above, unlike its sibling), dismissal text `"Retired out"`, and credits no bowler (no `isWicket` ball is ever created for it). `onStrike` now also excludes either retirement variant — a retired player has left the crease just as surely as a dismissed one, even if their own last ball happens to still be the innings' most recent so far.

**`components/Scorecard.tsx` needed zero changes for "retired -- out".** Since it sets `out: true`, it automatically flows through the EXISTING dismissal-text render branch (`row.out && row.dismissal`) with its own correct "Retired out" text and the existing dimmed, non-live styling — no new branch needed, unlike "retired -- not out" (v1.0.132), which deliberately keeps `out: false` and does need its own branch.

**Hardened — `lib/dataValidation.ts`.** `validateBall` now hard-REJECTS (a blocking `errors` entry, not the non-blocking `warnings` an ordinary unrecognized `dismissalType` gets) any ball reaching it with `dismissalType: "retired"` — this is deliberately NOT a warning, because a real feed reporting a retirement inline in its ball-by-ball sequence is the realistic, expected failure mode this whole round exists to guard against, not an edge case to log and move past. New `validateRetirement()` + `VALID_RETIREMENT_TYPES` validate the new `Innings.retirements` array the same way every other field here is validated. This closes the loophole even for a call path that bypasses `ingestMatchFeed()` entirely and calls `normalizeMatch()` directly against something that already contains a retirement-tagged ball.

**Mock data path, stated explicitly**: unchanged, and deliberately so. `lib/mockData.ts`'s hand-authored fixtures were never run through `normalizeMatch()` either (only `lib/matchGenerator.ts`'s procedurally-generated past/future matches are — this predates this round). `ingestMatchFeed()` exists to translate a genuinely different raw wire shape into Bawler's own; a hand-authored fixture already IS Bawler's own shape, so there's nothing for it to do there. No mock fixture sets `Innings.retirements` today.

**Real tests, not descriptions** (`npx tsx`, run directly against the shipped functions):

- Constructed a raw feed for a 2-over T20I innings (12 real deliveries) with a `RawFeedRetirementEvent` interleaved mid-over (after ball 4 of over 1, a "not_out" retirement). Ran through `ingestMatchFeed`: `result.ok === true`; over 1 and over 2 each report exactly 6 balls (not 7); total balls 12 (not 13); no ball anywhere carries `dismissalType: "retired"`; `Innings.retirements` contains exactly the one correctly-mapped record.
- `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` on the resulting balls: the over-1 bowler's figures are a clean `oversBowled: 1, economy: 9` — not the `1.1`/skewed-economy corruption v1.0.133 proved for the same shape of insertion directly into `balls`.
- `buildOverGroupCards(match, inn.balls, true)`: exactly 2 cards, one per real over — no phantom "current over" misresolution.
- `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` with `inn.retirements` passed through: the retired batter derives `out: false, retiredNotOut: true, dismissal: "Retired"`; the incoming batter is unaffected (`retiredNotOut: false`, normal ongoing not-out).
- `inn.wickets` for that same innings: `0` — the retired-NOT-out event correctly does not count as a dismissal.
- A second constructed feed exercising `"given_out"` (retired-out): `inn.wickets: 1` (correctly counted), derived card shows `out: true, retiredOut: true, dismissal: "Retired out"`.
- Malformed-feed defense-in-depth: a delivery event additionally tagged `dismissal_type: "retired"` (an internally inconsistent raw payload) run through `ingestMatchFeed` returns `{ ok: false }` with the exact new hard-rejection message — the backstop fires even for input the extraction step wasn't designed to see.
- Full regression re-run of every prior round's test (v1.0.131's R Pant/S Gill truncation cases, the reappearance audit, the ticker gate; v1.0.132/133's R Sharma not-out derivation): all identical to before, since `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`'s new third parameter defaults to `[]` and no existing fixture sets `retirements`.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean throughout.

**Verified scope**: new file `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`. Changed: `lib/types.ts` (`RetirementRecord`/`RetirementType`, `Innings.retirements`, `BattingEntry.retiredOut`), `lib/matchStatus.ts` (`isRetirementVisible`, `countWicketEquivalentRetirements`, extended `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`), `components/MatchView.tsx` (wickets calc + call site), `lib/dataValidation.ts` (hardened `validateBall`, new `validateRetirement`), plus the version bump. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched this round. `components/Scorecard.tsx` untouched this round (its v1.0.132 rendering already covers `retiredOut` for free via `out: true`).

## R Sharma retirement applied to live demo fixture, using the v1.0.134 side-channel — v1.0.135 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: v1.0.134 built the `Innings.retirements` side-channel and `RetirementRecord` type but shipped with zero mock fixtures actually using it — the AUS vs IND T20I live demo (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`) still left R Sharma's innings open-ended (106 off 49, no terminal event), which is the exact gap flagged back in v1.0.132/133: the Score tab could show three simultaneously "not out" batters once his balls had all played out, since nothing in the data ever closed his innings.

**Change — `lib/mockData.ts`, innings 2 of `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`**: added
```ts
retirements: [
  { playerId: "rsharma", playerName: "R Sharma", type: "retired-not-out", afterBallId: "ia-2-9.1" },
],
```
`afterBallId` points at the real id of his actual last ball (`ia-2-9.1`, over 9 ball 1 — the same over already used in v1.0.133's corruption proof, coincidentally) rather than an array index, per the mechanism's own design. `balls[]` was not touched, renumbered, or reshuffled — this is purely an addition to the side-channel array, which is exactly the point of having it. The next ball in the array, `ia-2-9.2`, is V Kohli's, confirming the retirement sits correctly between Sharma's last delivery and the next batter's first.

Also corrected the innings' static `battingCard` fallback entry for `rsharma` (the one used only if this match is ever read/rendered without ball data) to match: added `retiredNotOut: true` and changed `dismissal` from `"not out"` to `"Retired"`, so the fallback and the live-derived path agree. Per the user's explicit wording preference, the display text is `"Retired"`, not `"Retired not out"`.

**Verified — real `npx tsx` test against the shipped fixture and functions, not a description**:
- `deriveBattingCardFromBalls` run with the innings' full ball sequence (all 16.2 overs) and the new `retirements` array: R Sharma derives `out: false, retiredNotOut: true, dismissal: "Retired", runs: 106, ballsFaced: 49` — stats preserved exactly, terminal state now explicit.
- Applying the same exclusion rule `components/Scorecard.tsx` already uses for live-batter styling (`!row.out && !row.retiredNotOut && row.ballsFaced > 0`) to the full derived card yields exactly 2 batters: V Kohli (40, 33) and R Pant (9, 13) — not 3. Sharma is correctly excluded via `retiredNotOut`, not via `out`.
- Truncated the ball sequence at exactly `ia-2-9.1` (the moment his retirement takes effect) and confirmed the derivation flips to `retiredNotOut: true` at that exact point, not before: one ball earlier (`ia-2-9.0`), Sharma still derives as an ordinary not-out batter (`retiredNotOut: false`), proving `afterBallId`-gated visibility (`isRetirementVisible`, v1.0.134) works correctly on this real fixture, not just synthetic test data.
- `countWicketEquivalentRetirements` confirms this event never inflates the wicket count (`retired-not-out` is excluded by design) — the innings' 3 real wickets are unaffected.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: only `lib/mockData.ts` (this one innings) and a stale-comment update in `lib/types.ts` (the `Innings.retirements` doc comment previously said "no mock fixture uses this state yet" — no longer true). No changes to `lib/matchStatus.ts`, `lib/dataValidation.ts`, `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`, or `components/Scorecard.tsx` — all v1.0.134 machinery worked against this real fixture unmodified.

## Split the shared matchup/win-prob row into two independent boxes — v1.0.136 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: `MatchupCard.tsx`'s collapsed teaser used to be one box that shared its single line between the batter/bowler pairing and the "WIN PROB" readout (`WinProbBadge`, v1.0.121/v1.0.130) — tapping the pairing swapped that same box's content over to the full head-to-head stat breakdown, which meant the win-prob number physically disappeared whenever a viewer opened H2H stats. Requested fix: split this into two genuinely independent, side-by-side boxes within the same row footprint, so opening H2H stats can never affect the win-prob box's presence or size.

**Change — `components/MatchupCard.tsx`**: the collapsed/expanded content that used to be the component's entire return is now assigned to a local `matchupBox` variable (still gated on the same `expanded` state, same tap-to-expand interaction, identical H2H stat content -- nothing about that logic changed). The component's actual return is now:
```tsx
<div className="flex gap-2 items-start">
  <div className={`... ${winProb ? "w-[60%]" : "flex-1"}`}>{matchupBox}</div>
  {winProb && (
    <div className="flex-1">
      <WinProbBadge variant="boxed" label={winProb.label} pct={winProb.pct} onClick={onExpandWinProb} />
    </div>
  )}
</div>
```
The win-prob box's JSX is a sibling AFTER `{matchupBox}`, entirely outside the ternary that defines `matchupBox` -- toggling `expanded` re-derives `matchupBox`'s content only; the win-prob block is never inside that conditional, so it cannot be hidden, replaced, or resized by it. `items-start` (not `items-stretch`) on the row keeps the win-prob box's height independent too -- when the matchup box expands into its taller multi-row H2H card, the win-prob box keeps its own natural, unstretched height rather than growing to match.

The collapsed teaser button is now the entire left box (previously the name region and a separate small chevron button were split tap targets sharing the row with the win-prob badge) -- same open-on-tap interaction, `aria-label`, and data source, just no longer needing to share hit-area logic with a sibling. Names render via the same `formatPlayerName()` call as before (`batterDisplay`/`bowlerDisplay`), unchanged -- verified below this already produces the requested "Initial Surname" form (e.g. "V Kohli", "J Root") and is idempotent on the already-short strings the mock ball data stores, so there was no separate "abbreviation" bug to fix in that function; the previous cramped look was purely a consequence of the name box sharing its row with the win-prob badge, now resolved by giving it a dedicated 60%-width box.

**Change — `components/WinProbBadge.tsx`**: added a fourth variant, `variant="boxed"`, used only by this new right-hand box. Same fixed-white value, same "WIN PROB" label, same `winprob-pulse` micro-pulse-on-change animation as every other variant (color/pulse logic untouched) -- purely a new structural shape: fills its container edge-to-edge with its own `rounded-xl border border-line` + `#0B101C` background (matching `MatchupCard`'s own box styling) instead of floating as a smaller end-aligned translucent pill inside a shared row. `variant="compact"` (the old shared-row shape) is now unused anywhere in the codebase but left in place rather than deleted, in case a future shared-row context needs it again.

**Verified — real `npx tsx` + `react-dom/server` render against the live fixture**, not a description:
