- `formatPlayerName('R Sharma')` → `"R Sharma"`, `formatPlayerName('V Kohli')` → `"V Kohli"` (idempotent on already-short mock ball-data strings), `formatPlayerName('Joe Root')` → `"J Root"`, `formatPlayerName('Kuldeep Yadav')` → `"K Yadav"` (correctly derives from a full raw name) -- confirms the exact "Initial Surname" format requested, no double-abbreviation.
- Rendered `MatchupCard` for `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` with a real win-prob point: markup shows exactly two sibling `rounded-xl border border-line` boxes -- left box `w-[60%]` containing `"V Kohli vs P Cummins"` untruncated, right box containing `"Win Prob"` label + `"IND 95%"` value on its own line, exactly as specified.
- Rendered again with empty `winProbPoints` (`getLeadingTeamWinProb` returns `null`): the win-prob box is absent entirely (matches the pre-existing "hidden rather than broken" behavior) and the matchup box correctly falls back to `flex-1` (full width) rather than leaving a hole.
- Code-level trace confirms `WinProbBadge`'s new invocation sits structurally outside the `expanded`-gated `matchupBox` ternary — the only two things that can change the win-prob box are `winProb` (real data changes) and `onExpandWinProb`, never `expanded`.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `components/MatchupCard.tsx` (this is the only call site of `MatchupCard` in the app, confirmed via grep, so this single fix covers every place this row appears -- MatchView.tsx's live ball-by-ball view; no separate fallback-view or Digest-tab instance of this exact row exists). `components/WinProbBadge.tsx` (new `variant="boxed"`, additive only -- `variant="compact"`/`"large"` unchanged). No changes to `lib/mockMatchups.ts`, `lib/winProb.ts`, or `lib/playerName.ts` -- all underlying data/derivation logic is untouched, per the request.

## Equal 50/50 matchup/win-prob box widths + graceful long-name wrap — v1.0.137 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: follow-up to v1.0.136's box split. Two asks: (1) the two boxes were an uneven 60/40 split, not the requested clean 50/50; (2) full "Initial Surname" pairings (e.g. "R van der Dussen vs J Fraser-McGurk") have noticeably less room at 50% than they did at 60%, and the collapsed teaser's name spans were still using ellipsis-`truncate` from before the split, which would silently clip a real player's name.

**Change 1 — equal width (`components/MatchupCard.tsx`)**: both boxes are now `flex-1` (equal flex-grow, equal 0% basis) instead of the matchup box being a fixed `w-[60%]`. With exactly two flex children this always resolves to a clean 50/50 regardless of either side's content -- width is never computed from content on either side, so a long name pairing can't itself widen the matchup box at the win-prob box's expense. When there's no win-prob data at all, the win-prob box doesn't render and the lone `flex-1` matchup box naturally grows to fill the full row (verified below) -- no dangling gap, same fallback behavior as before, just no longer needing a separate width special-case to get there.

**Change 2 — graceful long-name handling (collapsed teaser only)**: removed `truncate` from the two name spans and changed the teaser `<button>`'s layout from `flex items-center` to `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-1.5 gap-y-1`. The team-color dots, "vs" label, and chevron stay `shrink-0`; the two name spans are the only wrap-eligible content. Chose wrap-to-a-second-line over the other two options offered (ellipsis truncation, font-size shrinking) because it's the only one that never hides part of a real player's name -- truncating or shrinking a name to fit a box reads as a data-display bug in a stats app, not a cosmetic tradeoff. This also keeps the box's height completely stable for the common case: most real pairings (e.g. "V Kohli vs P Cummins") are short enough to stay on one line exactly as before; `flex-wrap` only pushes content onto a second line for the rare long-name outlier, growing the box by exactly one text line rather than reserving extra height up front for every match regardless of its names. The expanded H2H header's own name row (which shares its line with the format badge, share button, and collapse chevron) is intentionally left unchanged -- out of scope for this request, and a much tighter, more crowded line where the same wrap treatment would risk pushing those other controls around; nothing in the request flagged that row as a problem.

**Verified — real `npx tsx` + `react-dom/server` renders**, not descriptions:
- Short-name case (`V Kohli` vs `P Cummins`): both boxes render with class `flex-1`; teaser shows both full names on the expected single-line markup; win-prob box shows `"Win Prob"` / `"IND 95%"` unchanged.
- Long-name case (`R van der Dussen` vs `J Fraser-McGurk`, formatPlayerName preserves both unabbreviated since neither matches the PLAYERS registry's short-form fields): both boxes still render `flex-1`; markup contains both full, uncut names (`html.includes(...)` true for each); the teaser button carries `flex-wrap` so the browser wraps them onto a second line only if the rendered width actually requires it; win-prob box's own markup is byte-for-byte identical in structure/content to the short-name case -- proving the long pairing has zero effect on it.
- No-win-prob-data case: win-prob box entirely absent, matchup box (still just `flex-1`, no special-casing) correctly fills the full row alone.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `components/MatchupCard.tsx` only. No changes to `components/WinProbBadge.tsx`, `lib/playerName.ts`, `lib/mockMatchups.ts`, or `lib/winProb.ts` -- purely a layout/CSS adjustment on top of v1.0.136's structural split, no data or derivation logic touched.

## Fixed real ~57/43 width skew: min-w-0 required alongside flex-1 — v1.0.138 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: v1.0.137 set both boxes to `flex-1` (`flex: 1 1 0%`), which is the textbook CSS pattern for two equal-width flex siblings -- but the user measured the actual deployed page at roughly 57/43, not 50/50. This is a well-known flexbox gotcha, not a Tailwind bug: `flex-basis: 0%` only sets the STARTING point before growth/shrink is distributed; browsers separately compute each flex item's `min-width: auto` default as its own min-content size (essentially, the width of its widest unbreakable run of text), and an item is never shrunk below that floor no matter what its `flex-basis`/`flex-grow` say. The matchup box's pairing text has a wider min-content floor than the win-prob box's short "TEAM XX%" text, so the browser was quietly giving the matchup box extra width to satisfy that floor before splitting the remainder -- exactly the "content influences outer width" failure mode the request explicitly said to avoid, just via a CSS mechanism that isn't visible by reading the `flex-1` class alone.

**Fix — `components/MatchupCard.tsx`**: added `min-w-0` to both box wrapper `<div>`s (`... flex-1 min-w-0` and `flex-1 min-w-0`). This is the standard, correct fix for this exact flexbox behavior -- it overrides the default `min-width: auto` with an explicit `0`, so `flex: 1 1 0%` is the ONLY thing left deciding each box's width. Neither box's content can floor its width above the 50% mark anymore, on either side, regardless of player name length or win-prob text length. Overflow is now handled entirely INSIDE the now-genuinely-constrained matchup box by the `flex-wrap` teaser layout already shipped in v1.0.137 (long pairings wrap to a second line rather than pushing the box wider) -- no change needed to that mechanism, it just wasn't actually being tested at a true 50% width until this fix.

**Defense-in-depth — `components/WinProbBadge.tsx`**: added `truncate max-w-full` to both lines of the `"boxed"` variant as a safety net. This box's width is now fixed purely by its flex-parent's `flex-1 min-w-0`, so nothing inside it should ever need to influence that width -- but if an unusually long team label ever did overflow, it now clips inside the box instead of being able to push the box wider (the same principle applied to `MatchupCard.tsx`'s own boxes, extended to this shared component for consistency).

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. A real browser computed-style check (not a visual read) against the deployed page, across at least two different live matches with different-length player-name pairings, is recorded in this same session's chat report to the user, since that's a runtime DOM measurement rather than something `npx tsx` can reproduce headlessly.

**Scope**: `components/MatchupCard.tsx` (two `min-w-0` additions, comment updates only otherwise) and `components/WinProbBadge.tsx` (`truncate max-w-full` on the `"boxed"` variant's two spans only -- `"compact"`/`"large"` variants untouched). No data/derivation logic touched.

## Fixed 60/40 grid split (was 50/50) + matched box heights via align-items:stretch — v1.0.139 (2026-07-29)

**Context**: two follow-up asks. (1) Change the ratio from equal 50/50 to a fixed 60/40 (matchup box larger). (2) The two boxes measured different heights (win-prob ~54px vs matchup ~29.5-31px) because `items-start` (deliberately set in a previous round to keep the win-prob box's size independent of the matchup box's expand/collapse state) let each box size purely to its own content -- this round explicitly asks for the opposite: both boxes should always match height, whichever is taller.

**Change 1 — 60/40 via CSS Grid, not flex percentages (`components/MatchupCard.tsx`)**: replaced the `flex-1`/`min-w-0` row with `<div className="grid gap-2 items-stretch" style={{ gridTemplateColumns: "60% 40%" }}>`. A `flex: 0 0 60%` / `flex: 0 0 40%` pair (the literal example given) was considered and rejected: zero-grow/zero-shrink flex-basis percentages are resolved against the FULL container width, so `60% + 40% + gap` always overflows the row by exactly the gap's width -- there's no room left for `flex-shrink` to absorb it since shrink is 0. CSS Grid's `gap` is accounted for natively in track sizing, so `gridTemplateColumns: "60% 40%"` plus `gap-2` always sums to exactly the container width, at any row width, with no overflow. `min-w-0` is kept on both grid item wrapper divs for the same underlying reason it was needed for the flex version in v1.0.138: grid items also default to `min-width: auto`, so without it a long enough pairing could still floor its column above 60% via the exact same content-based-floor mechanism.

**Change 2 — matched heights via `items-stretch` (`components/MatchupCard.tsx`)**: the row's `items-start` (from v1.0.136/137, explicitly chosen at the time to keep the win-prob box's size independent of the matchup box's own state) is now `items-stretch`. This is a deliberate reversal of that earlier decision, made because this round's request explicitly asks for matched heights instead of independent ones -- both boxes now size to whichever is taller, driven by content, on every render. Width independence from content (the min-w-0 fix) is unrelated and unaffected by this -- only height synchronization changed.

For the matched height to actually show (rather than just the outer bordered box growing while its content sits top-aligned inside empty space), the collapsed teaser `<button>` gained `h-full` (it previously had `w-full` only, no height rule) and `justify-center` alongside its existing `items-center`, so a short one-line pairing centers -- both vertically and horizontally -- within whatever taller height the row now assigns it. This also makes it visually consistent with the win-prob box's own `"boxed"` variant, which already centers its two-line content both ways.

**Verified — real browser `getBoundingClientRect()`**, reported to the user in this session's chat rather than duplicated here, since it's a runtime DOM measurement across two different live matches (short names and a long, wrapping pairing): both cases show a consistent 60/40 width ratio and identical heights between the two boxes.

**Scope**: `components/MatchupCard.tsx` only -- the row's className/style, the two grid item wrapper divs, and the collapsed teaser button's className. No changes to `components/WinProbBadge.tsx` (its `"boxed"` variant's own `h-full`/centering was already correct from v1.0.136/138), and no data/derivation logic touched.

## Fixed shot-direction line visibility on overhead pitch map — v1.0.140 (2026-07-31)

**Context**: the overhead pitch-map clip's shot-direction line (`components/BallGIF.tsx`, `OverheadView`) existed but was reported too thin and low-opacity to read at normal viewing size on both a six and a four -- needing a 2-3x zoom on screenshots to see it clearly.

**Step 1 findings (before any change)**: the line's exact prior values were `strokeWidth={isSix||isFour?"2.2":"1.4"}`, `opacity="0.75"`, `strokeDasharray={isAerial?"0":"4 4"}`. Color was already outcome-driven, not a separate hardcoded neutral: `stroke={isSix?"#A855F7":isFour?"#00E5FF":"#94A3B8"}` -- this already matched the six (`#A855F7`) and four (`#00E5FF`) colors used by this same component's own `OutcomeBadge`. There was, however, no wicket branch at all -- a wicket ball fell through to the same `"#94A3B8"` gray as a dot/single, silently mismatching `OutcomeBadge`'s wicket color (`#EF4444`). Separately, this local four-color (`#00E5FF`) does not match `lib/outcomeColors.ts`'s `OUTCOME.four.primary` (`#06B6D4`) -- a pre-existing discrepancy between two independent "four" color definitions in the codebase. `OutcomeBadge` was treated as ground truth for this fix since it's the actual badge rendered in this same view, not the unused `outcomeColors.ts` map.

**Step 2 fix**: `strokeWidth` unified to `"2.5"` for all outcomes (previously varied by outcome, all now increased), `opacity` to `"0.9"`, `strokeDasharray` scaled from `"4 4"` to `"6 4"` to stay visually proportionate to the thicker line (aerial shots remain solid, `"0"`, unchanged). Added the missing `ball.isWicket` branch to the color ternary using `"#EF4444"`, matching `OutcomeBadge` exactly. Added a new `<circle r="4">` endpoint marker at `(shotEndX, shotEndY)`, filled with the same outcome color as the line, gated by the same `!wasLeft` condition so it never renders where the line doesn't.

**Step 3 — no-shot gating**: the existing `wasLeft` gate (`shotType==="left"` for a genuine leave, or `isDot && |pitchX|>0.6` as a wide-miss heuristic) was structurally correct for its two explicit cases but never checked whether `shotAngle` was actually present -- `ball.shotAngle??0` silently defaults to `0` and would still draw a line for a delivery with no recorded placement at all (e.g. a real wide/no-ball with no bat contact). Confirmed via `lib/mockData.ts` that every one of the 953 balls with an `inningsNumber` also has an explicit `shotAngle` (`grep -c` on both returns 953), so this gap is currently unexercised by any fixture -- it's a real-data-readiness gap, not a visible bug today. Hardened `wasLeft` to `ball.shotType==="left"||!hasShotData||(isDot&&Math.abs(ball.pitchX??0)>0.6)` where `hasShotData=ball.shotAngle!=null`, directly enforcing "a line only ever appears when there's an actual recorded shot direction." No behavior change against any current fixture.

**Step 4 — single shared component confirmed**: `components/MatchView.tsx` renders exactly one `<BallGIF>` call (line ~751), fed by `currentBall`, which resolves from either live auto-advance (`selectedBallId===null`) or a tapped Moments card (`handleMomentSelect` sets `selectedBallId`, flowing through `activeBallIdx`). Both the live ticker and the Moments replay view render through this same `OverheadView` function -- this fix applies to both without any duplicate edit.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `components/BallGIF.tsx`'s `OverheadView` function only -- the `wasLeft` derivation and the shot-line/endpoint-marker JSX. No changes to `MiniBallGIF.tsx` or `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, which have their own independent shot-line rendering for different contexts (small previews, shareable social cards) not covered by this request's four named views (live ticker, Moments replay).

## Removed redundant tour-name banner from ball visualizer — v1.0.141 (2026-08-01)

**Context**: the live/ball-visualizer view's `ContextHeader` row rendered "{competition.name} · {teamA.shortName} vs {teamB.shortName}" directly above the pitch map, occupying a full-width row for context already shown in the main score header at the top of the match page. Requested removal, applied consistently across every match page.

**Investigation**: `ContextHeader` also accepted `scoreText` and `situationText` props (rendering an optional score line and a right-aligned situation pill), but grepping `MatchView.tsx` confirmed its one real call site (`<BallGIF ball={currentBall} match={truncatedMatch} fielders={fielders} loopMs={GIF_LOOP_MS} partnership={...} onShare={...} />`, line ~751) never passes either prop -- both are always `undefined` there. So in practice, `ContextHeader` only ever rendered the tour-name/team-name line; the score-line and situation-pill code paths were unreachable dead code specific to this component (a *separate* component, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, independently handles the actual share-card captions that use similar text -- `ContextHeader` was never involved in that flow). Confirmed via `grep -rn "ContextHeader"` that this component had exactly one definition and one call site, both inside `components/BallGIF.tsx` itself.

**Fix — `components/BallGIF.tsx`**: deleted the `<ContextHeader .../>` render call, the `ContextHeader` function definition, and the now-unused `situationText`/`scoreText` fields from `BallGIFProps` (and their destructuring in the main `BallGIF` function) rather than leaving them declared-but-dead.

**Consistency**: since v1.0.140 already established that `OverheadView`/`BallGIF` is a single shared component used for both the live ticker and the Moments replay view (no duplicate code path per match), this one edit removes the banner everywhere it appeared, across every match page, with nothing else to touch.

**Series/tour name relocation**: checked `InfoTab.tsx` — it already displays `match.competition.shortName ?? match.competition.name` (line 168) and `match.competition.name` (line 217), so series context already exists on the Info tab. No new UI was added there since the value already had a home.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. `grep -rn "ContextHeader\|situationText\|scoreText" components/BallGIF.tsx` after the edit returns nothing, confirming complete removal with no dangling references.

**Scope**: `components/BallGIF.tsx` only -- the `ContextHeader` function, its one call site, and the two now-dead prop fields. No changes to `MatchView.tsx`, `InfoTab.tsx`, or `MomentStoryCard.tsx`.

## Confirmed ingestMatchFeed() field gap, hardened graceful degradation, documented forward plan — v1.0.142 (2026-08-01)

**Context**: two follow-up diagnostics after v1.0.141. First: confirm whether `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`'s `ingestMatchFeed()` already maps a real provider's delivery-type/speed fields into `ballVariation`/`ballSpeedKmh`, the same way it already handles retirement events. Second, contingent on that being a gap: (1) confirm/harden safe degradation everywhere these fields are consumed, and (2) document the forward plan in ARCHITECTURE.md — without building the real mapping yet, since no provider is chosen.

**Diagnostic 1 — confirmed gap**: `RawFeedDelivery` (the raw-feed shape) and `adaptInnings()`'s ball-mapping function together handle exactly `id/over/ballInOver/batterId/batterName/bowlerId/bowlerName/runs/extras/extraType/isWicket/dismissalType/isBoundary4/isBoundary6`. Every visualizer-input field on `Ball` — `pitchX/pitchY/heightAtBounce/ballSpeedKmh`, `shotAngle/shotPower/shotLoft/shotType/shotIsAerial`, `bowlingArm/bowlingFrom/bowlingLength/bowlingLine/heightAtBatter/ballVariation/swingDirection/spinDirection/pace` — is absent from both, confirmed via grep returning zero matches for any of these terms anywhere in `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`. Unlike retirement (which got a dedicated `RawFeedRetirementEvent` type and full extraction treatment), there is no analogous type or mapping step for any of these fields.

**Diagnostic 2 / Fix — audited every consumer of these fields for safe degradation**: read through `components/BallGIF.tsx`, `DeliveryCard.tsx`, `MiniBallGIF.tsx`, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`, and `CommentaryFeed.tsx` line by line for every reference to the fields above.

Found safe already: `BallGIF.tsx`'s `SpeedChip` (`if (!speed) return null`), `TypeChip`/`formatVariation` (falls to `"Stock"`), all of `BowlerView`/`OverheadView`'s coordinate math (`?? <default>` throughout), `OverheadView`'s `wasLeft` gate (already hardened in v1.0.140 with `shotAngle == null`), `DeliveryCard.tsx`'s `TypePill`/`formatVariation`/`typeStyle`, `MomentStoryCard.tsx`'s speed readout (`{ball.ballSpeedKmh && (...)}`) and its own `formatVariation`/`getVariationColor`, and `CommentaryFeed.tsx`'s `formatLength` (falls to `"tight"`) and `shotDirection` (`?? 0` then a categorical bucket function that always returns a valid string). `heightAtBounce`/`heightAtBatter` are declared on `Ball` but consumed nowhere in the app, so carry no risk either way.

Found and fixed two real gaps, both matching a pattern the two "found safe" comparisons made obvious:

1. **`DeliveryCard.tsx`'s `SpeedDot`** defaulted to `const speed = ball.ballSpeedKmh ?? 0;` and rendered unconditionally — a ball with no speed data displayed a literal, misleading "0 KMH", not nothing. This is worse than a crash or an "undefined" leak because it looks like real data. Fixed to `if (!speed) return null`, exactly mirroring `BallGIF.tsx`'s already-correct `SpeedChip`.
2. **`MiniBallGIF.tsx`'s `wasLeft`** and **`MomentStoryCard.tsx`'s `noShot`** — both shot-line no-draw gates — never checked for a missing `shotAngle`, the exact gap `BallGIF.tsx`'s `OverheadView` had before v1.0.140 hardened it. With `shotAngle` undefined, both would compute `shotAngle ?? 0` and draw a fabricated straight-up-the-ground shot line, rather than omitting it. Both gates now also include `ball.shotAngle == null` (renamed locally as `hasShotData`), matching the v1.0.140 fix's exact reasoning and wording. No behavior change against any current mock fixture — every ball in `lib/mockData.ts` already sets `shotAngle` — this only closes the gap for future real-feed data.

**Verified**: a real `react-dom/server` render (`npx tsx`, not visual review) with a `Ball` object built by stripping every field above (i.e. exactly what `ingestMatchFeed()` produces today), rendered through `BallGIF`, `DeliveryCard`, `MiniBallGIF`, and `MomentStoryCard` for a dot, a four, a six, and a wicket — 16 combinations, zero crashes, zero `undefined`/`NaN` substrings in any rendered HTML. Three targeted follow-up checks confirmed: `DeliveryCard`'s stripped-four render contains no `"kmh"` text at all; `MiniBallGIF`'s stripped-four render shows the existing `"LEFT"` fallback text instead of drawing a line; `MomentStoryCard`'s stripped-four render contains neither the boundary-four stroke color nor its strokeWidth that only appear on the real shot-line element, confirming it was skipped rather than drawn at a fabricated angle. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. Test script written to a temporary `scripts/` file and removed afterward, per project convention (never committed).

**Documented — `ARCHITECTURE.md`**: added a new section ("Forward-looking gap...") laying out, field by field, exactly what's missing (matching Diagnostic 1 above word for word), plus the five-part forward plan as given: (b) pull one real sample response from the chosen provider before writing any mapping code — `RawFeedMatch`'s current shape is explicitly a placeholder, not a real contract; (c) speed is expected to be a direct structured-field mapping, comparable in effort to the existing `runs`/`extras` mapping; (d) shot direction and delivery type will likely require a commentary-text-interpretation layer recognizing phrases in that provider's own commentary strings, which cannot be designed generically ahead of time since providers phrase things differently — it has to be built against a real sample from whichever provider is actually chosen; (e) the target is categorical/zone-based accuracy (the same bar `lib/mockData.ts`'s existing plausible-approximation values already sit at), not ball-tracking-level precision. Explicitly noted the graceful-degradation fix above is a stopgap for the gap between "no provider chosen" and "the real mapping is built," not the destination itself.

**Scope**: `components/DeliveryCard.tsx` (one function, `SpeedDot`), `components/MiniBallGIF.tsx` (one line, the `wasLeft` derivation), `components/MomentStoryCard.tsx` (one line, the `noShot` derivation), `ARCHITECTURE.md` (new section, no edits to existing sections). No changes to `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts` itself — the real mapping is explicitly deferred, per this round's instructions, until a provider is chosen.

## Batter sparkline: suppress ducks, scale width to balls faced — v1.0.143 (2026-08-01)

**Context**: two exact, non-negotiable fixes to `components/Scorecard.tsx`'s per-batter runs-vs-balls sparkline (the small curve on each batting row's dismissal/"not out" line), following the earlier sparkline diagnostic (see the "batter-sparkline/scorecard lookup" note above) that root-caused empty/flat sparklines as a mock-data sparsity issue, not a code bug. This round is a real behavior change, not a diagnostic.

**Fix 1 — suppress entirely for `runs === 0`**: `BatterSparkline` now takes a `runs` prop and returns `null` immediately if `runs === 0`, before the existing `points.length < 2` check. A golden duck (0 off 1) was already caught by that length check, but a duck off several balls (0 off 5, say) produces `points.length >= 2` (the seed point plus one per ball, every one sitting at y=0) and used to render a flat, pointless line — exactly the artifact the earlier sparkline diagnostic traced for T Head/S Smith, except that case was caused by data sparsity while THIS case is caused by a real batter genuinely scoring zero. Both now render nothing.

**Fix 2 — proportional width by balls faced, scoped per innings**: `InningsCard` now computes `maxBallsFacedInInnings = Math.max(1, ...innings.battingCard.filter(r => r.runs > 0).map(r => r.ballsFaced))` — the highest `ballsFaced` among that INNINGS' own qualifying (runs > 0) batters only, never compared against the other team's innings in the same match. This value is threaded down through `BatterRow` to `BatterSparkline`, which now renders its `<svg>` at an explicit `style={{ width: (ballsFaced / maxBallsFaced) * 130 + "px" }}` instead of the previous `flex-1 min-w-[36px] max-w-[130px]` Tailwind sizing. 130px was the sparkline's own prior effective ceiling (`max-w-[130px]`), kept as the literal "full available container width" the ratio multiplies against, so the innings' top-balls-faced batter renders at the same visual size as before and everyone else scales down linearly and directly — no floor (the old `min-w-[36px]` is gone), no sqrt/log/other transform. Only the container's width changes; the curve's own data points still come from the untouched `buildSparklinePoints`/`downsampleSparkline`/`smoothPath` pipeline and its own fixed `W=100/H=20` viewBox math.

**Reactivity, proven not assumed**: `maxBallsFacedInInnings` is computed inline in `InningsCard`'s render body — deliberately NOT wrapped in `useMemo`, NOT cached in a ref or module variable. It recomputes from that innings' current `battingCard` on every render, so in a live match (where `MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` is itself recomputed via `useMemo` on every `liveBallIdx` tick — see v1.0.132's fix to that recomputation) every batter's width rescales as soon as ANY batter in that innings faces another ball, not just the one who got the new ball. Verified with a real `react-dom/server` harness (`npx tsx`, not visual review): rendered a two-batter innings, recorded both sparkline widths (130px, 104px), then re-rendered with a brand-new `match`/`innings` object reflecting one more ball faced by the first batter only, and confirmed the SECOND batter's width recalculated down (104px → 94.5px) purely because the innings-wide max shifted — proof this isn't cached from first render, the same failure class as the Digest narrative cache and the accent-color remount bugs referenced in the fix's own code comments.

**Edge cases verified with the same harness**: (1) duck suppression — a golden duck (0 off 1) and a duck off 8 balls both produced zero sparkline `<svg>` elements, alongside a real scoring batter who did get one; (2) proportional width — three batters at 40/20/10 balls faced (with the 10-ball batter having runs > 0) produced widths of exactly 130px/65px/32.5px, matching `(ballsFaced/40)*130` for each; (3) single-qualifying-batter edge case — one batter with `runs > 0`, `ballsFaced = 4` and nobody else on the card produced `maxBallsFacedInInnings = 4`, ratio `4/4 = 1`, width exactly 130px, no NaN/Infinity/divide-by-zero.

**Real-data-readiness**: the entire fix reads only `row.runs` and `row.ballsFaced` — both plain `number` fields on `BattingEntry` (never optional, see `lib/types.ts`), populated identically whether the row comes from `lib/mockData.ts` or a real feed via `ingestMatchFeed()`. Nothing here assumes a fixed array length, a specific ball count, or any other mock-data-specific shape.

**Scope of the fix**: `BatterSparkline` (the runs-vs-balls curve) is defined and used in exactly one place — `components/Scorecard.tsx`, three call sites inside `BatterRow` (out/dismissed, retired-not-out, currently batting), all feeding the Score tab's batting card, the only place this component renders anywhere in the app. `components/RecentFormGraph.tsx` (used on the player profile page) is a deliberately separate component with a different X-axis (a fixed match/spell index, not per-innings cumulative balls faced) — see that file's own header comment from v1.0.119 explaining why it duplicates rather than imports `BatterSparkline`'s smoothing logic; it is out of scope for this fix and untouched. No Digest surface renders this sparkline at all.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. Edge-case script written to a temporary `scripts/` file and removed afterward, per project convention (never committed).

## Fixed batter-sparkline lookup to merge split batter-identifier keys instead of falling back — v1.0.144 (2026-08-01)

**Context**: confirmed code bug from the prior round's diagnostic. `components/Scorecard.tsx`'s `ballsByBatter` map registers every ball under both its `batterId` and `batterName` keys (to handle either naming convention a match might use), but the row lookup — `ballsByBatter.get(row.playerId) ?? ballsByBatter.get(row.playerName)` — only ever returned ONE side. When a single batter's balls were split across both keys within the same innings (some tagged with their slug id, the rest with their full display name — confirmed in `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s ENG Innings 1), the `??` returned whichever key existed first and silently discarded the other, even when it held far more real data.

**Fix — `components/Scorecard.tsx`**: replaced the `??` fallback with a new `getBatterBalls(map, playerId, playerName)` function that looks up both keys and, whenever both exist, MERGES them — deduplicated by `Ball.id` (defensive: `registerBall` already dedupes within a single key, but this guards a future data shape where the same ball could reach both), then re-sorted by `over`/`ballInOver` ascending, since balls from two independently-populated keys are not guaranteed to already be in relative chronological order and `buildSparklinePoints`'s cumulative curve requires strict chronological input. When only one key holds data (the common case, and the only case in every OTHER match/innings checked), the function returns that key's array directly — no behavior change for correctly-tagged data. The fix does not depend on the underlying mock data ever being tagged consistently: it merges unconditionally whenever both keys are present, for any batter, in any match, so it is equally correct against a real feed that tagged some events by internal ID and others by name.

**Anti-pattern search**: grepped the full codebase for any other `map.get(id) ?? map.get(name)`-style dual-key fallback against a player-identity map. Found none — every other `.get(...) ?? ...` site in the codebase (`lib/events.ts`, `components/MatchView.tsx`, `components/DigestTab.tsx`, `lib/mockMatchups.ts`, `lib/transformers.ts`) is a single-key lookup with a plain default-value fallback (`?? 0`, `?? {}`, `?? null`, `?? "Unknown"`), not a second alternate-key lookup that could silently drop real data registered under a different key. `Scorecard.tsx`'s `ballsByBatter` lookup was the only instance of this specific anti-pattern.

**Verified** with a real script (`npx tsx`, temporary, not committed) run directly against `lib/mockData.ts`'s actual `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` fixture (not a synthetic construction): comparing the OLD `get(id) ?? get(name)` result against the NEW merged result for every batter in ENG Innings 1 —

| Batter | Official ballsFaced | OLD lookup count | NEW merged count |
|---|---|---|---|
| H Brook | 41 | 4 | 40 |
| B Stokes | 38 | 2 | 35 |
| J Bairstow | 15 | 3 | 21 |
| B Duckett | 24 | 51 | 66 |
| Z Crawley, J Root, M Livingstone, C Woakes | — | unchanged | unchanged |

B Duckett was an additional, previously-unreported case of the same split-tagging bug in this innings, also fixed by the same merge (his `??` lookup happened to resolve to the LARGER of his two keys, 51 of 66, so the symptom was less visible, but he was still losing 15 real balls). Every merged array's chronological order was confirmed strictly ascending by `over`/`ballInOver` for every batter. The four unaffected batters' counts are byte-identical before and after, confirming no regression for already-correctly-tagged data.

Re-ran the v1.0.143 proportional-width verification against the same fixture post-fix: widths are pixel-identical to before this change (Z Crawley 71.96px, B Duckett 27.86px, J Root 130px [max, 112 balls], H Brook 47.59px, B Stokes 44.11px, J Bairstow 17.41px) — confirming the width calculation (driven by `row.ballsFaced`, the official box-score column) is fully independent of the sparkline's own point count (driven by however many raw `Ball` objects the merge resolves), exactly as designed.

`tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `components/Scorecard.tsx` only — the `ballsByBatter` lookup and its one call site. No other files needed changes; the anti-pattern search found no other instances to fix.

## Replaced linear sparkline width scale with square-root scale — v1.0.145 (2026-08-03)

**Context**: confirmed live on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` (ENG Innings 2) that the v1.0.143 linear width formula (`ballsFaced / maxBallsFaced`) compresses every batter's sparkline toward illegibility whenever one batter's innings runs substantially longer than the rest -- a normal, common occurrence in real cricket, not a data artifact. With J Root on a 72-ball knock, Z Crawley (10 balls), B Stokes (16), and J Bairstow (15) rendered at 14%/22%/21% width respectively -- each a real, legitimate partial innings reduced to a barely-visible tick mark.

**Fix — `components/Scorecard.tsx`**: `BatterSparkline`'s `widthPx` calculation changed from `(ballsFaced / maxBallsFaced) * FULL_WIDTH_PX` to `(Math.sqrt(ballsFaced) / Math.sqrt(maxBallsFaced)) * FULL_WIDTH_PX` — square root specifically, no other exponent or transform. The top-balls-faced batter still renders at exactly 100% (`sqrt(max)/sqrt(max) = 1`), and every other batter compresses less aggressively than the straight ratio, since `sqrt` shrinks the *relative* gap between a large and a small `ballsFaced` value. This is a pure supersession of the v1.0.143 linear formula, not an addition alongside it — the old formula no longer exists anywhere in the code.

**Everything else explicitly unchanged, confirmed by inspection and test**: duck/golden-duck suppression (`if (runs === 0) return null`) untouched; `maxBallsFacedInInnings`'s per-innings-only scoping and its `Math.max(1, ...)` divide-by-zero guard in `InningsCard` untouched; the deliberately-uncached, recompute-on-every-render placement of that value untouched; the curve/point-drawing pipeline (`buildSparklinePoints`/`downsampleSparkline`/`smoothPath`/the fixed `W=100/H=20` viewBox math) completely untouched — only the container `<svg>`'s `style.width` line changed.

**Verified** with a real `react-dom/server` script (`npx tsx`, temporary, not committed) against the actual live `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` fixture (ENG Innings 2, `maxBallsFacedInInnings = 52` at time of test, via J Root):

| Batter | ballsFaced | `√ballsFaced ÷ √52 × 130` | Rendered width |
|---|---|---|---|
| Z Crawley | 18 | 76.4853px | 76.48529270389176px |
| B Duckett | 44 | 119.5826px | 119.58260743101398px |
| J Root | 52 (max) | 130.0000px | 130px |
| H Brook | 16 | 72.1110px | 72.11102550927978px |
| B Stokes | 12 | 62.4500px | 62.44997998398399px |
| J Bairstow | 9 | 54.0833px | 54.083269131959845px |

Every value matches to well beyond two decimal places. Also verified: (1) a constructed single-qualifying-batter innings (`ballsFaced = maxBallsFaced = 7`) rendered exactly 130px, no NaN/Infinity; (2) a two-batter reactivity check — P2 (8 balls) rendered at 116.2755px when P1 (a different batter) had faced 10 balls, then recalculated to 110.8644px purely because P1 faced one more ball (11) on a fresh render with a new `match`/`innings` object, with no change to P2's own data — confirming the max-balls-faced value and every batter's width still recompute live, not once. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `BatterSparkline` is defined and used in exactly one place, `components/Scorecard.tsx` (three call sites inside `BatterRow`, Score tab only) — same single location confirmed in the v1.0.143/v1.0.144 rounds. No other file needed changes.

## Real-feed adapter now derives battingCard/bowlingCard for any innings, not just live mid-innings — v1.0.146 (2026-08-03)

**Context**: a diagnostic audit of the Digest tab's real-data-readiness (four-part, chat-only, no code changed at the time) surfaced a confirmed gap: `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`'s `adaptInnings()` always returned `battingCard: []`/`bowlingCard: []` for a real ingested feed, deferred as "a separate concern, out of scope here." The only place that ever derived those fields from `balls` was `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch`, and only for a mid-innings, still-live innings — a COMPLETE innings (any finished match, or any already-finished innings within a still-live match) was spread through unchanged there, trusting `inn.battingCard` was already correct. For a real feed, that trust was misplaced: `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` would stay empty forever for any completed innings, with two silent symptoms — the Score tab's batting/bowling table would render empty for a finished real match, and the Digest tab's post-match "Performance" card (`buildPerformanceCard`/`computeMatchTopPerformers` in `components/DigestTab.tsx`) would always return `null` (no crash, since it's guarded, but no content either), along with the lead-in narrative's top-batter/bowler lines.

**Fix — single shared derivation, two callers, not two implementations**: `lib/matchStatus.ts`'s `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` — previously requiring a non-empty `originalCard` to supply player identity, since they only ever recomputed each existing entry's mutable stats from `balls` — now have `originalCard` as an OPTIONAL parameter (`= []`). When empty, two new helpers (`deriveBatterIdentitiesFromBalls`/`deriveBowlerIdentitiesFromBalls`) build the identity list directly from `balls` itself (one entry per distinct `batterName`/`bowlerName`, in order of first appearance, every stat field zeroed) — then the exact same existing per-player math runs unchanged on top of that synthesized base. `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`'s `ingestMatchFeed()` now calls these two functions (with no `originalCard`, since a real feed never has one) in the same `.map()` step that already computes `overs` from `raw.format` — `adaptInnings()` itself still can't do this (no match-level `format` at that per-innings scope, same reason `overs` was already deferred to the caller). `components/MatchView.tsx`'s live mid-innings branch needed NO code change at all — it already called these same two functions with a real `originalCard`, and `originalCard.length > 0` takes the untouched original branch, so its behavior is provably byte-identical to before.

**Also fixed — the smaller, related gap**: `RawFeedMatch` had no `result` field at all, so `manOfMatch`/`seriesStatus`/`excitement`/`highlightBadge`/`declared`/`follow_on` were never mapped. Added a new provisional `RawFeedResult` interface (mirrors `Match["result"]`'s own shape) plus `result?`/`series_status?`/`excitement?`/`highlight_badge?` on `RawFeedMatch` and `declared?`/`follow_on?` on `RawFeedInnings`, all explicitly labeled provisional (same "best-informed assumption, no live provider connected yet" convention as every other raw shape in this file) and wired into `ingestMatchFeed()`/`adaptInnings()`.

**Verified** with real `npx tsx` scripts (temporary, not committed) built directly against `ingestMatchFeed()`, not synthetic mock-data shortcuts:
- A constructed raw feed for a small finished T20 (3 batters, 2 bowlers, one wicket, boundaries) produced a `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` with correct `runs`/`ballsFaced`/`fours`/`sixes`/`strikeRate`/`out`/`dismissal` and `oversBowled`/`maidens`/`runsConceded`/`wickets`/`economy` for every player — matching hand-computed arithmetic exactly (e.g. A Batter 14 runs off 7 balls, strike rate 200; B Bowler 1/11 from 6 legal balls).
- `result.manOfMatch`/`seriesStatus`/`excitement` all mapped through correctly onto the ingested `Match` object.
- Feeding that same ingested match into `buildPostMatchDigest()` (`components/DigestTab.tsx`) produced a real `performance` card (topBat "A Batter", topBowl "F Bowler" — previously would have been omitted, `computeMatchTopPerformers` returning `{null, null}`) and a `match-summary` card with `manOfMatch: "A Batter"` and a non-empty top-batter/bowler narrative — confirmed only after correcting an initially-unrealistic test fixture where the 2nd innings' ball data didn't itself support the claimed `result`, which `lib/matchStatus.ts`'s `observableStateSupportsConclusion()` correctly refused to trust (a deliberate defense-in-depth check, unrelated to this fix, working exactly as designed).
- Score tab (`components/Scorecard.tsx`)'s `BatterRow`/`BowlerRow` were confirmed by inspection to read only fields present on the derived `BattingEntry`/`BowlingEntry` shape — `playerId`, `playerName`, `runs`, `ballsFaced`, `out`, `dismissal`, `onStrike`, `oversBowled`, `maidens`, `runsConceded`, `wickets`, `economy` — all populated.
- Live mid-innings regression: ran `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` against a real mock fixture (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, ENG Innings 1) with its real, non-empty `originalCard` — identity-list length matched the original card exactly (8 batters, 5 bowlers) and derived mid-innings stats were sane, confirming zero behavior change for the existing live-truncation path.
- All five pre-existing regression scripts (`edge-case-check.ts`, `digest-check.ts`, `series-category-check.ts`, `version-check.ts`) passed unchanged. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean (106/106 static pages).

**Scope**: `lib/matchStatus.ts` (two functions extended, two new private helpers added), `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts` (`adaptInnings()`'s return shape, `ingestMatchFeed()`'s innings-mapping step, new `RawFeedResult` interface + fields), `components/MatchView.tsx` (comment-only clarification, no logic change). No mock data, no other component, touched.

## Applied the same battingCard/bowlingCard-from-balls fix to the dormant ESPN/SportRadar scaffold transformers — v1.0.147 (2026-08-03)

**Context**: while doing final doc-consistency checks after v1.0.146, a grep for the old `battingCard: []`/`bowlingCard: []` pattern turned up the same shape of gap in `lib/transformers.ts` — a separate, currently-unused scaffold file of per-provider transform functions ("fill in the implementation when you get real API access," zero call sites anywhere in the app) for ESPN, SportRadar, and Cricbuzz. Investigated all three before touching anything, since not all three actually have the same bug:

- **`transformCricbuzzMatch`** — genuinely has nothing to fix. Its `innings` array is built as an empty shell (`const innings: Innings[] = []`) with an explicit, correct TODO to merge in `transformCricbuzzScorecard()`'s output separately — there is no ball data in scope at this call to derive anything from. `transformCricbuzzScorecard()` itself already correctly builds `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` directly from real Cricbuzz scorecard-endpoint fields (not balls) — a legitimately different, already-correct data source. No change made.
- **`transformESPNMatch`** and **`transformSportRadarTimeline`** — both DID have the bug: both already build a real per-innings ball array (`transformESPNBall`/`normalizeBall` calls happen right there in the same function) but left `battingCard: []`/`bowlingCard: []` with a "TODO: fetch/populate from scorecard endpoint" comment anyway, never deriving from the ball data already sitting right next to that line. Same root cause as v1.0.146's `ingestMatchFeed()` gap, just in a different file.

**Fix**: both functions now call `lib/matchStatus.ts`'s `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` (the same shared functions v1.0.146 generalized) on their own already-computed ball array, no `originalCard`. `format` was hoisted to a local `const` before the innings map in both functions (previously inlined directly in the return object, after innings were already built) so `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` has it in scope. Both functions' doc comments now explicitly note this is a ball-derived fallback, not a replacement for a real scorecard-endpoint merge — `transformESPNScorecard()`/`transformSportRadarScorecard()` still exist and should be preferred/merged over this whenever that endpoint is actually available, since a provider's official scorecard figures may not always reconcile 1:1 with a possibly-incomplete ball timeline (see BUILD-STATUS.md's existing ball-vs-card reconciliation caveat for the analogous mock-data case).

**Verified** with two real `npx tsx` scripts (temporary, not committed): a constructed ESPN raw match (2 batters, 1 bowler, one wicket, a four and a six) produced correct `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` via `transformESPNMatch` (A Batter 14 runs/3 balls/2 fours/1 six; C Batter out; B Bowler 1/14). A constructed SportRadar timeline produced correct batting/bowling runs and boundary counts via `transformSportRadarTimeline` (A Batter 10 runs/1 four/1 six; C Batter 4 runs/1 four; B Bowler conceded 14).

**Incidental finding, NOT fixed (out of scope for this round)**: building the SportRadar test surfaced a separate, pre-existing bug in the same dormant function — `transformSportRadarTimeline`'s own `const deliveries = timeline.filter(e => e.type === "delivery")` filters out anything but `"delivery"`-typed timeline events BEFORE the ball-mapping step's `isWicket: b.type === "wicket"` check ever runs on them, meaning `isWicket` can never evaluate true today for any SportRadar-sourced ball — a genuinely separate concern from the battingCard/bowlingCard gap this round fixes, in a still-unused file with no provider chosen. Flagged here rather than silently fixed alongside an unrelated, unapproved change; worth a dedicated look whenever SportRadar is actually considered as a provider.

**Scope**: `lib/transformers.ts` only (new import of `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`, `transformESPNMatch`, `transformSportRadarTimeline`). `transformCricbuzzMatch`/`transformCricbuzzScorecard` confirmed correct as-is, untouched. All four pre-existing regression scripts, `tsc --noEmit`, and `npm run build` clean.

## Three original punch-list cleanup items: aria-live on ball outcomes, branded match-404, dead-component removal — v1.0.148 (2026-08-03)

**Context**: a diagnostic-only pass had confirmed all three of these were still open from the project's original "bite-sized starter items" list — none had ever been implemented. This round fixes all three.

**1. `aria-live` on ball-outcome updates (`components/BallGIF.tsx`)**: the ball visualizer had no live-region announcement anywhere — confirmed via repo-wide search, `aria-live` appeared nowhere in the app's code before this change. Added a persistent, visually-hidden (`sr-only`) `<div aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">` inside `BallGIF`'s stable outer wrapper — deliberately NOT the inner `key={`${activeClip}-${ball.id}`}`-driven scene `<div>`, since a screen reader needs the announcing node itself to persist across renders (only its text content should change) to reliably pick up each update. `"polite"`, not `"assertive"`, since ball updates are frequent and `"assertive"` would interrupt whatever a screen-reader user is currently reading on every single ball — worse than saying nothing. The announced text is a real, specific outcome sentence, not a generic "content updated" — a new `ballAnnouncement(ball)` helper builds e.g. `"Four, R Sharma to V Kohli"` by reusing `outcomeKindOf()` (already imported, the same classification driving the existing visual `OutcomeBadge`/background) through a new `OUTCOME_WORD` lookup (`wicket`→"Wicket", `dot`→"Dot ball", `single`→"1 run", `two`→"2 runs", `three`→"3 runs", `four`→"Four", `six`→"Six", `extra`→"Extra"), and `formatPlayerName()` (already imported, the app's single sanctioned name formatter) for both bowler and batter. `OutcomeKind` was added as a type-only import from `@/lib/outcomeColors` to support the lookup map's type annotation.

**2. Branded 404 for a missing/synthetic match (`app/match/[id]/not-found.tsx`, new file)**: navigating to a non-existent match id previously fell through to Next.js's own generic, unbranded default 404 (confirmed live: plain "404: This page could not be found"), since `app/match/[id]/page.tsx`'s `notFound()` call had no `not-found.tsx` boundary anywhere in the tree to catch it. Added one scoped to the `[id]` segment specifically, matching the app's existing dark-theme tokens (`bg-bg`/`text-text-primary`/`text-text-secondary`, `bg-cyan`/`#FF6B35` accents) — a friendly, Bawler-voiced message ("This match doesn't exist") plus an explicit primary CTA button ("Back to matches", full pill, `bg-cyan text-bg`, same visual weight as the app's other primary actions like FollowSheet's "Update" button) linking to `/`. The CTA is deliberately not a reliance on the persistent `BottomNav` — it's front and center on the page itself, since a user shouldn't need to notice the nav bar to find a way back.

**3. Removed seven confirmed-dead legacy components + the orphaned `lib/metrics.ts`**: `components/ViewSwitcher.tsx`, `components/PressureGauge.tsx`, `components/DemoControls.tsx`, `components/MomentsCollapsible.tsx`, `components/ProjectedScore.tsx`, `components/AIMetrics.tsx`, `components/MiniWinProb.tsx`, `lib/metrics.ts`. A fresh grep immediately before deletion (re-run rather than trusting the earlier diagnostic audit alone) confirmed zero real import/usage of any of the eight — the only non-empty hits were the same coincidental name collisions already identified in the diagnostic pass: `calculatePressureGauge()`/`PressureGauge` interface in `lib/winProb.ts`/`lib/types.ts` (unrelated to the dead `PressureGauge.tsx` component), `calculateProjectedScore()`/`ProjectedScore` interface (unrelated to the dead `ProjectedScore.tsx` component, and actively used by `MatchCard.tsx`/`ScoreBar.tsx`), `computeAIMetrics()` inside `lib/metrics.ts` itself (part of the file being deleted, not an external usage), and a styling-convention code comment in `MatchCard.tsx` mentioning `MiniWinProb` by name (not an import). `InsightsPanel`, named alongside these in BUILD-STATUS.md's pending checklist, was explicitly out of scope for this round and left untouched.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean after all three changes together (106/106 static pages). A post-deletion grep across `app/`, `components/`, `lib/` for all eight deleted names/paths found zero remaining references anywhere outside the same pre-existing, unrelated collisions listed above.

**Scope**: `components/BallGIF.tsx` (aria-live region + helper), `app/match/[id]/not-found.tsx` (new file), eight files deleted (seven components + `lib/metrics.ts`). No other file changed.

## Spotlight 7-day recency window + "for you" reason line — v1.0.149 (2026-08-03)

**1. Spotlight past-match expiry**: `isSpotlightMatch()` in `lib/spotlight.ts` had no time-based cutoff — a past match clearing `hasCloseFinish()`/`hasMilestone()`/`hasContextStakes()` stayed eligible forever, only pushed out by the `SPOTLIGHT_MAX = 3` cap when other matches outranked it in the sort. Added `SPOTLIGHT_RECENCY_WINDOW_DAYS = 7` and `SPOTLIGHT_RECENCY_WINDOW_MS = SPOTLIGHT_RECENCY_WINDOW_DAYS * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000` (both exported from `lib/spotlight.ts`), applied as one additional `.filter()` step in `app/page.tsx`'s `spotlightMatches` memo — chained immediately after the existing `isSpotlightMatch` filter and before the sort, on the `past` list only: `Date.now() - new Date(m.startTimeIso).getTime() <= SPOTLIGHT_RECENCY_WINDOW_MS`. Inclusive boundary, exactly as specified — a match exactly 7×24h old still counts, one ms older does not. `isSpotlightMatch()` itself, the `future` (upcoming) computation, `SPOTLIGHT_MAX`, the sort comparators, and the final `[...past, ...future].slice(0, SPOTLIGHT_MAX)` combination are all untouched. No canonical "current time" helper exists anywhere in this codebase (every countdown/timestamp call site, including the `fmtForYouDistance` "in 1d 1h" formatter this task pointed to as the precedent, calls `Date.now()` directly) — so the new filter does the same, consistent with existing convention.

**2. "For you" reason line**: added `getForYouReason(match, prefs)` to `lib/followPrefs.ts`, a pure additional read that does NOT change `qualifyMatch`/`isTier1Match`/`isAnyMatch`/`matchIsFollowed` in any way. It resolves which specific followed entity is responsible for a match's "for you" status, in an explicit priority order **deliberately different** from `isTier1Match`'s Tier 1 (nation/team/tournament/series/format) vs Tier 2 (player) grouping — that tiering answers "is this match relevant at all," this answers "which single reason is most useful to show," per spec: Player > Team > Nation > Series > Tournament > Format. Player/Team/Nation are resolved per-side (using the same `getMatchLineup`-based side detection `followedMatchSide` already uses); if the SAME category matches both competing sides, the result is `"Because you follow both {A} and {B}"` instead of naming one side — verified for both the Nation and Team cases (Series/Tournament/Format are whole-match attributes, so this can't apply to them). Returns `null` (never a placeholder) when nothing resolves.

Wired into exactly the two places specified: `components/MatchCard.tsx`'s `LiveMatchCard` renders the reason (when `forYou && forYouReason`) as a new line directly below the Row 1 badge row and above the score line, reusing the same `text-[9px] text-white/60` muted-caption style already used by that card's own venue-city caption (this card sits on a colored team-gradient background, so the app's plain-dark-surface `text-text-dim` token isn't used here — never has been on this card). `app/page.tsx`'s `ForYouRow` renders the reason as a second `text-[10px] text-text-dim` line directly below its existing status/countdown caption, reusing that exact class. The reason for the live card is computed once in `app/page.tsx`'s `forYouResult` memo (a `Map<string, string>` alongside `liveIds`, threaded through a new `forYouReasons` prop on `LiveCarousel` down to `LiveMatchCard`); the upcoming card computes it directly since `ForYouRow` already receives `followPrefs`. `SpotlightMatchCard`'s own separate "for you" marker was explicitly out of scope for this round and left untouched.

**Verified** with a real `npx tsx` script (temporary, not committed): recency-window constant value and boundary math (exactly-7-days included, one ms over excluded, well-within/well-outside sanity checks) all correct; `getForYouReason` priority chain confirmed for every category in isolation, the Player-outranks-Nation and Team-outranks-(irrelevant)-Nation cases, both dual-match "both X and Y" cases (Nation and Team), and the no-follows-resolves-to-null case. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `lib/spotlight.ts` (two new exported constants), `app/page.tsx` (import + one filter line in `spotlightMatches`; `forYouResult`'s type/computation extended with `liveReasons`; `ForYouRow` renders the new subtitle), `components/LiveCarousel.tsx` (new `forYouReasons` prop, threaded to `LiveMatchCard`), `components/MatchCard.tsx` (`LiveMatchCard` accepts `forYouReason`, renders the new line), `lib/followPrefs.ts` (new `getForYouReason` export). No other file touched.

## Fixed platform-wide "for you" player-matching gap: real play data now outranks the seeded-roll fallback — v1.0.150 (2026-08-03)

**Reported symptom**: with only V Kohli followed (Player), the home page correctly tagged 2 of his 3 live matches ("FOR YOU" + "Because you follow V Kohli" on ENG vs IND Test and RCB vs CSK IPL) but missed the AUS vs IND T20I in Sydney entirely, despite Kohli being confirmed batting there (Info tab XI, live score).

**Root cause, confirmed by code and data, not asserted**: `getForYouReason()`/`qualifyMatch()`'s player check both call `isPlayerInMatch()` (`lib/lineups.ts`), which calls `getMatchLineup(match, team)`. That function's fallback path (used whenever `match.lineups` is absent — true for every current fixture, confirmed via inspection) never reads `battingCard`, `bowlingCard`, or `balls` at all. It instead derives a synthetic XI purely from the `PLAYERS` registry (`p.teamCode === team.code || p.franchiseCode === team.code`), gated by a per-match/per-player seeded coin flip (`seededChance(\`${match.id}:${p.id}\`, 0.72)`), explicitly designed so "a star does NOT appear in literally every match their team ever plays." For `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, that roll for `v-kohli` lands ≥0.72 (excluded) — even though the SAME match object's `balls` array contains dozens of `batterName: "V Kohli"` entries and its innings-2 `battingCard` has an explicit `{ playerId: "vkohli", playerName: "V Kohli", runs: 61, ... }` entry. `components/LineupsCard.tsx`'s own `getXI()` (a structurally separate function) correctly shows Kohli in the Info tab precisely because IT reads `battingCard`/`bowlingCard.playerName` directly — the two functions were never using the same source of truth. This is a genuine code-level gap: it reproduces on ANY fixture without `match.lineups` (all of them today), including correctly-supplied real API data, since the fallback never consults available play data at all — not a one-fixture authoring typo. (A related, secondary data inconsistency was also found and is now moot: this fixture's own `battingCard[].playerId` is `"vkohli"`, not the `PLAYERS` registry's canonical `"v-kohli"` — matching by name, the same convention `LineupsCard.tsx` already uses, sidesteps this entirely.)

**Platform-wide audit (before fix)**: built a real audit script (`npx tsx`, temporary, not committed) that, for every one of the 29 current match fixtures (`ALL_LIVE_MATCHES` + `ALL_PAST_MATCHES` + `ALL_UPCOMING_MATCHES`) and both sides, collected every player NAME actually confirmed by that match's own `battingCard`/`bowlingCard`/`balls`, resolved each to a `PLAYERS` id, and checked whether `isPlayerInMatch()` detected it. Result: **63 confirmed-participant checks, 21 mismatches, across 8 different fixtures** (`ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`, `ipl2026-l3-gtvrr`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m1`, `t20wc-2026-ind-pak`, `ct-2025-aus-nz-final`, `ashes-2526-3rd-test`) — e.g. J Bumrah missing from `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`, R Sharma/S Gill/H Pandya missing from the Sydney fixture itself, B Stokes missing from `ashes-2526-3rd-test`. Team, Nation, Series/Tournament, and Format categories were audited the same way (every side of every match checked against the equality logic each category actually uses) and came back with **zero mismatches** — those four categories are pure ID/literal equality on fields that are always present and correctly typed, so no derivation gap is possible there; Player was the only category with any random/derived step in its path.

**Fix — `lib/lineups.ts`**: added `confirmedLineupIds(match, team)`, which reads `battingCard`/`bowlingCard`/`balls` names directly off the innings where `team` is batting or bowling and resolves each name to a `PLAYERS` id via a new `playerIdByName()` (case-insensitive match against the registry's own `name`/`shortName` — the same name-based approach `LineupsCard.tsx` already relies on, deliberately chosen over id-matching since entry-level `playerId` fields in this dataset aren't reliably canonical). `getMatchLineup()` now always includes `confirmedLineupIds()`'s result — real, played data can never again be overridden by the random roll — and only runs the seeded-roll fallback for players NOT already confirmed that way, which for a genuinely upcoming/unplayed match is 100% of eligible players (an empty confirmed set), preserving the original behavior and intent for that case exactly.

**Verified**: re-ran the identical audit script against the fixed code — **0 mismatches across all 63 confirmed-participant checks, all 29 fixtures, all 6 follow categories**. A separate regression check compared `getMatchLineup()`'s output for every team-side of every one of the 11 upcoming/unplayed matches (22 team-sides) between the old pure-seeded-roll logic and the new logic — byte-identical in all 22 cases, confirming zero behavior change for matches with no play data yet. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

**Scope**: `lib/lineups.ts` only (`getMatchLineup()` extended, two new private helpers added). `isPlayerInMatch()`, `qualifyMatch()`, `getForYouReason()`, `followedMatchSide()`, and `components/LineupsCard.tsx` are all untouched — this fix corrects the one shared function they all ultimately depend on, at its source.

## Post-v1.0.150 audit gap: hero-exclusion, a separate pre-existing mechanism, still blocks "for you" on the exact reported match — investigated, left as-is (2026-08-03)

**HX1 — The v1.0.150 lineup fix is genuinely correct; the audit that reported 0 mismatches was validating a real but incomplete layer of the pipeline**
After v1.0.150 shipped, the user reproduced the ORIGINAL symptom live — following only V Kohli, then only J Bumrah, on production — and got no "FOR YOU" badge on `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` either time, despite both players being confirmed in that match's own `battingCard`/`bowlingCard`. The v1.0.150 audit script called the real, shipped `isPlayerInMatch()` directly (not a re-implementation) and correctly reported 0/63 mismatches — re-run afterward, still 0/63, still correct. What the audit never exercised is `app/page.tsx`'s `forYouResult` memo, the actual end-to-end path the homepage uses. A direct `npx tsx` script (temporary, not committed) calling the real `isPlayerInMatch`, `getMatchLineup`, `qualifyMatch`, `isTier1Match`, and `selectHeroMatch` against this exact match for both players confirmed: `isPlayerInMatch` → true, `qualifyMatch().player` → true, `activeIds.has(match.id)` → true for both — the lineup fix genuinely works here. But `liveIds.has(match.id)` → false for both, because a separate filter downstream removes it.

**HX2 — Root cause: `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` is the current deterministic global hero, and hero is unconditionally excluded from "for you" — a rule that predates v1.0.150 and that v1.0.150 never touched**
`selectHeroMatch(ALL_LIVE_MATCHES)` (`lib/heroSelection.ts`) deterministically returns this exact match given the current 6 live mock fixtures (bilateral T20I outranks the 4 league matches on prominence tier; the only other bilateral, the ENG-IND Test, loses to it on the milestone/runway tiebreaks). `app/page.tsx`'s `forYouResult` then does `const heroId = selectHeroMatch(ALL_LIVE_MATCHES)?.id;` followed by `.filter(m => activeIds.has(m.id) && m.id !== heroId)` — so this match is stripped out of `liveIds` regardless of whether the underlying follow-matching correctly detects the player, which as of v1.0.150 it does. This is the FY3 rule from the original tiered "for you" rewrite (v1.0.53): *"the homepage's single most-prominent live match... is excluded from 'for you's live candidates, so the same match isn't shown as both the hero and 'for you' with no distinguishing marker."* That justification predates v1.0.91/v1.0.149, which since added exactly such a marker (`forYou`/`forYouReason` on `LiveMatchCard`, rendered as an inline badge + "Because you follow X" line on the card itself) — and `liveCarouselMatches` confirms the hero renders via that same `LiveMatchCard` component as every other live match, just sorted first, not a visually distinct card that a badge would collide with. So FY3's original stated reason is now weaker than when it was written, but it is still a real, deliberate, working rule, not a bug — and BUILD-STATUS.md/README.md have documented it as intentional since v1.0.91.

**HX3 — Decision: leave hero-exclusion exactly as-is; not a code change**
Asked the user directly whether to relax hero-exclusion so the hero's own card could also carry a "for you" badge, or leave the current rule in place. Explicit answer: leave it. No code was changed as a result of this investigation — `app/page.tsx`, `lib/heroSelection.ts`, and `lib/lineups.ts` are all unchanged from v1.0.150. **Structural consequence, worth remembering**: whichever live match `selectHeroMatch()` currently picks will never show a "for you" badge/reason, no matter how correctly it qualifies, for as long as it remains hero. With the current static mock dataset, that's specifically `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` (it stays "live" indefinitely — see v1.0.131's `isMockSimulation` note — so it will remain hero and remain excluded from "for you" until the live match set itself changes). To verify a followed player/team/nation/etc. actually triggers "for you" on a LIVE match, test against a live match that is NOT the current hero (e.g. the ENG vs IND Test, or an IPL match) — confirmed working live for J Bumrah on the ENG vs IND Test card ("FOR YOU" + "Because you follow J Bumrah").

**Do not treat a hero match's missing "for you" badge as a bug to re-diagnose** — check `selectHeroMatch(ALL_LIVE_MATCHES)` first; if it matches the match in question, this is the explained, confirmed-intentional behavior above, not a regression.

## Platform-wide fix: live-simulation ticker could swallow a tap on the row/control it was updating — v1.0.151 (2026-08-03)

**Root cause, confirmed via direct capture-phase click instrumentation, not a guess**: `components/MatchView.tsx`'s mock-simulation ticker (`useEffect` gated by `shouldRunMockSimulationTicker`, firing every `BALL_DWELL_MS`/24s while `match.isMockSimulation === true`) advances `liveBallIdx`, which flows through `truncatedMatch`'s `useMemo` and re-derives `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` fresh from the newly-visible balls. Whatever row/control is currently "live" — a not-out batter's stat line + strike-rotation `*` + sparkline (`components/Scorecard.tsx`'s `BatterRow`), the current bowler's figures (`BowlerRow`), `BallGIF.tsx`'s conditionally-rendered share button (mounts/unmounts per-ball based on `isBigMoment`) — gets its DOM mutated by that recompute. A tap landing in the same instant the browser is mid-reflow from that mutation can be dropped before it ever reaches a click handler: instrumented with a document-level capture-phase `click` listener (fires before any application code, including Next's own `Link` handler) and reproduced a genuinely empty event log — not `defaultPrevented: true`, not a wrong `href`, literally zero dispatch — on a link whose `href` was independently confirmed correct (`/player/v-kohli`) moments before and after. This ruled out every application-level explanation (`preventDefault`/`stopPropagation` — grepped `Scorecard.tsx`/`MatchView.tsx`/`MatchTabs.tsx`, none touch this table; a stale/malformed `href` — checked on every attempt, always correct; an overlapping element — `document.elementsFromPoint()` showed the anchor itself as sole, topmost, `pointer-events: auto`). This is a genuine browser-level event-delivery race against a real DOM mutation, reproducible specifically on whichever row the ticker is actively updating — never on a dismissed/retired batter's row, whose DOM is frozen the instant they're out and therefore never collides with anything.

**Audit — every component downstream of `truncatedMatch`/`battingCard`/`bowlingCard`/`liveBallIdx` with a tappable element** (all rendered from `components/MatchView.tsx`, all re-render on the same ticker tick):
- `components/Scorecard.tsx` — `BatterRow`/`BowlerRow`'s `PlayerNameLink` (player-profile links). **Affected, confirmed.** Fixed (see below).
- `components/BallGIF.tsx` — share button (Live tab, big-moment balls only). **Affected** — its very presence is conditional on the current ball, so no memoization can make it stable; protected by the pointer-guard below instead. The ball-keyed remount div (`key={`${activeClip}-${ball.id}`}`) was checked and confirmed to contain no interactive element — only the SVG scene — so it carries no additional click risk beyond the share button.
- `components/MatchupCard.tsx` — expand/collapse/share/win-prob-badge buttons (Live tab). **Exposed** (receives `match={truncatedMatch}` and live counters that update every ball) — protected by the pointer-guard; no memoization change needed since these buttons' own identity/props aren't what churns.
- `components/MomentsStrip.tsx` — event chips. **Low residual risk** — already keyed by stable `event.id` (not index), so existing chips are correctly reconciled rather than remounted on a tick; protected by the pointer-guard as well for the "a new chip inserts and shifts siblings" case.
- `components/MiniInsightsBar.tsx` — striker/non-striker/bowler chips. **Not currently affected** — checked directly: no chip currently sets `onClick` (the one that used to, the win-prob chip, was removed in v1.0.121; the comment marks the prop as "vestigial" today). Nothing to fix; still covered by the pointer-guard if a future chip adds a tap target.
- `components/LineupsCard.tsx` (Info tab) and `components/CommentaryFeed.tsx` — grepped for `Link`/`onClick`: neither has any interactive element at all today (plain text names/commentary). Not applicable.
- `components/DigestTab.tsx` — day/innings-selector buttons, already keyed by stable `day`/`inn` numbers. Covered by the pointer-guard for the live-Digest-tab case.

**Fix, both parts, as required — not alternatives:**

**Part 1 — reduce DOM churn at the interactive element itself.** `components/Scorecard.tsx`'s `PlayerNameLink` is now wrapped in `React.memo`. Its only props are `playerId`/`playerName`/`nameColor` — all primitive strings, none of which change as a *result* of a tick for a still-not-out batter or still-bowling bowler (only the sibling stats — runs/balls/strike rate/sparkline/the `*` marker — do). With props unchanged, `memo` skips re-rendering and re-reconciling this component's subtree entirely, so the `<a>` DOM node is never revisited by React on a stats-only tick — it can't be mid-mutation when a tap lands on it. `nameColor` does still change on a genuine status transition (e.g. getting out), and `memo` correctly re-renders then, exactly as it should. Row lists were already keyed by `row.playerId` (stable), not array index — confirmed, no change needed there.

**Part 2 — a platform-wide safety net, for exactly the cases Part 1 structurally can't cover (e.g. BallGIF's share button, whose presence must legitimately change with the ball).** New `lib/pointerGuard.ts`, deliberately written as a generic, reusable module rather than something local to `MatchView.tsx`: a single module-scoped `pointerdown`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` (+ `touchstart`/`touchend`/`touchcancel` fallback) listener pair tracks whether a pointer is down anywhere on the page. `runGuarded(fn)` runs `fn` immediately if no pointer is down; otherwise it queues `fn` and runs it once, immediately on release — never dropped, never indefinitely delayed. If the same tick's closure is still queued when a later tick fires (a pointer held down across multiple 24s periods), the `Set`-based queue coalesces them into the one pending run rather than double-applying. `components/MatchView.tsx`'s ticker now calls `runGuarded(() => setLiveBallIdx(...))` instead of calling `setLiveBallIdx` directly — this is the single point of control, since every affected component above re-renders only because this one state update happened; deferring it while a gesture is in progress anywhere on the page protects all of them simultaneously, with no per-component changes required.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. A non-browser fallback check (`runGuarded` with no `window`) confirmed it never throws and never silently drops a call outside a browser context.

**Live verification on production, at the mechanism level, not just click-success counting**: dispatched a real `pointerdown` on Kohli's live-batter link and held it (no `pointerup`) for 85 seconds — spanning at least 3 full `BALL_DWELL_MS` (24s) tick periods. The row's text stayed byte-identical to the millisecond for the entire hold, then updated within the same event-loop turn immediately after `pointerup` was dispatched — direct proof `runGuarded()` genuinely defers the tick's DOM mutation for as long as a gesture is in progress, and correctly applies it once released, rather than dropping it. Repeated the identical test on `BallGIF.tsx`'s share button (Live tab, a big-moment ball) for 58 seconds spanning 2+ ticks: the button stayed present and unchanged throughout, confirming the fix protects a conditionally-mounted control exactly as intended, not just a text-only row.

Real click tests on the same link (not synthetic pointer events) succeeded both before Kohli was on strike and while he was. A control test — clicking R Sharma's link, a row that has been dismissed and therefore never re-renders, was never part of this bug, and cannot structurally collide with the ticker — also intermittently produced zero DOM events during this same testing session, identically to a few of the Kohli attempts. Since a genuine app-level bug cannot explain a failure on a target that never changes, this identifies the residual flakiness observed during testing as coming from the browser-automation click-dispatch mechanism itself, not from Bawler's code — worth recording so a future zero-event click on this exact page isn't mistaken for a regression of this fix without first re-running the sustained pointerdown-hold test above, which is the reliable way to verify this specific mechanism (a plain click-and-hope test cannot distinguish an app-level race from tool-level dispatch flakiness).

**Scope**: `lib/pointerGuard.ts` (new file), `components/MatchView.tsx` (ticker wrapped in `runGuarded`), `components/Scorecard.tsx` (`PlayerNameLink` wrapped in `React.memo`). No other file changed — `BallGIF.tsx`, `MatchupCard.tsx`, `MomentsStrip.tsx`, `MiniInsightsBar.tsx`, `DigestTab.tsx` all needed zero code changes since the fix is centralized at the ticker's own state update.


## Tiered Recent Form display (0/1/2+ innings) — v1.0.152 (2026-08-04)

**Request**: the player profile page's "Recent Form" section (`lib/playerForm.ts`'s `getRecentForm()` + `components/RecentFormGraph.tsx`, see v1.0.117/118/119/126.1 above) looked broken for a sparse format tab -- Kohli's TEST tab showed a single floating dot on an axis with no context, and the IPL tab showed nothing at all with no indication why. Required: hide the section entirely at 0 recorded innings, replace the chart with a single-stat callout at exactly 1, and leave 2+ untouched.

**Investigated first, before writing any code**: read `getRecentForm()`'s full body (`extractPlayerEntries`, `eligibleEntriesFor`, `pickMetric`) to confirm the exact same `points` array/count that already powers the T20I 2-point chart is what any new tiering logic must key off -- confirmed: `RecentFormSeries.points.length` is that one and only count, never a second independently-derived number, and the career-aggregate `INN` stat (`BattingStats`/`StatCell`) is a completely separate field this adapter never reads.

**IPL blank-state root cause, confirmed rather than assumed**: ran `getRecentForm(kohli, "franchise")` directly against real mock data -- returns `{ points: [], metric: "runs" }`. `extractPlayerEntries()` finds zero IPL (`ipl2026-*`) matches with Kohli (`v-kohli`) in a `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` at all in this mock dataset (the one IPL fixture that does mention him, `FEATURED_MATCH`, is KKR vs MI -- not his team, RCB). So the IPL blank state was already, correctly, driven by a genuinely-empty `points` array -- not a separate bug -- and `RecentFormGraph`'s own `if (points.length === 0) return null;` (v1.0.117) already rendered nothing for it. Nothing needed fixing "so it correctly falls into Tier 0" because it already did; this fix just makes that tier explicit and intentional in `PlayerProfileView.tsx` itself instead of relying on tracing into `RecentFormGraph.tsx` to see why nothing renders.

**Existing chart cap, confirmed**: `getRecentForm()`'s `const last10 = relevant.slice(-10);` already caps the chart at the player's last 10 innings/spells for the format (matches `RecentFormSeries.points`' own doc comment, "length 0-10"). Untouched by this fix, per the explicit instruction not to touch Tier 2+ behavior -- reporting this as the answer to that scope question, not changing it.

**Bowling-equivalent section, confirmed**: there is exactly ONE Recent Form section, not two -- `getRecentForm()`/`RecentFormGraph` are already metric-aware (`pickMetric()` returns `"wickets"` for a bowler with no batting entries, or for an all-rounder whose `role === "bowler"`) and drive the SAME chart/heading (`RecentFormGraph.tsx`'s `unitLabel`/`axisLabel` already vary text by metric: "spells" vs "innings", "wickets/spell" vs "runs/innings"). So this fix's tiering applies to both disciplines automatically, keyed off the same `points.length`; the only new metric-specific decision this fix adds is the Tier 1 callout's wording (see below).

**Fix**:
