- `lib/playerForm.ts` -- `PlayerInningsEntry` gained an `out: boolean` field (the real recorded `BattingEntry.out`, `false`/unused for a bowling entry), populated in `extractPlayerEntries()`. `RecentFormPoint` gained an optional `notOut?: boolean`, populated in `getRecentForm()` only when `metric === "runs"` (`notOut: !e.out`) -- never on a wickets-metric point, since a bowling spell has no not-out concept. This rides on the exact same real `out` value that already decided the entry's eligibility -- no second, separately-computed notion of a player's innings/not-out status.
- `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` -- new local `RecentFormSingleStat({ point, metric })` component, reusing the identical `card p-3` container and `text-[10px] uppercase tracking-widest text-text-dim font-semibold` heading typography `RecentFormGraph` already uses for its own heading, so a Tier 1 callout and a Tier 2+ chart look like the same shell with different contents. Heading text is exactly "Recent form" (renders uppercase via the shared CSS class, matching the existing convention) with no "· LAST N INNINGS" subtitle. Body line: `Only innings so far: {value}{"*" if notOut}` runs for a batting point, `Only spell so far: {value} wickets` for a bowling point -- the wording split is the same kind of metric-aware text variance `RecentFormGraph.tsx` already established for its own heading/axis labels, not a new independent rule. The recent-form render block now explicitly branches on `recentForm.points.length`: `>= 2` renders the existing `RecentFormGraph` unchanged, `=== 1` renders `RecentFormSingleStat`, `0` (or `recentForm` still `null` mid-fetch) renders neither -- nothing in that space, matching Tier 0.
- **No existing not-out-asterisk formatting helper found to reuse.** Grepped `components/` and `lib/` for anything that appends a not-out `*` to a number (the "254*" `highScore` convention cited as precedent) -- there is no such function anywhere; `highScore` values like `"254*"` are pre-formatted literal strings authored directly into `lib/mockData.ts`, not generated by code. `RecentFormSingleStat` appends the asterisk as a plain inline `${value}${notOut ? "*" : ""}` expression, matching that same literal convention, rather than introducing a new named helper for a single one-line use.
- Career aggregate stats grid (`BattingStats`/`BowlingStats`, `MAT`/`RUNS`/`AVG`/`SR`/`HS`/`INN`/`100s`/`50s`) -- untouched, confirmed by grep: no edit touched `StatCell`, `BattingStats`, or `BowlingStats`.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean. Ran `getRecentForm()` directly (temporary `npx tsx` script, deleted after use) against every player in `PLAYERS` across all 4 format tabs to enumerate every real Tier 0/1/2+ case in the current mock dataset, then re-ran against the updated code to confirm `notOut` populates correctly: `v-kohli`/test -> Tier 1, `121`, `out: true` -> no asterisk; `h-pandya`/franchise -> Tier 1, `22`, `out: false` -> genuine not-out case, asterisk renders; `j-bumrah`/test and `/franchise` -> Tier 1 on the WICKETS metric (`3` and `2` respectively), confirming the bowling-equivalent wording path is real and reachable, not speculative; `v-kohli`/odi and `/franchise` -> Tier 0 (0 points); `v-kohli`/t20i and every other 2+-point case -> unchanged Tier 2+ chart.


## Pitch report rework: isolated from mockData.ts, re-keyed per-match, made real-data-ready, extended to all 29 matches, safety tripwire added — v1.0.153-157 (2026-08-04)

**Background — why this took a mandatory safety process.** A diagnostic-only investigation (no code touched) confirmed pitch reports were fully built (`PitchReport` type, `PitchReportCard.tsx`, `PITCH_REPORTS` data, correctly wired into `InfoTab.tsx`) but only covered 5 IPL venues by id, so 13 of 29 matches (every international/bilateral fixture) never showed one — a pure data-coverage gap, not a broken wire-up. That same investigation found this exact feature had already caused a full-platform crash once: `changes and upgradations.md`'s v1.0.39 entry records that "a subsequent session added pitch reports for international venues but introduced a file truncation bug that deleted ~13,800 lines from `mockData.ts`," forcing `git reset --hard 5333611` + `git push --force`. `BUILD-STATUS.md`'s changelog table separately lists v1.0.42/v1.0.43 entries describing a second, similar-sounding incident and recovery, but grepping `mockData.ts` for every venue/fix mentioned in those two entries found zero trace of that work ever having existed in the codebase, and neither version has a corresponding `DECISIONS-LOG.md` or changelog entry the way every other version does — reported as an unresolved documentation gap in the diagnostic, not fabricated an explanation for. Given two apparent crash-and-rollback cycles on this exact feature, the follow-up fix request came with a mandatory process: work only on a dedicated branch (never `main`), record `mockData.ts`'s exact state before touching anything, never use `git push --force`/`git reset --hard` under any circumstance, and do the fix in 5 small steps — each with its own clean build/regression check — rather than one large change.

**Branch and baseline.** Created `feature/pitch-reports-rework` off `main` at `9d9fb85`. Recorded `mockData.ts`'s exact pre-work state: 15,241 lines, md5 `44369db3cbf2941e4006ae7b79e9609c`, 27 exports (all with line numbers).

**Step 1 (v1.0.153) — extraction, pure move.** Moved the `PitchReport` interface (`lib/types.ts`) and `PITCH_REPORTS` data (`lib/mockData.ts`) into a new dedicated `lib/pitchReports.ts`. Verified byte-for-byte: `mockData.ts` dropped by exactly 81 lines (15241 -> 15160), `types.ts` dropped by exactly 14 lines, and a temporary deep-equality script confirmed all 5 entries were unchanged. `git diff` confirmed these were the ONLY changes to each file — no match/ball/player data touched, reordered, or reformatted. The point: pitch-report edits can no longer, even in principle, touch the ~15,200-line match/ball/player object literal that was truncated last time — the two are now in structurally separate files.

**Step 2 (v1.0.154) — re-keyed venue id -> match id.** A venue's actual pitch condition is genuinely a per-match fact (a curated/relaid pitch, different weather, different dew that same night), not a fixed property of the ground — one shared report per venue stops being true the moment two different matches at the same ground get independently-authored conditions. `PITCH_REPORTS` is now `Record<Match["id"], PitchReport>` instead of `Record<Venue["id"], PitchReport>`; `venueId` stays on each entry as informational content, no longer the lookup key. `InfoTab.tsx`'s lookup changed from `PITCH_REPORTS[match.venue.id]` to `PITCH_REPORTS[match.id]`. Verified all 5 re-keyed entries resolve to real matches with a matching `venue.id`, and entry content (sliders/bullets/etc.) was unchanged — only the key shape changed.

**Step 3 (v1.0.155) — optional structured fields, real-data-readiness.** `paceFriendly`, `spinFriendly`, `bounceConsistency`, `expectedFirstInningsScore`, and `dewFactor` are now optional on `PitchReport` (`bullets` stays required). `PitchReportCard.tsx` now omits the corresponding section entirely when a field is absent (a `hasAnySlider` check gates the whole slider block, plus per-slider checks; `expectedFirstInningsScore`/`dewFactor` sections were already conditionally rendered and needed no change there) — matching the same "don't render misleading defaults" convention already used elsewhere in this app (`DeliveryCard.tsx`'s `SpeedDot`, `lib/playerForm.ts`'s empty-series handling). Verified via `react-dom/server` render tests across full/minimal/partial entries; the 5 existing full entries were confirmed to render identically to before.

**Step 4 (v1.0.156) — extended to all 29 matches, in 5 small batches.** Enumerated all 29 matches; 24 lacked a pitch report (including matches at the 5 previously-covered venues, which each now need their own per-match entry under the new model). Added entries in 5 batches of ~5 matches each, verifying `tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` (106/106 static pages), and a temporary key-resolution script after every single batch before writing the next one, then committing each batch separately (6 commits total across Steps 1-5, pushed to origin after each). Two Test-match entries (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` at Lord's, `ashes-2526-3rd-test` at the MCG) deliberately omit `expectedFirstInningsScore` and `dewFactor` — a 5-day match's conditions shift too much across days for one score range to mean anything, and dew is a minor daytime factor for a match played mostly in daylight; a genuine, deliberate use of the optional fields Step 3 built, not an oversight. Final state: all 29 matches have exactly one `PITCH_REPORTS` entry each, zero orphan keys (every key resolves to a real match).

**Step 5 (v1.0.157) — safety tripwire.** New `scripts/check-mockdata-integrity.ts`, following the same PASS/FAIL convention as the existing `scripts/version-check.ts`. It records `mockData.ts`'s line count and export list as a baseline (`scripts/mockdata-baseline.json`) and fails loudly (nonzero exit) if the line count drops more than 5% or any baseline export disappears — a threshold far below the ~90% line loss the real incident caused, so it's sensitive without being brittle against normal small edits. Wired into the existing `prebuild` npm script alongside `version-check.ts`, so it runs automatically on every `npm run build`; also exposed standalone as `npm run mockdata-check`. To intentionally shrink `mockData.ts` in a future reviewed refactor, re-running with `--update-baseline` accepts the new state. **Tested against a simulated bad truncation**: backed up the real `mockData.ts`, replaced it with a truncated 1,400-line copy (same shape as the historical incident), confirmed the script failed both checks with exit code 1 and named the missing exports, then restored the original file, confirmed via `diff`/`md5sum` it was byte-for-byte identical to the backup, and re-ran the script to confirm it passes clean again.

**Final regression pass.** `npm run build` clean end-to-end (106/106 static pages, including all 29 match pages, all 21 player profiles, and every schedule route) with both `prebuild` checks (`version-check.ts`, `check-mockdata-integrity.ts`) passing. Beyond the build, directly rendered `InfoTab` for all 29 matches (all show pitch-report content, zero throw), `DigestTab`/`Scorecard` for live/past/upcoming samples, and the home page component — all clean. Confirmed by grep that `lib/pitchReports.ts` has zero import relationship with the Schedule, Table, Player, or Home pages, so Spotlight/FOR YOU/Schedule were never in this change's blast radius to begin with.

**Process note for any future work in this area**: this is now the third round of work on pitch reports, and the second one to complete without incident (the first successful one, in a prior session, was the original 5-IPL-venue build; this one is the second). The isolation into `lib/pitchReports.ts` (Step 1) and the tripwire (Step 5) are both specifically meant to make a third crash structurally much harder — pitch-report edits can no longer touch `mockData.ts` at all, and if they somehow do anyway, `check-mockdata-integrity.ts` catches it at build time instead of at deploy time.

**Scope**: `lib/types.ts` (removed `PitchReport`, -14 lines), `lib/mockData.ts` (removed `PITCH_REPORTS` + updated one import line, -81 lines net: 15,241 -> 15,160), `lib/pitchReports.ts` (new file: type + all 29 entries), `components/InfoTab.tsx` (import + lookup key), `components/PitchReportCard.tsx` (optional-field-aware rendering), `scripts/check-mockdata-integrity.ts` (new), `scripts/mockdata-baseline.json` (new), `package.json` (`prebuild`/`mockdata-check` scripts, version bump). `main` was not touched until this entire arc was complete and regression-verified on the branch.

## Fixed battingCard/bowlingCard join-key bug at the root, platform-wide + a second, unrelated orphan-player gap — v1.0.158 (2026-08-04)

**Background.** A diagnostic-only investigation (no code touched) confirmed `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`'s Score tab showed every KKR batter/bowler at 0 while the header and strike-rate bar read correct live figures. Root cause: the innings' hand-authored `battingCard`/`bowlingCard` used full names (`"Rinku Singh"`) while its `balls` used short names (`"R Singh"`) for both `batterId` and `batterName`; `lib/matchStatus.ts`'s `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` joined a ball to a card row via `b.batterName === entry.playerName` — a silent, no-error zero the moment naming differs even slightly between the two data sources. Confirmed pre-existing (unrelated to the prior pitch-report-rework arc) via `git diff 9d9fb85 HEAD -- lib/mockData.ts`, which touched only `PITCH_REPORTS`/one import line, with zero mention of `kkrvmi` and none of the responsible components touched at all.

**Required fix, platform-wide, once, at the two shared functions** — not a per-match patch. The literal instruction was to switch the join key from name to id (`batterId`/`bowlerId`) exclusively, since these were confirmed to match correctly and consistently. A pre-fix one-directional audit (does an id-match exist where the name differs) found exactly 2 fixtures with the reported mismatch direction: `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` (14 findings across both cards) and `psl-2026-lah-kar-live` (1 finding — "fakhar" id vs "Fakhar Zaman"/"Fakhar Z" name variants).

**Mid-implementation regression discovery.** Before shipping the strict id-only version, a broader, BIDIRECTIONAL audit (checking both "id matches but name differs" AND "name matches but id differs" for every card entry in all 29 matches) found the opposite failure mode already live and correct: `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s battingCard/bowlingCard use real, distinct slug ids ("zcrwly", "jbumrah", etc.) that never match its balls' id fields at all — only the names line up consistently. Worse, several of its players (H Brook, B Stokes, J Bairstow, B Duckett) have their OWN balls split across BOTH conventions within the same innings — a pre-existing data inconsistency already documented and solved once before, locally, by `Scorecard.tsx`'s `getBatterBalls()` (v1.0.144/145, same match, same root cause, built for its sparkline). Switching the join to strict id-only would have zeroed or undercounted this currently-correct, currently-live Test match. Presented this conflict directly (id-only vs the literal instruction's "never use name" vs the union precedent already in this codebase) rather than silently picking either side — the resolved answer: **match by id OR name, merged** (a ball counts for a card entry if either field matches).

**Fix, as shipped.** New `samePlayer(id, name, entryId, entryName): boolean` in `lib/matchStatus.ts` — `id === entryId || name === entryName`. `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`'s per-entry ball filter, the retirement lookup (`RetirementRecord.playerId`/`playerName`), and the `onStrike` check all now go through this one predicate, replacing the old pure-name join and the briefly-implemented pure-id join alike. `deriveBatterIdentitiesFromBalls`/`deriveBowlerIdentitiesFromBalls` (the no-original-card fallback path) also now reconcile a player split across id/name within the balls themselves into one identity, using the same either-field logic.

**Second, unrelated bug found during the required post-fix broader-sample verification.** The full-platform sum-conservation sweep (derived batting runs must equal raw ball runs, for every innings with ball data) initially still failed on exactly one case after the union fix: `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` innings 2, 148 raw runs vs 126 derived (22-run gap). Root cause was NOT a join-key problem — investigated directly and confirmed the innings' hand-authored `battingCard` only lists England's top 8 batters; the tail order (S Broad, J Anderson, J Leach, M Wood) has genuine recorded deliveries (22 runs, 156 balls total) but zero card entry of any kind. No join key, however permissive, can attribute a ball to a row that doesn't exist — `baseCard.map()` only ever iterates the entries it's given. Confirmed via a dedicated orphan-scan (does any ball's batter/bowler fail to match ANY existing card entry by id or name) that this is the only innings across all 29 matches with this gap, and that it existed identically before this session's fix too (a name-only join would never have matched these players either, since there was never a card row with their name). Presented this as a new, distinct finding rather than silently expanding scope — approved to fix in the same pass. **Fix**: new `withOrphanIdentities()` in `lib/matchStatus.ts` — when `originalCard` is non-empty, appends a freshly-derived identity row (same shape `deriveBatterIdentitiesFromBalls`/`deriveBowlerIdentitiesFromBalls` already produce) for any ball-participant not matched (via `samePlayer()`) by any existing entry. Purely additive — never touches an existing entry's identity, stats, or order.

**Full-platform re-verification, after both fixes.** Re-ran the sum-conservation sweep across all 29 matches/all innings with ball data: `PASS` with zero mismatches, including `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` (previously the sole failure) and the two originally-flagged fixtures. Re-confirmed `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`'s KKR innings specifically: R Singh 46(31), A Russell 37(20), Bumrah 3.4-0-23-2 — all non-zero and consistent, matching the header's `121/2 (12.3 ov)`-era state. Re-ran the simulated mid-innings-truncation check (first 43 balls of the same innings) to confirm the fix also holds for `MatchView.tsx`'s live scrub path, not just the complete-innings case. Re-ran the bidirectional and orphan-entry audits one more time post-fix: both still report the SAME raw-data-level classifications as before (2 id/name-mismatched fixtures, 1 fixture with the split-convention pattern, 1 innings with orphan tail batters) — expected and correct, since those are properties of the underlying mock DATA, not of the derivation LOGIC; what changed is that derived stats are now correct regardless of which convention or gap a given fixture happens to have.

**Fixture data hygiene — deliberately not changed.** All of the underlying name/id inconsistencies found during the audits (both mismatch fixtures, the Test match's split convention, the missing tail-order card rows) remain exactly as they were in `mockData.ts`. The union-match + orphan-fill fix makes correctness independent of these inconsistencies, so cleaning them up is no longer required for correctness (unlike the originally-instructed strict-id-only path, which would have required rewriting `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s ~500 ball records to avoid a regression). Given this project's documented two-time history of `mockData.ts` truncation crashes from large edits to this exact file (see the pitch-report-rework entry above), and that the fix already fully resolves the reported symptoms without touching fixture data, recommending against making that edit now — flagging it here as a known, non-urgent hygiene item instead.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean after each of the two fixes (106/106 static pages, both `prebuild` tripwires passing). All temporary scripts (`scripts/audit-name-id-mismatches.ts`, `scripts/post-fix-verify.ts`, `scripts/investigate-test-match.ts` through `test-match4.ts`, `scripts/bidirectional-audit.ts`, `scripts/missing-card-entries-audit.ts`, `scripts/tsconfig.test.json`) deleted after use, per this project's convention.

**Scope**: `lib/matchStatus.ts` only (`samePlayer()`, `withOrphanIdentities()`, both new; `deriveBatterIdentitiesFromBalls`/`deriveBowlerIdentitiesFromBalls`/`deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` all updated to use them). No fixture data in `lib/mockData.ts` changed. No other component touched — the fix is entirely at the one shared derivation layer, so it applies automatically to `MatchView.tsx`'s live truncation path and `lib/matchFeedAdapter.ts`'s real-feed ingestion path alike, for every match, present and future, mock or real.

## Fixed the real gap: MatchView.tsx's isComplete branch never called the v1.0.158 derivation fix at all — v1.0.159 (2026-08-04)

**How this was caught.** After reporting v1.0.158 as shipped and verified, the user checked `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s "ENG Inn. 1" tab (England's completed 1st innings, 199/10) directly on production and found the batting card still stopped at 8 rows (C Woakes), with no rows for S Broad, J Anderson, J Leach, or M Wood — the exact gap v1.0.158's `withOrphanIdentities()` was supposed to close. The user was right and my prior "confirmed on production" claim was wrong for this exact tab/innings — I had verified the KKR match's Score tab (a still-live, in-progress innings) and confirmed the sum-conservation invariant via direct calls to `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`, but never exercised the specific code path this innings actually renders through.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the deployed code directly (not by intent/memory).** `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` memo has two branches per innings: an `else` (mid-innings) branch that calls `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls`, and an `isComplete` branch (any innings whose balls are fully consumed by the current playback position) that used to just do `innings.push({ ...inn, balls: truncBalls })` — spreading the raw, hand-authored `inn.battingCard`/`bowlingCard` through completely UNCHANGED, never calling either derive function. `isComplete` is true for every innings of every finished (`status: "post-match"`) match (since a finished match's `liveBallIdx` defaults to the last ball, so every innings is "fully consumed"), and for any already-finished innings within a still-live match — exactly England's 1st innings here, which had already wrapped by the time this was checked, while the match's 2nd innings (ENG Inn. 2) was still live and correctly went through the `else` branch (which is why my earlier live check on THIS match's live tab, on the OTHER innings, looked fine). The v1.0.158 fix in `lib/matchStatus.ts` was real, committed, and correct in isolation — it was simply never reached by this exact call site for a completed innings. Confirmed via `grep -n "withOrphanIdentities" lib/matchStatus.ts` (function exists, 3 call sites, all inside `lib/matchStatus.ts` itself) and by reading `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `truncatedMatch` memo directly — the `isComplete` branch had zero reference to either derive function before this fix.

**First attempted fix, caught before shipping.** Made the `isComplete` branch call `deriveBattingCardFromBalls(truncBalls, inn.battingCard, inn.retirements)` the same way the `else` branch does — mirroring the mid-innings path exactly. Verified directly against this exact innings with a real script before touching the browser (per the user's explicit "do not report this done again without walking through the exact reproduction yourself" instruction) and this caught a **new regression**: C Woakes and J Bairstow, both genuinely out on the hand-authored card ("b Bumrah", "c sub b Jadeja"), came back as "not out" with no dismissal text. Root cause: this fixture's `balls` array has no `isWicket: true` delivery at all for either dismissal — a separate, pre-existing gap in this fixture's ball-by-ball authoring (only 6 of the innings' 10 wickets are represented as flagged balls) that's independent of the id/name join question entirely. Full re-derivation from balls has no way to know about a dismissal that was never recorded as a ball, so it silently overwrote two correct entries with wrong ones — an actively worse failure mode than the missing rows this was meant to fix.

**Fix, as shipped — append-only, never rewrite an existing entry.** New `appendMissingIdentities()` in `lib/matchStatus.ts`: takes the hand-authored card and a purely-balls-derived card (computed with `deriveBattingCardFromBalls(balls, [], retirements)` / `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls(balls, [], format)` — empty `originalCard`, so every field comes from balls alone), and appends only the derived entries that don't match any existing card entry via `samePlayer()` (now exported from `lib/matchStatus.ts` for reuse). Every entry already in the hand-authored card is preserved by object reference — never remapped, recomputed, or touched — so Woakes' and Bairstow's real dismissal text survives exactly as authored, while Broad/Anderson/Leach/Wood get appended with stats computed from their own real balls (2(18), 0(66), 20(69), 0(3) respectively). `components/MatchView.tsx`'s `isComplete` branch now calls `appendMissingIdentities(inn.battingCard, deriveBattingCardFromBalls(truncBalls, [], inn.retirements))` (and the bowling equivalent) instead of spreading `inn.battingCard`/`bowlingCard` raw.

**Verified, in this order, before reporting anything:**
1. Direct script against `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s ENG Inn. 1 replicating the exact new `isComplete` logic: battingCard grew from 8 to 12 rows, all 4 new rows are the exact reported players with correct ball-derived stats, and every one of the 8 original entries is the SAME object reference post-fix (not just equal — identical), confirming zero risk of the Woakes/Bairstow-style regression.
2. Platform-wide sweep of the same append-only logic across all 29 matches/every innings with ball data: only this one innings gets any rows appended (8->12 batting, 5->5 bowling), zero duplicate rows anywhere, zero shrinkage anywhere, every original entry preserved by reference everywhere — confirming this doesn't newly affect any other fixture.
3. Re-ran the v1.0.158 sum-conservation sweep (`deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` called directly, unrelated to this branch): still 0 mismatches across all 29 matches — confirms this round's fix didn't regress the underlying derivation functions themselves.
4. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean (106/106 pages, both prebuild tripwires passing).
5. **Live, on production, the exact repro the user gave**: navigated to `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, opened the Score tab, selected "ENG Inn. 1" — battingCard now shows all 12 rows including S Broad, J Anderson, J Leach, M Wood with their real figures; re-checked "ENG Inn. 2" (the other, still-live innings) to confirm no regression there either.

**Scope**: `lib/matchStatus.ts` (`samePlayer` now exported, new `appendMissingIdentities()`), `components/MatchView.tsx` (`truncatedMatch`'s `isComplete` branch only). No fixture data changed. This closes the gap for every completed innings platform-wide, not just this one match — any other fixture with the same kind of incomplete hand-authored card, present or future, mock or real, now gets its missing rows appended automatically once its innings finishes, without risk to any dismissal already recorded correctly.

## Pitch Report redesign: compact stat-box row replaces sliders, avgFirstInningsScore replaces the predictive range — v1.0.160 (2026-08-05)

**Request.** Redesign the Pitch Report section (Info tab) for information density: replace the stacked full-width sliders (pace/spin/bounce/dew, each its own labeled progress bar) with a row of compact boxes, one per stat that has a value; replace "expected 1st innings score" (a predictive `{low, mid, high}` range shown as a gradient-bar gauge) with "avg 1st innings score," a single historical number, rendered as its own box in the same row. Explicit requirements: reuse the player profile's exact MAT/RUNS/AVG/SR stat-tile styling (not a lookalike), up to 4 boxes per row wrapping to a second row on a 5th, fewer-than-4-box rows stretch to fill evenly (never small boxes with empty space beside them), each box strictly optional (no field = no box, never a placeholder/zero), and the migration applied to literally every one of the 29 `PITCH_REPORTS` entries, not a sample.

**No dedicated branch this time — disclosed, not silent.** The prior pitch-reports-rework arc (v1.0.153-157, see entry above) mandated a dedicated branch specifically because two earlier incidents on this same feature truncated `mockData.ts` by ~13,800 lines from large direct edits to that file. This task never touches `mockData.ts` at all — `lib/pitchReports.ts` has been a fully isolated file since v1.0.153 (Step 1 of that arc), and the type change plus all data-shape edits stay entirely within it. Worked directly on `main`, same as every other round since that isolation was built, rather than re-invoking a process built for a different, no-longer-applicable risk.

**Type change — `lib/pitchReports.ts`.** `PitchReport.expectedFirstInningsScore?: { low: number; mid: number; high: number }` removed; replaced with `avgFirstInningsScore?: number`, same optional pattern as the other rating fields, with a doc comment explaining it's a genuine historical statistic (average score at the venue), never a per-match prediction, and must stay a single number in any future real-data mapping too.

**Data migration — all 29 entries, verified not just claimed.** A script (with a corrected self-check after an initial version's flawed assertion aborted the write before it could run — see Errors note below) converted all 27 real `expectedFirstInningsScore: {low, mid, high}` entries to `avgFirstInningsScore: <mid value>` (using the existing `mid`, as explicitly authorized). The 2 Test-match entries (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` at Lord's, `ashes-2526-3rd-test` at the MCG) never had the field and still don't — their explanatory "no score range, a 5-day match's conditions shift too much" comments were updated to reference `avgFirstInningsScore` by name instead of the retired `expectedFirstInningsScore`, so the prose stays accurate. Post-migration grep confirms zero remaining references to `expectedFirstInningsScore` anywhere in the codebase (type, data, or comments) and exactly 27 `avgFirstInningsScore:` data entries plus the 1 type declaration.

**Shared stat-tile, not a new lookalike.** `PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s local, unexported `StatCell` (the exact MAT/RUNS/AVG/SR tile: `flex flex-col items-center gap-0.5 px-2 py-3`, `text-base font-extrabold` value, `text-[10px] uppercase tracking-widest text-text-dim font-semibold` label, plus its null/NaN/empty-string guards) was extracted verbatim into a new `components/StatCell.tsx` and exported. `PlayerProfileView.tsx` now imports it instead of defining it locally — zero behavior change there, confirmed by diff (only the import line added and the local function body removed, `BattingStats`/`BowlingStats` untouched).

**`PitchReportCard.tsx` redesign.** Removed the `Slider` sub-component and its three call sites (pace/spin/bounce), the score-expectation gradient-bar block, and the separate dew-factor row -- replaced by one new section: a `boxes` array built from whichever of {paceFriendly, spinFriendly, bounceConsistency, avgFirstInningsScore, dewFactor} are actually defined (pace/spin/bounce rendered as `"N/10"` strings to preserve the 0-10 scale meaning without a visual bar; avg score as a plain number; dew as its capitalized string), each wrapped in `<div className="card"><StatCell .../></div>` -- reusing the same `.card` class (`rounded-2xl`, `bg-bg-surface`, `border-line`) already used everywhere else in the app, not a new border-radius. `boxes` is chunked into groups of at most 4; each chunk renders as its own `<div className="grid gap-3" style={{gridTemplateColumns: \`repeat(${row.length}, minmax(0,1fr))\`}}>` -- an explicit per-row equal-fraction grid (not one grid with a fixed column count and default wrap) specifically so a lone 5th box left over on a second row also stretches to fill the full width, rather than sitting at native size with empty space beside it -- satisfying the "must stretch evenly regardless of count" requirement even for that edge case. `gap-3` matches `InfoTab.tsx`'s own existing Date&Time/Weather two-card row, the established sibling-card gap value in this same tab, not a new number. A box's field being `undefined` means it's simply never added to the `boxes` array -- no placeholder, no zero, and the remaining boxes reflow automatically since row-chunking runs on `boxes.length`, not a fixed slot count.

**Migration-script error, caught before any write happened.** First attempt's self-check assertion (`"expectedFirstInningsScore" not in content.replace(new_type_field, "")`) was logically broken -- the replacement doc-comment text itself contains the substring "expectedFirstInningsScore" in its own prose, so the check couldn't behave as intended -- and threw before the file write (sequenced after the assertion) ever ran. Confirmed via `grep` that `lib/pitchReports.ts` was completely unchanged on disk at that point -- no partial or corrupted write occurred, since Python's exception fired before `open(path, "w")`. Rewrote the check to look for the actual type-declaration and data-entry patterns instead of diffing out the replacement text, re-ran, and confirmed both the write succeeded and the corrected checks (0 remaining `expectedFirstInningsScore?:` declarations, 0 remaining `expectedFirstInningsScore: {` data entries) passed for real before moving on.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` clean. `npm run build` clean (106/106 static pages, both `prebuild` tripwires -- `version-check.ts`, `check-mockdata-integrity.ts` -- passing, confirming this round never touched `mockData.ts`). Parsed `lib/pitchReports.ts`'s final data directly (not by eye) to compute the exact expected box count per match: 19 matches have all 5 fields (pace/spin/bounce/avg/dew) -> 4-then-1 row split; 8 matches have 4 fields (pace/spin/bounce/avg, no dew) -> single 4-box row; the 2 Test matches have 3 fields (pace/spin/bounce only) -> single 3-box row; 29/29 accounted for. Live-verified on production across representative cases from all three box-count groups plus the two Test-match 3-box entries specifically (the only entries with no avg-score box at all, to confirm that omission renders cleanly) -- see the shipping report for the full per-match table. Confirmed no leftover old-gauge UI (no three-number range, no labeled progress bars) anywhere, and that nothing else on the Info tab (Date & Time, Weather, Squads, Venue) regressed.

**Scope**: `lib/pitchReports.ts` (type + all 29 data entries + 2 comments), `components/StatCell.tsx` (new, extracted), `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` (import swap only), `components/PitchReportCard.tsx` (redesigned render, `Slider` removed), `package.json` (version bump). No fixture data outside `PITCH_REPORTS` touched; no other tab or component touched.

## Fixed a real layout bug in the v1.0.160 pitch-report box row: fixed 4-column chunking replaced with a per-match dynamic column count — v1.0.161 (2026-08-05)

**Bug report.** The v1.0.160 redesign chunked the pitch-report stat boxes into groups of a fixed 4 columns. That happened to look right for the 8 matches with exactly 4 fields, but was wrong for every other count: the 19 matches with 5 fields (adding Dew) wrapped their 5th box onto its own second row with 3 empty column slots beside it -- true whether or not that lone box was made to stretch to fill the row, since the row itself was still mostly empty. The 2 Test matches with 3 fields happened to fit in one chunk of the fixed grid, but only by coincidence of being under the fixed 4 -- a genuinely correct implementation shouldn't rely on that coincidence, and a future match with fewer than 4 fields would have reserved an empty slot in the same fixed grid.

**Root cause.** `boxRows` was built with `for (let i = 0; i < boxes.length; i += 4)` -- a literal, hardcoded chunk size of 4, unrelated to how many fields that specific match's report actually has.

**Fix, `components/PitchReportCard.tsx`.** The box list itself was already correctly built by checking each field's presence (unchanged from v1.0.160); what changed is the chunk size, now `MAX_ROW_COLUMNS = 6` -- a defensive cap only, not an active feature, since no match in today's data exceeds 5 fields. Every real match today therefore renders in exactly one row, with the CSS grid's column count set to that row's actual box count (`gridTemplateColumns: repeat(row.length, minmax(0,1fr))`), never a fixed number. A new `sizeForColumnCount()` maps <=4 boxes to the original "md" tile size, 5 to a new "sm" size, and 6 (the defensive cap) to "xs" -- so a denser row's boxes shrink slightly rather than overflowing or wrapping their own text. The "Avg score" label abbreviates to "Avg sc." only once a row is dense enough (5+ columns) to need it.

**Fix, `components/StatCell.tsx`.** Added an optional `size?: "md" | "sm" | "xs"` prop, default `"md"`. The `"md"` branch renders byte-for-byte the same markup this component has always rendered -- confirmed via `md5sum` that `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` (which never passes `size`) is untouched by this change, so the player profile's MAT/RUNS/AVG/SR tiles are unaffected.

**Safety process.** Worked on a dedicated branch (`fix/pitch-report-dynamic-columns`), never touched `main` until verified. Recorded an md5/line-count baseline for `lib/pitchReports.ts`, `components/PitchReportCard.tsx`, `components/StatCell.tsx`, and `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` before editing; confirmed after the fix that `lib/pitchReports.ts` and `PlayerProfileView.tsx` are byte-identical to baseline (this fix never needed to touch either file). No `git reset --hard` or `git push --force` used at any point.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean (106/106 pages, both prebuild tripwires passing). Live-checked all three distinct field-count groups on production after deploy -- 2 Test matches (3 fields, single row), 8 matches (4 fields, single row, "md" size unchanged from v1.0.160), and 19 matches (5 fields, single row at "sm" size, no wrap, no stretched lone box) -- at both desktop and a real phone-width viewport. Full platform regression pass (Schedule, Live, Scorecard, FOR YOU, Spotlight, player profile) confirmed nothing else was affected by the shared `StatCell` change.

**Scope**: `components/PitchReportCard.tsx`, `components/StatCell.tsx`, `package.json` (version bump) only. No fixture data touched.

## Fixed cross-match insight-card bleed: InsightV2 gets a required matchId, MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2's shared pool is scoped platform-wide — v1.0.162 (2026-08-05)

**Bug report.** On `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`'s Live tab, the ball-by-ball commentary feed showed two insight cards with no connection to the match — a "STAT" card about Shaheen/KAR/LAH (PSL content) and a "VIEW" card about Lahore/Arshad Iqbal — interleaved between real India-Australia deliveries.

**Root cause, confirmed in a prior diagnostics-only pass.** `InsightV2` (`lib/types.ts`) had no `matchId` field at all. `MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2` (`lib/mockData.ts`) was a single flat 14-entry array consumed by every match on the platform via `MatchView.tsx`'s `insightsProp ?? MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2` fallback — no call site anywhere (`app/match/[id]/page.tsx`) ever passes `insightsProp`, so literally every match got the same pool. The only filter, `visibleInsights`, checked ball-level scoping only (`!i.relatedBallId || seenBallIds.has(...)`); 12 of the 14 entries have no `relatedBallId`, so they rendered on every match unconditionally, placed at a position derived from a hash of the insight's own id (`CommentaryFeed.tsx`'s `buildFeed`) — a placement rule with no awareness of which match was open. Independently reproduced the same PSL cards bleeding into a second, unrelated match (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, the Lord's Test) during diagnostics, confirming this was systemic rather than a one-off data mistake.

**Fix — `matchId` made required, not optional.** `InsightV2.matchId: string` added as a required field (`lib/types.ts`), with a comment explaining why it can never be optional even for mock data: any future real-data ingestion path must supply it, or this exact class of bug becomes possible again. All 14 existing `MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2` entries were tagged with their correct real match id, verified against each match's actual recorded data rather than guessed:
- `ia-1`..`ia-4` (AUS vs IND content) -> `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`. Verified against the match's own battingCard/bowlingCard: T Head 52(34) and Bumrah 3/28 in 4 overs are this match's exact 1st-innings figures (not `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m1`'s Bumrah 2/28, nor `m3`'s), and `ia-2`'s "34 off 22" matches the match's own static Info-tab context line verbatim.
- `psl-1`, `psl-2` (PSL LAH vs KAR content) -> `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`. This match already exists in mock data (was not invented) — KAR's 165/7 innings with Shaheen taking 2 wickets, and Lahore's recorded chase state (138/5 in 17 overs against a 166 target) line up exactly with "KAR to 38/2" mid-innings and "Lahore need 28 off 18," respectively.
- `v2-1`..`v2-8` (KKR/MI content) -> `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`. Verified per-entry against the match: MI bats first with Bumrah bowling, KKR chases at Eden Gardens (Russell/Rinku Singh/Suryakumar Yadav all on their correct real-life teams for this fixture).

No entries were deleted and none were ambiguous — every one of the 14 mapped cleanly to a real, already-existing match.

**Fix — `MatchView.tsx`'s `visibleInsights` filter.** `insight.matchId === match.id` is now the primary, non-optional gate, applied before the existing ball-level `relatedBallId` filter (which remains as a secondary filter operating only within the current match's already-scoped insights). `match.id` and `insightsProp` were added to the `useMemo` dependency array (previously missing `match.id`, a separate staleness risk closed at the same time, not the reported bug's root cause).

**Audited for residual leak paths, per the explicit ask.** `CommentaryFeed.tsx`'s orphan-placement logic (`buildFeed`, hash-based timestamp for insights with no `relatedBallId`) only ever operates on whatever `insights` array it's handed — it performs no match lookup of its own, so once `MatchView.tsx` hands it an already-matchId-filtered array, there's no path left for a wrong-match insight to slip in through it. `MiniInsightsBar.tsx` (the only other real consumer of `visibleInsights`) receives the same already-filtered array. `InsightsPanel.tsx` also imports `InsightV2` but has zero call sites anywhere in the app — confirmed dead code, not a leak path.

**Safety process.** Worked on a dedicated branch (`fix/insight-matchid-scoping`), two incremental commits (type + data tagging, then the filter change), never rewriting `MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2` in one giant edit. Recorded an md5/line-count/export-list baseline for `lib/mockData.ts` before editing (15,160 lines, 26 exports) and confirmed after (15,174 lines — exactly +14, one line per tagged entry — 26 exports unchanged). No `git reset --hard` or `git push --force` used at any point.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean (106/106 pages, both prebuild tripwires passing, mockdata-integrity tripwire reporting 15,175 lines/26 exports against baseline). Live-checked all 4 matches that actually carry ball-by-ball data (the only ones whose Live tab mounts `CommentaryFeed` at all): `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` and `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` (the two originally-confirmed-broken matches) plus `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` and `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`. Confirmed the 2 live matches with empty `balls` arrays (`ipl2026-l2-rcbvcsk`, `ipl2026-l3-gtvrr`) still correctly render the "Ball-by-ball data unavailable" fallback, untouched by this change. Full platform regression pass (Home, Schedule, Score, Digest, Info, player profiles) confirmed nothing else was affected.

**Scope**: `lib/types.ts` (`InsightV2.matchId`, new required field), `lib/mockData.ts` (14 `matchId` tags added to `MOCK_INSIGHTS_V2`, no entries deleted or reworded), `components/MatchView.tsx` (`visibleInsights` filter + dependency array), `package.json` (version bump). No other fixture data or component touched.

## Info tab consolidation: venue folded into Date & Time, Match Context slimmed to toss+narrative, Surface row de-boxed — v1.0.163 (2026-08-05)

**Request.** Presentation-only, platform-wide (every match, every format, every status) change to the Info tab. No new data fields anywhere — venue name/city, toss result, and narrative text all already existed and were already rendered somewhere on the page; this only moves where some of it renders and adjusts styling.

**Date & Time card (`components/InfoTab.tsx`).** The city line (previously just `{match.venue.city}`) now prepends the venue name in bold, comma-separated: `<b>{match.venue.name}</b>, {match.venue.city}` — e.g. "Sydney Cricket Ground, Sydney". Reuses `match.venue.name`/`match.venue.city`, the same fields `PitchReportCard`'s own header already reads — no new data source. Falls back to city-only if `venue.name` is ever falsy (the type has it as a required string today, but the fallback exists per the explicit ask, for real-data-readiness). Every other line in the card (date, local/UTC time, countdown badge, competition/matchNumber footer) is untouched.

**Match Context card (`components/InfoTab.tsx`).** Removed two of the four previous lines entirely: the team-names + competition header line and the venue line (venue is no longer duplicated here now that it lives in Date & Time). The card now shows at most two lines: the toss line (unchanged content and its existing `match.toss &&` conditional) followed by the narrative line (unchanged content and its existing `match.summary &&` conditional). One edge case required a real design decision rather than a literal copy-paste: the narrative's divider (`border-t`) used to always have real content above it (team header + venue were unconditional), so it never looked broken. With those two lines gone, a pre-match match with a narrative but no toss yet (a combination that couldn't previously produce a "content-free" divider) would otherwise render a stray border line directly under the "MATCH CONTEXT" label with nothing above it to separate from. Fixed by making the divider conditional on `match.toss` also being present — pre-match-with-narrative-only now renders as a single clean line, no floating border.

**Pitch Report Surface row (`components/PitchReportCard.tsx`).** The Surface row itself never had its own box/border/background (verified by reading the component before touching it) — what read as a separated "card section" was the `border-t border-line pt-3` that used to sit on the stat-box row's wrapper, directly below Surface, plus the outer `space-y-4` gap. Grouped Surface and the stat-box row into one `space-y-2` sub-block with no divider between them, matching the plain-text/no-divider treatment now used for the Match Context toss line. Surface's own label/value font sizes are untouched. The "How this pitch behaves" bullets section below (its own `border-t border-line pt-3` divider, sibling of the new Surface+stat-row group under the outer `space-y-4`) was not touched at all.

**Scope check.** Grepped for other call sites of both components before editing: `InfoTab` and `PitchReportCard` are each imported exactly once, both from `components/MatchView.tsx`'s Info tab render — no other page or component reuses either card, so there was no risk of an unrelated view picking up this change unexpectedly.

**Safety process.** Dedicated branch (`fix/info-tab-consolidation`), one commit covering all three cards together (small enough diff — 63 insertions/43 deletions across two files — that splitting further would have added process overhead without a real incremental-verification benefit; typecheck/build were still run and confirmed clean before merging). No `git reset --hard`/`git push --force` used. `lib/mockData.ts` was never touched by this round at all.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean (106/106 pages, both prebuild tripwires passing). Live-checked, after deploy: a pre-match match (toss line correctly absent, only narrative shows if present), a live match, and a completed match, confirming the Match Context card's line count is genuinely state-dependent rather than fixed. Checked one match each from T20I, Test, IPL, and PSL for the Date & Time venue+city line and to confirm Match Context no longer shows team names, tournament name, or venue. Checked the Surface row at both desktop and phone-width viewports for the no-box/no-divider/compact-row rendering. Full platform regression pass (Home, Schedule, Score, Digest, player profiles) confirmed nothing else was affected. See the shipping report for exact match IDs checked per card.

**Scope**: `components/InfoTab.tsx`, `components/PitchReportCard.tsx`, `package.json` (version bump). No fixture data touched.

## Fixed bowler chip silently disappearing on name-mismatch: samePlayer() matching + balls-derived fallback — v1.0.164 (2026-08-05)

**Bug report.** On the Live tab's top chip strip (`components/MiniInsightsBar.tsx`), the bowler chip ("3/28 P Cummins"-style) was missing entirely on `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` at multiple points in the match, while the two batter chips beside it always rendered fine. Separately reported and confirmed in the same diagnostics pass: the chip's cyan-vs-white coloring (`wickets >= 2 -> cyan`) and the batter chips' green-at-50-runs coloring were both intentional, unchanged since the chip strip's introduction, and NOT the cause — this fix is scoped only to the chip's visibility.

**Root cause, confirmed via diagnostics before any fix was proposed.** The bowler chip's lookup was `live.bowlingCard.find(b => b.playerName.includes(currentBowlerName) || currentBowlerName.includes(b.playerName))` — fragile substring matching, and the ONLY signal it checked was `playerName`, ignoring `playerId` entirely even though both `Ball.bowlerId` and `BowlingEntry.playerId` exist and were already populated. On `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`'s hand-authored fixture (`buildInnings1`/`buildInnings2` in `lib/mockData.ts`), every bowler's `playerId` already matched its ball data's `bowlerId` exactly (both are the same short "F Lastname" string, e.g. `"J Bumrah"`), but `playerName` used a different convention per innings — full first names ("Jasprit Bumrah", "Hardik Pandya") or dotted abbreviations ("P. Krishna", "G. Coetzee") — neither of which is a literal substring of the ball data's short form. The lookup always missed, and the chip was silently dropped with no fallback and no error. The batter chips a few lines above hit the exact same kind of name mismatch (battingCard has "Rinku Singh", balls say "R Singh") but never broke, because they already have a fallback: if the name-keyed lookup misses, they compute runs/balls directly from `live.balls`. The bowler chip had no equivalent.

**Fix — two parts, both required, landed as separate commits.**

1. **Robust matching** (`components/MiniInsightsBar.tsx`): replaced the `.includes()` check with `samePlayer(currentBowlerId, currentBowlerName, b.playerId, b.playerName)` — the exact same id-or-name union predicate (`id === entryId || name === entryName`) that `lib/matchStatus.ts`'s `deriveBattingCardFromBalls`/`deriveBowlingCardFromBalls` already use for every other ball-to-card join in the app, imported and reused rather than reimplemented. This alone resolves `kkrvmi`'s reported symptom, since the `playerId`s were already aligned.
2. **Balls-derived fallback**, required regardless of (1) actually resolving today's case, because id/name alignment isn't guaranteed for every future match (mock or real): if the `samePlayer()` lookup still misses, the chip now calls `deriveBowlingCardFromBalls(live.balls, [], match.format)` — the same derivation function the Score tab relies on for correct legal-ball-over counting, maiden detection, and run-out exclusion from wickets — and looks up the current bowler in the purely-balls-derived result. This mirrors the batter chips' existing fallback pattern precisely, so the bowler chip can no longer depend entirely on a name/id lookup succeeding.

Batter chip logic is completely untouched — confirmed via `git diff` showing only the chip-3 block and the new import line changed. Cyan/green coloring thresholds are untouched.

**Fix — data.** Normalized `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`'s two `bowlingCard` arrays' `playerName` fields to the same short form already used by `playerId` and by the ball data: innings 1 — "Pat Cummins"->"P Cummins", "V. Chakravarthy"->"V Chakravarthy", "S. Narine"->"S Narine", "A. Russell"->"A Russell", "U. Yadav"->"U Yadav", "N. Rana"->"N Rana"; innings 2 — "Jasprit Bumrah"->"J Bumrah", "P. Krishna"->"P Krishna", "G. Coetzee"->"G Coetzee", "P. Mishra"->"P Mishra", "K. Yadav"->"K Yadav", "Hardik Pandya"->"H Pandya". Only the 12 `playerName` string values changed — array shape, ordering, and every numeric field untouched. Both the code fix and the data fix were required per the explicit ask: the code fix makes this class of bug survivable everywhere, the data fix corrects the one fixture that was actually wrong today.

**Safety process.** Dedicated branch (`fix/bowler-chip-name-matching`), two incremental commits — code fix first (matching + fallback), verified clean (`tsc --noEmit`) before touching data; data fix second, verified against the mockdata-integrity tripwire (15,161 -> 15,175 lines, net zero from this commit's 12 same-line string edits, since it doesn't add/remove lines; 26 exports unchanged) and `npm run build` before merging. No `git reset --hard`/`git push --force` used. Batter chip logic's exclusion from the diff was explicitly confirmed, not assumed.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean (106/106 pages, both prebuild tripwires passing). Live-checked `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi` at multiple points across the innings, confirming the bowler chip now reliably shows correct wickets/runs for whichever bowler is current, and that cyan correctly triggers once a bowler reaches 2+ wickets. Re-checked all three previously-working matches (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`) to confirm the bowler chip and its coloring are unaffected by the matching-logic change. Full platform regression pass (Home, Schedule, Score, Digest, Info, player profiles) confirmed nothing else was affected. See the shipping report for exact match IDs and points-in-match checked.

**Scope**: `components/MiniInsightsBar.tsx` (matching + fallback logic), `lib/mockData.ts` (12 `playerName` string corrections in `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`'s two `bowlingCard` arrays), `package.json` (version bump). No other fixture data or component touched.

## First-run onboarding flow: swipe team picker, player picker, cricket-personality quiz, reveal, first-session quest — v1.0.165 (2026-08-06)

**Request.** A gamified first-run onboarding flow, built entirely on existing mock data and explicitly independent of the real-data-integration/native-packaging work in progress. Five steps: (1) swipeable team-picker cards with a 4-tier "real moment" fallback (live -> upcoming within 14 days -> completed within 30 days -> skip) and a one-time "who do you love to hate" rival prompt; (2) a player picker from followed teams' rosters, deduplicated strictly by registry id and tagged with every affiliation; (3) a 3-question either/or cricket-personality quiz whose answers double as the app's only format-preference input; (4) a capped 2-3s cosmetic "Building your feed..." reveal; (5) a small floating first-session checklist (follow a team / open a live match / read a pitch report).

**Trigger contract.** `shouldShowOnboarding() = !isOnboardingComplete() && !hasAnyFollow(getFollowPrefs())` (`lib/onboarding.ts`). The `isOnboardingComplete()` flag is required in addition to the follows check, specifically for the skip-everything case: a user who skips every step ends up with zero follows, and without an explicit completion flag `hasAnyFollow()` alone would show onboarding again on every subsequent open. `markOnboardingComplete()` is called unconditionally at the end of the flow (`OnboardingFlow.tsx`'s `finishOnboarding()`), whether the user completed or skipped every step.

**Ambiguities flagged back to the user before building, resolved via direct questions rather than guessed:**
- **Team roster** — 16 teams: the 6 national sides with real live/upcoming/recent match data in this mock dataset (IND, AUS, ENG, NZ, PAK, SA) plus all 10 IPL franchises. Fixed, deterministic order (`lib/onboardingTeams.ts`'s `CURATED_NATION_CODES`/`CURATED_FRANCHISE_CODES`), not shuffled, so "N of 16" and the underlying deck are stable run to run.
- **First-session quest trigger looseness** — loose: "open a live match" fires on visiting any live match's page regardless of which tab it lands on (`components/MatchView.tsx`, gated on `match.status === "live"`); "read a pitch report" fires whenever `InfoTab.tsx` actually renders a real `PitchReportCard` for the current match (gated on `pitch` being truthy) — no dwell-time or scroll requirement either way.
- **Rival-prompt repeat rule** — the build spec's own wording was self-contradictory ("shown once per team followed" vs. "not repeated once a rival is already picked"). Resolved as: ask once total, after the first team followed, regardless of whether the user picks a rival or skips, never again for the rest of the onboarding session.
- **Entry point** — a dedicated `/onboarding` route (`app/onboarding/page.tsx` wrapping `OnboardingFlow.tsx`), not a modal over `/`. `app/page.tsx` checks `shouldShowOnboarding()` on mount and `router.replace("/onboarding")`s before the home feed is allowed to render at all for a new user (gated via a `redirectPending` flag using the same pattern as the page's existing `isBooting` hydration-safe skeleton gate, so there's no flash of home content first).

**Data-model confirmation done before building, per the spec's own explicit instruction to check first.** The spec worried `PLAYERS` might only store one team per player, which would have required a data-model extension before player dedup/tagging could work. Confirmed by reading `lib/types.ts`: `PlayerProfile` already models exactly the two affiliations the spec's own example needs (`teamCode` for national, `franchiseCode` for franchise), and `PLAYERS` is keyed uniquely by id — so iterating `Object.values(PLAYERS)` once per entry (`lib/onboardingPlayers.ts`'s `getRelevantPlayers()`) already IS the dedup, with no per-team duplication to collapse in the first place. No data-model extension was needed.

**Reuse discipline, per the spec's explicit "don't reimplement lookup logic" instruction.** `lib/onboardingTeams.ts`'s `getTeamMoment()` calls `lib/teamSchedule.ts`'s existing `getTeamSchedule()` for all four fallback tiers rather than deriving match state itself. `lib/onboardingPlayers.ts`'s `getPlayerMoment()` reuses `lib/playerActivity.ts`'s `isPlayerCurrentlyLive()` for the live tier and a new `getLastInningsHeadline()` added to `lib/playerForm.ts` (itself built by extending that file's existing private `extractPlayerEntries()`/formatting helpers, not a parallel derivation path) for the recent-innings tier.

**Bug found and fixed during verification, not by code review alone.** `getTeamMoment()`'s tier-1 (live) headline initially computed "opponent" as `otherTeamName(match, followedCode)` — whoever isn't the followed team. That's wrong whenever the followed team is currently bowling: it named the batting side as its own opponent (e.g. "KKR 130/3 vs KKR" when MI, the followed team, is bowling and KKR is batting). Caught by an actual `npx tsx` run against all 16 curated teams, not by inspection. Fixed by deriving `battingSide`/`bowlingSide` directly from the current innings' `battingTeam` field and using the bowling side (never the followed team's own identity) as the named opponent in that specific string.

**Format preference has no separate settings step, by design.** The 3-question quiz (`lib/onboardingQuiz.ts`) is the only format-preference input in the whole flow, per the spec. Two axes — `style` (aggressive/grinder, from Q1 alone) and `formatLean` (short/long/balanced, from whether Q2 and Q3 agree) — combine into exactly 6 cells, each mapped to one of 6 named personas (Boundary Hunter, Bazball Believer, Momentum Chaser, Death-Over Closer, Test Purist, Session Reader) with final, non-placeholder copy and a `formatTags` array. `persistFormatTags()` (`QuizStep.tsx`) merges those tags into `FollowPrefs.formats` — the exact same field the Filter sheet's own Format category already reads/writes, per the spec's explicit "no second, parallel preference system" instruction.

**Reveal step is cosmetic, capped, never a real wait.** `RevealStep.tsx`'s `REVEAL_DURATION_MS = 2200` is a fixed `setTimeout`, independent of how much the user actually followed — a user who followed nothing still sees the same-length "Setting things up" transition as a user who followed 5 teams and 3 players (whose subtext instead names them, capped at 3 combined names via `joinWithCap()`).

**Locked-preview nudge scope decision.** When a user tries to skip step 1 or step 2 having followed nothing at all yet, `LockedPreview.tsx` shows a blurred "Follow a team to unlock this" nudge — but only once across the whole onboarding session, not once per step. That "shown-once" flag is lifted to `OnboardingFlow.tsx` and passed down to both `TeamPickerStep` and `PlayerPickStep` as shared state, reasoning: the trade-off nudge is meant to be one moment in the whole session, not a per-step nag repeating itself in step 2 right after the user already saw and dismissed it in step 1.

**Safety process.** Built on a dedicated branch (`feature/first-run-onboarding`), never `git reset --hard`/`git push --force`, one incremental commit per phase (data model -> team picker -> player picker -> quiz -> reveal+quest -> route wiring), with `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` run clean after every commit and `lib/mockData.ts`'s md5/line-count/export-count baseline reconfirmed unchanged after each (15,174 lines, 26 exports throughout — the only mockData edits were in-place `funFact` field additions to 16 existing team objects, not insertions).

**Verified — logic level, exactly what was tested and observed (browser-interactive verification of gestures/animation timing was not performed — see below).** Two `npx tsx` harnesses, run against the real modules (not reimplemented test doubles), covering every item on the original ask:
- New-user trigger: `shouldShowOnboarding()` returns `true` with zero prefs and no completion flag. Confirmed.
- Existing-user suppression: returns `false` both for a user with an actual follow, and separately for a completed-onboarding user with zero follows (the skip-everything case). Confirmed both.
- Multi-affiliation dedup: `getRelevantPlayers([India, RCB])` returns V Kohli exactly once, tagged `["IND","RCB"]`, not as two rows. Confirmed.
- All 4 fallback tiers individually: across the 16 curated teams, tier 1 (live) hit 9 teams, tier 2 (upcoming <=14d) hit 5, tier 3 (recent <=30d) hit 2, tier 4 (none) hit 0 naturally — so a synthetic zero-fixture team (`code: "ZZZ"`) was constructed and confirmed to correctly fall through to `null` (tier 4).
- Skip-everything path: prefs stay empty/valid, `isOnboardingComplete()` becomes `true`, the first-session quest initializes with `followTeam: false` (not hardcoded `true`), and `shouldShowOnboarding()` stays `false` afterward. Confirmed.
- Quiz persona correctness: all 6 possible answer combinations (not just 2) mapped to their correct, distinct persona id. Confirmed.
- Reveal timing cap: `REVEAL_DURATION_MS` read directly from the component source, confirmed `2200` (within the 2000-3000ms requirement).
- First-session quest lifecycle: initializes correctly from real follow outcome, checks off items via `markQuestItem()` and the change persists across a fresh `getFirstSessionQuest()` read (simulating an app close/reopen), stays visible once complete until `markCompletionAnimated()` is explicitly called (confirming the card doesn't just vanish the instant the 3rd item completes), manual `dismissFirstSessionQuest()` hides it immediately even with zero items checked, and `markQuestItem()` before `initFirstSessionQuest()` ever ran is a confirmed no-op (a non-onboarded user's quest never appears). All confirmed.
- 15/15 checks passed.

A second `npx tsx` + `react-dom/server` harness smoke-rendered the actual leaf components (not test doubles) to catch render-time crashes `tsc`/`npm run build` can't: `TeamCard` (with and without the optional `funFact` field), `TeamMomentCard` (real tier-1 headline plus constructed tier-2/tier-3 headlines), `LockedPreview`, `RivalPrompt` (with candidates and with zero candidates), `SwipeCard` wrapping a real `TeamCard`, `RevealStep` (with real followed names, and with zero names for the skip-everything case), `QuizStep`'s first question, and `FirstSessionQuest` in both its pre-hydration states. 15/15 rendered without throwing. `OnboardingFlow.tsx` itself was excluded from this harness (it calls `useRouter()`, which needs a real Next.js router context this harness doesn't provide) and was instead covered by `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` succeeding end to end, plus the redirect logic being exercised indirectly through the `shouldShowOnboarding()` checks above.

**What was NOT verified.** Interactive browser verification of the actual swipe/drag gesture physics, the rival-prompt one-time-per-session gating as clicked through in a real session, the quiz's tap-through flow, and the reveal transition's real visual timing was not performed — a Vercel preview URL lookup for this branch was attempted and came back empty (likely a client-rendered page or network restriction on the lookup itself), and no other live-browser path was available in this session. Everything above was verified at the logic/component-render level against the real modules and components, not against hand-rolled test doubles, but the interactive/animation layer itself remains unverified and should be spot-checked in a real browser before this ships to real users.

**Scope**: `lib/types.ts` (`Team.funFact`), `lib/followPrefs.ts` (`rivalTeam` field + `FollowCategory` type fix), `lib/mockData.ts` (16 in-place `funFact` additions, no line-count change), `lib/onboarding.ts`, `lib/firstSessionQuest.ts`, `lib/onboardingTeams.ts`, `lib/onboardingPlayers.ts`, `lib/onboardingQuiz.ts` (all new), `lib/playerForm.ts` (`getLastInningsHeadline()` added), `components/onboarding/*` (10 new components), `components/FirstSessionQuest.tsx` (new), `components/MatchView.tsx`/`components/InfoTab.tsx` (quest-item marking), `app/onboarding/page.tsx` (new route), `app/page.tsx` (redirect trigger + quest mount), `package.json` (version bump).

## Fixed: onboarding step 1 had no way to actually follow a team — added tap-to-follow/skip buttons — v1.0.166 (2026-08-06)

**Bug report, from real-browser testing on the live v1.0.165 deploy.** A fresh user landing on `/onboarding` step 1 ("1 of 16 teams: India") had no way to progress the flow. Direct testing confirmed: clicking the card did nothing, dragging with a mouse did nothing (or so it initially appeared), a synthetic touchstart/touchmove-x10/touchend sequence dispatched at the DOM level did nothing, and inspecting the rendered page found only two buttons anywhere on screen — "Skip" and the bottom-nav "Filter" — no heart, checkmark, or follow button existed at all, despite the original build spec explicitly requiring one ("swipe right OR tap a heart/check button").

**Root cause, traced via real Chrome interaction (Claude in Chrome tools against the actual live deploy), not guessed:**
- `SwipeCard.tsx` already exposes a `registerHandle` prop specifically for this ("lets the parent trigger a swipe programmatically (heart/X buttons)," per its own doc comment) — but `TeamPickerStep.tsx` never wired it up, and never rendered any button. This is the real, confirmed bug: the plumbing existed, the UI control did not.
- Separately, to understand why the bug report's own manual mouse-drag test also appeared to fail: re-tested directly against the live deploy with a real Chrome mouse-drag (`left_click_drag` past the 80px threshold) and confirmed it works correctly — the card follows India and advances to the moment card. A small (sub-threshold) drag correctly snaps back without progressing — that's the existing threshold logic working as designed, not a bug.
- The synthetic touch-event sequence from the bug report was instrumented directly (added temporary `pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup` listeners, then re-ran that exact `touchstart`→`touchmove`×10→`touchend` dispatch against the live page) and confirmed to produce **zero** pointer events. This is because `SwipeCard` listens exclusively to Pointer Events, and no browser promotes a scripted/untrusted `TouchEvent` dispatched via JS into a trusted `PointerEvent` — only genuine hardware touch input, processed through the browser's own native input pipeline, gets that promotion. This fully explains why that specific test registered nothing; it does not indicate the swipe gesture is broken for real touchscreen users, whose taps and drags do generate real trusted `PointerEvent`s that `SwipeCard` already handles correctly.

**Fix.** `TeamPickerStep.tsx` now holds a ref to the currently-active (top) card's `SwipeCardHandle` and renders two always-visible circular buttons below the card stack — an X (skip) and a heart (follow) — that call `handle.swipeLeft()`/`swipeRight()` directly. This is the exact same `runExit()`-driven code path a real swipe already triggers (`onSwipeRight`→`handleFollow`, `onSwipeLeft`→`handleSkip`), so there is exactly one follow/skip implementation, not a duplicate one bolted on beside it. Following a team now works with a single tap or click, with zero dependency on gesture support of any kind, satisfying the explicit requirement that this "must work independently of any swipe/drag gesture."

**Safety process.** Dedicated branch (`fix/onboarding-tap-follow-button`), one commit for the code fix (verified `tsc`/`build` clean before merging), one commit for the version bump. No `git reset --hard`/`git push --force`. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched by this round (baseline unchanged: 15174 lines, 26 exports).

**Verified — real end-to-end browser interaction on the redeployed v1.0.166 live URL, step by step, not a general "verified" claim:**
1. Cleared `localStorage`, navigated to `/onboarding` as a fresh user. The two new buttons render exactly as designed below the card.
2. Tapped the heart on India (a national team) → followed correctly, advanced to the tier-1 live-moment card reading "ENG 88/4 (Over 28.0) vs IND" — this also confirms, on screen, that the v1.0.165-era opponent-naming bug (which could previously read e.g. "KKR 130/3 vs KKR" when the followed team was bowling) is genuinely fixed, not just fixed in the underlying function. Tapped "Continue" into the rival prompt, tapped "Skip."
3. Tapped the X seven more times to skip AUS/ENG/PAK/NZ/SA/MI/CSK/KKR, reaching "10 of 16 teams: Royal Challengers Bengaluru." Tapped the heart to follow RCB (a franchise team) → correctly advanced straight to its own live-moment card ("RCB 148/5 (Over 16.4) vs CSK") with **no repeated rival prompt** (matches the "ask once total" resolution). Tapped Continue, then the step-level "Skip" to move to step 2 with 2 teams followed.
4. Step 2 ("Pick your players") rendered **V Kohli — IND · RCB · Batter as exactly one row**, tagged with both affiliations — the multi-affiliation dedup fix confirmed visually, not just via the earlier unit-level test. Tapped "+ Follow" on that row → flipped to "✓ Following," and its own moment card correctly read "V Kohli is live right now."
5. Tapped Continue into the quiz, answered all three questions "A" → resolved to "Boundary Hunter," matching the earlier function-level persona mapping. Tapped Continue into the reveal, which read "Building your feed… Matching IND, RCB & V Kohli to what's live right now" and landed on the home feed within the expected ~2.2s cap.
6. On the home feed: "YOUR PLAYERS" correctly showed V Kohli; the floating "GET STARTED" checklist showed "Follow your first team" pre-checked, the other two unchecked. Opened the live AUS-vs-IND match from the hero card (real action #1) and separately opened its Info tab, which rendered a real Pitch Report card (real action #2). Navigated back home: `localStorage`'s `bawler:firstSessionQuest` read `{"followTeam":true,"openLiveMatch":true,"readPitchReport":true,"dismissed":false,"completionAnimated":true,"initialized":true}` and the floating card was gone from the page — confirming it auto-dismissed itself only after all three were checked and its own completion-celebration flag was set, not merely on completion.
7. Re-navigated directly to `/onboarding` as this now-established user: the route itself still renders the flow (it has no self-gate — only the home page's redirect logic, which is the actual delivered trigger contract, suppresses it). Re-navigating to `/` afterward confirmed the real, intended behavior: the home page did **not** redirect into onboarding again, matching `shouldShowOnboarding() === false` for a user with real follows. This distinction (route always reachable directly vs. the home page never auto-triggering it again) is noted for the record but was not something the original ask required fixing, and no behavior change was made here.
8. Cleared `localStorage` again for a full skip-everything run: tapped the step-level "Skip" on step 1 with zero follows → the "Follow a team to unlock this" locked-preview nudge appeared correctly (with working "Pick a team"/"Skip anyway" controls); tapped "Skip anyway." Step 2 rendered its own empty-state text ("Follow a team in the previous step to see suggestions here…") and its own "Skip," which was tapped — **no locked-preview repeated**, confirming the "shown once across the whole session" design decision live, not just in code. Answered the quiz "B"/"B"/"B" → resolved to "Test Purist," a second distinct persona confirmed live (in addition to "Boundary Hunter" above). The reveal correctly showed the generic "Setting things up" subtext (no team/player names, since none were followed) and landed cleanly on the home feed with all sections rendering normally and the checklist showing all three items unchecked — no crash, no broken section, in either skip-heavy path.

Both the tap/click-only interaction style (steps 2-6, 8 above) and the swipe/drag gesture path (the mouse-drag re-test in the root-cause section) were independently confirmed working against the real deployed page in this pass.

**Scope**: `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`, `package.json` (version bump). No other file touched.

## Fixed: England rendered a generic flag emoji instead of a real flag on onboarding's team-picker card — v1.0.167 (2026-08-07)

**Bug report.** On `/onboarding` step 1, every national team's circular avatar showed initials-looking text ("IN" for India, "AU" for Australia) except England, which showed a generic flag emoji (🚩-looking placeholder) instead. Reproduced live: England is card 3 of 16.

**Root cause, traced (not guessed) — and it overturned the bug report's own working assumption.** `components/onboarding/TeamCard.tsx` rendered `team.flagEmoji` -- a raw Unicode character -- directly for every national team. That depends entirely on the viewer's OS/browser having a matching color-emoji glyph:
- India's and Australia's flag emoji are simple *regional-indicator* sequences (their two-letter ISO codes, e.g. "I"+"N" for India). On this Chrome/Linux environment, which lacks a color-flag glyph for them, the unsupported-fallback rendering shows the sequence's two embedded letters as plain text -- "IN", "AU". This LOOKED like intentional two-letter initials, but was never a designed feature; it was an accidental, platform-dependent side effect of a missing emoji glyph.
- England's flag is a structurally different Unicode construction -- an Emoji Tag Sequence representing the England subdivision flag (not a country-code pair). Its unsupported-fallback is the generic black-flag base character, not letters. Same underlying defect (unreliable raw-emoji rendering), different, uglier failure mode -- not a special England-only bug.

Confirmed this wasn't a data gap (England's `flagEmoji`/`shortName`/`country` fields are all populated correctly in `lib/mockData.ts`, same shape as every other nation) -- the bug was purely in how the onboarding card chose to render a national flag.

**The rest of the app had already solved this correctly.** `components/MatchCard.tsx`, `components/SplitTeamBg.tsx`, and `components/FollowSheet.tsx` all render real flag *images* from flagcdn.com via a small `FLAG_ISO` code map (each file's own comment documents per-file duplication of that map as the established convention here), with England already correctly mapped to `"gb-eng"` -- MatchCard's own comment even notes this explicitly solves the Windows-rendering problem. `components/onboarding/TeamCard.tsx` never reused this; its own header comment claimed "no dedicated TeamBadge/TeamLogo component exists... confirmed by search," which was simply incorrect/stale.

**Decision point, surfaced to the user rather than assumed:** fixing this "generally" had two materially different, both-valid outcomes -- (a) match the app's own established flagcdn.com image convention (what every other surface does, English flag renders as a real image, but visually changes India/Australia's onboarding card from accidental "IN"/"AU" text to real flag images), or (b) compute literal two-letter text for every nation from data, preserving the "IN"/"AU" look. User chose (a) -- reuse the established, real, working pattern.

**Fix.** `TeamCard.tsx` now carries the same `FLAG_ISO` map (22 nations, matching `FollowSheet.tsx`'s full list) and renders `<img src="https://flagcdn.com/w160/{iso}.png">` for any national team with a map entry -- real image, works identically on every OS/browser, matches how flags render everywhere else in the app. Any national team without a `FLAG_ISO` entry (none exist among the curated 16 today) falls back to computed text (`team.shortName`), never to an emoji glyph -- so the fallback chain is now: real image → computed text, with no path that can render an unreliable Unicode glyph. Franchise teams are untouched (their shortName-in-circle badge was never broken).

**Safety process.** Dedicated branch (`fix/onboarding-england-flag`). `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched (baseline unchanged: md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports). Version bumped via `package.json` only (`lib/version.ts` derives from it; `scripts/version-check.ts` PASS).

**Scope**: `components/onboarding/TeamCard.tsx`, `package.json` (version bump). No other file touched. `components/MatchCard.tsx`, `components/SplitTeamBg.tsx`, `components/FollowSheet.tsx` (the other 3 flag-image render sites) were inspected and confirmed already correct -- not modified.

## Diagnostic: tab-switch stale-content bleed on match pages — root cause traced, NOT fixed yet, no code changed (2026-08-07)

**Scope of this entry**: diagnosis only, per explicit instruction. No files were modified as part of this investigation; `components/MatchTabs.tsx` and `components/MatchView.tsx` are unchanged.
