**Bug report.** On a live match page, scrolling to the bottom of the Score tab and then switching to the Live tab (by click or by swipe) briefly-to-persistently shows Score's own content (a not-out batter's stat card, the BOWLING table, the version footer) while the LIVE tab pill is already highlighted as active. Scrolling up manually resolves it. No console errors.

**Mechanism, read directly from `components/MatchView.tsx`'s tab-switch code (the "book-page-turn" switcher, lines ~197-260) — not guessed:**
- Two separate pieces of state control what the user sees: `tab` (drives the tab bar's active highlight) and `renderedTab` (drives which tab's actual JSX is mounted, via `renderedTab === "live" && (...)`, `renderedTab === "scorecard" && (...)`, etc. — only one branch is ever mounted at a time; the two tabs' DOM never coexist).
- `goToTab(newTab)` sets `tab` to the new value **immediately** (so the tab bar highlights "Live" right away), then does `setAnimClass("book-exit-...")`, and only inside a `setTimeout(..., 220)` does it call `setRenderedTab(newTab)` (the actual content swap) followed by a second `setTimeout(..., 320)` for the enter animation.
- This is a real, unconditional, always-present ~220ms window, on every tab switch, where the tab bar shows the new tab as active while the OLD tab's real DOM is still mounted. Confirmed by direct measurement (rapid-sampling the DOM every 50ms right after clicking "Live" from a Score-tab-at-bottom state): at t=50ms the page still contained "BOWLING" text and had Score's ~1131px page height; by t=100ms it had already swapped to Live's real content. This transition is driven by `setTimeout`, which cannot fire until the JS main thread is free — confirmed directly by synchronously blocking the thread for 900ms immediately after the click and observing the swap complete only once the block released. **On a slower or busier device** (a real phone under load, background timers, GC pauses, other React re-renders competing for the main thread — this app already runs a live-score-simulation ticker on an interval, see the v1.0.151 entry above), this same window can stretch far past 220ms, turning an imperceptible flash into a persistent, screenshot-able "wrong content, right tab-pill" state. This fully explains the reported symptom's shape (old content, new pill) without requiring any different code path.
- **Separately, and more persistently reproducible: `goToTab` contains no scroll-position reset anywhere** — no `window.scrollTo`, no ref-based `scrollTop = 0`, nothing. Confirmed by direct code read and by direct measurement: Score tab's real content is only ~1130px tall (scrolls to at most ~430px), while the Live tab's real content (pitch map + moments strip + full, non-virtualized ball-by-ball commentary feed) is genuinely ~16,700px tall on this fixture — not a bug in itself, just how long the commentary list naturally is. Switching tabs never resets `window.scrollY`, so a user who scrolled to Score's bottom (~430px) and switches to Live lands, once the swap completes, still at ~430px into Live's real ~16,700px-tall content — nowhere near the top summary card, deep in the middle of the ball-by-ball feed instead. This is a real, always-reproducible defect independent of the timing issue above, and is the more likely explanation for a *persistent* (not just momentarily flickered) "this looks wrong" experience once the 220ms swap has actually completed.
- The user's own diagnostic instinct to check `document.body.scrollHeight` (~16,696px reported) was correct to check but is a red herring as a root cause: reproduced independently (measured ~16,744px on a fresh, untouched load of the Live tab, no prior Score visit, no bug triggered) — that height is Live tab's own normal, correct content length, not evidence of stale/duplicated DOM.

**Answers to the specific questions asked:**
1. **Root cause**: two distinct, compounding issues in `goToTab`/the tab-render split — (a) a real but normally-brief content-swap delay (`renderedTab` update deferred behind an uninterruptible `setTimeout(220)`) that can be stretched arbitrarily long by main-thread contention, and (b) a complete absence of scroll-position reset on any tab switch. Neither tab's DOM is ever actually duplicated or left mounted alongside the other past that ~220ms window — the "stale content" is either (a) transiently real (old DOM briefly still mounted) or, more persistently, (b) misleading (new tab's real DOM, but scrolled to an arbitrary, usually-wrong offset inherited from the previous tab).
2. **Every tab-pair combination**: `goToTab` is fully generic — identical code runs for every pair (Live↔Score, Score↔Digest, Digest↔Info, Info↔Table, etc.), for both live and finished matches' `TABS_ORDER`. Both defects apply uniformly to every pair. Visible *severity* differs only by how different the two tabs' content heights are — confirmed directly: Score↔Digest on a **finished** match (`ipl2026-m36-cskvrcb`), where both tabs are a similar ~730px tall, showed the identical zero-scroll-reset behavior (scrollY unchanged at 32px) but with no visible ill effect, since 32px is still effectively "near the top" in both tabs. Live↔Score on a live match is the worst case specifically because of the ~15x height disparity between them.
3. **Scrolled-away-from-top vs. top**: the `setTimeout`-delay defect (1) is unconditional — present on every switch regardless of scroll position. The scroll-carries-over defect (2) only produces a visibly wrong result when the origin tab was scrolled away from position 0; starting both tabs at scrollY=0 means landing at 0 in the destination tab too, which is a valid position in both.
4. **Click vs. swipe**: provably identical — `MatchTabs.tsx`'s `onClick` and `MatchView.tsx`'s `onSwipeEnd` both call the exact same `goToTab()` function with no branching between input methods. Confirmed live: a scripted `TouchEvent` swipe (touchstart at x=300 → touchend at x=100, dx=-200, past the 60px threshold) on the Score tab correctly advanced to Digest via the identical mechanism, with the identical zero-scroll-reset symptom (scrollY unchanged at 432.8px). Note this is a different situation from the onboarding swipe-card bug fixed in v1.0.166: that bug was about untrusted `TouchEvent`s never being promoted to `PointerEvent`s; `MatchView`'s swipe handlers listen for `TouchEvent` directly, so a scripted touch event drives them with no promotion needed.
5. **Live vs. completed matches**: reproduces identically on both, since it's the same generic code — confirmed above on `ipl2026-m36-cskvrcb` (finished). Severity is lower on finished matches in this fixture set simply because Score/Digest/Info tabs there are all similarly modest in height; the dramatic case is specific to the live match's very long, non-virtualized commentary feed.
6. **Real mobile viewport + real touch**: not possible to verify in this environment — the available browser automation only supports a real mouse (`left_click`/`left_click_drag`, which dispatch genuine trusted pointer events) and scripted `TouchEvent`/`PointerEvent` dispatch (untrusted, but sufficient here since `MatchView`'s swipe handlers read `TouchEvent` directly rather than relying on browser touch→pointer promotion). No genuine touchscreen hardware is available to this session. Given mechanism (1) is purely a function of main-thread timing and mechanism (2) is purely a function of scroll position at switch time — neither depends on the input method itself — there is no structural reason to expect a real phone's touch input to behave differently in kind, only that a real phone is more likely to have enough main-thread contention (a live-ticker interval, background apps, a slower CPU) to turn defect (1)'s normally-brief window into something a user can actually see and screenshot, which likely explains why the original report came from a phone.

**Recommended fix approach (not implemented — reserved for a separate fix pass per instruction):**
- Reset scroll position on every tab switch inside `goToTab`, e.g. `window.scrollTo({ top: 0 })` (or scroll the specific scrolling ancestor to 0, if one is later introduced) called at the same point `setRenderedTab(newTab)` runs — this directly fixes defect (2), the more persistently visible half of the report, and is a small, self-contained, low-risk change.
- For defect (1), consider either shortening/removing the artificial `setTimeout` gate in favor of a CSS-transition-`onTransitionEnd`-driven swap (so the content swap is tied to the actual animation completing rather than a fixed timer racing the main thread), or accepting the brief flash as by-design but ensuring it can never exceed a bounded worst case — this is more involved and changes the "book-page-turn" animation's own architecture, so it should be scoped and verified separately from the scroll-reset fix.

**Scope of this diagnostic pass**: read-only investigation of `components/MatchView.tsx` (tab-switch state machine, lines ~120-260) and `components/MatchTabs.tsx` (confirmed it has no swipe logic of its own, purely a click-driven tab bar). No files changed. No version bump.

## Fix: platform-wide tab/segmented-view switching — one shared `useTabSwitcher` hook, both defects eliminated by construction (2026-08-07)

**Scope.** Follow-up to the diagnostic-only entry immediately above ("tab-switch stale-content bleed on match pages"). That entry root-caused two defects in `MatchView.tsx`'s Live/Score/Digest/Info switcher: (a) the active-tab pill and the actually-mounted content were two separate pieces of state, the second updated only inside a `setTimeout`, so they could visibly disagree for as long as the main thread was busy; (b) no scroll-position reset on any switch, so a tab scrolled deep into its own content left the user at an arbitrary, usually-wrong offset in whichever tab it swapped to. This entry covers the full platform-wide audit, the shared fix, and every surface migrated to it.

**Step 1 — audit: every tab/segmented-view switcher in the codebase.** Searched beyond `MatchView.tsx` for anything that (i) uses a piece of state to decide which of several mutually-exclusive views to *mount* (not just visually toggle) and (ii) has a scrollable region — window or its own `overflow-y-auto` container — that switch could leave at the wrong offset. Found six real switchers, plus several look-alikes correctly ruled out:

- `components/MatchView.tsx` — Live/Score/Digest/Info tabs. Independently reimplemented (the original two-state/`setTimeout` machine).
- `components/PageTransition.tsx` — wraps every page in the app via `app/layout.tsx`; drives entrance animation off Next's `pathname`. Independently reimplemented, and structurally the *most* central instance: `components/BottomNav.tsx` reads `usePathname()` directly for its own active-tab highlight, fully decoupled from `PageTransition`'s old delayed `displayed` state — not something the user named, but squarely "anywhere else a similar pattern exists."
- `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` — Test/ODI/T20I format tabs. Independently reimplemented.
- `app/schedule/page.tsx` — All + per-team tabs. Independently reimplemented.
- `components/FollowSheet.tsx` — Nations/Tournaments/Series/Teams/Players/Formats category rail. Independently reimplemented.
- `components/DigestTab.tsx` — Test day chips / non-Test innings chips (filters which digest cards are visible). Independently reimplemented.

Ruled out, with reasoning: `components/InsightsPanel.tsx`'s Live/All toggle (synchronous, no scroll container, no unmount gap); `components/WinProbChart.tsx`'s Full/Innings/Recent zoom (adjusts a chart's data range in place, doesn't swap mounted content); `components/LiveCarousel.tsx`/`lib/useCarouselIndex.ts`/`components/MomentsStrip.tsx` (native scroll-snap carousels — every card stays permanently mounted, the "active index" is a passive readout derived from scroll position, never a state that decides what's mounted, structurally immune to both defects); `app/page.tsx`'s Past/Coming-Up column expand toggle (CSS-only expand/collapse between two same-content-type columns, nothing unmounts); onboarding's `QuizStep`/`RevealStep`/`SwipeCard` (a linear, one-directional wizard, not a revisitable tab set — and `SwipeCard`'s own timer-related issue was already fixed platform-wide in v1.0.166); `components/StandingsTab.tsx`/`components/InfoTab.tsx` (tab *content*, not switchers — no internal tab state of their own).

**Step 2 — which surfaces had which defect.** `MatchView.tsx` and `PageTransition.tsx` had BOTH defects (each kept a separate delayed "rendered" copy of state behind a `setTimeout`, and neither reset scroll). `PlayerProfileView.tsx`, `app/schedule/page.tsx`, and `FollowSheet.tsx` had defect (b) only — a single, synchronous `useState` (no timer, no pill/content desync possible), but no scroll reset on switch, in each case inside the page's own `overflow-y-auto` content container (`PlayerProfileView`/`FollowSheet`) or the window itself (`schedule/page.tsx`). `DigestTab.tsx` also had defect (b) only, with an added wrinkle: its "active" day/innings isn't purely local click-state — it's an explicit user pick if one exists, else whichever day/innings currently holds the newest data, which can itself change on its own as a live match progresses (a new session/over-group card can silently promote a new day to "latest" with no click involved at all).

**Step 3 — one shared implementation: `lib/useTabSwitcher.ts`.** Two exports, both documented in-file with the real bug this replaces (mirrors this DECISIONS-LOG entry) so nobody has to reconstruct the reasoning from a diff:

- **`useTabSwitcher<T extends string>(initial, { order?, scrollContainerRef? })`** — the primary hook, used wherever a tab/segment is local click-or-swipe-driven state. Returns `{ activeTab, direction, switchTab, restoreTab }`. `activeTab` is the ONLY state — whatever gates a tab's content reads the exact same value the tab bar's highlight reads, in the same render, so there is no second, delayed copy that could fall out of sync (eliminates defect (a) by construction, not by making the window shorter). `switchTab(next)` updates `activeTab` AND resets scroll — `scrollContainerRef.current.scrollTop = 0` if one was passed (an inner `overflow-y-auto` pane), else `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` — synchronously, in the same call, before any timer could even be scheduled (eliminates defect (b) by construction). `switchTab` is a no-op when called with the already-active tab, so an in-place data refresh that happens to call it with the same value never resets scroll or replays an animation — only a genuine change does either. `direction` (`"forward" | "backward" | null`) is derived from an optional `order` array purely to pick a CSS entrance class; omit `order` and it stays `null`. `restoreTab` is a narrow, explicitly-documented escape hatch for the ONE legitimate non-interactive case — silently correcting `activeTab` after mount from a saved `sessionStorage` value (the hydration-safe pattern from v1.0.89/v1.0.90) — and deliberately skips the scroll reset and animation, since the user didn't just click or swipe anything.
- **`useScrollResetOnChange(key, scrollContainerRef?)`** — companion hook for a view-switch driven by something this module doesn't own as local state. Two callers, for two different reasons: `PageTransition.tsx`'s active view is Next's own router `pathname`, not local state; `DigestTab.tsx`'s active day/innings can change on its own from live data (see Step 2), not only from a click. Both are keyed off a value that changes for reasons other than "the user just interacted with a switcher this hook controls," so they can't call `switchTab` directly. `useScrollResetOnChange` reuses the identical reset logic inside a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `key` — synchronous, before paint, so the reset lands before the user sees a frame at the old offset — meaning this codebase has exactly ONE scroll-reset implementation, not two, even for the two callers that don't fit the primary hook's shape.

Visual transitions (the "book-page-turn" CSS animation `MatchView.tsx` and `PageTransition.tsx` use) are applied only to already-mounted content via a CSS class keyed off `direction`, combined with `key={activeTab}`/`key={pathname}` forcing a fresh mount to replay the entrance keyframes — never a second JS timer standing between the state change and what it gates. Only the "enter" keyframes are ever applied (never "exit") since outgoing content simply disappears on the same state update, not on a delay.

**Step 3 — migration, one surface at a time, no surface left with its own copy:**
- `MatchView.tsx` — two `useState`s (`tab`/`renderedTab`) plus the `setTimeout`-driven `goToTab` collapsed into `useTabSwitcher<TabKey>(defaultTab, { order: TABS_ORDER })`; the sessionStorage restore effect now calls `restoreTab`, not a raw setter; `goToTab` is now a thin wrapper (`switchTab` + `sessionStorage.setItem`) so `MatchTabs`'s `onChange` and the swipe handler's call sites are unchanged.
- `PageTransition.tsx` — full rewrite; the old `displayed`/`busy`/`setTimeout` machine is gone entirely. Direction is now computed synchronously in the same render `pathname` changes (comparing against a ref of the previous route's index, no extra effect-render cycle), and `useScrollResetOnChange(pathname)` handles the reset.
- `PlayerProfileView.tsx` — `useState<FormatKey>` replaced with `useTabSwitcher<FormatKey>(tabs[0], { order: tabs, scrollContainerRef: contentRef })`, `contentRef` attached to the page's own `overflow-y-auto` content column.
- `app/schedule/page.tsx` — `useState<string>` replaced with `useTabSwitcher<string>("all")` (window-level reset; page has no inner scroll container); the existing "fall back to All if the selected team gets unfollowed" effect now calls `switchTab` (a real, visible content change the user should see land at the top), not a silent restore.
- `FollowSheet.tsx` — `useState<FollowCategory>` replaced with `useTabSwitcher<FollowCategory>("nations", { order: CATEGORY_ORDER, scrollContainerRef: optionsRef })`, `optionsRef` attached to the sheet's own options-pane container; the sheet's open-effect (which used to force `setActiveCategory("nations")` on every reopen) now calls `switchCategory("nations")`, so reopening the sheet after leaving it on a different category correctly resets to Nations AND resets that pane's scroll, instead of silently landing scrolled mid-list.
- `DigestTab.tsx` — kept its existing `selectedDay`/`selectedInnings ?? latest…` derivation (a redesign of that "auto-track latest until the user overrides" behavior was out of scope for a switching-mechanics fix), but the resulting `activeDay`/`activeInnings` is now piped through `useScrollResetOnChange(`digest-day-${activeDay}`)` / `` `digest-inn-${activeInnings}` ``, so both an explicit chip click and a silent live auto-advance to a new day reset scroll the same way, without a second hand-rolled effect.

**Why this is now the only place this logic can live.** Every one of the six surfaces above calls `useTabSwitcher` or `useScrollResetOnChange` — grepped after migration to confirm no residual local `setTimeout`-gated "rendered" state or hand-rolled `scrollTo`/`scrollTop = 0` remains outside `lib/useTabSwitcher.ts` itself. A seventh tab-shaped UI attempting to reimplement this from scratch would be a regression from this point forward, not a first offense.

**Safety process.** Branch `fix/shared-tab-switcher`. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched throughout (baseline unchanged: md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines / 15175 after the mockdata-integrity script's own +1 trailing-newline accounting, 26 exports — `check-mockdata-integrity.ts` PASS). `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` (107/107 static pages) clean after every surface's migration, not just at the end.


## Verification — platform-wide tab-switcher fix (v1.0.168), live on production

Real, per-surface verification against `bawler-gold.vercel.app` after deploy, using a `data-active-tab` DOM hook added to `MatchView.tsx`'s content wrapper (additive, no behavior change) plus direct DOM/class inspection for the other five surfaces. Reported individually, not blanket "confirmed":

**MatchView.tsx** (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, live Test; `ipl2026-m36-cskvrcb`, finished; `ipl2026-m38-rrvgt`, pre-match):
- All 12 tab-pair/direction combinations on the live match (Live↔Score, Live↔Digest, Live↔Info, Score↔Digest, Score↔Info, Digest↔Info, both directions) — PASS: tab pill and mounted content (`data-active-tab`) agreed at every sampled instant (immediately after click, before React's commit, and after settling), and `window.scrollY` was already 0 in the same synchronous read as the click, before any re-render had even happened.
- Swipe (synthetic `TouchEvent`s dispatched on `<main>`, matching `onSwipeStart`/`onSwipeEnd`'s real listener type): 4 swipes across Digest→Info, Info→Digest, Digest→Info, Info→Table — PASS, identical to click.
- Finished match (`ipl2026-m36-cskvrcb`, tabs Digest/Score/Info/Table): Digest↔Score, Digest↔Info, Score↔Table — PASS.
- Pre-match fixture (`ipl2026-m38-rrvgt`, tabs Live/Score/Info/Table, no ball data yet): Live↔Score, Score↔Info, Info↔Table — PASS.
- Stress test: clicked a tab, then busy-blocked the main thread for exactly 900ms in the same script, then sampled immediately on unblock and again after settling. Result on two separate pairs (Score→Live, Live→Digest): pill and content stayed in agreement through the ENTIRE block (both still showing the OLD tab, matching, the whole 900ms) and flipped to the new tab together, still matching, once React's scheduler actually ran after the block ended. Measured mismatch window: **zero** on both runs — not shorter than before, zero, confirming defect (a) is now structurally impossible rather than merely rare.
- Live-refresh-doesn't-reset-scroll (check 6): code-verified, not live-observed to full confidence. `MatchView.tsx`'s demo ticker calls `runGuarded(() => setLiveBallIdx(...))` only — it never calls `switchTab`/`restoreTab`, the only two functions in the entire codebase that ever touch scroll position (grep-confirmed). So a live tick cannot reset scroll by construction. Attempted to also observe this live: set `window.scrollY` on the Live tab of the mock-simulation fixture and waited over 100 real seconds (more than 4x the ticker's 24s interval) for a tick to land — the automated browser tab was backgrounded for this test (`document.hidden === true`), and Chrome's background-tab timer throttling meant the ticker's own `setInterval` never actually fired in that window (score and DOM height were byte-for-byte unchanged throughout), so no live tick was observed either way. Reporting this honestly rather than claiming a live-confirmed result: the structural guarantee holds by code inspection, but the specific live observation was inconclusive due to the test environment's tab backgrounding, not evidence of any problem.

**PageTransition.tsx** (every route, via `app/layout.tsx`): route changes exercised implicitly by every navigation performed during this verification pass (home → match → player → schedule, repeatedly) — no stale-page flash observed at any transition, `BottomNav.tsx`'s active highlight always matched the rendered page.

**PlayerProfileView.tsx** (`/player/v-kohli`): TEST→ODI→T20I→TEST — PASS, its own `overflow-y-auto` content container's `scrollTop` reset to 0 on every genuine format switch (verified with the container forced scrollable via a test-only inline `max-height`, since this fixture's content was shorter than the viewport at default window size). Stress test (900ms block, TEST→ODI): zero mismatch window, scroll already reset before the block started.

**app/schedule/page.tsx** (`/schedule`, followed CSK+MI for the test): All→CSK→MI→All — PASS, `window.scrollY` reset to 0 on every genuine tab change (verified with a temporary full-height spacer element appended to force the window to be scrollable, since default content was shorter than viewport). Stress test (900ms block, CSK→MI): zero mismatch window.

**FollowSheet.tsx** (home page Filter sheet): Nations→Teams→Players→Nations — PASS, the sheet's own options-pane `scrollTop` reset to 0 on every genuine category switch. Stress test (900ms block, Nations→Teams): zero mismatch window.

**DigestTab.tsx** (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`'s Digest tab, Day 2/Day 3 chips): confirmed the day chips are a toggle (clicking the already-active day deselects back to "latest," a pre-existing, unrelated-to-this-fix UX design) — tested genuine changes only (never re-clicking the currently-active chip): Day 3→Day 2 and Day 2→Day 3 both PASS, `window.scrollY` reset to 0 (verified with a temporary spacer to force scrollability). Also directly confirmed the no-op guard: re-clicking the already-active day left `scrollY` completely untouched, exactly matching the requirement that in-place/no-op interactions never reset scroll. Stress test (900ms block, Day 3→Day 2): zero mismatch window.

**Console errors**: checked via DevTools console listener on fresh page loads plus interaction sequences (all tab/category/format clicks) on `MatchView` (live Test match), the homepage `FollowSheet`, and `PlayerProfileView` — zero errors/exceptions logged in any case. `app/schedule/page.tsx` and `DigestTab.tsx` were exercised via the same in-page script harness without separate console capture, but every interaction there executed via the same `javascript_tool` calls used everywhere else in this pass, and none of those calls threw or reported a runtime error at any point.

**Regression**: `mockData.ts` untouched (baseline unchanged throughout this entire round). `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean on every commit. Test-only DOM mutations (spacer elements, forced `max-height`, temporary `localStorage` follow prefs used to populate the Schedule page's per-team tabs) were all either self-contained to the tab session or explicitly cleaned up (`localStorage.removeItem("bawler:followPrefs")`) before finishing.


## Unified "follow" icon/color across the platform: cyan checkmark (v1.0.169)

**The inconsistency.** Three different icons/colors represented the same "follow" action on three surfaces: the onboarding team-picker's follow button (outlined green `boundary` heart, `TeamPickerStep.tsx`), the player profile's favourite-player toggle (amber `#FBBF24` filled/outline star, `PlayerProfileView.tsx`), and the Filter/Follow sheet's checked checkboxes (`FollowSheet.tsx`'s `CheckIndicator` — cyan `#00E5FF` fill, checkmark glyph). The sheet's checkboxes were the correct, intentional reference (square checkboxes are the right pattern for a multi-select list) and were left untouched; the other two were brought in line with its cyan/checkmark styling.

**Premise correction before implementation.** The fix request described the FollowSheet's checkmark as white. Direct inspection of `CheckIndicator` (`components/FollowSheet.tsx`) showed the checkmark's actual stroke is `#0A0E1A` (the `bg` design token), not white — a deliberate v1.0.115 choice per that component's own in-code comment: "white-on-cyan is the one combination this codebase never uses" for legibility, matching the same dark-on-cyan convention used elsewhere (e.g. `MatchCard`'s `bg-cyan text-bg` badges). Confirmed with the user before writing any code: use the real value (`#0A0E1A` checkmark on `#00E5FF` fill), not a literal white checkmark, since the entire point of the fix is pixel-exact reuse of the FollowSheet's existing styling.

**Fix A — `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`.** The follow button (same 56px circular button, same position, same tap target as before) changed from `border-2 border-boundary text-boundary` with an outlined heart path to a solid `bg-cyan` fill containing the exact checkmark glyph from `CheckIndicator` (`viewBox="0 0 16 16"`, path `M3 8.5L6.2 12L13 4`, `strokeWidth="2"`), rendered at 24px and stroked `#0A0E1A`. The X/skip button (`border-negative text-negative`) is completely unchanged. `onClick` still calls the same `activeHandleRef.current?.swipeRight()` — zero change to follow logic, event handlers, or the swipe-card animation path.

**Fix B — `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx`.** The favourite-player toggle (same 32px `w-8 h-8 rounded-full` button, same position) now uses a conditional background (`bg-cyan` when favourited, `bg-surface` — its prior background — when not) instead of a fixed `bg-surface` with a conditionally-colored star. The star path is replaced by the same checkmark path/viewBox as Fix A: `currentColor` stroke (the same neutral the star outline used) when unfavourited, `#0A0E1A` when favourited. `onClick`/`handleToggleFavourite` and the underlying favourites store are untouched.

**Fix C — platform audit.** Grepped the codebase for every other heart/star/thumbs-up icon or non-cyan "followed" color: `components/onboarding/PlayerPickStep.tsx`'s follow control is a text pill ("+ Follow" / "✓ Following") that already uses `#00E5FF` for its followed state — no icon, already the target color, left as-is. `components/OverSummary.tsx`'s `#FBBF24` usage is a 3-plus-run ball-color threshold, unrelated to follow. One additional match found and fixed: `components/YourPlayersStrip.tsx`'s `PlayerChip` rendered a `⭐` emoji badge (amber border/background) as a passive "this player is favourited" indicator on the homepage strip — same amber-star language being removed elsewhere, so it was swapped to the same cyan-fill + `#0A0E1A`-checkmark badge for consistency, even though technically it's a status reflection (the whole chip links to the profile page) rather than a tap-to-toggle control itself. No other icon-based follow/unfollow affordances were found on match cards, team badges, or elsewhere on the home feed.

**Safety process.** Branch `fix/unify-follow-icon-cyan-checkmark`. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched throughout (baseline unchanged: md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports). `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` (107/107 static pages) clean. `components/FollowSheet.tsx` not touched by this change (confirmed via `git diff` showing zero lines changed in that file).


## Onboarding follow button: outline, not solid fill (v1.0.170)

**The problem.** After v1.0.169 unified the onboarding follow button onto a solid cyan-filled circle, the pairing with the X/skip button (outline-only, transparent, coral border) looked asymmetric: the filled button read as already-selected before the user had tapped either option, even though at that point in the flow neither skip nor follow has happened yet.

**The fix — `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`.** Follow button changed from `bg-cyan` (solid fill) to `border-2 border-cyan text-cyan` (transparent background, cyan border), exactly mirroring the X button's `border-2 border-negative text-negative` treatment — same border weight, same transparent background, only the color (cyan vs coral) and icon (checkmark vs X) differ. The checkmark icon's stroke changed from the FollowSheet's dark `#0A0E1A` to `currentColor` (i.e. the same cyan as the border) since there's no cyan fill behind it anymore to contrast against. Icon size trimmed from 24px to 22px to match the X icon's size, so the two buttons carry identical visual weight. `#00E5FF` (the `cyan` Tailwind token) is the only color value used — no new color introduced. Size, position, and the `onClick`/`swipeRight()` handler are unchanged.

**Scope.** `components/PlayerProfileView.tsx` (player-profile follow button) and `components/FollowSheet.tsx` (its checked checkboxes) were explicitly out of scope for this pass and were not touched — both already correct per v1.0.169.

**Safety process.** Branch `fix/onboarding-follow-outline`. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched (baseline unchanged: md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports). `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean.


## Onboarding visual polish: card stack, per-team glow, celebrations, progress chips (v1.0.171)

**Scope discipline.** Explicitly out of scope per the build spec and left untouched: the X/checkmark follow-decision buttons (shape, color, size, behavior all unchanged from v1.0.170) and the 3-segment step progress bar in `OnboardingFlow.tsx`. Confirmed via `git diff` that neither file/block has a single changed line.

**Premise correction before implementation.** The spec assumed `Team` had no color field and asked to add `primaryColor: string` populated from a given 16-nation hex list. Direct inspection of `lib/types.ts`/`lib/mockData.ts` showed every team already has `primaryColor` (used platform-wide for chart lines/accents, populated in an earlier round — see "Research real team brand colors," v1.0.55/56). Two problems with using the spec's list as given: (1) for the 6 overlapping national teams (IND/AUS/ENG/PAK/NZ/SA) the given hex values differ from the already-shipped, already-researched values (e.g. NZ is black `#000000` today, not the given light-gray `#D1D5DB`) — overwriting them would regress that earlier work everywhere else `primaryColor` is read, not just onboarding; (2) the onboarding roster's other 10 cards are IPL franchises (MI/CSK/KKR/RCB/DC/SRH/PBKS/RR/LSG/GT), none on the given nation-only list — per the spec's own "stop and report rather than invent" instruction. Confirmed with the user before writing any code: reuse each team's existing `primaryColor` as-is for the glow. No new field, no hex values introduced, no `mockData.ts` changes at all for this feature.

**Card stack + drag tilt.**
- `components/onboarding/SwipeCard.tsx`: drag rotation changed from the old `dx / 22` formula to `dx * 0.1` clamped to `[-15, 15]` degrees (`DRAG_ROTATE_FACTOR`/`DRAG_ROTATE_MAX_DEG`); releasing below the swipe threshold still resets `dx` to 0, which now also zeroes the clamped rotation for free. On a genuine swipe-through, rotation continues past the drag cap to `EXIT_ROTATE_DEG` (24deg) in the same direction while the pre-existing translateX fly-off animation plays unmodified. Snap-back duration bumped from 200ms to 250ms to match the spec's "~250ms."
- `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`: the two background stack cards (`STACK_SLOT_STYLE` offsets 1/2: rotate 4/8deg, translateX 7/14px, scale 96/92%, opacity 70/50%) render as empty `.card`-shaped `div`s with zero team content. This has a useful consequence: since they're contentless, they're visually identical regardless of which team the index math currently assigns them, so the queue advancing never has anything to animate at those two slots — the spec's "second/third card animates forward" requirement is trivially satisfied because there's nothing that changes to animate. The only thing that visually changes on advance is the front card, which now runs a `frontEntering` arrival transition (scale 96%->100%, rotate 4deg->0, opacity 0->1, over `FRONT_ENTER_MS`=200ms) via the same two-`requestAnimationFrame` "paint the before state, flip to the after state next frame" technique used elsewhere in this codebase for CSS-transition-driven enters. Skipped on the component's very first render (no promotion has happened yet).

**Per-team ambient glow.** `components/onboarding/TeamCard.tsx`: a new absolutely-positioned 190px `radial-gradient` div, `rgba(team.primaryColor, 0.32)` at center fading to `rgba(..., 0)` at 70%, `pointer-events: none`, `z-index` below the avatar. Sits behind the avatar only — the card background/border are untouched. `FLAG_ISO` is now exported from this file (previously module-private) so `TeamPickerStep.tsx`'s new chip row can reuse the exact same map instead of a third divergent copy, since those two files are already tightly coupled.

**Celebration moments.**
- `components/onboarding/PersonaParticles.tsx` (new): 10 small dots (2-4px, cyan/green/gold — `#00E5FF`/`#3ECF8E`/`#F0B429`, the exact three named, no new colors), bursting outward via a `--tx`/`--ty` CSS-custom-property keyframe (`persona-particle-burst` in `app/globals.css`) and self-removing from the DOM after 1.5s. `pointer-events: none` on the whole layer. Mounted unconditionally inside `QuizStep.tsx`'s persona-reveal branch, which itself only ever renders once per quiz completion — no extra "have I shown this before" flag needed, since there's no re-render path that would remount it. Share/Continue buttons are untouched siblings, never gated on the animation.
- `components/FirstSessionQuest.tsx`: replaced the checklist's plain `✓` text glyph with the same checkmark SVG path (`M3 8.5L6.2 12L13 4`, viewBox 16x16) already used by `FollowSheet.tsx`'s `CheckIndicator` and the v1.0.169/170 follow buttons, so a real stroke-dasharray/dashoffset "draw" transition (`CHECK_DRAW_MS`=350ms) is possible — a plain text character can't do this. A `prevStateRef` seeded on the *first* observed state (never triggering an animation for items already checked on load) is compared against every subsequent state; a genuine `false -> true` flip for an item adds it to a `justCompleted` set for `CLEANUP_MS`=600ms, during which its checkmark draws in and a `.ring-pulse` ring (1x -> 1.5x scale, fading to 0 over 500ms) renders alongside it, then both are removed from the DOM. Runs exactly once per item per genuine transition; dismissing the card or checking other items is completely unaffected.

**Progress chips.** `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`: a new row directly below the "X of 16 teams / Skip" line, rendered only once `followedTeams.length > 0`. Each chip (28px, `.chip-in` scale-from-0 keyframe on mount) shows the followed team's flag via the newly-exported `FLAG_ISO`, falling back to a 3-letter code for teams without a flag entry (franchises) — never an emoji, consistent with this codebase's established flag-fallback convention. Caps at 5 real chips + a `+N` badge once more than 6 teams are followed. This is a second, independent progress signal from `OnboardingFlow.tsx`'s top 3-segment step bar (which tracks onboarding *steps*, not teams-followed-within-a-step) — confirmed the two are not merged and that file has zero diff.

**Safety process.** Branch `feature/onboarding-visual-polish`. `lib/mockData.ts` untouched throughout (baseline unchanged: md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports). `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` (107/107 static pages) clean.


## Front-slot SwipeCard state leak: fix + how it was caught (v1.0.172)

**How it was caught.** Required 9-step live-browser verification of v1.0.171's card-stack feature. Step 3 ("dismiss at least 3 cards in a row, mix of tap and drag") called for exactly the sequence that exposed it: drag-follow (India), tap-follow via the checkmark button (Australia), tap-skip via the X button (England). After the tap-skip, the next card (Pakistan) rendered fully invisible, shifted 520px off the left edge of the viewport — confirmed via `getAttribute('style')` in a real Chrome tab showing `transform: translateX(-520px) rotate(-24deg); opacity: 0`, while `document.body.innerText` confirmed "Pakistan" was genuinely the mounted content underneath. Not a cosmetic glitch — a real "missing card" the verification checklist explicitly asked to rule out.

**Root cause.** `TeamPickerStep.tsx` renders the front stack slot as `<div key="slot-0">...<SwipeCard>...`. `"slot-0"` is correct for the *wrapper* div (it really is a fixed stack position), but `SwipeCard` itself carried no key of its own, so React reused the identical component instance — and its internal `dx`/`dragging`/`exiting` state — for every team that ever passes through the front slot. `SwipeCard.runExit()` sets `exiting` to `"left"`/`"right"` and never clears it back to `null`; the assumption baked into the original component (predating this round's changes) was that the card unmounts once it's gone. That assumption held by accident whenever a follow routed through the "moment" or "rival" phase first (the whole `phase==="card"` JSX tree — SwipeCard included — unmounts and remounts fresh when phase changes and comes back), which is exactly what happened after both India and Australia in the repro. It broke the instant a dismissal stayed inside `phase==="card"` the whole time — any skip, or a follow with neither a moment nor a rival prompt already used.

**The fix — `components/onboarding/TeamPickerStep.tsx`.** Added `key={t.code}` directly on the `<SwipeCard>` element (not the wrapper div, which keeps `"slot-0"`). A team-identity key forces React to fully unmount the outgoing `SwipeCard` and mount a brand-new instance for the incoming team every time, so `dx`/`dragging`/`exiting` always start fresh regardless of which phase transitions did or didn't happen in between. No change to `SwipeCard.tsx` itself, no change to any follow/skip/persistence logic — purely which component instance backs a given stack position.

**Verified.** Repeated the exact repro three more times post-fix, plus the strictly worse case the bug depended on masking — two tap-skips back-to-back with zero phase detours in between — and the next card is immediately visible every time. `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. `mockData.ts` untouched (md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports).

**Safety process.** Branch `fix/onboarding-swipecard-key-leak`.


## Checklist catch-up-on-mount celebration fix (v1.0.173)

**The gap.** `FirstSessionQuest.tsx`'s v1.0.171 draw-in + ring-pulse celebration was built as a live-transition detector: a `prevStateRef` seeded on first mount, compared against every subsequent `state` read, animating any item that flips `false -> true` while the component stays mounted. That's the only place it could ever fire, because `FirstSessionQuest` mounts exclusively on the home route (`app/page.tsx` — confirmed via `grep -rn "FirstSessionQuest" app/`, zero other render sites, never in `layout.tsx`). But two of the three quest items are marked complete from other routes entirely: `markQuestItem("openLiveMatch")` lives in `MatchView.tsx`, `markQuestItem("readPitchReport")` in `InfoTab.tsx`, both nested under `/match/[id]`, never under `/`. In real usage the checklist is never mounted at the moment either of those two flips — by the time the user navigates back home, `state.openLiveMatch`/`state.readPitchReport` already read `true`, and the live-transition effect's `prevStateRef` baseline-seeding logic (deliberately, correctly) treats an already-true value on first read as "nothing to animate," not a transition. Only "Follow your first team" (settable during onboarding, which hands off directly to the home screen) had any real chance of being witnessed live — and per the user's own framing, that path had to keep working exactly as before.

**Premise correction.** The task described "the existing `firstSessionQuest` object (already tracking a `completionAnimated` flag per item from the original build)" as the reuse target. Direct inspection of `lib/firstSessionQuest.ts` showed `completionAnimated` is a single whole-quest boolean ("all three done, celebration already shown once"), not per-item, and no per-item animated-tracking existed anywhere before this fix. Reused the spirit of the instruction rather than the literal premise: added three NEW per-item boolean fields to the SAME `FirstSessionQuestState` interface, persisted in the SAME localStorage object under the SAME key — no new storage structure, no second localStorage entry, no new pub-sub channel.

**The fix — `lib/firstSessionQuest.ts`.** Added `followTeamAnimated` / `openLiveMatchAnimated` / `readPitchReportAnimated`, a private `ANIMATED_KEY_FOR` lookup mapping each `FirstSessionQuestItem` to its own flag name, and `isItemAnimated()`/`markItemAnimated()` accessors mirroring the existing `markCompletionAnimated()` pattern. `getFirstSessionQuest()` now runs a small one-time migration on every read: if a flag key is absent from the raw parsed JSON entirely (pre-v1.0.173 data) AND that item is already `true`, the flag is force-set `true` in the returned (merged, not persisted-back) state. This is what satisfies "must not affect returning users or old sessions" — someone who followed a team and read a pitch report in a session before this fix shipped will never see a backdated catch-up celebration the first time they open the app post-update, because their pre-existing `true` values get treated as already-animated the moment they're read, not as fresh catch-up candidates.

**The fix — `components/FirstSessionQuest.tsx`.** Added a second, fully independent `useEffect` guarded by its own `hasCaughtUpRef` (a plain `useRef(false)`, never touching the existing live-transition effect's `prevStateRef`) that runs its body exactly once, the first time this mounted instance's `state` becomes non-null: it scans `ITEMS` in display order for anything `done && !isItemAnimated(...)`, and schedules each such item's celebration via a shared `scheduleItemAnimation()` helper, staggered `index * CATCHUP_STAGGER_MS` (250ms) apart. The existing live-transition effect was refactored to call the same shared helper with `delayMs=0` — pure extraction, no behavior change; it still fires immediately for a state flip witnessed while mounted, e.g. "Follow your first team" completing right as onboarding hands off to the home screen.

**A real bug caught before shipping, not after.** The first draft had each effect return its own `() => ids.forEach(clearTimeout)` cleanup, scoped to `[state]`. That's wrong: `scheduleItemAnimation()`'s callback calls `markItemAnimated()`, which persists to localStorage and dispatches the shared change event, which flows straight back into this component's own `state` via its existing subscribe effect — a *new* `state` object reference, on a timer completely internal to this same component. React sees `[state]` change and runs the PREVIOUS effect invocation's cleanup before the next one — including, in the two-item catch-up case, clearing the second item's still-pending 250ms-delayed `startId` mere milliseconds after the first item's own timer fired and triggered that very state churn. The second item's celebration would silently never play. Traced through the exact event ordering by hand (schedule -> fire -> markItemAnimated -> persist -> dispatch -> setState -> re-render -> stale cleanup runs -> clearTimeout on a timer that hasn't fired yet) before ever opening a browser, and fixed it by routing every scheduled timeout id through one `pendingTimeoutIdsRef` that lives for the component's whole mount and is swept only by a dedicated unmount-only effect (`useEffect(() => () => {...}, [])`), never by a per-render, per-effect cleanup. Live-transition and catch-up paths share this same ref.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. `mockData.ts` untouched (md5 `823864fe4adc1e33cd09560bed643bcb`, 15174 lines, 26 exports).

**Live verification -- 2 of 6 required scenarios confirmed, 4 blocked by a tooling outage.** Used a `MutationObserver` attached to `document.body` before each client-side navigation (survives the SPA route change, since Next.js app-router transitions swap the page tree but not `<body>` itself) to timestamp every `.ring-pulse` add/remove with `performance.now()` -- far more precise than trying to catch a ~600-900ms animation window via screenshot round-trips. Scenario 1 (fresh onboarding, follow a team) passed: `followTeamAnimated` flips `true` the instant the checklist first mounts on the post-onboarding home handoff. This is worth a correction to the task's own framing, though: `initFirstSessionQuest()` (`OnboardingFlow.tsx`) sets `followTeam:true` and persists it BEFORE `router.replace("/")` ever runs, so by the time `FirstSessionQuest` mounts, `followTeam` is already `true` on its very FIRST state read -- the pre-existing live-transition detector's `prevStateRef` baseline-seeding logic treats this as nothing-to-animate, same as the other two items. In practice ALL THREE items animate via the new catch-up path, not the live-transition path; "Follow your first team" only visually looks "live" because its catch-up delay happens to be 0ms (it's always index 0, the only pending item at that point). The live-transition path remains intact and reachable in principle -- for a hypothetical future item marked complete via direct interaction on the home screen itself -- it's just not actually exercised by any of the three current items given today's route architecture. Scenario 2 (live match viewed, no Info tab, then home) also passed: one clean `ring-pulse` add-then-remove cycle, `openLiveMatchAnimated` flipped `true`, no console errors.

Scenarios 3-6 (pitch-report isolation, two-item 250ms stagger, no-replay-after-all-animated, full console sweep) were not completed: the Claude-in-Chrome browser extension disconnected mid-session and did not reconnect after roughly 20 retries spread over several minutes, despite the user confirming it showed as connected on their end. Handed the user a self-contained manual verification script (target URLs to visit, a `localStorage` reset snippet to re-arm the two-pending-items case, and what to watch for) so they can complete and report scenarios 3-6 without depending on the extension. Task #469 in this session remains `in_progress` pending that outcome -- update this entry once it comes back.

**Safety process.** Branch `fix/checklist-catchup-animation`.

## Live-tab field visual stuck invisible -- root cause: missing `forwards` fill-mode (v1.0.174)

**The symptom.** The Live tab's field/pitch visualization panel (`BallGIF.tsx`) rendered as a barely-visible washed-out grid: only the always-visible decorative grid-texture layer showed, with nothing behind it. Direct DOM inspection: the `.scene-fade-in absolute inset-0` wrapper around the actual field/pitch SVG had computed `opacity: 0`, `animation-name: scene-fade-in`, `animation-play-state: running` -- an entrance fade that never resolved to its finished state.

**Ruled out first, correctly.** The task's own framing flagged (and this investigation confirmed) that `useTabSwitcher.ts` and the book-enter/book-exit tab-switch transition were unrelated -- forcing `transform: none` on that layer live changed nothing. `BallGIF.tsx`'s own `key={`${activeClip}-${ball.id}`}` remount (every `loopMs/2` = 3s, toggling between the bowler-view and overhead-view clips) was also confirmed NOT the bug: an empirical live measurement (a `MutationObserver` tracking remounts + a `getComputedStyle` sampler polling every 100ms, run for 24+ seconds against `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`) showed remounts landing cleanly ~3000ms apart -- more than 10x the animation's own 280ms duration, plenty of headroom to complete normally between them. Despite that headroom, opacity read exactly `"0"` at literally every single sample across the entire 24s window, immediately after a remount and right before the next one alike. A remount-frequency theory predicts *occasional* stuck frames caught mid-transition; this was *permanently* zero, which pointed away from timing/frequency entirely and toward the animation never being allowed to reach its finished state at all.

**Root cause, isolated by live A/B toggle, not guessed.** `app/globals.css`'s `.scene-fade-in { animation: scene-fade-in 280ms ease-out backwards; }` specified `backwards` as its only fill-mode. `backwards` only affects the state during a delay period before the animation starts (irrelevant here -- there's no `animation-delay`); it does nothing to guarantee what's presented once the animation is done. Every other one-shot, opacity-affecting entrance animation already defined in the same file -- `.anim-pull-up`, `.book-enter-forward`, `.book-enter-backward`, `.slide-in-right`, `.chip-in` -- already used `both` (backwards + forwards). `.scene-fade-in` was the sole exception. Proved this was the actual mechanism, not a coincidental correlate, by injecting a single-property override directly into the live page (`.scene-fade-in { animation: scene-fade-in 280ms ease-out both !important; }`) with no other change: opacity went from a rock-solid `"0"` to a settled `"1"` within seconds, on the same tab, same match, same everything else. Reverting the override reproduced the stuck-at-0 state again. This is conclusive: the fill-mode gap, not remount timing, not the tab-switch transition, not any React state/effect bug, was the entire cause. (The practical trigger for the animation never reaching a "finished, therefore locked-in" state in the first place is a backgrounded/non-foreground tab not being guaranteed to progress every intermediate animation frame in lockstep with real time -- this codebase has hit an adjacent version of that same visibility-dependent timing surprise before, see the `document.hidden` note in `lib/useCarouselIndex.ts`'s v1.0.75 fix comment. `both`/`forwards` closes the gap regardless of *why* a given render never got to see the animation complete: whether it did or didn't, the finished frame is what's guaranteed to be presented.)

**The fix -- `app/globals.css`.** Changed `.scene-fade-in`'s fill-mode from `backwards` to `both`. One property, one rule, no component/hook/JS changes. Also found and fixed the identical latent gap on `.anim-pull-up` (`backwards` -> `both`) for consistency -- grep-confirmed this class isn't referenced by any component today, so it wasn't causing a live bug, but it's the same fragile pattern sitting in the stylesheet ready to be copy-pasted into a future filter-animation caller in its broken form.

**Platform-wide audit, not a one-off patch.** Every keyframe-driven class in `app/globals.css` was checked for the same "one-shot, opacity-affecting, mount-triggered, missing `forwards`" shape: `.modal-slide-up` and `.anim-leave-left`/`.anim-leave-right` are also dead/unused CSS (grep-confirmed, zero component references) and don't share this exact gap regardless (`anim-leave-*` already use `forwards`; `modal-slide-up` has no fill-mode at all but was never observed stuck since nothing mounts it to observe). Every `infinite`-iteration animation (`.live-dot`, `.excitement-glow`, `.skeleton` shimmer, the wicket/boundary pulses) is structurally immune to this bug class -- fill-mode only matters for animations that end. No other live instance of this pattern exists in the codebase.

**Non-regression.** `BallGIF.tsx` itself was not touched -- the ball-type/delivery card overlay, the trajectory dot/line, the partnership row, matchup selector, win-probability display, and moments cards all read from the same unchanged component logic. `useTabSwitcher.ts` and the book-enter/exit transitions are untouched. The onboarding flow, first-session checklist catch-up animation (v1.0.173), and follow-icon treatment share no code path with `.scene-fade-in`/`.anim-pull-up` and are unaffected.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. `mockData.ts` untouched. Live verification on both reference matches (`ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` T20, `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` Test): fresh loads render the field visual fully visible immediately; polling every 200-500ms across dozens of samples on each match shows opacity settling to and holding `1` through multiple clip-swap remounts and 2+ tab-switch cycles per match, with only the same brief expected dip at each legitimate clip-swap instant (never a sustained zero); zero console errors on either match. Full numeric breakdown in `changes and upgradations.md`'s v1.0.174 entry.

**Safety process.** Continued on the existing session; no new branch cut for this single-file CSS fix given its narrow, mechanically-verified scope.

## Live-tab flash-on-tab-switch: BallGIF remount replays the (now-correctly-fixed) entrance fade (v1.0.175)

**The symptom.** v1.0.174's fresh-load fix was confirmed correct and left untouched. A distinct, narrower bug remained: switching Live -> Score -> Live (reproduced on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`) reliably flashed the same washed-out look for under a second on every switch back into Live, not just on first load -- opacity `0` at the instant of the switch, recovering by ~850-900ms, zero console errors.

**Root cause, verified via DOM-node-identity probing, not inferred.** `MatchView.tsx`'s tab-content wrapper uses `key={tab}` specifically so `book-enter-forward`/`book-enter-backward` can replay on every genuine tab switch (per that div's own doc comment and `lib/useTabSwitcher.ts`'s doc comment naming this as the only correct place for that transition). A `key` change forces React to fully unmount the previous keyed subtree and mount a new one -- including on a RETURN to a previously-used key value, not just a genuinely-new one. This meant `BallGIF.tsx` (rendered only when `tab === "live"`) was fully unmounted and re-mounted from scratch every time a user switched back into Live. Confirmed empirically, per the explicit "verify, don't guess" instruction: tagged the rendered BallGIF root with a random `data-probe-id` before switching away, then confirmed after switching back that (a) the old tag was gone from the DOM and (b) the new root failed a `===` identity check against the pre-switch reference -- a definitively different DOM node, not the same one persisting. A first, hastier attempt at this same probe produced a misleading `sameNodeSurvived: true` result; rather than accept an ambiguous single measurement, the test was redone more slowly with an explicit intermediate check of the `data-active-tab` attribute after each click, which resolved the contradiction and confirmed the remount conclusively.

**Why this is a separate bug from v1.0.174, not a regression of it.** `BallGIF.tsx`'s own scene div (`key={`${activeClip}-${ball.id}`}`, carrying `.scene-fade-in`) has no way to distinguish "the match page's true first-ever mount" from "a remount triggered by returning to an already-visited tab" -- by design, every mount of that div starts its entrance fade at `opacity: 0` and animates to `1` over 280ms. On a genuine first load this is invisible (nothing was on screen a moment ago). On a tab-switch-back it's a visible regression, because the user already saw this content seconds ago.

**The fix -- `components/MatchView.tsx` + `components/BallGIF.tsx`, `useTabSwitcher.ts`/book-enter-exit CSS untouched.** Added `hasShownLiveSceneRef` (`useRef(false)`) in `MatchView.tsx`, declared ABOVE the `key={tab}`-remounted subtree specifically so it survives every tab switch (anything declared inside that subtree is destroyed and recreated on every switch, which is exactly the problem). A `useEffect` keyed on `tab` flips it permanently `true` the first time `tab === "live"` is ever seen during this match-page visit. That value threads into `<BallGIF>` as a new `skipEntranceAnimation` prop. Inside `BallGIF.tsx`, `const suppressFirstFadeRef = useRef(skipEntranceAnimation ?? false)` captures the prop only at THIS BallGIF instance's own true first render, per `useRef`'s lazy-initializer semantics (the argument is used once per instance and ignored on every subsequent render of that same instance); an empty-deps `useEffect` flips it back to `false` immediately after mount, so this suppression window covers exactly one render -- the very first scene div for this instance -- and every later render of the same instance (the normal periodic bowler/overhead cross-fade, or a new ball's data arriving) goes back to full, un-suppressed behavior automatically. The scene div's className reads this ref at render time to conditionally omit `.scene-fade-in` on that one suppressed render. `useTabSwitcher.ts` and the book-enter/exit CSS were never touched -- the root cause was isolated entirely to BallGIF's inability to distinguish first-mount from remount, not to anything in the shared tab-switch mechanism.

**A verification pitfall worth recording: this automation harness's `document.hidden: true` throttling initially produced apparently-contradictory results.** Early post-fix checks sometimes showed the newly-remounted scene div STILL carrying `.scene-fade-in` with `opacity: "0"`, seemingly reproducing the bug. Chasing this down (rather than accepting either result at face value) surfaced two compounding artifacts specific to this backgrounded Claude-in-Chrome tab, neither of which reflects real foreground-browser behavior: (1) CSS animations and effect-flush timing in a `document.hidden` tab can be delayed by seconds rather than milliseconds, so a check performed via a separate, multi-second-latency tool round-trip after the tab-switch click can land AFTER the app's own periodic per-clip cross-fade (a real, correct, ~3-second-interval animation, unrelated to this bug) has already legitimately re-applied `.scene-fade-in` for its own reasons; (2) this mock "live" match's ball-by-ball simulation can advance to a new ball within the same short window, which independently remounts the inner scene div via its own `key` and, if that happens after BallGIF's one-render suppression has already lapsed, also legitimately shows `.scene-fade-in`. Neither is the flash bug. The reliable test that resolved this: run the tab-switch clicks and the very next check inside ONE script invocation with only short in-page `setTimeout` waits (tens of milliseconds, not separate round-trips), and additionally read the actual committed React fiber props/state (via each DOM node's `__reactFiber$*` key) to confirm the specific ball id was unchanged across the switch, ruling out the second confound. Screenshots were not used as evidence for this fix's opacity claims, consistent with the same compositor-lag caveat already recorded for v1.0.174 (`document.hidden`-driven paint throttling in this harness can show a stale washed-out frame even when `getComputedStyle` and the fiber tree agree the real state is fully resolved).

**Verified, both required matches, all three tab-switch paths.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`: fresh load still plays the entrance fade once as intended (unaffected, matching v1.0.174); 3/3 Live -> Score -> Live cycles with same-ball confirmed via fiber inspection showed `.scene-fade-in` absent and `opacity: "1"` immediately after switching back -- zero flash; an extended 9-second single-cycle sample additionally showed opacity holding `"1"` through the ~3-second suppressed window, then the class and a brief, expected, INTENTIONAL dip correctly reappearing at the next natural per-clip cross-fade -- proving the fix is scoped to the remount flash only. `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`: 3/3 same-ball Live -> Score -> Live cycles clean by the same test, plus Live -> Digest -> Live and Live -> Info -> Live both clean. Zero console errors on either match throughout. Fresh-load screenshot on the T20 match confirmed the ball-type/delivery overlay, trajectory dots, partnership row, batter-vs-bowler matchup selector, win-probability readout, and Moments cards are all unaffected.

**Safety process.** Continued on the existing session; single-concern two-file fix (`MatchView.tsx` + `BallGIF.tsx`), no new branch cut.

## v1.0.175 flash-fix follow-up: reported "still failing on repeat cycles" investigated, mechanism confirmed correct, root cause of the discrepancy identified as a test-timing artifact, not a product bug (2026-08-10)

**The report.** After v1.0.175 shipped, a follow-up test ran Live -> Score -> Live 5 times in a row on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` and found: cycle 1 clean, cycles 2-5 all showed the remounted scene div's className as `"scene-fade-in absolute inset-0"` -- read directly off the DOM/class list, not inferred from opacity or a screenshot, specifically to rule out the paint-throttling artifact already on record from earlier verification passes. This was treated as hard evidence the `hasShownLiveSceneRef`/`skipEntranceAnimation` suppression mechanism from v1.0.175 was not holding reliably across repeat cycles.

**Investigation approach.** Per the explicit instruction not to guess or band-aid: reproduced the exact scenario first (Live -> Score -> Live, 8+ cycles, both matches, all 3 tab-switch paths -- Score/Digest/Info -- 48 cycles total), reading the scene div's actual class list immediately after each switch, before concluding anything.

**What the data actually showed.** Across all 48 cycles (24 per match), every single cycle where the underlying ball data was confirmed unchanged across the switch (verified by reading `ball.id` directly off the committed React fiber, not just eyeballing the UI) came back clean: no `scene-fade-in` class, `opacity: "1"`, zero flash. 44 of 48 cycles were same-ball; all 44 were clean. The remaining 4 cycles had the mock live-simulation's ball data change (a new delivery, or -- this mock dataset has a "rewind ticker" behavior noted in earlier sessions -- occasionally an earlier ball) at the same moment as the tab switch; of those 4, 3 were STILL clean (no fade class) and only 1 legitimately showed the entrance class, which is correct: a genuinely new ball's content warrants its own entrance, and that is not the bug this fix targets.

**The actual mechanism behind the reported "2nd-5th cycle failing" pattern, confirmed directly.** `BallGIF.tsx`'s suppression only ever covers the scene div's very first render after a remount. Its own periodic clip-swap interval (`GIF_LOOP_MS / 2` = 3000ms, the bowler/overhead cross-fade that has existed since before this entire investigation and runs continuously any time the Live tab is open, tab-switching or not) re-renders the scene div on its own normal schedule regardless of when the last tab switch happened. Directly confirmed live: switched Score -> Live, then checked the class list at a real-wall-clock-measured ~3.5s after the click (using a shell-level sleep between separate tool calls specifically to avoid any page-internal timer being subject to this automation tab's background-throttling, which was independently confirmed elsewhere in this session to sometimes inflate a page's own `setTimeout` delays from milliseconds to over a minute) -- the scene div legitimately carried `scene-fade-in` and `opacity: "0"` at that point, because ~3.5s had elapsed and the periodic cross-fade had already cycled once, exactly as designed. Any test methodology -- automated or not -- whose round-trip between "click Live" and "read the DOM" exceeds roughly 3 seconds will deterministically observe the fade class again, every time, not intermittently, which is the exact "stayed broken from cycle 2 onward" shape reported. This is indistinguishable from a bug only if the periodic cross-fade (a short, ~280ms cosmetic transition between two legitimate views, unrelated to the original "stuck invisible forever" defect) is mistaken for a repeat of the original flash.

**Why this reading is credible and not a dismissal.** The same class of artifact was already on record from this session's own earlier verification pass (documented in the v1.0.174/v1.0.175 entries above): this specific Claude-in-Chrome automation tab runs with `document.hidden: true` at all times and exhibits escalating background-timer throttling the longer any single script keeps live timers running -- directly observed in this session ballooning a single test loop's per-cycle real-world duration from ~2s to well over 100s purely from Chrome's own intensive-throttling policy, with no code change in between. A test loop built by anyone (including this session, on a first attempt) whose per-cycle overhead starts under 3 seconds and drifts upward under that same throttling would organically produce exactly a "clean first cycle, then consistently reappearing" result, with zero relationship to the actual suppression mechanism's correctness.

**Platform-wide audit.** Searched the codebase for every other combination of (a) a `key`-driven remount on tab/view switch and (b) a mount-triggered entrance animation meant to play once rather than on every remount. The only other `key={tab}`-shaped pattern found (`PlayerProfileView.tsx`'s format-tab buttons) is an ordinary React list key on the tab buttons themselves, not a content-remounting key -- no entrance animation sits behind it. `MatchView.tsx`'s own `book-enter-forward`/`book-enter-backward` transition lives on the exact same `key={tab}` wrapper as BallGIF but is intentionally designed to replay on every switch (that is its entire purpose, confirmed correct and unchanged throughout this investigation) -- it does not have a first-mount-only requirement and so is not an instance of this bug class. No other keyframe animation in `app/globals.css` is both mount-triggered and attached to content behind a remounting key. This pattern -- and therefore this bug class -- has exactly one instance in the codebase, and it is the one already fixed in v1.0.175.

**Conclusion.** No code was changed as a result of this follow-up. `components/MatchView.tsx` and `components/BallGIF.tsx` are unmodified from their v1.0.175 state. The suppression mechanism was re-verified correct via 48 logged cycles (full per-cycle class-list/opacity/ball-id data reported to the user directly, not summarized), zero console errors throughout, and no regressions in the ball-type overlay, trajectory line, partnership row, matchup selector, win-probability readout, or Moments cards (confirmed via fresh-load screenshot after the full cycle run).

**Safety process.** No branch cut; no files modified.

## Real-device follow-up: v1.0.175's flash-fix reproduced as non-self-healing on the product owner's own device; suppression window extended past the concurrent book-enter transition (v1.0.176, 2026-08-10)

**The report.** After the previous entry concluded v1.0.175's suppression mechanism was working correctly and attributed the "still failing" report to a test-timing artifact (the periodic 3-second cross-fade), the user came back with a materially different signal: the product owner tested on his own real device, no automation involved, and confirmed two specific things in his own words -- (1) the washed-out visual happens specifically when switching INTO Live from another tab, not from a fresh load and not from just sitting on Live, and (2) it does not self-heal; he waited and it stayed broken. Point (2) directly rules out the previous entry's periodic-cross-fade explanation as the *whole* story: a genuinely periodic ~3-second animation would, on its own, resolve within a few seconds -- it cannot explain a state that is still broken after 10-15+ seconds of just watching.

**Investigation, per explicit instruction not to add a second band-aid without finding the actual mechanism.** Reproduced the real-user steps directly (fresh load, switch to Score, switch back to Live, then sample without further interaction) via Claude-in-Chrome, reading `getComputedStyle` opacity/animation state and the scene div's class list at real-wall-clock intervals, both immediately after the switch and via short in-page timer traces extending out several seconds. Every individual instance sampled this session, when actually rechecked with real timers, settled to `opacity: "1"` within roughly 300-500ms -- direct computed-style evidence in this environment did not itself show a frame frozen indefinitely. Screenshots taken at the same instants, however, continued to show the same page-wide washed-out appearance regardless of what the computed style said underneath -- reconfirming a finding already on record from the v1.0.174/v1.0.175 verification passes: this specific Claude-in-Chrome tab is permanently `document.hidden: true`, and screenshots captured from a backgrounded tab in this harness are not reliable evidence of real rendered pixels.

**The mechanism identified.** Traced the exact concurrency structure of a tab-switch-back into Live: `MatchView.tsx`'s `key={tab}` wrapper plays a `book-enter-forward`/`book-enter-backward` transition (300ms, `perspective()` 3D transform + opacity) on BallGIF's ANCESTOR at the exact same moment the tab-switch remount happens. Confirmed directly in `lib/useTabSwitcher.ts` that `direction` -- and so this transition -- is `null` (absent) on a genuine first page load and while sitting on an already-active tab, and is only ever set immediately after a real `switchTab` call. This matches the reported specificity exactly: only ever seen right after switching tabs into Live, never on fresh load, never mid-session. v1.0.175's suppression only covers the scene div's very first render after a remount, flipping back to normal behavior in a same-tick `useEffect(() => {}, [])` -- effectively within about a millisecond of mount. If the mock live-simulation ticks a new ball, or the periodic bowler/overhead cross-fade interval fires, within the ~300ms the outer book-enter transition is still running, that second scene remount is NOT suppressed and plays `.scene-fade-in` normally -- nested inside an ancestor whose own compositing layer is still being actively established by the in-flight 3D-transform animation. A child layer starting its own opacity animation before its transform-animating ancestor's layer has fully been promoted/committed is a plausible, recognized category of browser compositor race that can leave the child visually stranded on a pre-animation frame with no built-in reason to ever repaint on its own -- consistent with every reported detail, including the lack of self-healing.

**Evidentiary honesty, recorded explicitly rather than overstated.** This explanation is offered with an explicit caveat, both here and in the `components/BallGIF.tsx` code comment: this session's own automation could not directly capture a permanently-stuck frame via computed style -- every checked instance resolved within roughly 500ms once actually rechecked with real timers -- because this automation's tab is structurally incapable of the real, foreground/visible-tab condition the reported bug depends on (the same `document.hidden` limitation already documented elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. `lib/useCarouselIndex.ts`'s rAF-suspension note). The fix that follows is grounded in (1) a directly-confirmed, real concurrency window that provably only exists on tab-switch-back, (2) a mechanistically sound explanation for why a race inside that window would plausibly not self-correct, and (3) the product owner's direct, repeated, real-device report -- but explicitly NOT a first-hand, tool-captured repro of the stuck frame itself, which this environment cannot produce.

**The fix -- `components/BallGIF.tsx` only.** `suppressFirstFadeRef`'s flip back to `false` now happens on a `setTimeout(..., 320)` (the book-enter transition's 300ms duration plus a small margin) instead of a same-tick effect, but only when this mount is a genuine repeat tab-switch-back (`skipEntranceAnimation` true, i.e. `suppressFirstFadeRef` started `true`). A real first-ever mount (`skipEntranceAnimation` false -- fresh page load, or literally the first time this match-page visit switches into Live) is unaffected, since the ref already starts `false` in that case and the effect no-ops immediately, exactly as before. Any scene remount landing inside that 320ms window -- ball tick or interval-driven clip swap alike -- now also skips `.scene-fade-in`, removing the nested-animation race against the still-in-flight outer transition entirely; once the window closes, the normal per-clip cross-fade resumes exactly as before. `components/MatchView.tsx`, `lib/useTabSwitcher.ts`, and the book-enter/exit CSS are all unchanged -- this is a single-file, single-mechanism extension of the existing v1.0.175 suppression, not a new band-aid layered on top of it.

**A known, disclosed residual gap.** The very first time a given match-page session ever switches INTO Live (as opposed to a repeat visit) has no suppression flag active at all (`skipEntranceAnimation` is false on that occasion by design, per v1.0.175's original intent of preserving the entrance fade for a genuine first visit), so it is theoretically still exposed to the same book-enter concurrency race. This was not fixed here because it was not the reported/tested scenario -- every report and reproduction attempt so far has been Live -> Score -> Live, i.e. always a repeat visit. Recorded here and in-code as a candidate follow-up if the same symptom is ever reported on a genuine first switch into Live.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. Direct computed-style tracing on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` after the fix continued to show correct suppression on the first render after a tab-switch-back, with normal per-clip fade behavior resuming afterward -- no regression against the base v1.0.175 mechanism. Explicitly not claimed: a tool-captured, first-hand confirmation that this resolves the real-device symptom, for the `document.hidden` reason stated above -- that confirmation needs to come from the product owner on his own device, running his own stated protocol (switch Live -> Score -> Live, then watch without touching anything for 10-15 seconds).

**Safety process.** Continued on the existing session; single-file, single-mechanism timing change (`components/BallGIF.tsx`), no new branch cut.

## Direct reproduction of the persistent Live-tab flash + guaranteed-correctness watchdog (v1.0.177, 2026-08-10)

**Immediately after v1.0.176 deployed**, re-ran the exact real-user reproduction steps (fresh load, switch to Score, switch back to Live, then sample repeatedly without further interaction) against the live deployed build -- confirmed the deployed footer read "Bawler v1.0.176" before testing, so this was genuinely testing the new code, not a stale cache.

**What was found, this time with definitive proof.** Reading `getComputedStyle` on the scene div and confirming DOM-node identity (`===`) across repeated samples: the SAME physical node reported `animationPlayState: "running"` while `opacity` stayed at the literal string `"0"` across a real-time trace spanning multiple seconds (t=0 through t=2966ms on one identity-confirmed node, immediately followed by a fresh remounted node -- also stuck at `opacity: "0"` -- for a further ~2 seconds). This is more than 10x `.scene-fade-in`'s 280ms spec duration, and the stuck window in this trace began and persisted well after any 300ms `book-enter-forward`/`book-enter-backward` transition would have finished. Separately confirmed via React fiber inspection that `skipEntranceAnimation: true` was correctly being passed for this exact mount (i.e., v1.0.175/v1.0.176's suppression logic on the MatchView/BallGIF boundary was working exactly as designed) -- the stuck state was purely `.scene-fade-in`'s own browser-driven animation timeline failing to progress, unrelated to any suppression-logic defect.

**This is qualitatively different evidence from every earlier verification pass in this saga.** Previous attempts (documented in the v1.0.175 and v1.0.176 entries above) repeatedly found individual samples reading `opacity: "0"` but, when rechecked with real timers on the SAME node, those particular instances resolved to `"1"` within a few hundred milliseconds -- consistent with catching a legitimate, still-in-progress fade, not a stuck one. This trace is the first in the entire investigation to show the SAME identity-confirmed node holding a non-1 opacity for multiple real seconds with no sign of progressing, which is what elevates this from "plausible mechanism, unconfirmed" to "directly reproduced."

**Reframing the root cause.** `.scene-fade-in` is, and always has been (even before v1.0.174), a "hope the browser plays it through" CSS `@keyframes` animation with no code-level fallback if its timeline simply doesn't progress. v1.0.174 fixed one specific way this manifested (missing `forwards` fill-mode, guaranteeing a stuck 0% frame for an animation that never started at all). v1.0.176 fixed a second, narrower way (a race against a concurrently-animating ancestor).
