This is a third, more fundamental way: independent of fill-mode and independent of any concurrent ancestor animation, the browser can simply fail to advance the animation's own timeline in real time. The most likely trigger in this automation environment is Chromium deprioritizing compositor-driven animation timelines for a `document.hidden` tab (this session's tab is permanently backgrounded), but the underlying fragility -- an animation with no fallback if the browser ever deprioritizes it, for any reason -- is not inherently automation-specific, and is a substantially better fit for the product owner's real-device report (persistent, does not clear on its own) than either of the two previous, narrower fixes.

**The fix -- `components/BallGIF.tsx` only, additive to v1.0.176.** Added a `ref` to the scene div and a `useEffect` scoped by the same deps that define the div's own `key` (`activeClip`, `ball.id`), so it re-arms exactly once per genuine remount. 400ms after a non-suppressed mount (280ms animation spec + margin), it unconditionally sets `element.style.animation = "none"` -- which overrides the running keyframe animation, since an inline `none` always wins over an author-stylesheet `animation` shorthand for the same property -- immediately followed by `element.style.opacity = "1"`. This guarantees the correct visible end state regardless of whether `.scene-fade-in`'s own timeline ever actually completes on its own; if the animation already finished normally (the common case), this is a harmless no-op. Uses `setTimeout`, not `requestAnimationFrame`, on purpose: this codebase already established, in `lib/useCarouselIndex.ts`, that rAF is fully suspended (not merely throttled) on a hidden/backgrounded tab while `setTimeout` continues to run. v1.0.176's suppression-window logic is untouched and remains in place -- the two fixes are complementary: v1.0.176 reduces how often a risky concurrent-animation window is even created, and this watchdog guarantees correctness for every `.scene-fade-in` instance that does start, for whatever reason its own timeline might stall.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. Post-deploy, re-ran the identical direct reproduction methodology (DOM-node identity + real-time `getComputedStyle` polling) that caught the pre-fix stuck state, this time against the v1.0.177 build. Result: switching Live -> Score -> Live and sampling the SAME identity-confirmed node across a real-time trace out to ~8 seconds showed `opacity: "1"` at every single sample point, through multiple further remounts (`sameNode` correctly flipping to `false` partway through as the periodic clip-swap continued normally) -- a direct contrast to the pre-fix trace, which showed the same test protocol producing a flat, unrecovering `opacity: "0"` for 3+ seconds. A second repeat cycle hit a CDP tooling timeout mid-script (this session's own previously-documented background-tab timer escalation, not a page bug); the very next short, separate check immediately after showed the scene had legitimately re-entered a fresh fade (`opacity: "0"`, a new remount, expected) and, one second later, a follow-up check confirmed the watchdog had already corrected it to `opacity: "1"` with `style="animation: ... none; opacity: 1;"` visible in the inline style attribute -- i.e., caught the watchdog in the act of correcting a real instance, not just observing an already-settled state. Zero console errors throughout. Fresh screenshot after the cycles confirmed no regression in the score header, ball-type/delivery card, partnership row, matchup selector, win-probability readout, or Moments cards.

**Safety process.** Continued on the existing session; single-file, additive fix (`components/BallGIF.tsx`), no new branch cut.

## "For You" badge copy honesty: explicit follows vs. the onboarding default (v1.0.182, 2026-08-11)

**The bug, confirmed directly.** On a test account that skipped team selection and skipped player selection during onboarding, the homepage still showed a "FOR YOU" badge on a live match with the subtext "Because you follow T20." The account's actual stored `FollowPrefs`: `teams: []`, `formats: ["T20", "T20I", "Hundred"]`. The copy reads as if the user made a deliberate, personal choice to follow T20 when, by their own account, they made no such choice.

**Tracing the mechanism -- the finding contradicts the bug report's own hypothesis.** The bug report speculated that these three formats "get auto-assigned as a fallback default when a user skips onboarding entirely." Direct code tracing (grepping every write site to `FollowPrefs.formats` across the entire repo) found this is NOT what happens -- there is no fallback-default assignment anywhere in the codebase prior to this fix. The only place `formats` is ever written is `components/onboarding/QuizStep.tsx`'s `persistFormatTags()`, called exclusively from `answer()` when a user completes all 3 questions of the cricket-persona quiz -- which maps to one of 6 fixed personas in `PERSONA_TABLE` (`lib/onboardingQuiz.ts`), each carrying its own `formatTags`. The exact set observed, `["T20", "T20I", "Hundred"]`, is precisely Boundary Hunter's (or Death-Over Closer's) `formatTags` -- meaning the reported test account almost certainly answered the quiz for real (or, more likely given the timing, clicked through it back before v1.0.181 added a Skip control to the quiz, when it had no bypass at all and every onboarding completion necessarily ran through `persistFormatTags()`). This is intentional, by-design behavior, documented in `lib/onboardingQuiz.ts`'s own top-of-file comment: the quiz IS the app's format-preference-capture mechanism, "there is no separate plain preference-settings step." `skipQuiz()` (v1.0.181's `SKIP_PERSONA`) never calls `persistFormatTags()` and carries `formatTags: []`, so the CURRENT build's true "skip everything" path (team, player, and quiz) left a user with zero followed formats -- no badge shown at all, not a mislabeled one.

**A genuine ambiguity, resolved with the user before writing code.** The fix's own spec explicitly listed "the quiz" as one of three explicit-choice channels (alongside the team-picker and the "Follow your cricket" settings sheet) -- but its own required test also expected that completing the quiz, after skipping team and player selection, should still produce "Popular in X" wording. Those two instructions conflict: if quiz answers are explicit, a real quiz completion should read "Because you follow X"; if the test's literal expectation must hold, quiz answers can't always be explicit. Rather than silently pick one interpretation for a decision with real product implications, this was surfaced to the user directly with both options laid out. **Decision: quiz answers are treated as explicit** (per the spec's literal wording) -- a user who genuinely completes the quiz gets "Because you follow X," unchanged from today. Only a brand-new fallback (below), for users who skip team, player, AND the quiz, gets "Popular in X." This means the literal "complete the quiz" sub-case in the required test sequence will show "Because you follow X," not "Popular in X" -- a deliberate, acknowledged deviation from that one test's literal wording, in favor of the spec's explicit categorization and the more defensible product behavior (a real quiz answer is a real choice).

**Implementation.** `lib/followPrefs.ts`: added `FollowPrefs.defaultFormats: MatchFormat[]`, always a subset of `formats`, threaded through `emptyFollowPrefs()`/`sanitizeFollowPrefs()`/`prefsEqual()` the same way every other category already is. Added `DEFAULT_FALLBACK_FORMATS = ["T20", "T20I", "Hundred"]` (matching the exact set from the original bug report) and `applyOnboardingFallbackIfNeeded()`, called once from `OnboardingFlow.tsx`'s `finishOnboarding()` immediately before `markOnboardingComplete()` -- after every step has already had its chance to write a real follow. It's a no-op whenever the user has ANY real follow already (`hasAnyFollow()`); otherwise it assigns the fallback set into both `formats` and `defaultFormats`. `getForYouReason()`'s format branch (still lowest priority, still gated by the exact same `prefs.formats.includes(match.format)` check `qualifyMatch()` uses) now returns `"Popular in {format}"` when that format is flagged in `defaultFormats`, else the existing `"Because you follow {format}"`. `components/FollowSheet.tsx`'s `toggle()` additionally clears a format out of `defaultFormats` whenever the user manually taps it (on or off) in the "Follow your cricket" sheet, so a deliberate re-confirmation there is never left silently flagged as a default.

**Why the gate is "zero follows of any kind," not "zero formats specifically."** This makes the fallback trigger exactly the "skip everything" case the product decision was actually about (skip team, skip player, skip/never-complete the quiz) -- not merely "quiz was skipped," which would also fire for a user who explicitly followed 5 teams but happened to skip the quiz, diluting an already-personalized account with an unnecessary default. A genuine mixed state (an explicit follow alongside a default-fallback format) is still reachable in practice -- e.g. full-skip onboarding (gets the default formats), then later explicitly following a team through the Follow sheet -- and was used for exactly that test below.

**Non-regression.** `qualifyMatch()`, `isTier1Match()`, `isAnyMatch()` are byte-for-byte untouched and never read `defaultFormats` -- confirmed by diff. This guarantees the SET of matches surfaced as "for you" cannot change due to this fix for any already-existing followPrefs state; the new fallback only ever adds signal for accounts that previously had none. Onboarding's team-picker, player-picker, quiz content, quiz's own Skip control, progress bar, and every prior round of fixes are untouched.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean. Isolated logic verification via a localStorage-mocked `tsx` script (not committed, scratch-only) confirmed: (1) full-skip onboarding produces `formats`/`defaultFormats` both `["T20","T20I","Hundred"]`, and `getForYouReason()` on a T20 match returns exactly `"Popular in T20"`; (2) a simulated honest quiz completion (`formats` set the same way `persistFormatTags()` would) leaves `defaultFormats: []`, and the same match returns exactly `"Because you follow T20"`; (3) a mixed account (default fallback formats plus a separately, explicitly-followed team) resolves each match's reason independently and correctly -- the explicitly-followed team's own match returned `"Because you follow RCB"` while an unrelated T20 match returned `"Popular in T20"`, both from the exact same `FollowPrefs` object, confirming the distinction is genuinely per-preference, not per-account.

**Doc sync addendum -- live verification results (2026-08-11, same day, v1.0.182 deployed).** Ran all 3 required sequences against the deployed build at `bawler-gold.vercel.app`, driving real onboarding flows via actual DOM button clicks (Skip on team-picker's locked-preview prompt, Skip on player-picker, Skip on the quiz), and a real Follow-sheet interaction -- not simulated state:

1. Fresh onboarding, skip team (via the locked-preview "Skip anyway" path), skip players, skip the quiz -> landed on home with `formats`/`defaultFormats` both `["T20","T20I","Hundred"]` in real `localStorage`. All 4 live "for you" badges shown (`ipl2026-l2-rcbvcsk`, `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`, `ipl2026-l3-gtvrr`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live`) read exactly "Popular in T20" -- none read "Because you follow T20". (Per the resolved ambiguity above, the "complete the quiz" sub-case of this test was intentionally not run as originally worded, since quiz answers are treated as explicit -- see that discussion for why.)
2. Fresh onboarding, explicitly followed India (tapped the checkmark "Follow this team" button on the first card, worked through its moment card and rival prompt), then skipped players and the quiz -> landed on home with `nations: ["IND"]`, `formats: []`. The one live "for you" match (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`) read exactly "Because you follow India" -- unchanged from pre-fix wording.
3. Mixed case: took the sequence-1 account (default fallback formats already active) and, from the actual "Follow your cricket" settings sheet, explicitly followed RCB. Result: RCB's own live match (`ipl2026-l2-rcbvcsk`) correctly switched to "Because you follow RCB" (team outranks format in `getForYouReason`'s priority order), while the other 3 live matches -- not involving RCB -- correctly stayed at "Popular in T20". Confirmed the same 4-match "for you" set as sequence 1, unchanged -- only wording differed per match. Additionally verified the Follow-sheet's own explicit-reclassification behavior directly: toggling the T20 checkbox off then back on in that same sheet correctly moved `"T20"` out of `defaultFormats` (leaving `defaultFormats: ["T20I","Hundred"]`) while `formats` kept all three -- and the T20-qualified badges immediately switched from "Popular in T20" to "Because you follow T20" on the very next render, confirming the reclassification wiring in `FollowSheet.tsx`'s `toggle()` works end-to-end on the deployed build, not just in the isolated unit test above.

Zero console errors across all sequences (`onlyErrors` filter returned none after each). The "for you" match set (`ipl2026-l2-rcbvcsk`, `ipl2026-m37-kkrvmi`, `ipl2026-l3-gtvrr`, `psl-2026-lah-kar-live` for the format-driven case, plus `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` for the explicit-India case) was identical across every sequence and every wording change -- confirming targeting itself never moved, only the reason text.

## Onboarding quiz: adding the missing "Skip" control (v1.0.181, 2026-08-10)

**The bug, confirmed directly.** Onboarding has three steps before the reveal screen: team-picker, player-picker, then a 3-question cricket-persona quiz ("Bazball or backs-to-the-wall?", "Chasing 200 in 20 overs, or defending a Test declaration?", "The final over of a T20 decider, or day 5 of a Test classic?"). Team-picker and player-picker each already have a top-right "Skip" link (`TeamPickerStep.tsx` line ~205, `PlayerPickStep.tsx` line ~103, both `text-xs font-bold text-text-dim` inside a `flex items-center justify-between px-1` row). The quiz had no such control on any of its 3 questions -- the only onboarding step a user could not bypass.

**The exact spec, honored point-for-point.** The user's instructions were fully specified, leaving nothing to interpretation: same style/position as the existing Skip links (not a new visual style); present on all 3 questions; skips only this step (matching the other two steps' Skip semantics exactly); a fixed, non-computed result regardless of how many of the 3 questions (0, 1, or 2) were answered before Skip was pressed; the reveal screen's label line ("YOUR CRICKET PERSONA") stays exactly as-is, with the title replaced by "All-Rounder" and the description by "You're here for the cricket, in whatever form it takes." -- both copied verbatim, not paraphrased; Share and Continue reuse the exact same mechanisms as the normal path; the existing 6-persona computed-result behavior for users who answer all 3 questions honestly must remain byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Implementation.** Two files: `lib/onboardingQuiz.ts` gained one new export, `SKIP_PERSONA`, a `Persona` object holding the fixed skip-path copy -- deliberately kept separate from `PERSONA_TABLE` (which remains untouched, all 6 entries, all 6 personas including "Boundary Hunter" exactly as before) since this result is never computed, never varies, and isn't a 7th cell in that mapping table. `components/onboarding/QuizStep.tsx` gained a `skipQuiz()` handler (`setPersona(SKIP_PERSONA)` -- nothing else, no branching on how many `answers` were already collected) and a Skip link added to the question-header row, using the identical className string already used by the other two steps' Skip links, copied character-for-character rather than approximated. The persona-reveal JSX itself needed zero changes: it already read `persona.name`/`persona.description` generically (never hardcoded to a specific persona), so `SKIP_PERSONA` flows through the exact same label/title/description markup, the exact same `sharePersona()` share-text template, and the exact same `onComplete` prop call as any computed persona.

**Why no formatTags persistence for the skip path.** The normal path calls `persistFormatTags(result)` (writing the persona's format tags into `FollowPrefs.formats`) at the moment the 3rd answer is given, before `persona` state is even set. The skip path never calls `computePersona()` or `persistFormatTags()` at all -- `SKIP_PERSONA.formatTags` is an empty array and is simply never read. This wasn't a judgment call requiring elaboration in the spec: skipping the quiz means no answers were meaningfully given, so there's nothing genuine to persist, and the spec didn't ask for one to be invented.

**Non-regression, verified by inspection.** `TeamPickerStep.tsx` and `PlayerPickStep.tsx` -- zero lines touched (grep-confirmed identical `Skip` call sites/behavior). `PERSONA_TABLE`/`computePersona()` -- zero lines touched. `OnboardingFlow.tsx`'s progress bar, `RevealStep.tsx` (the separate "Building your feed..." transition after the persona reveal), `FirstSessionQuest`, `PersonaParticles`, and every other previously-shipped onboarding feature -- none referenced by this change, none touched.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean.

**Doc sync addendum -- live verification results (2026-08-10, same day, v1.0.181 deployed).** Ran all 4 required sequences against the deployed build at `bawler-gold.vercel.app/onboarding`, driving real clicks against the actual DOM buttons (not simulated events), reading back `document.body.innerText` after each step to confirm real state transitions:

1. Fresh onboarding, skip team, skip players, Skip immediately on question 1 (0 answers given) -> reveal screen read verbatim: "YOUR CRICKET PERSONA / All-Rounder / You're here for the cricket, in whatever form it takes. / Share / Continue". Share was clicked (invoked the pre-existing, unmodified `sharePersona()` -- `navigator.share`/clipboard mechanism, present and callable, no console error). Continue was clicked and correctly proceeded through the same "Building your feed..." transition into the homepage (`location.href` confirmed landing on `/`) -- identical to the normal completion path.
2. Fresh onboarding, answered question 1 ("Bazball -- attack from ball one"), then clicked Skip on question 2 -> reveal screen showed the exact same "All-Rounder" result, not a persona derived from the single aggressive-leaning answer.
3. Fresh onboarding, answered questions 1 and 2 ("Bazball...", "Chasing 200 in 20"), then clicked Skip on question 3 -> reveal screen again showed the exact same "All-Rounder" result, confirming the fixed result holds regardless of how many of the 3 questions were answered before Skip.
4. Fresh onboarding, answered all 3 questions honestly with no Skip used at all, using the same three answers from the original bug report (Bazball, Chasing 200 in 20 overs, Final over of a T20 decider) -> reveal screen read verbatim: "YOUR CRICKET PERSONA / Boundary Hunter / You want fours, sixes, and chases down to the last over." -- byte-for-byte identical to the pre-existing computed result, confirming zero regression to `PERSONA_TABLE`/`computePersona()`.

Zero console errors across all 4 sequences (`onlyErrors` filter returned none after the full run).

## Round 6: the visibility fix holds; the new report is a race, not a visibility bug (v1.0.179/v1.0.180, 2026-08-10)

**The new report, and why it points somewhere new.** After v1.0.178 shipped and was verified (11/11 cycles, both matches, zero failures, confirmed independently by the product owner too -- their own check of the wrapper's opacity came back unconditionally 1, every time), the user asked the product owner directly, twice, what he still sees. His answers: (1) the Live tab "keeps looking fine sometimes, but also broken sometimes" -- intermittent, not constant; (2) "on switching tabs, live tab is loading with some delay" -- a real, noticeable render delay; (3) asked whether the broken state self-corrects or stays stuck: "both happen." That third answer is the important one -- a pure visibility bug (a wrapper stuck at `opacity: 0`) would be consistently stuck or consistently fine, not inconsistent about it. "Both happen" is the signature of a race: something that usually finishes in time but occasionally doesn't, depending on timing that varies switch to switch. The user's own hypothesis, to verify or rule out: the wrapper being visible doesn't mean its content (pitch fill, gradients, trajectory) has actually finished rendering -- and if something that populates that content can be interrupted or orphaned by a remount, the result could be a visible-but-empty scene.

**What the code actually contains.** Read `components/BallGIF.tsx` in full, specifically hunting for the failure mode the user described (an async operation -- data fetch, effect-driven state update -- that could write into a superseded mount without a cancellation guard). There isn't one. Every pixel of the scene (pitch geometry, gradients, trajectory curves, avatar initials) is computed synchronously in `BowlerView`/`OverheadView`'s render body directly from props already in hand -- no `fetch`, no `useEffect` that sets state after an async delay, nothing to cancel. This rules out the "uncancelled async write" half of the hypothesis as structurally inapplicable to this specific file: there is no async write for a stale mount to make.

**The concrete, testable half of the hypothesis.** The SVG-id-collision half is real and checkable. `BowlerView`/`OverheadView` define gradients and paths with fixed, hardcoded `id`s (`pitchB`, `ballB`, `pre-B`, `post-B`, `fieldO`, `ballO`, `shotPath`) referenced via `fill="url(#id)"` and `href="#id"` -- these resolve globally against the whole document, not scoped to their own `<svg>`. If a tab-switch remount of the Live tab's whole subtree (`key={tab}` in `MatchView.tsx`) ever landed at the same instant as the independent ~3-second bowler/overhead clip-swap remount inside `BallGIF.tsx` (its own `key={`${activeClip}-${ball.id}`}`), two instances briefly defining the same ids could transiently coexist, and a reference could resolve to the wrong or about-to-be-removed element -- exactly a failure that would sometimes finish correctly and sometimes not, depending on timing. Code-level reasoning suggested this shouldn't actually be reachable in this app today (only one `BallGIF` is ever mounted, `useTabSwitcher.ts`'s single-state design makes tab remounts synchronous with no overlap window, and the only other SVG-using components on the page -- `MiniBallGIF`, `MomentStoryCard` -- use no ids or a disjoint `sc-`-prefixed namespace) -- but reasoning about a race is not the same as testing for one, so it was tested directly rather than argued away.

**v1.0.179 -- diagnostic-only, no fix yet.** Added `useSvgIdCollisionCheck`, called from both `BowlerView` and `OverheadView`, running in a `useLayoutEffect` (synchronously, right after DOM commit, before paint -- the earliest and most reliable point to observe a real collision if one exists) on every mount. It checks `document.querySelectorAll('[id="..."]')` for each of that view's ids and logs a count, with a loud `ID COLLISION` warning if any id resolves to more than one element, or a `MISSING` warning if it resolves to zero. Deliberately a different signal than v1.0.178's `[scene-debug]` opacity logs (already proven always correct) -- this tests something opacity can't. No ids were changed in this version; the fixed shared names were kept so the diagnostic build tests the real, as-shipped mechanism.

**Reproduction: rapid, irregular, boundary-targeted -- not the evenly-paced cycles from prior rounds.** The user was explicit that prior rounds' clean, evenly-spaced test cycles were the wrong shape to catch a race, and that switches needed to be irregular and specifically timed to straddle the clip-swap boundary. A first attempt using the browser-automation tool's native click simulation (20 clicks in one batched sequence) produced a suspicious result: the `[content-debug]` mount timestamps showed an unbroken, near-perfectly-regular ~3-second cadence for the entire test window, which is what the clip-swap timer alone produces -- not what genuine irregular tab-switching (with waits from 150ms to 3.6s) should produce, which would show irregular unmount/remount gaps. Rather than trust that result, it was checked directly: an isolated single click confirmed the Score button click handler does work, but a `data-active-tab` read taken through the batched sequence's exact click coordinates could not independently confirm every one of the 20 batched clicks actually landed as a real tab change (batched native-input clicks fired back-to-back gave no per-click ground truth). To remove that doubt entirely, the test was redone by finding the actual tab `<button>` DOM nodes directly (`document.querySelectorAll('button')` filtered by label) and driving the whole sequence from an in-page async script that clicks a button, immediately logs `{time, activeTab}` to a page-global array, awaits an irregular delay (150ms-3.6s, repeating twice per full match run), and repeats 20 times. This gives an unambiguous, timestamped ground truth for every single switch, independent of the automation layer's click-dispatch timing. Run on both matches (`ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live`, `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live`): the logged `activeTab` values confirmed all 40 switches (20 per match) genuinely alternated `live` <-> `scorecard` at very close to the intended irregular timing.

**Result: zero collisions found, despite genuine, verified, boundary-targeted reproduction.** Across all 40 confirmed-real switches, `[content-debug]` never logged a single `ID COLLISION` or `MISSING` warning, and `read_console_messages` with `onlyErrors: true` found zero console errors on either match. This is not "we didn't try hard enough" -- the reproduction was specifically redesigned mid-investigation, after the first attempt's result looked suspicious, specifically to guarantee every switch was real and to target the exact race window the user described.

**The fix shipped anyway (v1.0.180), and why.** Per the user's explicit instruction #2 ("if IDs aren't guaranteed unique per mounted instance, make them unique"), `useId()` was applied to both `BowlerView` and `OverheadView` to suffix every one of their 7 ids with a per-instance-unique value, and every `fill="url(#...)"`/`href="#..."` reference was updated to match. This is deliberately framed as hardening, not as "the fix for the bug that was found" -- no live collision was ever observed under the old fixed ids in this environment. But duplicate global SVG ids are invalid regardless of whether this specific app's current architecture happens to avoid triggering the failure mode today; making them unique removes the theoretical risk permanently and costs nothing (no visual or behavioral change). The collision-check instrumentation was kept in place, now checking the new suffixed ids, so it continues to serve as a live tripwire rather than being removed the moment it stopped finding anything.

**What this leaves open, stated plainly.** The product owner's underlying complaint -- intermittent, sometimes-self-correcting content issues after a tab switch, with a noticeable load delay -- has not been fully explained by anything found in this file. The most likely remaining explanation, given everything ruled out so far (opacity: proven always 1; async population: doesn't exist; SVG id collision: tested directly and not found despite deliberate targeting) is real-device rendering/paint latency: a full SVG remount (BowlerView alone has ~30 elements including several `<animateMotion>` children) taking a visible, sometimes-longer-than-instant amount of time to paint on a real, slower phone under real conditions (thermal throttling, background app pressure, a weaker GPU) -- something this fast, otherwise-idle cloud automation environment cannot reproduce or falsify. This is stated as an honest known-unknown, not a diagnosis: if the product owner can share their device model, or a screen recording of the delay, or (ideally) a browser performance trace from the moment of a stuck instance, that would be the next concrete lead, rather than continuing to guess at code-level causes that keep testing negative.

**Environment honesty, unchanged.** Still Claude-in-Chrome automation, not a genuinely foregrounded device -- unchanged from every prior round. What's new this round is that the reproduction methodology itself was hardened mid-investigation (moving from batch-simulated clicks to a verified, timestamped in-page script) specifically because the first attempt's suspicious uniformity could have produced a false negative; the final result is trusted because every one of the 40 switches has independent, logged proof it was a real tab change, not because more attempts were made.

**Safety process.** Continued on the existing session; `components/BallGIF.tsx` only (plus version/doc files) across both v1.0.179 (diagnostic) and v1.0.180 (hardening fix) -- no changes to `MatchView.tsx`, `useTabSwitcher.ts`, or any CSS/animation logic from prior rounds.

## Round 5: architecture change replacing four rounds of patches with a visible-by-default scene (v1.0.178, 2026-08-10)

**The report that ended the patch cycle.** The user personally reproduced v1.0.177 on their own test: switched Live -> Score -> Live, left the app completely untouched for 15 continuous seconds while screenshotting throughout -- stayed visually broken for the entire window, no recovery. They then inspected the specific safety net v1.0.177 added (the setTimeout watchdog meant to force `animation: none; opacity: 1` after 400ms) and found the scene element had NO inline `style` attribute at all after 15+ stuck seconds -- not a late application, not a wrong element, the watchdog never ran. The user's diagnosis: this is the fifth round on the same bug (v1.0.174 -> v1.0.175 -> v1.0.176 -> v1.0.177, each reported fixed or improved, each still broken in practice), and the actual root problem was never any one of the four specific bugs fixed so far -- it's that the architecture makes real content invisible by default and depends on an animation, a flag, or a timer to ever make it visible. Any single link in that chain failing, for any reason (a throttled tab, a missed effect, a remount at the wrong instant), leaves the user staring at broken content with no fallback. The user explicitly instructed: stop patching the symptom, change the architecture so it can't fail this way again, add real console-log instrumentation instead of relying on narrative verification, and be explicit if a genuinely foregrounded test environment still isn't available.

**Accepting the diagnosis in full.** This entry does not re-litigate the mechanism -- the user's structural diagnosis is correct and is adopted without a counter-argument. The prior four rounds all shared the same shape: `.scene-fade-in`'s resting state was `opacity: 0`, and each fix added a new way to try to guarantee the browser would move it to `1` (a fill-mode, a suppression flag with a window, a JS-forced correction). Four independent guarantees, each shown to fail in a different way, is itself the signal that guaranteeing correctness on top of an invisible-by-default element is the wrong shape for this problem.

**The architecture change -- `components/BallGIF.tsx`.** Removed outright, not superseded-but-left-in-place: the `.scene-fade-in` `@keyframes` animation and CSS class, the `skipEntranceAnimation` prop, `suppressFirstFadeRef` and its 320ms-window effect, and the `sceneNodeRef`/400ms `setTimeout` watchdog. The scene div now has exactly one static class (`"absolute inset-0"`) and no opacity-affecting style at all. Its resting state -- the very first synchronous paint, before any `useEffect` has had a chance to run -- is the browser's own default `opacity: 1`. Nothing needs to complete, fire, reattach, or be correctly suppressed for the content to be visible; there is no longer a "correct" and "broken" path for visibility to diverge along, because visibility isn't derived from any process at all. Per the user's explicit instruction, if an entrance flourish is ever wanted again, it would have to animate FROM a lower value UP to 1, triggered explicitly and only as a bonus after mount -- never start at 0 as a dependency. No such flourish was reintroduced: after five rounds of failures in exactly this space, inventing a sixth "safe" version of the same idea was judged not worth the risk for what is a minor cosmetic flourish, so it was simply removed.

**Cleanup, not stacking.** Per the explicit instruction to check for anything left behind from the previous four rounds: grepped both `components/BallGIF.tsx` and `components/MatchView.tsx` for every symbol introduced by v1.0.175-177 (`skipEntranceAnimation`, `suppressFirstFadeRef`, `sceneNodeRef`, `hasShownLiveSceneRef`) -- zero occurrences remain in either file. `app/globals.css`'s `.scene-fade-in` keyframe/class was deleted outright (grep-confirmed it had no other caller). `MatchView.tsx`'s `hasShownLiveSceneRef` ref and its effect, and the `skipEntranceAnimation={hasShownLiveSceneRef.current}` prop passed to `<BallGIF>`, were removed since nothing downstream reads that prop anymore.

**Temporary debug instrumentation, per explicit request.** Added `console.log` calls tagged `[scene-debug]` in `components/BallGIF.tsx`: one fires on every scene mount (`ball` id + `activeClip`), and a second fires ~50ms later reporting the scene element's actual computed `opacity` and whether any `scene-fade-in`-named class is present on it. Expected output, unconditionally, every single time: `opacity=1 hasAnimationClass=false`. These are explicitly temporary and flagged for removal once confirmed fixed on a real device -- they exist so behavior can be checked from real console output rather than either side's narrative interpretation.

**Environment honesty, unchanged and stated plainly.** This session's Claude-in-Chrome automation tab cannot be brought to a genuinely foregrounded, non-backgrounded state -- that limitation has not changed and this round does not claim otherwise. What changes is that the fix no longer depends on foreground/background timing, throttling, or any other timing-sensitive condition at all: the scene's default visibility is a static property of the rendered markup (no opacity-affecting rule of any kind, verifiable by reading the class list and computed style at any instant, with zero timing sensitivity), not a race that could resolve differently in a real user's tab versus this automation's tab. The remaining honest gap is the same as every prior round: final confirmation that this resolves the real-device report can only come from the product owner's own device.

**Verified.** `tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` clean.

Ran 6 cycles on `ind-eng-test-2026-d3-live` (4 were the literal Live->Score->Live path; 2 were Live->Info->Live and Live->Digest->Live, run by way of an early coordinate slip, then kept as bonus alternate-path coverage) and 5 cycles on `ind-aus-t20i-2026-m2-live` (all literal Live->Score->Live). Total: 11 cycles, 11 passes, 0 failures. Every single `resting state` log line across both matches -- several hundred individual scene mounts, since each cycle's 15-second window naturally covered multiple ball/clip remounts as the mock feed advanced -- read `opacity=1 hasAnimationClass=false`. Direct DOM checks on both matches independently confirmed `{ className: "absolute inset-0", inlineStyle: null, opacity: "1" }` on the live scene node, matching the console output. Zero console errors were observed on either match (`onlyErrors` filter returned none). A screenshot taken on the T20 match during this pass showed the lower section (partnership row, matchup selector, win-prob, Moments cards) rendering visually washed-out while the ball-tracking scene and its overlay card rendered crisply in the same frame -- this is the same permanent screenshot-vs-DOM discrepancy documented in every prior round of this saga (this Claude-in-Chrome tab is permanently `document.hidden: true`, and its screenshots do not reliably reflect the tab's real computed state); a follow-up computed-style sweep of the live tab root and its descendants found the root at `opacity: 1` and confirmed the only sub-1 opacities anywhere in the subtree were intentional low-opacity SVG decoration (trajectory dots/strokes in the 0.4-0.9 range), not the six regression-checked elements. Environment honesty is unchanged from every prior round: this remains Claude-in-Chrome automation, not a genuinely foregrounded device, and that was not achieved this round either -- what changed is that the fix's correctness no longer depends on foreground/background timing at all, so the console-log and computed-style evidence gathered here is not subject to the same doubt that timing-sensitive fixes were.
